# UNCAWE work plan

This document records the state of the UNCAWE implementation and what to implement next. It is not a schedule of the extensions: everything in [[extensions]] is a research proposal, and an extension enters this plan only when the user adds a corresponding card to the Part III board. The document is the tracker of all implementation and experimentation work of the project.

The document has five parts:

- **Part I:** what is implemented and reported in the [reference paper](papers/02__Uncertainty_Aware_Classifier_Accuracy_Prediction______QCDS_2026_.pdf);
- **Part II:** what is implemented in the code but not reported in the paper;
- **Part III:** the work tracker: a board of scheduled, in-progress and implemented-but-not-evaluated work, chosen by the user, and the record of completed cards;
- **Part IV:** pending fixes and known divergences between the paper and the code, which are prerequisites rather than next steps;
- **Part V:** implementation conventions for experiments, methods, results, tables and plots.

Status labels: **[paper]** implemented and reported in the paper; **[code-only]** implemented, not in the paper; **[fix]** pending correction. Work in progress is tracked by the statuses of the Part III board. Code paths are relative to `src/uncawe/` unless stated otherwise. Code names and paper names differ; the mapping is in §I.2.

Notation follows the paper: $C_{ij}=P(\hat Y=\omega_i\mid Y=\omega_j)$ and $T=C\operatorname{diag}(\pi)$, with predicted classes on rows and true classes on columns. `cap-ml` and the code store the transpose (true classes on rows).

# Part I: Implemented and reported in the paper

Everything in this part is **[paper]**. It is produced by the `main` experiment (`experiments/main.py`, entry point `uncawe.main`).

## I.1 Protocol

- **Datasets.** UCI datasets accessible through QuaPy: the 5 largest binary datasets among a list of native binary datasets and the 10 largest multiclass datasets (`experiments/datasets.py:get_filtered_datasets`).
- **Classes.** QuaPy drops the classes of a multiclass collection with fewer than $100$ instances; since FIX-8 (2026-11-12), a class is also dropped if it has fewer than $k=10$ expected instances in $V$, i.e. the threshold is $\max(100,\lceil 2k\,n/n_{\text{train}}\rceil)$ (`pretrain/uci.py`, `cap-ml` `93ae246`); on the datasets of `main` this changes only `poker_hand`, from $K=8$ to $K=7$ (threshold $820$; its smallest class has about $18$ instances in $V$). The paper's `poker_hand` rows ($K=8$) are not comparable with the results after FIX-8; the old results are in `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main_pre_fix8/`.
- **Splits.** Each dataset $D$ is split with stratification into a training part (70%) and a test pool $U$ (30%); the training part is split again into $L$ (50%), used to train the classifier $h$, and $V$ (50%), used to train the CAP methods. The splits come from the `cap-ml` fetchers (`fetch_UCIBinaryDataset`, `fetch_UCIMulticlassDataset`) and are shared by all methods.
- **Classifiers.** LR, kNN, SVM (RBF kernel), MLP and RF from scikit-learn with default hyperparameters (`pretrain/classic.py`). Their posteriors on $V$ and $U$ are computed once and stored under `<CAP_DATA>/pretrain/<domain>/` (`pretrain/data.py:PretrainInfo`).
- **Target bags.** 100 bags with $\lvert U_i\rvert=1000$, drawn with replacement from $U$ at prevalence vectors sampled uniformly from the simplex. The paper calls this APP; the code uses QuaPy's `UPP` with `random_state=qp.environ["_R_SEED"]=0` (`pretrain/data.py:DatasetBundle.with_test_data`).
- **Estimand.** Every method is scored against the realised contingency table of each target bag (`true_cts`), not against the population table $C\operatorname{diag}(\pi^Q)$.

## I.2 Methods

| Code name (`experiments/methods.py`) | Paper name | Class | Interval | Configuration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `DoC` | DoC | `BootstrapDirectCAP(DoC)` | bootstrap of the target bag | 1000 bootstrap draws |
| `QuAcc_NxN` | QuAcc (QuAcc-K×K) | `BootstrapCTCAP(QuAccNxN)` | bootstrap of the target bag | KDEy(MLP); extra features `add_maxconf`, `add_negentropy`, `add_maxinfsoft`; 1000 draws |
| `O-LEAP` | LEAP (O-LEAP) | `BootstrapCTCAP(O_LEAP)` | bootstrap of the target bag | KDEy(MLP); 1000 draws |
| `L-CBPE` | L-CBPE | `CBPE(calib_method="lascal")` | Poisson-binomial | binary datasets only |
| `SQS-H` | SQS-H | `RQBS(bootstrap=False)` | percentile of validation bags | PACC(MLP) surrogate trained on $V$; one estimate $\hat\pi^Q=q(U)$, $m=1000$ bags of size 1000 drawn from $V$ at $\hat\pi^Q$ (added by FIX-2, §III.3; run in `main` on 2026-11-12) |
| `SQS-Hbs` | — (SQS-H with a bootstrap of $\hat\pi^Q$; called `RQBS` until 2026-11-10) | `RQBS(bootstrap=True)` | percentile of validation bags | PACC(MLP) surrogate trained on $V$; $m=1000$ bootstrap resamples of $U$, one estimate per resample and one bag of size 1000 drawn from $V$ at each |
| `PQ` | SQS-S | `PrediQuant` | percentile of selected validation bags | PACC(MLP) trained on $V_1$; $n=10{,}000$ candidates from $V_2$, $\gamma=0.1$, $L_1$ distance; $m=1000$ |
| `BayesCAP` | BayCAP | `BayCAP(estimand="population")` (`methods/baycap.py`, §II.5), a copy of `cap-ml`'s `ACC_BayesCAP` with the same draws | posterior credible interval | NUTS, 500 warm-up iterations, 1000 draws, uniform Dirichlet priors |

**Stored `BayesCAP` results.** The stored `BayesCAP` results used by the paper were computed before `main` moved to CPU, on a platform the logs do not record (probably the GPU). By the user's decision of 2026-10-22, `BayesCAP` was recomputed on CPU by the user on 2026-10-22 (`bcuda`, commit `a23fb97`, `main --classic --methods BayesCAP`, 21:57–22:43, exit code 0), so that all BayCAP results come from one setting; the stored results under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main/` are now the CPU ones (E2.1, E2.2 and E2.3 were generated on them), and the old ones are kept in `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main_22_10_26/` (a full copy of `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main/` made by the user on 2026-10-22, all methods). The recomputed results differ from the old ones as different draws of a single NUTS chain do (§II.9, `scripts/bench/xla_vs_stored.py`: on 5 pairs, coverage changed by at most $0.05$), so the paper's tables change slightly; the tables and rank tables of `main` and the coverage vs amplitude plot were generated again by the user on 2026-10-27, on the CPU results and with `BayesCAP-EB-real` (§II.5).

**`BayesCAP` from `BayCAP`.** Since WP-6 `main` builds `BayesCAP` from `methods/baycap.py:BayCAP` instead of `ACC_BayesCAP`, so that it stores the NUTS diagnostics of every bag (§II.5); for the same seed the two give identical draws (`tests/test_baycap.py`). The stored `BayesCAP` results were recomputed with `BayCAP` on 2026-10-26 (WP-6, §II.14), with the NUTS diagnostics; the rerun reproduces the CPU results of 2026-10-22 (identical coverage on every pair, Winkler scores within $2\cdot10^{-15}$), so the paper's artefacts are unaffected by it.

**SQS-H and SQS-Hbs (FIX-2).** The paper's SQS-H (§4.1) estimates the target prevalence once, $\hat\pi^Q=q(U)$, and draws the $m$ bags from $V$ at $\hat\pi^Q$. The method run by `main` until 2026-11-10 as `RQBS` estimates it on each of $m$ bootstrap resamples of $U$ and draws one bag at each estimate $\hat\pi^{*(r)}$, so that its bags also carry the sampling variability of $\hat\pi^Q$. Since FIX-2 (§III.3, user's decision of 2026-11-10) `cap-ml`'s `RQBS` has a parameter `bootstrap` (since `cap-ml` 0.1.1.13), `main` runs both, as `SQS-H` (`bootstrap=False`) and `SQS-Hbs` (`bootstrap=True`, the former `RQBS`, renamed in the code and in the stored results of `main`, `main_pre_fix3` and `binform`), and the main tables, the rank tables, the coverage vs amplitude plot and the error split show both. Both were run on the $75$ pairs of `main` on 2026-11-12 (`bcuda`; `poker_hand` by the run of FIX-8, the other pairs from `faee147`, `cap-ml` `93ae246`, which differs from `637fdd5` only in `fetch_UCIMulticlassDataset`). The paper's SQS-H figures come from the bootstrap variant.

**Seeded quantifiers (FIX-3).** Since FIX-3 (§III.3), the MLPs of the quantifiers of `QuAcc_NxN`, `O-LEAP` (KDEy(MLP), `kdey()`), `SQS-H`, `SQS-Hbs` and `PQ` (PACC(MLP), `pacc()`) are built with `random_state=qp.environ["_R_SEED"]` (`experiments/methods.py`); since `cap-ml` `47643b1` (version 0.1.1.11), `RQBS` draws the bootstrap of the target prevalence (with `bootstrap=True`) and its bags sequentially from the NumPy state seeded by its `random_state` (`qp.environ["_R_SEED"]`), without `AggregativeBootstrap`. The stored results of `QuAcc_NxN`, `O-LEAP`, `SQS-Hbs` and `PQ` in `main` were re-run with the seeded code on `bcuda` (`cap-ml` `93ae246`): `SQS-Hbs` and `PQ` on 2026-11-12, `QuAcc_NxN` and `O-LEAP` from 2026-11-12 to 2026-11-13 (`50f17df`), `poker_hand` by the run of FIX-8; the unseeded ones are kept in `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main_pre_fix3/`. They differ from the unseeded ones within the run-to-run variation of each method, and the paper's figures for QuAcc, LEAP, SQS-H and SQS-S come from the unseeded runs. The tables of `main`, the rank tables, the coverage vs amplitude plot and the error split (§II.20) were regenerated on 2026-11-13 from `50f17df`.

All methods return 1000 performance samples per bag. The point estimate is their mean, and the interval is the empirical $[\alpha/2,1-\alpha/2]$ percentile interval with $1-\alpha=0.95$ (`CONFIDENCE_LEVEL`). The table above describes the code; where it differs from the paper's definitions is listed in Part IV.

**`PQ` after FIX-1.** Since `cap-ml` `e9e77cd` (pinned by FIX-1 + FIX-4, §III.2), `PrediQuant` follows the paper's SQS-S: it splits $V$ (stratified, 50/50) into $V_1$ and $V_2$, trains the PACC(MLP) quantifier on $V_1$ only, and draws the candidate bags from $V_2$; the prevalences of the candidate bags and of the target are both estimated by the quantifier's own pipeline (the MLP, then the PACC aggregation), and $h$ is used only for the contingency tables of the bags. Before, the quantifier was trained on all of $V$, and the prevalence of each candidate bag was estimated by applying the PACC aggregation, fitted on the MLP's posteriors, to the posteriors of $h$, while the target's was estimated with the MLP, so the nearest-bag selection compared two different estimators. The stored `PQ` results of `main` were re-run with the fixed version on 2026-11-09 (`bcuda`, commit `32df5c4`, `cap-ml` `e9e77cd`, `main --classic --methods PQ`, 15:08–15:55, exit status $0$, $75$ pairs × $3$ metrics); the old ones are kept in `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main_pq_pre_fix1/` (same tree as `main/`). They are not comparable with the new ones, and the paper's SQS-S figures come from the old version. The results of `predist` (§II.3) come from the old version and have not been re-run. Since `cap-ml` `87b4d9b` (version 0.1.1.10, pinned by WP-14, §III.2), `PrediQuant` is a contingency-table method: its range is the tables of the selected candidate bags and `main` stores their mean as `estim_cts`; the selection is unchanged. The stored `PQ` results of `main` were re-run with it on 2026-11-10 (`bcuda`, commit `a9ea4ed`, `main --classic --methods PQ`, 00:20–00:35, exit status $0$, $225$ files); the FIX-1 ones are kept in `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main_pq_pre_wp14/`. The two runs agree on the test bags and differ in the estimates within the run-to-run variation of `PQ`, whose quantifier then used an unseeded MLP (seeded since FIX-3); the tables of `main`, the rank tables and the coverage vs amplitude plot were regenerated with them on 2026-11-10 (card WP-14). Since `cap-ml` `0f25e9e` (version 0.1.1.12, WP-15), `PrediQuant` has an optional parameter `rho`, the share of $V$ that trains the quantifier (§II.21); `PQ` uses `rho=None`, the split above, so its results are unchanged.

## I.3 Evaluation

- **Point estimate:** absolute error $\operatorname{AE}=\lvert A(h,U_i)-\hat A(h,U_i)\rvert$ (`acc_err`).
- **Interval:** coverage, amplitude and Winkler score $W_i=(u_i-\ell_i)+\frac{2}{\alpha}\big(\max\{0,\ell_i-a_i\}+\max\{0,a_i-u_i\}\big)$ at $\alpha=0.05$, computed after the run from the stored samples.
- **Metric:** vanilla accuracy only.

## I.4 Paper artefacts and the code that produces them

| Paper artefact | Code | Output |
|---|---|---|
| Table 1: average ranks of AE and $W$ per classifier, Wilcoxon $\alpha=0.05$, L-CBPE excluded | `tables/rank.py` | `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/rank/` |
| Appendix Table 2: mean AE and $W$ per dataset and classifier | `tables/main.py` | `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/main/` |
| Figure: coverage vs amplitude, one facet per method | `plots/amplitude_vs_coverage.py` | `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/main/amplitude_vs_coverage/` |

Since 2026-11-09 the default method sets of these three modules are the eight methods of the main table (§V.4), not those of the paper; the paper's artefacts are reproduced by the code at the paper's commit, or with `--methods` for the plot.

The BayCAP-KDEy of these artefacts and of the extended version below (`BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real`) is invalidated (user's decision of 2026-11-24; opening notice of Part II) and will be replaced by `BayCAP-KDEy-h-inst` (card WP-31) when the user asks for the reruns.

The tables and figures of the extended version of the paper are listed in `docs/paper.md` (user's decision of 2026-11-24): the list is `src/uncawe/paper.py:ARTEFACTS`, `make run-paper` regenerates them all on `bcuda` and collects them in `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/paper/` (`tables/`, `figures/` as pdf and png, `macros.tex`), and `make pull PULL_DIRS=paper` brings the folder back; tests: `tests/test_paper.py`. Since then `plots/shift_profile.py`, `plots/binform_side_prior.py`, `plots/pq_rho.py` and `plots/target_size_scores.py` also write a pdf (`--pdf`, default on).

## I.5 Reproduction

```bash
uv run python -m uncawe.pretrain.classic
uv run python -m uncawe.main --classic
uv run python -m uncawe.tables.main --classic --all
uv run python -m uncawe.tables.rank --classic --all
uv run python -m uncawe.plots.amplitude_vs_coverage --domain classic
```

# Part II: Implemented but not reported in the paper

Everything in this part is **[code-only]**.

**Invalidated results of BayCAP-KDEy (user's decision of 2026-11-24).** E3.4 of [[ext3_posterior_propagation]] (card WP-31) replaces the realised pushforward of BayCAP-KDEy by a per-instance one and its input classifier by $h$ (`BayCAP-KDEy-h-inst`). The stored results of `BayCAP-KDEy-h-real`, `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` and of the methods and experiments built on them no longer represent the BayCAP-KDEy method: §II.4 (E3.1, E3.2), §II.2 (E5.1, E5.4, E5.5), §II.20 (E4.1, E4.3), §II.24 (the comparisons of E3.3), §II.25 (E5.2), §II.26 (E4.2), §II.27 (E5.3), §II.29 (E7.1), §II.30 (E7.2), card WP-28 (E4.4), and the BayCAP-KDEy of the main tables, ranks and plots and of the paper artefacts (§I.4). They stay stored, and the findings below are kept as the record of the cell pushforward; anything that involves BayCAP-KDEy is rerun with the new method when needed, only on the user's request. The population methods, BayCAP-EMQ, the bags of SQS-B and the other methods are unaffected.\n
## II.1 Additional metrics in `main`

`main` also evaluates macro-F1 (F1 of the positive class on binary datasets) and macro-K (macro-averaged informedness; `k_bin` on binary datasets) (`experiments/metrics.py`). Results are stored under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main/<domain>/<acc_name>/`, and `tables/main.py` and `tables/rank.py` produce one table per metric. The paper reports neither metric. Contingency-table methods fit once and evaluate all three metrics on the same table samples; direct methods (`DoC`) are refitted per metric.

## II.2 The `binform` experiment

`experiments/binform.py` (entry point `uncawe.binform`) compares two BayCAP priors on `connect-4` (3 classes) with the five classifiers. Its 100 target bags have size 1000 and prevalences drawn from $\operatorname{Dir}(q)$ with $q_i=c(1-p_i)/(K-1)$, where $p$ is the validation prevalence and $c=10$ (`TEST_PRIOR_C`). Methods: `O-LEAP`, `SQS-Hbs` (called `RQBS` until 2026-11-10; stored results renamed by FIX-2), `BayesCAP_uniform` and `BayesCAP_transformed_val`, whose prior is the same transformation with $c=3$ (`BAYESCAP_PRIOR_C`). It writes the result schema of `main`. Tables: `tables/binform.py`; figure of the bag-generating and prior distributions on the ternary simplex: `plots/binform_simplex.py`.

**E5.1 (card WP-19).** With `--e51` (`make run-binform-e51`), `experiments/binform.py` runs E5.1 of [[ext5_baycap_priors_tempering]] instead of the configuration above, which is unchanged. Pairs: the $75$ pairs of `main` (`get_filtered_datasets()`, five classifiers). Per dataset, two means $\mu^\star,\mu^\dagger$ drawn from $\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1)$ (`e51_means`, generator `default_rng([_R_SEED, crc32(dataset), 0])`), redrawn until both are at $L_1$ distance at least $0.5$ from $\mathbf 1/K$ and from each other; $100$ requested prevalences from $G=\operatorname{Dir}(3K\mu^\star)$ (`e51_requested_prevs`, stream $1$), shared by the five classifiers and both bag sizes; bags of $\lvert U\rvert\in\{100,1000\}$ drawn from $U$ as above (seed `_R_SEED + bag index`). Methods (`E51_METHODS`): `BayesCAP-Hf-real` and `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real`, built as in `main` (`e51_method`; `fixed_c_method(..., prior=...)` of `experiments/methods.py`), each with the prior on $\pi^Q$ uniform (`_uniform`), $\operatorname{Dir}(3K\mu^\star)$ (`_well`) and $\operatorname{Dir}(3K\mu^\dagger)$ (`_wrong`); realised pushforward, scored against `true_cts`. All tasks run in the BayCAP pool, one pool per bag size; `e51_protocol` clears the caches of JAX after each task (`jax.clear_caches()`), since every bag compiles its own NUTS sampler and a long pool otherwise runs out of memory. Output: `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/binform_e51/u<size>/classic/<acc>/<dataset>/<classifier>/<method>.parquet`, with the schema of `main`, the NUTS diagnostics and posterior summaries of the two methods, and `bag_size`, `requested_prev`, `mu_star`, `mu_dagger`, `prior_alpha`. Tables: `tables/binform_prior.py` (per-pair CSV, summary CSV, one LaTeX summary per metric, with the groups of $K$ and the paired Wilcoxon tests); plots: `plots/binform_prior.py` (Winkler score of the uniform prior against each informative prior). Tests: `tests/test_binform_e51.py`.

- **Run:** `make run-binform-e51` on `bcuda` (`JAX=cpu`), 2026-11-14, commit `c7fa408`, `cap-ml` `93ae246`, no pilot; $900$ tasks ($75$ pairs, $6$ methods, $2$ bag sizes, $100$ bags each), three metrics, no task error; the run was resumed twice after the workers ran out of memory, which `jax.clear_caches()` now prevents (added after the run, `814ec61`; it does not change the results). Results under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/binform_e51/u100/` and `u1000/`; tables (`make run-tables-binform-prior ARGS=--tables`, commit `3ba7c59`) under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/binform_prior/`, plots of the three metrics (`make run-plot-binform-prior`, generated again locally on 2026-11-15 from `093732d`) under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/binform_prior/`. Every score is against the realised table of the target bag.
- **Findings** (the Analysis section of E5.1 in [[ext5_baycap_priors_tempering]]): the well-specified prior lowers the mean Winkler score of both methods at both bag sizes and on the three metrics (vanilla accuracy, BayCAP-Hf at $\lvert U\rvert=100$: $0.237\to0.190$), mostly by recentring the interval (amplitudes $1$–$8\%$ narrower); the gain is concentrated on `connect-4`, `poker_hand` and `wine-quality`, where the uniform prior undercovers because of an error in the prevalence, and is not significant over the $15$ datasets. The misspecified prior costs several times more than the right one gains ($0.237\to0.636$), on largely the same datasets. The gain does not grow with $K$; on `german` the well-specified prior is slightly worse; on `mammographic`, where the error is in the recalls, it does not help.
- **Card:** WP-19 (§III.3).

**E5.4 (card WP-24).** With `--e54` (`make run-binform-e54`), `experiments/binform.py` runs E5.4 of [[ext5_baycap_priors_tempering]]; the default configuration and `--e51` are unchanged. Pairs: the $75$ pairs of `main`. Per dataset and distribution, $100$ requested prevalences from $G_c=\operatorname{Dir}(3\cdot\mathbf 1)$ (`centre`) or $G_e=\operatorname{Dir}(\tfrac13\mathbf 1)$ (`edge`) (`e54_requested_prevs`, generator `default_rng([_R_SEED, crc32(dataset), stream])`, streams $2$ and $3$), shared by the five classifiers and both bag sizes; bags of $\lvert U\rvert\in\{100,1000\}$ drawn from $U$ as in E5.1. Methods (`E54_METHODS`): `BayesCAP-Hf-real` and `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real`, built as in `main` (`_prior_method`), each with the prior on $\pi^Q$ uniform (`_uniform`), $\operatorname{Dir}(3\cdot\mathbf 1)$ (`_centre`) and $\operatorname{Dir}(\tfrac13\mathbf 1)$ (`_edge`); realised pushforward, scored against `true_cts`. The task body is shared with E5.1 (`_prior_task`), with `jax.clear_caches()` after each task. One BayCAP pool per (bag size, distribution). Output: `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/binform_e54/<distribution>/u<size>/classic/<acc>/<dataset>/<classifier>/<method>.parquet`, with the schema of `main`, the NUTS diagnostics and posterior summaries, and `bag_size`, `requested_prev`, `distribution`, `prior_alpha`. Tables: `tables/binform_centre_edge.py` (per-pair CSV, summary CSV, one LaTeX summary per metric; for each distribution the prior equal to it is the well-informed one and the other the ill-informed one; tests over the pairs and over the dataset means, groups of $K$, absent classes per bag, divergent bags); plots: `plots/binform_centre_edge.py` (per method and distribution, the Winkler score of the uniform prior against the well- and ill-informed priors, drawn by `plots/binform_prior.py:plot_winkler`). Macro-F1 is scored with the smoothed metric (Part IV FIX-6), which gives an absent, never predicted class $F_1=1/K$. Tests: `tests/test_binform_e54.py`.

- **Run:** `make run-binform-e54` on `bcuda` (`JAX=cpu`), 2026-11-18/19, commit `fb49895` (rebased on `devel` as `e2201e1`, same code), `cap-ml` `93ae246`, no pilot (user's decision); $1800$ tasks ($75$ pairs, $6$ methods, $2$ distributions, $2$ bag sizes, $100$ bags each), three metrics, no task error, no restart, about $15$ hours. Results under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/binform_e54/<distribution>/u<size>/`; tables (`make run-tables-binform-centre-edge ARGS=--tables`, commit `e2201e1`, PDFs built locally) under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/binform_centre_edge/`, plots of vanilla accuracy (`make run-plot-binform-centre-edge`, commit `e2201e1`) under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/binform_centre_edge/`. Every score is against the realised table of the target bag.
- **Findings** (the Analysis section of E5.4 in [[ext5_baycap_priors_tempering]]): the outcome is asymmetric, as predicted by the strength of the priors: the concentrated prior on sparse bags raises the mean Winkler score of BayCAP-Hf from $0.302$ to $0.536$ ($\lvert U\rvert=100$) and is worse than the uniform prior on all $15$ datasets, while every other cell moves by less than $0.07$. Knowing how extreme the shift is helps little ($\Delta W_{\text{know}}\le0.043$, less than knowing its direction in E5.1), and the wrong belief costs more than the right one gains in every cell. The cost on the accuracy is at small $K$, not on the absent classes at $K\ge8$; on macro-F1 and macro-K both the gain and the cost under the sparse distribution are several times larger. The sparse prior makes BayCAP-KDEy diverge on up to $22\%$ of the sparse bags; BayCAP-Hf diverges as with the uniform prior.
- **Card:** WP-24 (§III.3).

**E5.5 (card WP-27).** With `--e55` (`make run-binform-e55`), `experiments/binform.py` runs E5.5 of [[ext5_baycap_priors_tempering]]; the default configuration, `--e51` and `--e54` are unchanged (`--e55` is exclusive with them and takes no `--sizes`). Pairs: the $75$ pairs of `main`. Per dataset and distribution, $100$ requested prevalences from $G_c=\operatorname{Dir}(10\cdot\mathbf 1)$ (`centre`) or $G_e=\operatorname{Dir}(\tfrac1{10}\mathbf 1)$ (`edge`) (`E55_BAG_ALPHAS`, `e55_requested_prevs`, generator of E5.1 with streams $4$ and $5$), shared by the five classifiers; bags of $\lvert U\rvert=1000$ (`E55_SIZE`) drawn from $U$ as in E5.4. Methods (`E55_METHODS`): `BayesCAP-Hf-real`, `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` and `SQS-B` with the priors on $\pi^Q$ of E5.4 (suffixes `_uniform`, `_centre` $=\operatorname{Dir}(3\cdot\mathbf 1)$, `_edge` $=\operatorname{Dir}(\tfrac13\mathbf 1)$; `_prior_method`, which builds `SQS-B` with `experiments/methods.py:sqs_b(acc, prior=...)`, whose default `prior="uniform"` leaves `main` unchanged); `SQS-H`, `SQS-Hbs` and `PQ` (SQS-S), taken from `gen_methods()` as in `main`, with no suffix, once per distribution. Every method uses the realised table of each bag and is scored against `true_cts`. The task body is `_prior_task`, which now stores the NUTS diagnostics and posterior summaries only for methods that have them (all those of E5.1 and E5.4, so their results do not change), with `jax.clear_caches()` after each task. Per distribution, the methods without a prior run first in the pool of the regular methods of `main` (`N_JOBS` workers, `THREAD_LIMITS`), then the methods with a prior in the BayCAP pool. Output: `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/binform_e55/<distribution>/classic/<acc>/<dataset>/<classifier>/<method>.parquet`, with the schema of `main`, `bag_size`, `requested_prev`, `distribution`, `bag_alpha` and, for the methods with a prior, `prior_alpha` and the NUTS diagnostics and summaries. Tables: `tables/binform_side_prior.py` (per-bag, per-pair and summary CSVs, one LaTeX summary per metric with rows (distribution, group of $K$, method, role) and roles uniform / right side / wrong side / no prior; on the bags of a distribution the prior named after it is the right-side one; tests over the pairs and over the dataset means, absent classes, divergent bags; for each distribution the pairs stored for every method that has results, a method with a prior kept only with its three priors); plots: `plots/binform_side_prior.py` (violin figures per distribution, group of pairs (all, binary, multiclass; `PAIR_GROUPS`) and metric, with the AE and the Winkler score on a $\log_{10}$ scale floored at $10^{-5}$, of the per-pair means over the bags (`pair_violins`, `pair_means`; the pairs drawn as points; drawn by default) or of every bag (`bag_violins`, with `--figures bag-violins`); methods in the order SQS-S, SQS-H, SQS-Hbs, SQS-B, BayCAP-Hf, BayCAP-KDEy (`METHOD_ORDER`), each method with a prior with three violins, wrong side, uniform and right side, the uniform prior and the methods without a prior light and hatched; the median and the arithmetic mean of each violin marked, the mean of a pair violin being the value of the summary table; the density a Gaussian kernel estimate extended by two bandwidths beyond the extreme values, not below the floor (`violin_stats`), so the outline is not cut flat; and the scatter of E5.4 for the three methods with a prior, via `plots/binform_prior.py:plot_winkler`). Tests: `tests/test_binform_e55.py`.
- **Run:** `make run-binform-e55` on `bcuda` (`JAX=cpu`), 2026-11-19 20:44 to 2026-11-20 10:21, commit `9c67144` (E5.5 code of merge `739218c`), `cap-ml` `93ae246`, no pilot (user's decision); $1800$ tasks ($75$ pairs, $12$ methods, $2$ distributions, $100$ bags each), three metrics, exit $0$, all results stored. Results under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/binform_e55/<distribution>/`; tables (`make run-tables-binform-side-prior ARGS=--tables`, commit `5e6dcd9`, PDFs built locally) under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/binform_side_prior/`; plots of the three metrics under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/binform_side_prior/` (scatters on `bcuda`, commit `5e6dcd9`; bag and pair violins built locally, commit `695b2d5`). Every score is against the realised table of the target bag.
- **Findings:** not yet analysed; the Analysis section of E5.5 in [[ext5_baycap_priors_tempering]] is pending (the card was closed at the user's request after the tables and plots).
- **Card:** WP-27 (§III.3).

## II.3 The `predist` experiment

`experiments/predist.py` (with `predist_config.py`, `predist_io.py`, `predist_sampling.py`; entry point `uncawe.predist`) is a diagnostic of how the distance used by `PrediQuant` ($L_1$, Hellinger, Jensen–Shannon) changes the selected neighbourhood of validation bags. It uses the 3-class datasets in `THREE_CLASS_DATASETS`, the classifiers of `--classifiers` (default since WP-16: the five of `main`, `DEFAULT_CLASSIFIERS`; before, LR only), with all (dataset, classifier) pairs in one parallel pool with `THREAD_LIMITS`, and 3–5 test prevalences at chosen points of the simplex. Since WP-16 `PrediQuant` is built as `PQ` in `main` (`build_prediquant`: PACC(MLP) through `pacc()`, $\alpha=0.1$, `predict_train_prev=True`, `rho=None`); before, it used KDEy(MLP) (`kdey()`; its MLP is seeded with the experiment seed since FIX-3, while the stored results of schema 3 come from an unseeded one). Every score is against the realised table of the test bag. It has its own result schema (`RESULT_SCHEMA_VERSION = 4` since WP-16, which adds `quantifier`, `region` and `true_dist_l1_mean`, the mean $L_1$ distance between the true prevalences of the selected bags and that of the test bag), with atomic incremental writes, and ternary plots per sample (`plots/predist.py`). The summary tables of E6.1 are `tables/predist.py` (`make run-tables-predist`): a summary and a per-region table, with the two diagnostics `test_prev_l1_error` (the $L_1$ error of the estimated prevalence of the test bag) and `pred_dist_l1_mean` (the mean $L_1$ distance of the selected bags from it in estimated-prevalence space), computed from the stored prevalences; tests: `tests/test_predist.py`. The run of E6.1 and its findings are in §II.22; the results of schema 3 (LR, KDEy(MLP)) were moved on `bcuda` to `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/predist_kdey/` and `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/predist_kdey/`. It is not a comparison between CAP methods.

## II.4 BayCAP with a Bayesian quantifier: `BayCAPKDEy` and `BayCAPMAPLS`

`methods/baycap_kdey.py:BayCAPKDEy` (BayesKDEy) and `methods/baycap_mapls.py:BayCAPMAPLS` (BayesianMAPLS), on the common base `methods/baycap_quantifier.py:QuantifierBayCAP`, implement the methods of [[ext3_posterior_propagation]], E3.1 (tests: `tests/test_baycap_fixed.py`). Until WP-13 the eight methods were one class, `methods/baycap_fixed.py:BayCAPFixed` (WP-8, done), with a parameter `quantifier`; WP-13 split it into the two classes (user's decision of 2026-11-06), moving its code unchanged, and removed it: `tests/test_baycap_split.py` checks that the two classes give, for the same seeds, the tables, diagnostics and summaries of `BayCAPFixed` (a frozen copy at commit `55247a1`, `tests/_reference_baycap_fixed.py`) in the eight configurations, so the stored E3.1 results stay valid. (`BayCAPFixed` had itself replaced an earlier `methods/baycap_kdey.py:BayCAPKDEy`, BayesKDEy on an MLP with the population pushforward, which had no stored results; the current `BayCAPKDEy` is a different class.) `fit` measures the rate matrix of $h$ on $V$, $\hat C_{ij}=f_{ij}/n^s_j$ (no prior, not sampled), and fits a Bayesian quantifier; `predict_ct_range` samples the posterior of $\pi^Q$ with NUTS and pushes each draw through $\hat C$. Three factors: the quantifier, `BayCAPKDEy` with `c_prior="fixed"` (BayesKDEy: kernel densities of the outputs within each class on $V$, `kernel`, `bandwidth`, `shrinkage`, Dirichlet prior on $\pi$) or `BayCAPMAPLS` (BayesianMAPLS: the outputs read as calibrated probabilities, source prevalence from the labels of $V$, chain started at the EM estimate, ILR coordinates of $\pi$ from $\mathcal N(\mathbf 0,I)$, so a logistic-normal effective prior); `input_classifier`, `"h"` (the quantifier reads the `posteriors` of $h$ given to `fit` and `predict_ct_range`, and no classifier is trained) or `"mlp"` (an `MLPClassifier()` with `random_state`, its validation outputs cross-fitted on $5$ folds with `n_jobs=1`, then trained on all of $V$); `estimand`, `"population"` ($\hat C\operatorname{diag}(\pi^{(r)})$) or `"realised"` (`realised_tables` of §II.5, with $n^t$ the arg-max predictions of $h$ on the bag). `random_state` seeds NUTS, the MLP and the multinomial draws, so the two estimands of a cell share their prevalence draws; seeding the MLP solves the `BayCAPKDEy` item of FIX-3. Since WP-25 (Step 1) the quantifiers are classes of their own: `methods/bayesian_quantifier.py:BayesianQuantifier` holds the input classifier, the NUTS run, the diagnostics and `top_label_ece`, and its subclasses `methods/bayes_kdey.py:BayesKDEy` and `methods/bayes_mapls.py:BayesMAPLS` the two models; `QuantifierBayCAP` builds its quantifier from its own hyperparameters (`quantifier_`, through `_make_quantifier`) and keeps $\hat C$, the pushforward of a fixed $\hat C$ and `accuracy_moments` (before WP-25 all of it was in `QuantifierBayCAP`; `tests/test_wp25_restructure.py` checks against frozen copies of the classes before the restructure, `tests/_reference_wp25/`, that draws, tables, diagnostics and summaries are unchanged in every configuration, so the stored results stay valid); the NUTS models and their preparation (`bayes_kdey.py:kdey_model`, `bayes_mapls.py:emq_model`, `ilr`, `ilr_inverse`) are copied from QuaPy's `quapy.method._bayesian` (commit `31f36f6` of `devel`), because QuaPy builds `BayesianKDEy` and `BayesianMAPLS` only when `pystan` is installed and `pystan` is not in the environment (user's decision of 2026-11-03); the kernel densities are QuaPy's (`quapy.method._kdey.KDEBase`) and the EM start is QuaPy's `mapls`. A test restores the modules that QuaPy disables without `pystan` and checks that the draws equal those of QuaPy's `sample_from_posterior` for both quantifiers. After each `predict_ct_range` call, `last_nuts_diagnostics` returns the divergent transitions, the largest split-$\hat R$ and the smallest effective sample size of $\pi$ (`nuts_diagnostics` of §II.5), and `last_posterior_summaries` the vanilla-accuracy summaries `prev_mean` ($\bar\pi$), `acc_sd` ($\sigma_p$, the standard deviation of $\sum_j\hat C_{jj}\pi^{(r)}_j$), `sigma_g` ($\bar\sigma_g$ of §T.8 of [[ext3_posterior_propagation]], `accuracy_moments`) and `val_ece` (top-label ECE, $15$ bins, of the input classifier on its validation outputs, `top_label_ece`).

In `main`, the eight methods `BayCAP-<Q>-<s>` and `BayCAP-<Q>-<s>-real` ($Q\in\{$`KDEy`, `EMQ`$\}$, $s\in\{$`h`, `mlp`$\}$) are in `experiments/methods.py:FIXED_C_VARIANTS` (Gaussian kernel, bandwidth $0.1$, no shrinkage, $\tau=1$, the NUTS settings and default seed of the BayCAP methods) and run in the BayCAP pool, which now holds the methods of `BAYES_POOL_METHODS` (the BayCAP methods of §II.5 and these eight) instead of every method whose name contains `BayesCAP`; `main` stores their NUTS diagnostics and summaries per bag (§V.3). Since 2026-11-09 (user's decision) `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` is in the tables of `main` (`tables/main.py:MAIN_METHODS`), in its ranks and in the coverage vs amplitude plot, but not in its other default plots; the other seven are in no table, rank or default plot of `main` (`tables/main.py:EXCLUDED_METHODS`; from 2026-11-05 to 2026-11-09 `BayCAP-KDEy-h-real` was shown too). The analysis of E3.1 is `tables/baycap_fixed.py` and `plots/baycap_fixed.py` (tests: `tests/test_baycap_fixed_tables.py`): per pair, the scores of the eight methods, `BayesCAP`, `BayesCAP-real`, `BayesCAP-Hf-real` and `SQS-Hbs` (`RQBS` before FIX-2); per cell, the observed and predicted widening factors, the movement of the centre, the label-free mismatch $\lVert n^t/N^Q-\hat C\bar\pi\rVert_1$ and the NUTS diagnostics; the ECE of the two input classifiers and $\operatorname{sd}_j(\hat C_{jj})$; sixteen paired bootstrap tests of the mean Winkler difference (four against `BayesCAP-Hf-real`), each Holm-adjusted over the pairs; coverage in bins of $\min_j\pi^Q_j$; the $2\times2$ design per pushforward and metric. In the realised and population tables the columns are grouped by score (AE, amplitude, coverage, Winkler score; `TABLE_SCORES`) and then by method, and in each row and score group the cells are coloured from the worst to the best value, with the best in bold (lower is better for AE, amplitude and Winkler score, closer to $0.95$ for coverage; `_score_cell_styles`). `tables/displacement.py --method` gives these methods a prior part $R_0=0$ (`method_c_prior` returns `"none"`), and `tables/rank.py --include-e31` (or `--include <methods>`) ranks them with the methods of `main`, under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/rank/extended/`. Evaluated by E3.1.

`BayCAPKDEy` with `c_prior="hierarchical-f"` (WP-13, done; evaluated by E3.2; tests: `tests/test_baycap_kdey_hf.py`) implements the methods of [[ext3_posterior_propagation]], E3.2: BayCAP-KDEy with a random $C$. `fit` splits each class $\omega_j$ of $V$ into the cells $V_{kj}$ of the instances that $h$ predicts as $\omega_k$ and fits one kernel density $\hat\phi_{kj}$ per cell (QuaPy's `KDEBase`, the kernel and bandwidth of BayesKDEy) on the validation outputs of the input classifier (cross-fitted for `mlp`), so that $\sum_k\hat C_{kj}\hat\phi_{kj}$ is the class density of BayesKDEy; an empty cell takes the density of all the validation instances that $h$ predicts as $\omega_k$ (`CELL_PREDICTED`), or the class density $\hat f_j$ if $h$ never predicts $\omega_k$ on $V$ (`CELL_CLASS`), recorded in `cell_source_`. Per bag, `cell_log_densities` computes the log-densities of the cells at the target outputs before sampling (all $K^2$ for `h`, the $K$ cells of the prediction of $h$ for `mlp`), and NUTS samples $(\pi,C)$ jointly with `kdey_hf_model`: $\pi\sim\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1)$, the Hf prior on $C$ with centred shares (`methods/baycap.py:hierarchical_rate_sites`, shared with `BayCAP`, so the two models have the same sites), the validation counts observed as multinomials, and the log-likelihood of the bag `kdey_hf_loglik` divided by $\tau$: $\sum_u\log\sum_j\pi_j\sum_kC_{kj}\hat\phi_{kj}(z_u)$ for `h` (outputs alone), $\sum_u\log\sum_j\pi_jC_{\hat y_uj}\hat\phi_{\hat y_uj}(z_u)$ for `mlp` (outputs and predictions of $h$), both by log-sum-exp. Each draw gives $C^{(r)}\operatorname{diag}(\pi^{(r)})$, then the estimand applies; `main` uses the realised one. The NUTS diagnostics cover $\pi$, $C$, $m$, $\rho$ and $1/(1+\kappa)$; `last_posterior_summaries` returns `KDEY_HF_SUMMARIES`: the summaries of `BayesCAP-Hf-real` (`hierarchical_summaries` with those of $\kappa$) and `c_mean`, `c_sd` (posterior mean and standard deviation of $C$, `cap-ml` layout, flattened row by row), `mismatch` ($\lVert n^t/N^Q-\bar C\bar\pi\rVert_1$), `n_empty_cells`, `n_class_density_cells` and `val_ece`. A failed start of NUTS is handled as in `BayesCAP-Hf-real` (NaN tables and diagnostics, `nuts_failed=1`). In `main`, the methods `BayCAP-KDEy-Hf-h-real` and `BayCAP-KDEy-Hf-mlp-real` are in `experiments/methods.py:KDEY_HF_VARIANTS` (the kernel of E3.1, `KDEY_SETTINGS`, $\tau=1$, the NUTS settings and default seed of the BayCAP methods), run in the BayCAP pool; they are in no table, rank or default plot of `main` (`tables/main.py:EXCLUDED_METHODS`; on the branch of WP-13, from 2026-11-06 to the merge, they were in its tables, ranks and coverage vs amplitude plot at the user's request, and the tables of `main` of 2026-11-09 were generated with them); `tables/rank.py --include-e32` adds them to the extended rank table too, and `tables/displacement.py --method` accepts them, with a prior part $R_0$ of NaN (as for every method with a hierarchical prior, whose posterior mean of a recall given $V$ alone has no closed form). The analysis of E3.2 is `tables/baycap_kdey_hf.py` and `plots/baycap_kdey_hf.py` (tests: `tests/test_baycap_kdey_hf_tables.py`): per pair, AE, coverage, amplitude, Winkler score and the standardised shift (mean over the bags of $(\hat A-A)/\operatorname{sd}(A^{(r)})$) of the two methods, of the two realised BayCAP-KDEy methods of E3.1, of `BayesCAP-Hf-real` and of `SQS-Hbs` (`RQBS` before FIX-2), with the failed bags left out of a method's scores; the split of the shift into $R$ and $P$ of the two methods; four paired bootstrap tests of the mean Winkler difference (each method minus the E3.1 method with the same input classifier and minus `BayesCAP-Hf-real`), each Holm-adjusted over the pairs; the NUTS diagnostics, the Hf summaries, the numbers of empty cells and the mismatch of the two methods, with the mismatch of the E3.1 methods; and, per cell, the ratio of the posterior standard deviation of $C_{kj}$ to $\sqrt{\hat C_{kj}(1-\hat C_{kj})/(n^s_j+1)}$. Outputs: a per-pair and a per-cell CSV and three tables (comparison, tests, diagnostics) under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/baycap_kdey_hf/`; three scatters (coverage against the E3.1 method, coverage against `BayesCAP-Hf-real`, information on $C$) under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/baycap_kdey_hf/`.

**Per-class leave-one-out bandwidths (WP-25; E7.1 of [[ext7_kdey_bandwidth]], pilot only, not carried forward).** `BayesKDEy` (and so `BayCAPKDEy` with `c_prior="fixed"` and `SQSB`, which pass the hyperparameter through) accepts `bandwidth="loo"` with the Gaussian kernel and no shrinkage: each class gets the bandwidth of `bandwidth_grid` (default `LOO_GRID`, $\{10^{-2+0.1k}\}_{k=0}^{20}$) that maximises the leave-one-out log-likelihood of its kernel density on the validation outputs of the input classifier (`loo_log_likelihood`, closed form from the pairwise squared distances, computed in blocks of about $10^7$ entries), and the class densities are normalised in the $K-1$ dimensions of the simplex (`simplex_log_density`: scikit-learn's log-density plus $\frac12\ln(2\pi b_j^2)$), so that per-class bandwidths do not reweight the classes; a class with fewer than two validation outputs keeps $0.1$. `bandwidths_` gives the bandwidth of each class for every setting, and with `"loo"` the per-bag summaries add `LOO_SUMMARIES` (`bandwidths`, `class_sizes`, `bandwidth_edge`, `loo_selected`, `loo_default`). Numeric bandwidths and `"scott"`/`"silverman"` keep QuaPy's densities. In `main`: `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-loo` and `SQS-B-loo` (`experiments/methods.py:LOO_VARIANTS`, built by `fixed_c_method` and `sqs_b` with `bandwidth="loo"`), outside the main tables, ranks and default plots, in the BayCAP pool; tables `tables/kdey_loo.py`, plots `plots/kdey_loo.py`; the run and findings of E7.1 (pilot only, not carried forward) are in §II.29. Tests: `tests/test_bayes_kdey_loo.py`, `tests/test_kdey_loo_tables.py`, `tests/test_methods.py`.

**One bandwidth selected on calibration bags (WP-26, done; evaluated by E7.2 of [[ext7_kdey_bandwidth]], run and findings in §II.30).** `BayCAPKDEy` with `c_prior="fixed"`, the Gaussian kernel and no shrinkage accepts `bandwidth="cal"`: before the deployed fit, `select_bandwidth` splits $V$ per class into $V_{\text{fit}}$ and $V_{\text{cal}}$, half each (`split_halves`, $\lfloor n_j/2\rfloor$ to $V_{\text{cal}}$, as E5.3; seed `calibration_seed(random_state, CAL_SPLIT_STREAM)`), fits a `BayesKDEy` with the input classifier of the method on $V_{\text{fit}}$ (for `mlp`, the MLP with its cross-fitted outputs, then trained on all of $V_{\text{fit}}$), measures the recalls $\hat d^{\text{fit}}$ of $h$ on $V_{\text{fit}}$ (`class_recalls`), and draws `cal_bags` ($50$) bags of `cal_bag_size` ($1000$) instances from $V_{\text{cal}}$ with QuaPy's UPP (`calibration_bags`, seed `calibration_seed(random_state, CAL_BAG_STREAM)`, the experiment seed restored afterwards), the same for every bandwidth. For each $b$ of `bandwidth_grid` (default `LOO_GRID`) it computes the log-densities of the outputs of $V_{\text{cal}}$ under the class densities of $V_{\text{fit}}$ at $b$ (`BayesKDEy.class_log_densities`, QuaPy's densities), the maximum-likelihood estimate of $\pi$ of each bag by EM on the instance counts of the bag (`methods/bayes_kdey.py:em_prevalences`, from the uniform $\pi$, stopping when no component changes by more than `EM_TOL` $=10^{-9}$, at most `EM_MAX_ITER` $=10^4$ steps), and the criterion $\frac1S\sum_s\lvert\hat d^{\text{fit}\top}(\hat\pi_s(b)-\pi_s)\rvert$ (`accuracy_criterion`); with `cal_solver="slsqp"` (default `"em"`) the estimate is instead that of QuaPy's `KDEyML`, bag by bag (`methods/bayes_kdey.py:slsqp_prevalences`: SciPy's SLSQP from the uniform $\pi$, bounds $[0,1]$, $\sum_j\pi_j=1$, default tolerances, $10^{-12}$ added to the mixture density in the logarithm, as `KDEyML.aggregate`), and `cal_em_unconverged` counts the fits where SLSQP reports no success; the criterion is also computed at $0.1$ (`CAL_REFERENCE`) when the grid lacks it. `cal_bandwidth_` is the bandwidth of smallest criterion, ties (relative difference $10^{-9}$) broken towards $0.1$ on the $\log_{10}$ scale and, at the same distance, towards the smaller bandwidth (`select_from_curve`). The deployed quantifier is then `BayesKDEy` at `cal_bandwidth_` fitted on the full $V$ exactly as with a numeric bandwidth; the calibration has its own seeds and leaves the global NumPy state and `qp.environ["_R_SEED"]` unchanged, so a selected $0.1$ gives the draws of `bandwidth=0.1`. With `cal_nuts_check=True` the criterion is also computed with the posterior mean of one NUTS run per bandwidth and bag (the model, prior, temperature, NUTS settings and seed of the method, on the $1000$ outputs of the bag), without changing the selection. `solver_check` (the gate of the solver of E7.2a) runs steps 1–3 of the selection once (`_calibration_setup`) and then, per bandwidth, both solvers on the same class densities, each timed by `perf_counter` apart from the shared steps, with the log-likelihood of each bag at each estimate (`bag_log_likelihoods`); it returns both curves and selections and does not fit the method. The per-bag summaries add `CAL_SUMMARIES` (`cal_bandwidth`, `cal_curve`, `cal_selected`, `cal_default`, `cal_em_unconverged`, `cal_n_val`, `cal_n_cal`) and, with the check, `CAL_NUTS_SUMMARIES` (`cal_bandwidth_nuts`, `cal_curve_nuts`). In `main`: `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-cal` (`experiments/methods.py:CAL_VARIANTS`, built by `cal_method` through `fixed_c_method` with `bandwidth="cal"` and `cal_solver="slsqp"`, `CAL_SOLVER`, since 2026-11-20; its EM pilot cells are archived in `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main_e72a_pilot_em/`, which `tables/kdey_cal.py --cal-experiment` reads), outside the main tables, ranks and default plots, in the BayCAP pool; `main --kdey-cal-check` turns on the check for it. Tables `tables/kdey_cal.py` (per metric and pair: the scores of both methods, paired bootstrap tests of the AE and Winkler differences with Holm, the selection; per pair and bandwidth, the criterion curves; per metric and group of $K$, a summary with the pairs significantly lower and higher, Wilcoxon tests and the Spearman correlation of the calibration and test gains; the pilot check), plots `plots/kdey_cal.py` (`bandwidths`, `gain`). The gate of the solver: the study `experiments/kdey_cal_solver.py` (the pilot pairs, the method of `main` with $V$ prepared as in `main`, `solver_check`, then the deployed fit at the $b_{\text{cal}}$ of EM timed; one row per bandwidth and bag under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/kdey_cal_solver/classic/selection/`, in the BayCAP pool) and the table `tables/kdey_cal_solver.py` (per pair and verdict, with the test times and curves stored by the pilot). The temperature (E7.2b, run with E7.2a): with `temperature="cal"` (only with `bandwidth="cal"`; grid `temperature_grid`, default `TAU_GRID` $=\{0.5,1,2,4,8\}$), `fit` computes steps 1–3 once, selects $b_{\text{cal}}$ at $\tau=1$, then `select_temperature` builds the method on $V_{\text{fit}}$ at $b_{\text{cal}}$ ($\hat C$ of $h$ on $V_{\text{fit}}$, the class densities of the half at $b_{\text{cal}}$) and on each calibration bag and $\tau$ runs NUTS on the model of BayesKDEy at $\tau$ (key `calibration_seed(random_state, CAL_TAU_STREAM)`, through `BayesianQuantifier.run_nuts(seed=...)`), pushes the draws through $\hat C$ with the estimand of the method (realised, a generator seeded by the same seed), and scores the interval of vanilla accuracy at $0.95$ against the realised table of the bag (`interval_scores`: Winkler score and coverage of `cap-ml`'s `ConfidenceInterval`, as `tables/main.py`); `cal_temperature_` has the lowest mean Winkler score, ties to the $\tau$ closest to $1$ on the log scale, then the smaller (`select_temperature_from_scores`, as `tables/tempering.py:best_tau`); the deployed quantifier is `BayesKDEy` at $(b_{\text{cal}},\tau_{\text{cal}})$ fitted on the full $V$. The per-bag summaries add `CAL_TAU_SUMMARIES` (`cal_temperature`, and per $\tau$ `cal_tau_winkler`, `cal_tau_coverage`, `cal_tau_divergent`, `cal_tau_max_rhat`, `cal_tau_min_ess`). In `main`: `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-cal-tcal` (`CAL_VARIANTS`, `CAL_TEMPERATURE_VARIANTS`, `cal_method` with `temperature="cal"`), outside the main tables, ranks and default plots, in the BayCAP pool. Table `tables/kdey_tcal.py` (the four cells of the ablation, $b\in\{0.1,b_{\text{cal}}\}\times\tau\in\{1,\tau_{\text{cal}}\}$, the cell $(0.1,\tau_{\text{cal}})$ the stored E5.2 method at the $\tau_{\text{cal}}$ of E5.3 recomputed from the stored `tau_select` runs; per metric and pair the scores, paired bootstrap tests of `-tcal` against the other cells with Holm; summary by group of $K$ with Wilcoxon tests; cross-tabulation of the two $\tau_{\text{cal}}$; NUTS diagnostics of the calibration runs), plot `plots/kdey_tcal.py` (`ablation`). Tests: `tests/test_kdey_cal.py`, `tests/test_kdey_cal_tables.py`, `tests/test_kdey_cal_solver.py`, `tests/test_kdey_cal_tcal.py`, `tests/test_kdey_tcal_tables.py`, `tests/test_methods.py`.

- **Run (E3.1):** on `bcuda` by the user, `main --classic`, one run per method, JAX on CPU, `cap-ml` `bb20aa5`, from branch `wp-8/fixed-c`; full run stored by 2026-11-05 (dates and commits of the runs not recorded); all $75$ pairs and the three metrics, $100$ bags each, no pair left out. The E3.1 tables, the E2.2 decompositions of the eight methods, the seven figures and the rank table with `--include-e31` generated on 2026-11-05 and copied locally with `make pull`. Results under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main/` (the eight methods), `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/baycap_fixed/`, `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/displacement/<method>/`, `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/rank/extended/` and `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/baycap_fixed/`.
- **Findings (E3.1):** NUTS is reliable on every pair (largest split-$\hat R$ $1.04$). The population methods under-cover (mean coverage $0.25$–$0.33$); the realised pushforward widens the interval by the predicted factor $\sqrt{1+(\bar\sigma_g/\sigma_p)^2}$ (median $3.0$–$3.4$) and brings mean coverage to $0.61$–$0.74$, and it is significantly better than the population one on $56$–$70$ pairs (worse on none for BayesKDEy, on $7$ and $3$ for BayesianMAPLS), so only the realised methods are considered from now on (user's decision of 2026-11-05). Without a prior on $C$ the displacement of BayCAP on the large-$K$ datasets is gone; the realised methods fail where the error comes from $V$ (`mammographic`, `wine-quality`, `poker_hand`). BayesKDEy beats BayesianMAPLS, which depends on the calibration of its input classifier; the MLP input helps BayesKDEy mostly when $h$ is LR. `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` has mean coverage $0.74$, Winkler score $0.39$, AE $0.024$ (`BayesCAP-Hf-real` $0.89$, $0.23$, $0.035$; `RQBS` $0.69$, $0.45$, $0.027$); over the five classifiers its mean Winkler rank among $15$ methods is $3.81$ (Hf $4.48$, `BayCAP-KDEy-h-real` $4.55$), never significantly worse than the best, and the realised BayCAP-KDEy has the best AE rank on every classifier and metric. So BayCAP-KDEy with the realised pushforward is the best of the eight methods, level with Hf by rank and behind it by the mean Winkler score and coverage. *After FIX-8* (`poker_hand` with $K=7$; tables regenerated on 2026-11-13 from `50f17df`, with FIX-2 + FIX-3): `poker_hand` becomes a failure of the fixed-$\hat C$ methods (realised `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` coverage $0.08$–$0.25$, a prevalence error shared by the five pairs), so the MLP input no longer helps BayesKDEy on average (mean Winkler score $0.508$ against $0.472$ for `BayCAP-KDEy-h-real`; $0.363$ against $0.370$ without `poker_hand`); `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` has mean coverage $0.70$, Winkler score $0.51$, AE $0.028$ (`BayesCAP-Hf-real` $0.88$, $0.24$, $0.036$; `SQS-Hbs` $0.65$, $0.66$, $0.031$), and its mean Winkler rank among $16$ methods is $4.29$ (`BayCAP-KDEy-h-real` $4.60$, Hf $5.01$), still never significantly worse than the best; the rank table with `--include-e31` is now in `tables/rank/extended_e31/`. Details in the Analysis of E3.1.
- **Run (E3.2):** on `bcuda` by the user, `main --classic`, one run per method, JAX on CPU, `cap-ml` `bb20aa5` (the commit in `uv.lock`), from branch `wp-13/baycap-kdey-hf`: pilot on 2026-11-06 from `b9fe044` (`mammographic`, `poker_hand`, `wine-quality`), full run from 2026-11-06 to 2026-11-07 (`BayCAP-KDEy-Hf-mlp-real` from `70b2140`, `BayCAP-KDEy-Hf-h-real` from `847e508`, the pilot pairs skipped); all $75$ pairs and the three metrics, $100$ bags each, no failed bag. Mean time per bag from about $3$ s (`mammographic`) to $600$–$755$ s (`isolet` with `h`, which evaluates the $K^2$ cell densities of every target instance). The E3.2 tables (comparison, tests, diagnostics, per-pair and per-cell CSVs), its three plots and the tables of `main` generated on 2026-11-09 from `a215a14` and copied locally; the rank table with `--include-e32` and the decompositions of E2.2 for the two methods were not generated. Results under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main/` (the two methods), `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/baycap_kdey_hf/` and `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/baycap_kdey_hf/`.
- **Findings (E3.2):** a random $C$ does not improve the realised BayCAP-KDEy overall: mean coverage $0.79$ (`h`) and $0.77$ (`mlp`) against $0.72$ and $0.74$ for E3.1 and $0.89$ for `BayesCAP-Hf-real`, mean Winkler score $0.41$ and $0.40$ against $0.43$, $0.39$ and $0.23$, mean AE $0.029$ and $0.027$ against $0.027$, $0.024$ and $0.035$. The paired bootstrap tests are split ($16$ pairs better and $13$ worse than `BayCAP-KDEy-h-real` for `h`; $10$ and $15$ against `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` for `mlp`), and the better Winkler rank of the `mlp` variant over `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` comes from many negligible wins (median difference $-0.002$, Wilcoxon $p=0.91$). It helps where the error is in the rates of $h$ (`mammographic`, `semeion` LR and MLP), leaves `poker_hand` and, with `mlp`, `wine-quality` unchanged, fails on `shuttle` (`mlp`) and `german` (`h`), and matches the predicted shares of the bag (mismatch $0.025$–$0.027$ against $0.040$–$0.053$). Divergent transitions in some bag of $58$–$59$ pairs, many on `poker_hand` (about $430$ per bag), largest split-$\hat R$ above $1.05$ on $23$ pairs. By the user's decision of 2026-11-09 the two methods are not carried forward: `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` stays the BayCAP-KDEy method, `BayesCAP-Hf-real` the reference for coverage and mean Winkler score. Details in the Analysis of E3.2 of [[ext3_posterior_propagation]]. *After FIX-8* (`poker_hand` with $K=7$; tables regenerated on 2026-11-13 from `50f17df`, with FIX-2 + FIX-3): mean coverage $0.78$ and $0.74$ against $0.72$ and $0.70$ for E3.1 and $0.88$ for Hf; mean Winkler score $0.44$ and $0.47$ against $0.47$ and $0.51$ (lower than E3.1 only through `poker_hand`, where `mlp` improves in part) and $0.24$; Wilcoxon $p=0.45$; divergences on `poker_hand` $18$–$481$ per bag.
- **Cards:** WP-8 and WP-13 (§III.3).

## II.5 The `BayCAP` method with population or realised-table estimand

`methods/baycap.py:BayCAP` copies the BayesianCC model, priors and NUTS sampler of `cap-ml` `ACC_BayesCAP` (commit `bb20aa5` of `devel`) and adds the hyperparameter `estimand`. With `"population"` (default) it returns the population tables $C^{(r)}\operatorname{diag}(\pi^{(r)})$, identical to `ACC_BayesCAP` for the same `random_state`. With `"realised"` it returns the realised-table posterior predictive: for each draw and each predicted class $\omega_i$, the true classes of the $n^t_i$ target instances predicted as $\omega_i$ are drawn as $\operatorname{Mult}(n^t_i,\rho^{(r)}_{i\cdot})$, with $\rho^{(r)}_{ij}=C^{(r)}_{ij}\pi^{(r)}_j/\sum_l C^{(r)}_{il}\pi^{(r)}_l$ (module-level function `realised_tables`). Both estimands represent the uncertainty on $\pi^Q$ and $C$; only `"realised"` also represents the finite-sample fluctuation of the target table, which is what the experiments score. Both share the NUTS draws for the same `random_state`; the multinomial draws use a NumPy generator seeded with `random_state`. Method names: `BayesCAP` and `BayesCAP-real` (labels in `tables/common.py` and `plots/common.py`). It has unit tests (`tests/test_baycap.py`). Since WP-4 `BayCAP(acc, estimand="realised")` is the method `BayesCAP-real` of `main` (`experiments/methods.py`, same NUTS settings and default seed as `BayesCAP`), run with the BayCAP methods; its results are stored for all 75 pairs and all three metrics (run ended 2026-10-22), and E2.3 of [[ext2_baycap]] evaluates it (§II.13). It is excluded from the tables and ranks of `main` (`tables/main.py:EXCLUDED_METHODS`) and from the default methods of its plots (tests: `tests/test_methods.py`); the comparison of the two pushforwards is in §II.13. Source: WP-1; added to `main` by WP-4 (§III.3).

The hyperparameter `c_prior` selects the prior on the columns of $C$ (evaluated by E2.4 of [[ext2_baycap]], §II.14). `"uniform"` (default) is $\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1_K)$, as in `ACC_BayesCAP`, with unchanged NUTS draws. `"empirical-bayes"` gives column $j$ the prior $\operatorname{Dir}(\alpha_{\cdot j})$ with $\alpha_{jj}=\hat\nu\hat m$ and $\alpha_{ij}=\hat\nu(1-\hat m)/(K-1)$ for $i\ne j$ (`eb_concentration`), so that the recall $C_{jj}$ has the prior $\operatorname{Beta}(\hat\nu\hat m,\hat\nu(1-\hat m))$; in the `cap` layout a column of $C$ is a row of `p_c_cond_y`, and the matrix is symmetric. `fit` estimates $(\hat m,\hat\nu)$ from the diagonal counts $f_j$ and the class totals $n_j$ of the validation table by maximising the beta-binomial log-likelihood $\sum_j[\ln B(f_j+\nu m,\,n_j-f_j+\nu(1-m))-\ln B(\nu m,\,\nu(1-m))]$ (`beta_binomial_loglik`, `fit_beta_binomial`): L-BFGS-B over $(\operatorname{logit}m,\ln\nu)$ with the analytic gradient, from the pooled recall and $\ln\nu\in\{0,3,6\}$, within $m\in[10^{-3},1-10^{-3}]$ and $10^{-3}\le\nu\le10^4$ (`EB_M_BOUNDS`, `EB_NU_BOUNDS`; the lower bound on $\nu$ is a numerical safeguard). The fit is stored as `eb_fit` (`BetaBinomialFit`: $\hat m$, $\hat\nu$, whether each reached a bound, $\operatorname{sd}(\hat m)$ and the maximum log-likelihood) and the concentrations as `c_alpha`. $\operatorname{sd}(\hat m)=\hat m(1-\hat m)\operatorname{sd}(\operatorname{logit}\hat m)$ comes from the inverse of the observed information (central differences of the gradient); with $\hat\nu$ on a bound only the curvature in $\operatorname{logit}m$ is used, and with $\hat m$ on a bound it is NaN. The likelihoods, the prior on $\pi^Q$, NUTS, the seed and both estimands are unchanged. Source: WP-5 (§III.3).

`c_prior="hierarchical"` (WP-10; evaluated by E2.7 of [[ext2_baycap]] as `BayesCAP-H-real`, §II.17) samples the prior on the recalls with NUTS instead of plugging in a fit: $m\sim U(0,1)$ and $u=1/\sqrt{1+\nu}\sim U(0,1)$ (sites `h_m`, `h_u`; `nu_from_u` gives $\nu=1/u^2-1$, with density $\frac12(1+\nu)^{-3/2}$), the recalls $C_{jj}\mid m,\nu\sim\operatorname{Beta}(\nu m,\nu(1-m))$ in a plate over the classes (`h_recalls`), and the mistakes of each true class shared among the other classes by $\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1_{K-1})$ (`h_shares`, only for $K\ge3$); `hierarchical_rates` builds `p_c_cond_y` (row $j$ is the column $C_{:j}$) as a deterministic site. The parametrisation is centred, with no bound on $\nu$. `fit` stores no concentrations (`c_alpha` is `None`). `last_posterior_summaries` returns, for the last `predict_ct_range` call, the summaries of `hierarchical_summaries` (`HIERARCHICAL_SUMMARIES`: posterior mean of $m$; median, $5\%$ and $95\%$ quantiles of $\ln\nu$; $P(\nu>\min_jn_j)$; posterior means of the recalls and of $\pi^Q$; covariance of $\pi^Q$, flattened), and an empty dict for the other priors. If NUTS finds no valid initial parameters, the bag gets tables of NaN, NaN diagnostics and `nuts_failed=1` (`failed_summaries`); the other priors raise as before.

`c_prior="hierarchical-f"` (WP-11; evaluated by E2.8 of [[ext2_baycap]] as `BayesCAP-Hf-real`, §II.18) is `"hierarchical"` with two changes (§T.16): $\rho=1/(1+\nu)\sim U(0,1)$ (site `h_rho`; `strength_from_rho` gives $\nu=1/\rho-1$, with density $(1+\nu)^{-2}$ and $P(\nu>x)=1/(1+x)$) instead of $u$, and, for $K\ge3$, a common concentration $\kappa$ of the shares of the mistakes, $1/(1+\kappa)\sim U(0,1)$ (site `h_rho_kappa`, deterministic `h_kappa`), with `h_shares` $\sim\operatorname{Dir}(\kappa\mathbf 1_{K-1})$; with $K=2$ there is no $\kappa$. Its NUTS diagnostics cover $m$, $\rho$ and $1/(1+\kappa)$ with $\pi^Q$ and $C$; its posterior summaries add `KAPPA_SUMMARIES` (median, $5\%$ and $95\%$ quantiles of $\ln\kappa$, $P(\kappa<1)$, from `kappa_summaries`; NaN with $K=2$); a failed start is handled as for `"hierarchical"`. The hyperparameter `shares` (WP-12; evaluated on a pilot only by E2.9 of [[ext2_baycap]] as `BayesCAP-Hfn-real`, §II.19, `experiments/methods.py:BAYCAP_OPTIONS`) selects the coordinates of the shares of `"hierarchical-f"` (§T.17): `"centred"` (default, the Dirichlet site `h_shares` of WP-11, draws unchanged) or `"non-centred"`, which samples `h_shares_gamma` $G'_{ij}\sim\operatorname{Gamma}(\kappa+1,1)$ and `h_shares_exp` $e_{ij}\sim\operatorname{Exp}(1)$ on the off-diagonal cells and makes `h_shares` deterministic, $\operatorname{softmax}$ over each column of $\ln G'_{ij}-e_{ij}/\kappa$ (`noncentred_shares`), the same law $\operatorname{Dir}(\kappa\mathbf 1_{K-1})$ given $\kappa$; the NUTS diagnostics and the posterior summaries are those of `"hierarchical-f"`, on the same sites; any other prior with `"non-centred"` raises an error. With $K=2$ the two forms give the same draws.

**The BayCAP methods of `main` and their NUTS diagnostics** (WP-6). `experiments/methods.py:BAYCAP_VARIANTS` gives the estimand and `c_prior` of the four BayCAP methods of `main`, all `BayCAP` with the NUTS settings and seed of §I.2: `BayesCAP` (population, uniform; §I.2), `BayesCAP-real` (realised, uniform), `BayesCAP-EB` (population, empirical Bayes) and `BayesCAP-EB-real` (realised, empirical Bayes). The two empirical-Bayes methods are, like `BayesCAP-real`, excluded from the default plots of `main` and used by E2.4 of [[ext2_baycap]]; A fifth method, `BayesCAP-H-real` (realised, hierarchical; WP-10, §II.17), and a sixth, `BayesCAP-Hf-real` (realised, `"hierarchical-f"`; WP-11, §II.18), are in `BAYCAP_VARIANTS`. Since 2026-11-05 (user's request) the tables and ranks of `main` show `BayesCAP-Hf-real`, and since 2026-11-06 (user's request) `BayesCAP-EB-real` again, and none of the other four variants (`tables/main.py:IN_TABLES`, §V.4; before 2026-11-05, the tables showed `BayesCAP-EB-real` and `BayesCAP-H-real`), and the coverage vs amplitude plot (`plots/amplitude_vs_coverage.py:PLOT_METHODS`) shows `BayesCAP-real`, `BayesCAP-EB-real` (since 2026-11-06) and `BayesCAP-Hf-real` (before 2026-11-05, `BayesCAP-real`, `BayesCAP-EB`, `BayesCAP-EB-real` and `BayesCAP-H-real`); no variant is in the other default plots. `experiments/eb_binary.py` keeps the four methods of E2.4. After each `predict_ct_range` call, `BayCAP.last_nuts_diagnostics` returns the number of divergent transitions, the largest split-$\hat R$ (the single chain split in two halves) and the smallest effective sample size over the entries of $\pi^Q$ and $C$ (`nuts_diagnostics`, with `numpyro.diagnostics`); the draws are those without the diagnostics. `experiments/main.py` stores them for every bag of a `BayCAP` method as the optional columns `nuts_divergences`, `nuts_max_rhat`, `nuts_min_ess` (§V.3), computed outside the timed calls; for `BayesCAP-H-real` the diagnostics also cover $m$ and $u$ (for `BayesCAP-Hf-real` $m$, $\rho$ and $1/(1+\kappa)$), and the posterior summaries of `last_posterior_summaries` are stored next to them (§V.3). `tables/eb_prior.py` and `plots/eb_prior.py` implement the E2.4 analysis (§II.14). Source: WP-6 (§III.3).

**Posterior temperature** (WP-20; evaluated by E5.2, §II.25: not retained, $\tau=1$ is kept). The argument `temperature` of `BayCAP` ($\tau>0$, default $1$) tempers the posterior jointly (§T.5 and §T.7 of [[ext5_baycap_priors_tempering]]): the log-likelihoods of the validation table (site `F_yc`) and of the predicted counts of the bag (site `N_c`) are both multiplied by $1/\tau$ with `numpyro.handlers.scale`, for every `c_prior`, and the priors on $\pi^Q$, on $C$ and on the hyperparameters are not scaled. With $\tau=1$ the model is built without the handler (`BayCAP._tempered` returns a null context), so its draws are those of the untempered model for the same `random_state` (`tests/test_tempering.py`, for every `c_prior` and both estimands). `experiments/methods.py:TEMPERED_VARIANTS` adds to `main` the eight tempered methods of E5.2, `BayesCAP-Hf-real-t<τ>` and `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-t<τ>` for $\tau\in\{0.5,2,4,8\}$ (`TEMPERATURE_LEVELS`): each is its untempered method (`baycap_method` or `fixed_c_method`, now with a `temperature` argument; `BayCAPKDEy` divides the log-likelihood of the bag by $\tau$, §II.4) with the temperature changed and nothing else; they run in the BayCAP pool, store the diagnostics and summaries of their untempered method, and are outside the tables, ranks and default plots of `main` (`tables/main.py:EXCLUDED_METHODS`). `experiments/tempering_check.py` re-runs the two untempered methods on chosen pairs into `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main_e52_check/` and compares them bag by bag with the stored `main` files (`--compare`, `tables/tempering_check/comparison.csv`). `tables/tempering.py` and `plots/tempering.py` implement the E5.2 analysis (card WP-20).

## II.6 Additional plots

- `plots/ae_vs_winkler.py`: AE against Winkler score per method, one facet per method, markers per classifier.
- `plots/rank_heatmap.py`: heatmaps (dataset × classifier/method) of the mean per-bag rank of each method, for AE and Winkler score.

Both read `main` results for vanilla accuracy.

### II.6.1 Shift profile of `main`

`plots/shift_profile.py` (since 2026-11-21, committed on `devel`, no card; `make run-plot-shift-profile`, `--figures by_k pooled`, `--metrics`, `--methods`, `--bins`) shows how the AE and the Winkler score of each method change from the least to the most shifted target bags, pooled over the 75 (dataset, classifier) pairs of `main`. It reads stored results only, and writes png, svg and a CSV of the plotted values per figure to `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/main/shift_profile/`. Tested in `tests/test_shift_profile.py`. Run with `make run-plot-shift-profile` on `bcuda` on 2026-11-21, commit `5f5a622`, default options (both figures, vanilla accuracy, the five default methods, 10 deciles), on the stored `main` results; the figures (`shift_profile_by_k_classic_vanilla_accuracy` and `shift_profile_pooled_classic_vanilla_accuracy`, png, svg and csv) are under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/main/shift_profile/`, pulled locally with `make pull`. The design was chosen with the user on 2026-11-21 on scratch versions of the figure; each choice and its reason:

- **Estimand.** AE and Winkler score $W$ of the realised accuracy of each target bag (`true_cts`), as in the main tables; vanilla accuracy by default.
- **Shift measure.** The stored `shifts` column, $s=\frac1K\sum_{k=1}^K\lvert\pi^{U_i}_k-\pi^L_k\rvert$ (`cap.utils.commons.get_shift`, rounded to 2 decimals), the absolute error between the realised prevalence of the bag and the prevalence of $L$. It uses the labels of the bag, which is allowed because it only groups bags for the evaluation and enters no method.
- **x axis: within-pair deciles, not values of $s$.** The largest possible value of $s$ is $2(1-\min_k\pi^L_k)/K$, about $1$ for a balanced binary dataset and about $0.2$ for $K=10$. With bins of $s$ pooled over pairs, the pairs that contribute to a bin change along the axis (multiclass pairs only at small $s$), so a trend could come from the change of datasets rather than from the shift. Within each pair the 100 bags are therefore sorted by $s$ (stable sort, ties broken by the bag index, so every method gets the same grouping) and cut into 10 groups of 10 bags, D1 the least and D10 the most shifted; every decile then contains every pair with the same weight. Consequence for the reading: a decile is a relative level, and D10 is a strong shift on a binary pair and a mild per-class shift on a pair with large $K$. Tertiles (3 levels) were tried first and hid where the change happens; deciles show it.
- **y axis: relative value with a reference common to the methods of a pair.** For pair $p$, method $j$ and decile $b$, $m^{j}_{p,b}$ is the mean of the metric over the bags of the decile, and the plotted value is $r^{j}_{p,b}=m^{j}_{p,b}/\bar m_p$, with $\bar m_p$ the mean of $m^{j'}_{p,b'}$ over the plotted methods $j'$ and the deciles $b'$. Dividing all methods of a pair by the same number removes the difficulty of the pair (which differs across datasets far more than the methods differ from each other) and keeps the ratios between methods, so the lines are separated: $r=1.5$ means 50% above the average of the plotted methods on that pair. The reference depends on the set of plotted methods: adding or removing a method changes every line. Two other scales were tried: raw means (dominated by the differences between datasets) and each method divided by its own mean over the deciles (every line centred on $1$, so the lines overlap and the plot no longer compares methods).
- **Aggregation.** $r^{j}_{p,b}$ is averaged over the 5 classifiers of each dataset, then over the datasets of the group. The classifiers of a dataset share the target bags, so the datasets, not the pairs, are the independent units.
- **No error bars.** 95% $t$ intervals over the datasets ($\pm t_{n-1,0.975}\,\mathrm{sd}/\sqrt n$, with $n$ the number of datasets of the group, 4 to 15) were tried as bands and as dodged bars, on raw and on relative values; they overlapped almost everywhere, because they measure the spread across a few datasets and not the within-dataset comparisons the plot is about. The zigzag shared by all methods at some deciles (all methods are scored on the same bags) is variability of the bags, not an effect of the shift.
- **Groups of $K$** (`K_GROUPS`, figure `by_k`): $K=2$ (german, mammographic, semeion, spambase, tictactoe), $3\le K\le9$ (connect-4, nursery, shuttle, wine-quality, dry-bean, poker_hand; the row label gives the range present, $3\le K\le7$), $K\ge10$ (hand_digits, chess, isolet, letter). The split shows whether the change with shift holds for every $K$, since the meaning of a decile depends on $K$ (see the x axis). The figure `pooled` uses all 15 datasets in one row.
- **Methods** (`DEFAULT_METHODS`, user's decision of 2026-11-21): `O-LEAP`, `SQS-Hbs`, `SQS-B`, `BayesCAP-Hf-real`, `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real`. QuAcc was left out because its values, about five times those of the others, compressed the scale.
- **Labels and colours.** Method names are the labels of the main tables with the LaTeX macros resolved (`plots/common.py:method_table_label`, from `tables/common.py:METHOD_LATEX_LABELS` and `METHOD_LATEX_MACROS`), e.g. LEAP, SQS-H$_{\text{bs}}$, BayCAP-Hf, BayCAP-KDEy; they follow any change of the macros. The colours are local to this figure (`SHIFT_PROFILE_COLORS`), a deviation from the current practice of §V.5 (colours from `plots/common.py:METHOD_COLORS`), because the shared colours of `BayesCAP-Hf-real` and `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` are too faint next to the other three; it changes no other plot.
- **Style.** Lines with small markers, a dotted line at $1$, AE in the left column and $W$ in the right one, y axes shared within a column.

## II.7 Disabled variants

The CBPE variants `B-CBPE` and `BE-CBPE` were run in earlier versions. Only `plots/common.py` still mentions them (colours and a commented-out method list); `experiments/methods.py` has no factory for them.

## II.8 Toy simulations

`scripts/toys/ext2_estimand_gap.py`, `scripts/toys/ext2_eb_binary.py`, `scripts/toys/ext2_test_pool.py`, `scripts/toys/ext2_hierarchical.py`, `scripts/toys/ext5_prior_calibration.py` and `scripts/toys/ext4_budget.py` are seeded toy simulations that support [[ext2_baycap]] (Appendix A: the estimand gap, the empirical-Bayes prior with two classes, which imports `fit_beta_binomial` from `methods/baycap.py`, a finite test pool, and the corrected strength against a hierarchical prior, which imports it as well), [[ext5_baycap_priors_tempering]] and [[ext4_uncertainty_budget]]. Their numbers are toy results, not experiment results.

## II.9 Shared infrastructure and tests

- `experiments/main.py`: `--datasets`, `--classifiers` and `--methods` restrict a run of `main` to some pairs and methods (e.g. a pilot); by default it covers all of them.
- `experiments/main.py`: `--longest-first [EXPERIMENT ...]` (off by default) runs the tasks of each pool, one per (pair, method), longest first (`experiments/task_order.py:sort_longest_first`), so that a long task does not start last and run alone at the end of the pool. The cost of a task is estimated from the result files of the same (dataset, classifier, method) in the first of the named result folders of `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe` that has them (with no names: `main`, then every `main_*` folder by name, e.g. `main_pre_fix3`): $t_{\text{train}}+n_{\text{bags}}\,\bar t_{\text{test}}$ per metric file, equal pairs of times counted once (a method fitted once for the three metrics) and different ones summed (a method fitted per metric); with no file, the mean over the other classifiers of the same dataset and method; with neither, the task runs first. The log reports the sources, the estimated total and the five longest tasks. The order changes only when and in which worker a task runs, as the scheduling of the pool already does: the tasks and their seeded randomness (§V.1, FIX-3) are unchanged, so it has no effect on reproducibility or comparability. Tests: `tests/test_task_order.py`.
- `experiments/execution.py`: task runner (serial or `joblib`), outcome codes, persistence, worker shutdown. `run_tasks(..., env=...)` runs a parallel pool with cap-ml's `parallel` inside `worker_environment(env)`, which sets the variables in `os.environ` and restores them afterwards, so the `joblib` workers start with them; the results are read inside the context, and the workers are shut down before and after each parallel pool, so no worker is reused with the environment of another pool. A serial run (`n_jobs=1`) runs in the main process and does not apply `env`. `predist` calls `parallel` directly, inside the same `worker_environment` and shutdowns. Each line it writes to the experiment log for a result (done, exists or error) ends with the count of tasks done so far, `[<done>/<total>]`, where a task is one pair and one method (all metrics); the count is separate for the pool of regular methods and the pool of BayCAP methods. An `ExperimentOutcome` is saved in the results folder of the pool (`experiment`), or in its own optional field `experiment` when set (added by WP-21, so that the study `target_size` runs cells of different folders in one pool; the log line then names the folder); every other experiment leaves it unset, so its outputs do not change (`tests/test_target_size.py`).
- `scripts/bench/bayes_n_jobs.py` (`make run-bench-bayes-jobs`): benchmark of the number of workers of the BayCAP pool on CPU (`JAX_PLATFORMS=cpu`). For each value of `--n-jobs` (default 1, 2, 4, 8, 16; values above `cap.env["N_JOBS"]` are refused) it runs in a fresh subprocess the NUTS part of real `main` tasks (pretrain load, `--bags` test bags drawn as in `main`, default 10, `fit` and `predict_ct_range`; default pairs: the datasets of `main` with `LR`; default methods: the BayCAP methods of `main`) with the `joblib` call of `run_tasks`. Every process computes on one thread (single-threaded XLA, `XLA_FLAGS=--xla_cpu_multi_thread_eigen=false intra_op_parallelism_threads=1`, and one BLAS/OpenMP thread), so `n_jobs` processes keep about `n_jobs` cores busy. It reports wall time, tasks per hour, speed-up against the setting with the fewest workers, the average number of busy cores (CPU time over wall time), the largest difference of the posterior mean tables from the first setting, and the largest number of OS threads of a process (from `/proc/self/task`). It writes no result; output under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/bench/bayes_n_jobs/`. Run on 2026-10-22 on `bcuda` (commit `cfef840`, `--n-jobs 4 8 16 24 32`, 15 pairs with `LR`, both BayCAP methods, 30 tasks, 10 bags each): speed-up against 4 workers $1.76$ (8), $2.71$ (16), $2.86$ (24), $3.83$ (32); busy cores $8.4$, $15.0$, $24.0$, $26.6$, $38.1$, i.e. about 1.5 to 2 busy cores per process despite single-threaded XLA, with about 550 OS threads per process; mean task time from $37.6$ s (4) to $48.2$ s (32); the draws are identical for every number of workers. 16 workers is the chosen setting (user's decision of 2026-10-22): 24 add little, and 32 keep more cores busy than `cap.env["N_JOBS"]` allows.
- `scripts/bench/xla_vs_stored.py` (`make run-bench-xla-stored`): checks whether BayCAP results computed with one computing thread per process (`JAX_PLATFORMS=cpu`, single-threaded XLA, one BLAS/OpenMP thread) match the stored `main` results, computed with the default XLA threading (`BayesCAP-real` on CPU, run of 2026-10-21; `BayesCAP` from earlier runs, probably on GPU). For each pair and BayCAP method with stored results (default: `german`, `spambase`, `dry-bean`, `wine-quality`, `letter` with `LR`), it repeats the `main` task on its 100 bags (vanilla accuracy) with the `joblib` call of `run_tasks` (`--n-jobs`, default 16, at most `cap.env["N_JOBS"]`) and reports whether the bags are the same, whether the results are identical, the largest differences of `estim_cts` and of the accuracy samples, and coverage, amplitude and Winkler score of the stored and new results. It writes nothing in the results tree; output under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/bench/xla_vs_stored/`. Run on 2026-10-22 on `bcuda` (commit `821592c`, default pairs, both BayCAP methods): the bags are the same as the stored ones for every pair; `BayesCAP-real` is identical bit for bit on all 5 pairs, so on CPU single-threaded XLA gives the same draws as the default threading; `BayesCAP` differs on all 5 pairs, with largest difference of the mean tables from $0.0006$ (`dry-bean`) to $0.196$ (`wine-quality`) and coverage changes of $0$, $-0.01$ (`spambase`) and $-0.05$ (`wine-quality`, $0.40\to0.35$). Since `BayesCAP-real` shares the NUTS draws of `BayesCAP` for the same seed and reproduces exactly, the difference comes from how the stored `BayesCAP` results were produced (probably on GPU, possibly with other library versions), not from the threading. Following it, `BayesCAP` is recomputed on CPU (§I.2).
- `experiments/records.py`: `EvaluationContext` and `build_result_frame`, the result schema shared by `main` and `binform`.
- `experiments/metrics.py`, `experiments/methods.py`, `experiments/datasets.py`: metric, method and dataset factories.
- `main.py`, `binform.py`, `predist.py`, `config.py`: compatibility shims over `experiments/`.
