- `tests/`: protocol tests for `main`, `binform` and `predist`, and tests of the runner, the result frame, `BayCAPKDEy`, `BayCAPMAPLS` and `BayCAP` (`uv run python -m unittest discover -s tests`; `tests/` has no `__init__.py`, so plain `python -m unittest` finds no tests).
- `Makefile` (repository root): runs experiments on `bcuda` (default, `WHERE=remote`) or on the local machine (`WHERE=local`). `make init` creates `REMOTE_DIR` (default `/home/volpi/uncawe-devel`) and copies `env.json`, `sorted_sets.json` and `output/` from the old folder `/home/volpi/uncawe` without overwriting; `make setup` pushes `src/`, `scripts/`, `tests/`, `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock` with rsync and runs `uv sync --locked`; `make run-<name>` starts one experiment in a detached tmux session `uncawe-<name>` (`GPU=<index>` sets `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES`, `JAX=cpu|cuda` sets `JAX_PLATFORMS`, `ARGS=...` appends arguments); `make status`, `logs`, `attach`, `stop` manage runs; `make explog EXP=<experiment>` (`main`, `binform`, `predist`, `pretrain.classic`) shows the last `LOG_LINES` (default 50) lines of the experiment log `<OUT_DIR>/<experiment>.log` written by `util.get_logger`, with `OUT_DIR` resolved as the code resolves it, and `FOLLOW=1` keeps reading it; `make pull` copies tables and plots back; `make fetch-data` copies pretrain files and datasets from `bcuda` to the local `cap.env` paths; `make pull-results` and `make push-results` merge result files of `main`, `binform`, `predist` between the two machines with `--ignore-existing`. Local runs need an `env.json` entry for the local hostname with `OUT_DIR`; `env.json` is tracked in the repository, with the entries of every machine.

## II.10 The estimand-gap analysis of BayCAP

`tables/estimand_gap.py` (tests: `tests/test_estimand_gap.py`) approximates, on stored `main` results and without new runs, how a realised-table posterior predictive would change BayCAP's accuracy intervals ([[ext2_baycap]], E2.1). `main` stores BayCAP's accuracy samples but not its table draws, so the predictive is approximated for accuracy only: each approximate predictive sample is the stored accuracy sample plus independent $\mathcal N(0,\hat\sigma_g^2)$ noise, with $\hat\sigma_g^2=\sum_i n^t_i\bar\rho_{ii}(1-\bar\rho_{ii})/(N^Q)^2$, $n^t$ the predicted counts of the bag and $\bar\rho$ the stored mean table `estim_cts` (paper layout) with each row normalised to one; both are label-free, and labels are used only for scoring against the realised table (`true_cts`). Per (dataset, classifier) pair, at $\lvert U\rvert=1000$ and for vanilla accuracy, it reports coverage, amplitude and Winkler score ($\alpha=0.05$) of BayCAP and of the approximate predictive, the ratio $\hat\sigma_g/\hat\sigma_p$, the median number of validation instances per class, a gate summary and suggested pilot datasets, as LaTeX and PDF. The noise is seeded per pair (seed $0$).

- **Run:** 2026-10-14, local run on `eosisti` (`OUT_DIR=./output`), on the old `BayesCAP` results (§I.2, kept in `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main_22_10_26/`). Generated again on 2026-10-22 on `BayesCAP` recomputed on CPU, by the user, and copied locally with `make pull` (PDF built at commit `a23fb97`); results under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/estimand_gap/`, read by E2.3 (`tables/pushforward.py`). `cap-ml` commit: not recorded at run time (`cap-ml` is pinned to the `devel` branch, FIX-4, and `uv.lock` is not tracked).
- **Findings** (75 pairs, CPU results): 53 under-cover (coverage below $0.90$); 5 are brought within $[0.93,0.97]$ with a lower Winkler score (3 on the old results); 24 have $\hat\sigma_g/\hat\sigma_p<0.3$; 56 have a lower Winkler score under the approximation. The ratio ranges from $0.07$ (`poker_hand` LR) to $1.78$ (`letter` RF; $1.50$, `dry-bean`, on the old results). On large-$K$ datasets BayCAP is biased low (LR: `isolet` $-0.18$, `letter` $-0.07$), and its coverage is $0.00$ on `isolet` and `letter` for every classifier. E2.3 found the approximation accurate: its coverage is within $0.013$ of the exact realised pushforward on average (correlation $0.998$). *After FIX-8* (`poker_hand` with $K=7$; tables regenerated on 2026-11-13 from `50f17df`, with FIX-2 + FIX-3): $51$ pairs under-cover, $23$ have $\hat\sigma_g/\hat\sigma_p<0.3$; the other numbers are unchanged.
- **Card:** WP-2 (§III.3).

## II.11 The displacement analysis of BayCAP

`tables/displacement.py` and `plots/displacement.py` (tests: `tests/test_displacement.py`) explain, on stored `main` results and without new runs, why BayCAP's accuracy intervals are displaced ([[ext2_baycap]], E2.2). BayCAP targets the population table and is scored against the realised table (`true_cts`); the analysis uses target labels and is not a method. Per (dataset, classifier) pair, at $\lvert U\rvert=1000$ and for vanilla accuracy, it splits the error of BayCAP's point estimate in each bag into a recall term and a prevalence term, $\hat A-A=\sum_j\pi_j(\hat r_j-r_j)+\sum_j(\hat\pi_j-\pi_j)\hat r_j$, with $\hat\pi,\hat r$ from the stored mean table `estim_cts`; it computes the prior part of the recall term on the validation counts $f_{ij}$ rebuilt from the stored validation posteriors and labels, $\sum_j\pi_j\left(\frac{f_{jj}+1}{n^s_j+K}-\frac{f_{jj}}{n^s_j}\right)$ (for the empirical-Bayes methods of §II.5, $\frac{f_{jj}+\hat\nu\hat m}{n^s_j+\hat\nu}$ in place of $\frac{f_{jj}+1}{n^s_j+K}$: `method_c_prior`, `smoothed_recalls`; WP-6); and it reports the conditioning of $\hat C$ on vectors summing to zero ($\kappa_0$ and $1/\sigma_{\min}$, from the singular values of $Q^\top\hat CQ$), the accuracy of $h$ on $V$, coverage, amplitude, Winkler score, and the coverage $\mathcal C_s$ predicted from the mean shift and amplitude alone (normal approximation). Outputs: a per-pair CSV and one table (diagnostics, interval, error) as LaTeX and a landscape PDF under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/displacement/`; two scatters (recall term against its prior part; $1/\sigma_{\min}$ against accuracy on $V$, coloured by coverage and by shift) under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/main/displacement/`. Both scripts take the method as `--method` (default `BayesCAP`); for any other method, e.g. `BayesCAP-real` (WP-4), the outputs go to a subfolder named after the method of these two folders.

- **Run:** 2026-10-20 on `bcuda`, all 75 pairs (commit `e0e2f94`, dirty only for a local `WHERE` default in the `Makefile`; Python 3.12.11; `cap-ml` `bb20aa5`); results under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/displacement/` and `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/main/displacement/`, copied locally with `make pull`; table regenerated with the merged layout on 2026-10-20. On 2026-10-22 the table was generated again by the user on `BayesCAP` recomputed on CPU (§I.2), now with the predicted coverage $\mathcal C_s$, and for `BayesCAP-real` (WP-4, `--method BayesCAP-real`, under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/displacement/BayesCAP-real/`); the two scatters were drawn again on 2026-10-23 on `bcuda` (commit `975f2ac`) for both methods, the ones of `BayesCAP-real` under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/main/displacement/BayesCAP-real/`.
- **Findings** (the same on the old and on the CPU results): coverage follows the coverage predicted from the shift alone (correlation $0.98$; 10 pairs more than $0.05$ below it on the CPU results); the $\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1)$ prior on $C$ accounts for every pair with coverage near $0$ (large $K$, recall term equal to its prior part); a badly conditioned $\hat C$ displaces the interval only when the prevalence error has the same sign in every bag (`german`, `wine-quality`), and otherwise widens it; the accuracy of $h$ on $V$ does not act on its own; on binary datasets a smaller recall error remains that the prior does not explain. Consistency checks: the mean of the accuracy samples equals the accuracy of the mean table within $1.3\cdot10^{-6}$; coverage agrees with E2.1 (53 of 75 pairs below $0.90$); $\hat C$ is singular on vectors summing to zero for 7 pairs. *After FIX-8* (`poker_hand` with $K=7$; tables regenerated on 2026-11-13 from `50f17df`, with FIX-2 + FIX-3): $8$ pairs more than $0.05$ below the predicted coverage (`poker_hand` MLP and RF no longer); `poker_hand` (smallest validation class $18$ instead of $3$) shows the pattern of a fixed-sign prevalence error, with kNN and RF displaced; coverage agrees with E2.1 ($51$ of $75$).
- **Inputs:** the first run used the old `BayesCAP` results (§I.2), kept in `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main_22_10_26/`; the table of 2026-10-22 uses the CPU results.
- **Card:** WP-3 (§III.3).

## II.12 Code to move to `cap-ml`

Components of this repository that should eventually move to `cap-ml` (owned by the user), so that other projects can use them. Until they move, they live here; when one moves, its entry records the `cap-ml` commit and the change of import paths, and leaves this section.

| Component | File | Tests | State | Notes for the move |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `BayCAP`, `realised_tables`, `fit_beta_binomial`, `beta_binomial_loglik`, `eb_concentration`, `BetaBinomialFit`, `nu_from_u`, `strength_from_rho`, `noncentred_shares`, `hierarchical_rates`, `hierarchical_rate_sites`, `hierarchical_summaries`, `kappa_summaries`, `failed_summaries` | `methods/baycap.py` | `tests/test_baycap.py`, `tests/test_tempering.py` | §II.5; method `BayesCAP-real` of `main` since WP-4, evaluated by E2.3 (§II.13); `c_prior="empirical-bayes"` (WP-5) evaluated by E2.4 (§II.14); since WP-6 also `BayesCAP`, `BayesCAP-EB`, `BayesCAP-EB-real` of `main`, with `nuts_diagnostics` and `last_nuts_diagnostics`; `c_prior="hierarchical"` and `last_posterior_summaries` (WP-10) for `BayesCAP-H-real`, evaluated by E2.7 (§II.17); `c_prior="hierarchical-f"` (WP-11) for `BayesCAP-Hf-real`, evaluated by E2.8 (§II.18); `shares="non-centred"` (WP-12, in progress, not evaluated) for `BayesCAP-Hfn-real`; `temperature` (WP-20) for the tempered `BayesCAP-Hf-real-t<τ>` of E5.2, evaluated by it and not retained (§II.25) | A copy of `ACC_BayesCAP` (commit `bb20aa5`) with the hyperparameters `estimand` and `c_prior`; the move can add `estimand` (default `"population"`), `c_prior` (default `"uniform"`), `realised_tables` and the empirical-Bayes fit to `ACC_BayesCAP` itself, instead of a second class; `model` takes the concentrations of the prior on $C$ as an argument (`c_alpha`); the NUTS diagnostics need `extra_fields=("diverging",)` in `mcmc.run`, which leaves the draws unchanged; with the hierarchical prior `model` takes `c_alpha=None` and samples $(m,u)$, the recalls and the shares, and `p_c_cond_y` becomes a deterministic site; `"hierarchical-f"` samples $(m,\rho)$ and, for $K\ge3$, $1/(1+\kappa)$ instead; with `shares="non-centred"` the shares are a deterministic site built from Gamma and exponential sites (`noncentred_shares`); the hierarchical sites are built by `hierarchical_rate_sites` (WP-13), shared with `BayCAPKDEy`; `temperature` wraps the two observed sites of `model` in `numpyro.handlers.scale(1/τ)` and leaves the model unchanged at $\tau=1$, which `ACC_BayesCAP` could take as an argument with the same default. Uses `cap.models.confidence` internals (`P_C_COND_Y`, `P_TEST_C`, `P_TEST_Y`, `BayesCAP`). |
| `SQSB`, `forward_bag_variances`, `luck_spreads`, `bag_tables` | `methods/sqs_b.py` | `tests/test_sqs_b.py` | §II.24; method `SQS-B` of `main` (WP-18), evaluated by E3.3 | Built like `RQBS` (same smoothing, `sampling_index` and seeding of the bags); it could become an option of `RQBS` that takes the prevalences from a Bayesian quantifier instead of the bootstrap, once `BayesKDEy` has moved. Uses `cap-ml`'s private `_temp_np_seed` and `cap.models.utils.smooth`, `BayesKDEy` of `methods/bayes_kdey.py` and the private `_validate_prevalence_samples` of `methods/baycap_quantifier.py`. |
| `AC`, `CentralPoissonInterval`, `central_bounds`, `lascal_calibrate`, `recover_temperature`, `poisson_binomial_pmf` | `methods/ac.py` | `tests/test_ac.py` | §II.31; method `AC` of `main` (WP-29), evaluated by E8.1 | Average Confidence for any number of classes, with LasCal per bag and the central Poisson-binomial interval; with `K=2` it gives the confidences, point estimate and probability mass function of `CBPE(calib_method="lascal")`, so the move could generalise `CBPE` to `K>2` and add the central interval as an alternative to `PoissonConfidenceInterval` (an `interval=` option), instead of a second class. `lascal_calibrate` calls the `lascal` package as `cap/calib.py:LasCal` does but returns the selected temperature (recovered from the calibrated logits, since the package does not return it) and a fallback flag; the move could make `LasCal` return them. Two issues of `cap-ml` that the move should fix: the fallback of `cap/calib.py:LasCal` calls the module's own `softmax(preact, temp, biases)` with `axis=1`, so it would raise instead of falling back; and neither wrapper detects the case where the package selects no temperature (all losses NaN, `optim_temp=-1`), where the returned logits are divided by $-1$. Uses `cap-ml`'s private `cap.models._cbpe.PoiBin`. |
| `worker_environment`, the `env` argument of `run_tasks` | `experiments/execution.py` | `tests/test_experiment_execution.py` | §II.9, used by `main`, `binform`, `predist` | Could become an `env=` argument of `cap.utils.commons.parallel`, next to `temp_force_njobs` (which sets only `OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS`); `run_tasks` would then pass it through. The shutdown of the reusable `loky` executor before and after the pool belongs with it. |
| `BayesianQuantifier`, `top_label_ece`; `BayesKDEy`, `kdey_model`, `loo_log_likelihood`, `simplex_log_density`, `em_prevalences`, `slsqp_prevalences`, `bag_log_likelihoods`; `BayesMAPLS`, `emq_model`, `ilr`, `ilr_inverse`; `QuantifierBayCAP`, `accuracy_moments`; `BayCAPKDEy`, `kdey_hf_model`, `kdey_hf_loglik`, `split_halves`, `calibration_bags`, `calibration_seed`, `class_recalls`, `accuracy_criterion`, `select_from_curve`, `interval_scores`, `select_temperature_from_scores`; `BayCAPMAPLS` | `methods/bayesian_quantifier.py`, `methods/bayes_kdey.py`, `methods/bayes_mapls.py`, `methods/baycap_quantifier.py`, `methods/baycap_kdey.py`, `methods/baycap_mapls.py` | `tests/test_baycap_fixed.py`, `tests/test_baycap_split.py`, `tests/test_baycap_kdey_hf.py`, `tests/test_wp25_restructure.py`, `tests/test_bayes_kdey_loo.py`, `tests/test_kdey_cal.py`, `tests/test_kdey_cal_solver.py`, `tests/test_kdey_cal_tcal.py` | §II.4; the E3.1 configurations evaluated by E3.1 (WP-8, as `BayCAPFixed` until WP-13); `BayCAPKDEy` with `c_prior="hierarchical-f"` (WP-13, evaluated by E3.2, not carried forward); `bandwidth="loo"` (WP-25, evaluated by the pilot of E7.1, §II.29, not carried forward); `bandwidth="cal"` and `temperature="cal"` (WP-26, evaluated by E7.2, §II.30) | The quantifier classes (WP-25) are the natural unit of the move, since QuaPy has the same split between quantifiers and the methods that use them. The NUTS models of BayesKDEy and BayesianMAPLS are copies of QuaPy's (`quapy.method._bayesian`, commit `31f36f6`), because QuaPy builds those classes only with `pystan`; in `cap-ml` the classes could use QuaPy's quantifiers directly where `pystan` is a dependency, or keep the copies. `kdey_hf_model` is the project's own model (not in QuaPy), and uses `hierarchical_rate_sites` of `methods/baycap.py`. The classes use QuaPy's private `_resolve_dirichlet_prior`, `mapls` and `quapy.method._kdey.KDEBase`, `realised_tables`, `nuts_diagnostics`, `hierarchical_summaries` and `failed_summaries` of `methods/baycap.py`, and scikit-learn's `MLPClassifier`; `calibration_bags` uses QuaPy's `UPP` and restores `qp.environ["_R_SEED"]`, which QuaPy's `temp_seed` overwrites. |

Moving a component changes no result if the code moves unchanged, but `uncawe` then needs the new `cap-ml` commit, and a pin bump (§V, `cap-ml` pin); experiments that use the component (`main` after WP-4) should record the `cap-ml` commit they ran with.

## II.13 The comparison of the population and realised pushforwards of BayCAP

`tables/pushforward.py` and `plots/pushforward.py` (tests: `tests/test_pushforward.py`) compare, on the stored `main` results, `BayesCAP` (population pushforward, population table) with `BayesCAP-real` (realised-table posterior predictive, §II.5, realised table), both scored against the realised table (`true_cts`) ([[ext2_baycap]], E2.3). Per (dataset, classifier) pair with both methods stored, at $\lvert U\rvert=1000$ and for vanilla accuracy, the table reports coverage, amplitude and Winkler score ($\alpha=0.05$) of both, means over the bags (`bag_scores`, `interval_scores`); a paired bootstrap test of the mean per-bag Winkler difference, realised minus population (`winkler_tests`, `bootstrap_mean_test`: bags paired by position and checked on their realised accuracy, $10{,}000$ resamples seeded per pair with `SEED` and `pair_rng` of `tables/estimand_gap.py`; columns `winkler_diff_mean`, `winkler_ci_low`, `winkler_ci_high` for the $95\%$ percentile interval, `winkler_diff_median` as a description, `winkler_p` two-sided and `winkler_p_holm` Holm-adjusted over the pairs, `holm`); and the approximate coverage and the ratio $\hat\sigma_g/\hat\sigma_p$ read from the CSV of §II.10, with the largest gap between the population coverage computed here and the one stored there as a check. Outputs: a per-pair CSV and one table as LaTeX and PDF under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/pushforward/`; two scatters (population against realised coverage, coloured by $\hat\sigma_g/\hat\sigma_p$; exact against approximate coverage) under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/main/pushforward/`. The E2.2 table and scatters of `BayesCAP-real` are produced by the scripts of §II.11 with `--method BayesCAP-real`.

- **Run:** on `bcuda`, JAX on CPU, `cap-ml` `bb20aa5`. `BayesCAP-real`: `main --classic` from commit `78487c2`, 2026-10-21 15:22 to 2026-10-22 06:36, exit code 0, all 75 pairs and the three metrics. `BayesCAP` recomputed on CPU: commit `a23fb97`, 2026-10-22 (§I.2). Tables of §II.10, §II.11 (both methods) and of this section generated on 2026-10-22 (commit `a23fb97`), the table of this section again with the bootstrap test on 2026-10-23, the scatters of §II.11 for both methods on 2026-10-23 (commit `975f2ac`); copied locally with `make pull`. Results under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main/` (method `BayesCAP-real`), `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/pushforward/`, `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/main/pushforward/`, `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/displacement/BayesCAP-real/` and `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/main/displacement/BayesCAP-real/`.
- **Findings:** the realised interval is the population one widened by $\sqrt{1+(\hat\sigma_g/\hat\sigma_p)^2}$ (within $\pm5\%$ on every pair), with the same centre (shifts within $0.009$), so the causes of the displacement of §II.11 are unchanged. Coverage never drops; mean coverage rises from $0.60$ to $0.68$, and the pairs at or above $0.95$ from $12$ to $28$. The Winkler score is significantly lower on $31$ pairs, all with a displaced population interval, significantly higher on $9$ pairs that already covered ($+0.004$ to $+0.011$), and not significantly different on $35$ (Holm, $0.05$). The approximation of §II.10 predicts the exact coverage within $0.013$ on average (correlation $0.998$). $7$ of the $10$ pairs whose population interval is too narrow for its shift (§II.11) are no longer so. *After FIX-8* (`poker_hand` with $K=7$; tables regenerated on 2026-11-13 from `50f17df`, with FIX-2 + FIX-3): pairs at or above $0.95$ from $12$ to $29$; Winkler score significantly lower on $34$ pairs (the three displaced `poker_hand` pairs added), higher on $9$, not different on $32$; $7$ of the $8$ pairs too narrow for their shift are no longer so.
- **Card:** WP-4 (§III.3).

## II.14 The empirical-Bayes prior on the recalls of BayCAP

`tables/eb_prior.py` and `plots/eb_prior.py` (tests: `tests/test_eb_prior.py`) compare, on the stored `main` results, the four BayCAP methods of §II.5: `BayesCAP` and `BayesCAP-real` ($\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1)$ prior on the columns of $C$) with `BayesCAP-EB` and `BayesCAP-EB-real` (empirical-Bayes prior, `c_prior="empirical-bayes"`), population and realised pushforward respectively, all scored against the realised table (`true_cts`) ([[ext2_baycap]], E2.4). Per (dataset, classifier) pair with all four methods stored, at $\lvert U\rvert=1000$ and for vanilla accuracy, it reports: the check, computed from the validation counts alone (`recall_estimates`, `recall_error`, `check_table`): the mean over the bags of $\Delta^{(b)}=\sum_j\pi^{(b)}_j(\tilde r_j-r^{(b)}_j)$ and of $\lvert\Delta^{(b)}\rvert$ for the plain, $\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1)$ and empirical-Bayes estimates $\tilde r_j$ of the recalls, the fit $(\hat m,\hat\nu)$ with the bounds reached, and the left-out standard deviation $\sum_j\pi_j(1-w_j)\operatorname{sd}(\hat m)$ with $\pi$ uniform (`left_out_sd`); the E2.2 scores and terms of the four methods (`tables/displacement.py:pair_table`); per pair, the share of bags with divergent transitions, the largest split-$\hat R$ and the smallest effective sample size (`diagnostics_table`, from the stored columns `nuts_divergences`, `nuts_max_rhat`, `nuts_min_ess`); within each pushforward, the paired bootstrap test of the mean Winkler difference, empirical Bayes minus $\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1)$, Holm-adjusted over the pairs (`tables/pushforward.py:winkler_tests`); and the predicted and observed change of the recall term. Outputs: one per-pair CSV and two tables (comparison; prior), as LaTeX and landscape PDF, under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/eb_prior/`; two scatters (check against variant; coverage with the two priors, one panel per pushforward) under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/main/eb_prior/`. The E2.2 table and scatters of the two new methods are produced by the scripts of §II.11 with `--method`.

- **Run:** on `bcuda` by the user, JAX on CPU, `cap-ml` `bb20aa5`: `main --classic` for `BayesCAP-EB`, `BayesCAP-EB-real`, then `BayesCAP` and `BayesCAP-real` after deleting their stored files, all on 2026-10-26, all $75$ pairs and the three metrics; the E2.4 tables and plots and the E2.2 outputs of `BayesCAP-EB` and `BayesCAP-EB-real` generated on 2026-10-27 and copied locally with `make pull`. Commit of the runs: not recorded (branch `wp-6/eb-prior-eval` before the merge). Results under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main/` (methods `BayesCAP-EB`, `BayesCAP-EB-real`, and `BayesCAP`, `BayesCAP-real` with diagnostics), `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/eb_prior/`, `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/main/eb_prior/`, `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/displacement/BayesCAP-EB/`, `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/displacement/BayesCAP-EB-real/` and the corresponding folders of `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/main/displacement/`.
- **Findings:** the rerun of `BayesCAP` and `BayesCAP-real` reproduces the scores of §II.13 (identical coverage on every pair). The check predicts the change of the recall term from the $\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1)$ to the empirical-Bayes prior almost exactly (correlation $0.9995$, slope $0.97$). With the empirical-Bayes prior the large-$K$ displacement disappears (`isolet`, `letter`: realised coverage from $0$ to $0.84$–$0.96$); with the realised pushforward the pairs with coverage at least $0.90$ go from $34$ to $52$, mean coverage from $0.68$ to $0.84$, and the mean Winkler score from $0.84$ to $0.32$, significantly lower on $40$ pairs and higher on $10$ (Holm, $0.05$). The population interval with the empirical-Bayes prior is too narrow for its shift on $26$ pairs, the realised one on $3$. On binary datasets no pair gains coverage and $11$ lose more than $0.05$: $\hat\nu$ reaches its upper bound on four `mammographic` pairs and the interval becomes too narrow. NUTS diverges in more than half the bags on $18$ pairs with the empirical-Bayes prior (none with $\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1)$), all with a concentration below $0.17$, and does not mix on `isolet` (split-$\hat R$ up to $2.9$, or NaN); no pair whose concentrations are all at least $1$ diverges. *After FIX-8* (`poker_hand` with $K=7$; tables regenerated on 2026-11-13 from `50f17df`, with FIX-2 + FIX-3): with the realised pushforward the pairs at least $0.90$ go from $36$ to $51$, mean coverage from $0.68$ to $0.83$, mean Winkler score from $0.85$ to $0.32$, significantly lower on $41$ pairs and higher on $10$; NUTS diverges in more than half the bags on $17$ pairs (`poker_hand` LR, SVM, RF).
- **Card:** WP-6 (§III.3).

## II.15 The empirical-Bayes prior on the binary datasets of `quapy`

[[ext2_baycap]], E2.5: the four BayCAP methods of §II.14 on the $25$ binary datasets of `quapy` that `main` does not use (`UCI_BINARY_DATASETS` without `german`, `mammographic`, `semeion`, `spambase`, `tictactoe`). `pretrain/classic_binary.py` trains the $5$ classifiers of `pretrain/classic.py:gen_classifiers` on them, with the split of `cap-ml`'s `fetch_UCIBinaryDataset`, and stores them under the pretrain domain `classic_binary` (`<CAP_DATA>/pretrain/classic_binary/`); `--sizes` prints the sizes of $L$, $V$, $U$ and the class counts without training. `experiments/eb_binary.py` runs `BayesCAP`, `BayesCAP-real`, `BayesCAP-EB` and `BayesCAP-EB-real` (`experiments/methods.py:BAYCAP_VARIANTS`) with the protocol of `main` (`experiments/main.py:exp_protocol`, called with `experiment="eb_binary"`: $100$ UPP bags of size $1000$ from $U$ with the seed of `main`, methods fitted on all of $V$, the records, three metrics and NUTS diagnostics of `main`), all in the BayCAP pool (`BAYES_N_JOBS`, `BAYES_ENV`), with `--datasets`, `--classifiers` and `--methods` filters; results under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/eb_binary/classic_binary/`. The population methods target $C\operatorname{diag}(\pi^Q)$, the realised ones the realised table; all are scored against the realised table. `tables/eb_binary.py` builds the per-pair table of `tables/eb_prior.py` (`compute_pair_table`, which takes the experiment and the domain) on the pairs of E2.5 and on the binary pairs of `main`, and adds, from the validation counts alone, $n^s_1$, $n^s_2$, $\hat r_1$, $\hat r_2$, $|\hat r_1-\hat r_2|$ and the gap statistic $z$ (`fit_diagnostics`, `gap_statistic`), the predicted pull averaged over the bags (`pull`, equal to $R_0$ of the empirical-Bayes methods), the change of coverage within each pushforward, the size of the test pool (`n_test`, from the pretrain files, `load_pool_sizes`), the smaller Beta parameter of the empirical-Bayes prior, $\min(\hat\nu\hat m,\hat\nu(1-\hat m))$ (`eb_alpha_min`), and the ratio of the realised amplitudes, empirical Bayes over $\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1)$ (`realised_amplitude_ratio`); `analysis_summary` reduces the table to the summaries of the E2.5 Analysis (scores, failures of the uniform prior, the bound by band of $\lvert z\rvert$, pairs with a finite $\hat\nu$, divergences), per source and with the pairs of E2.5 split at $\lvert U\rvert=100$ (`SMALL_POOL`), printed by `--tables`; outputs: one CSV and two tables (comparison; fit), as LaTeX and landscape PDF, under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/eb_binary/`. `plots/eb_binary.py` draws the three scatters of E2.5 (`fit`, `collapse`, `pull`) under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/eb_binary/`. Tests: `tests/test_eb_binary.py`. `main`, its pretrained classifiers, results, tables and ranks are unchanged.

- **Run:** on `bcuda`, JAX on CPU, commit `ffa378f`, `cap-ml` `bb20aa5`: pretraining of the $5$ classifiers on the $25$ datasets on 2026-10-28; the four methods in one invocation of `experiments/eb_binary.py`, 2026-10-28 14:33 to 2026-10-29 01:45, all $125$ pairs and the three metrics ($500$ cells); tables and plots generated on 2026-10-29 and copied locally with `make pull`. The stored CSV predates the columns `n_test`, `eb_alpha_min` and `realised_amplitude_ratio`, which it gets when the tables are generated again. Results under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/eb_binary/classic_binary/`, `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/eb_binary/`, `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/eb_binary/`. Test pools: $45$ to $1642$ instances, below $100$ on $9$ datasets ($45$ pairs).
- **Findings:** $\hat\nu$ reaches its upper bound on all $11$ pairs with $\lvert z\rvert<1$, on $11$ of $35$ with $1\le\lvert z\rvert<2$ and on $2$ of $74$ with $\lvert z\rvert\ge2$; with $\lvert z\rvert<1$ the realised coverage falls by $0.35$ on average, with $\lvert z\rvert\ge2$ by $0.03$ ($0.005$ on pools of at least $100$ instances). With a finite $\hat\nu$ the check predicts the change of the recall term (correlation $0.971$), but the change falls as the gap between the two recalls grows; the harm on those pairs comes from a narrower interval (median $0.76$ times the uniform amplitude on the $13$ pairs with a significantly higher Winkler score) around a centre already displaced. The uniform prior covers $0.854$ on pools of at least $100$ instances and $0.668$ on smaller ones, with a recall error mostly outside its prior part, consistent with cause 4. Realised Winkler score with the EB prior: significantly lower on $41$ pairs, higher on $31$ (Holm, $0.05$). NUTS diverges in more than $10\%$ of the bags on $20$ EB pairs, all with a Beta parameter below $1$, and in $15\%$ of the bags on average with either prior on the small datasets; on `iris.1` the fit is degenerate ($\hat\nu$ at its lower bound).
- **Card:** WP-7 (§III.3).

## II.16 Zero-cost screens for a hierarchical prior on the recalls of BayCAP

[[ext2_baycap]], E2.6. `tables/hierarchical.py` computes, from the validation counts alone, the posterior of $(m,\nu)$ of a hierarchical Beta prior on the recalls of each classifier, on the grid of `scripts/toys/ext2_hierarchical.py` ($200$ values of $m$ in $[0.0025,0.9975]$, uniform; $200$ values of $\ln\nu$ in $[\ln0.1,\ln10^4]$; three priors on $\nu$, `NU_PRIORS`: $\nu^{-3/2}$, log-uniform, flat), and the mixture moments of the recalls and of accuracy (`mixture_posterior`, `recall_moments`, `accuracy_moments`); the $\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1)$ and empirical-Bayes posteriors are the same objects with one point (`uniform_posterior`, `eb_posterior`). On the pairs of `main` (all $75$) with the four BayCAP methods stored, it reports per pair: the stored coverage, amplitude and Winkler score of the four methods; the D4 prediction of the draws of `BayesCAP` and `BayesCAP-real` under another prior (`predict_draws`: shift by the change of the posterior-mean accuracy at the posterior-mean prevalence of the bag, read from `estim_cts`, and rescale so that the variance due to $C$ is swapped), scored against `true_cts`, for the empirical-Bayes prior (validated against the stored `BayesCAP-EB`, `BayesCAP-EB-real`) and for each hierarchical prior; the diagnosis of D4 (`centre_split`: per bag, the change of the centre between the stored uniform and empirical-Bayes posterior-mean tables split exactly into a recall part and a prevalence part, next to the shift of D4; and the empirical-Bayes prediction repeated with the stored prevalence part added, `ebprev`, and with the stored centre, `ebcentre`); D1 (ratio of the 90% widths of the posterior and prior of $\ln\nu$, posterior mean of $m$, mass with $\nu\ge10^3$, change and standard deviation of accuracy due to $C$ at the uniform prevalence and at the vertices, range of the predictions over the priors); D2 (leave-one-class-out predictive p-values, `loo_pvalue`, and vertex shifts $z_j$); D3 (`alpha_risk`, $P(\min(\nu m,\nu(1-m))<1\mid V)$, and the width of $\ln\nu$; amended with `posterior_alpha_risk`, $P(\min_j\min(a_j,b_j)<1\mid V)$ for the parameters $a_j=\nu m+f_{jj}$, $b_j=\nu(1-m)+n_j-f_{jj}$ of the posterior of the recalls, the classes perfect and never right on $V$, `funnel_risk`, $P(\nu>\min_jn_j\mid V)$, and `eb_posterior_low_share`, the share of cells of $C$ with an empirical-Bayes posterior Dirichlet parameter below $1$; all next to the stored share of divergent bags of `BayesCAP-EB`). `analysis_summary` reduces the table to the summaries of the Analysis (validation with the diagnosis, prediction, screens; over all pairs and per group of $K$), printed by `--tables`. The predictions keep the estimand of the method they start from (population for `BayesCAP`, realised for `BayesCAP-real`), all scored against the realised table; D4 uses target labels only for scoring, so it is an analysis. Outputs: one CSV and two tables (validation; screens), as LaTeX and landscape PDF, under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/hierarchical/`. `plots/hierarchical.py` draws the six scatters of E2.6 (`validation`, `centre`, `prediction`, `sensitivity`, `exchangeability`, `sampler`) from that CSV, under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/hierarchical/`. Tests: `tests/test_hierarchical.py`. No method, experiment or stored result changes.

- **Run:** on `bcuda` by the user, 2026-10-29 (tables at 14:59, plots at 15:53), `cap-ml` `bb20aa5`, on the stored results of the four BayCAP methods of §II.14; copied locally with `make pull`, PDFs generated locally. Commit of the run not recorded; the CSV has the columns of `e892104`. A first run on the same day, without the diagnosis of D4 and the amended D3, gave the same validation and prediction numbers. Results under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/hierarchical/` and `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/hierarchical/`.
- **Findings:** D4 predicts the realised coverage of the empirical-Bayes method within $0.05$ on $0.69$ of the pairs ($0.84$, $0.80$, $0.44$ for $K=2$, $3\le K\le7$, $K\ge8$). The shift of D4 equals the stored recall part (correlation $1.00$); the stored prevalence part matters only on `wine-quality` and `poker_hand` (with it, $0.72$ within $0.05$; with the stored centre, $0.76$); the $18$ pairs that still miss by more than $0.05$ have an amplitude error of the same sign as the coverage error, so D4 misses the change of the spread (hypothesis, not tested: through the variance of the estimated prevalences). D1: the range of the predicted coverage over the three priors on $\nu$ has median $0$; the width ratio is not meaningful for the flat prior. D2: no pair has both a p-value below $0.01$ ($8$ pairs) and $\lvert z_j\rvert>2$ ($2$ pairs). D3: Spearman correlation with the divergences of `BayesCAP-EB` $0.49$ (prior parameters), $0.73$ (posterior parameters), $0.82$ (empirical-Bayes cells below $1$); for the hierarchical model, posterior-parameter risk above $0.5$ on $22$ pairs, all with a class perfect or never right on $V$, and funnel risk above $0.05$ on $12$ (`mammographic`, `hand_digits`, `spambase`, `nursery` MLP), overlapping on $2$. Predicted hierarchical prior (log-uniform): mean realised coverage $0.80$, $0.90$, $0.92$ by group, Winkler score $0.30$, $0.42$, $0.27$ (uniform $0.28$, $0.43$, $1.82$; empirical Bayes $0.53$, $0.30$, $0.12$). *After FIX-8* (`poker_hand` with $K=7$; tables regenerated on 2026-11-13 from `50f17df`, with FIX-2 + FIX-3): `poker_hand` moves to the group $3\le K\le7$ of `tables/hierarchical.py:K_GROUPS`, kept by the user's decision (groups of $25$, $30$, $20$ pairs); D4 within $0.05$ on $0.71$ of the pairs ($0.84$, $0.70$, $0.55$), with the stored prevalence part $0.73$, with the stored centre $0.77$; $17$ pairs still miss; D3 Spearman $0.45$, $0.70$, $0.80$; predicted hierarchical prior: coverage $0.80$, $0.87$, $0.95$ and Winkler score $0.30$, $0.54$, $0.07$ by group (uniform $0.28$, $0.56$, $2.01$; empirical Bayes $0.53$, $0.32$, $0.06$).
- **Card:** WP-9 (§III.3).

## II.17 BayCAP-H: the hierarchical prior on the recalls of BayCAP in `main`

[[ext2_baycap]], E2.7. The method `BayesCAP-H-real` is `BayCAP(c_prior="hierarchical", estimand="realised")` (§II.5), in `experiments/methods.py:BAYCAP_VARIANTS`, run by `main` in the BayCAP pool with the bags, seeds and NUTS settings of the other BayCAP methods; it was in the tables and ranks of `main` and in the coverage vs amplitude plot until 2026-11-05, when `BayesCAP-Hf-real` replaced it (§II.5, §V.4); it is in no table, rank or plot of `main` now. `tables/hierarchical.py:NU_PRIORS` holds the log-densities in $\ln\nu$ of the priors of the grid, with the prior of BayCAP-H (`unifu`); the E2.6 screens keep their three priors (`SCREEN_PRIORS`). `tables/baycap_h.py` reads the stored results of `BayesCAP-H-real`, `BayesCAP-real` and `BayesCAP-EB-real` and reports per pair, vanilla accuracy, realised pushforward: AE, coverage, amplitude and Winkler score of the three methods (those of BayCAP-H without its failed bags); the paired bootstrap tests of the mean Winkler difference, BayCAP-H minus each of the other two, Holm over the pairs; the NUTS diagnostics and the share of failed bags; the posterior of $m$ and $\ln\nu$ of the run; the D3 indicators and the D4 prediction for the prior of the run, computed with the functions of `tables/hierarchical.py`; the residual variances $s^2-V_C(\bar\pi)$ of `BayesCAP-real` and BayCAP-H and the prevalence part $\bar r^\top\Sigma_\pi\bar r$ of BayCAP-H; and a failure class for the pairs with more than $10\%$ divergent bags (`failure_class`: boundary first, then funnel if the mean posterior $P(\nu>\min_jn_j)$ exceeds $0.5$, else other). `analysis_summary` reduces the table to the summaries of the Analysis, per group of $K$, printed by `--tables`. Outputs: one CSV and two tables (comparison; sampler and checks), as LaTeX and landscape PDF, under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/baycap_h/`. `plots/baycap_h.py` draws four scatters (`coverage`, `d4`, `spread`, `d3`) from the CSV, under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/baycap_h/`. Tests: `tests/test_baycap.py`, `tests/test_baycap_h.py`, `tests/test_main_protocol.py`, `tests/test_methods.py`.

- **Run:** on `bcuda` by the user, `main --classic` for `BayesCAP-H-real`, JAX on CPU, `cap-ml` `bb20aa5`, from branch `wp-10/baycap-h`; all $75$ pairs and the three metrics, no failed bag; date and commit of the run not recorded. Tables and plots generated on 2026-11-02 and copied locally with `make pull`. Results under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main/` (method `BayesCAP-H-real`), `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/baycap_h/`, `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/baycap_h/`. The tables, ranks and plots of `main` have not been generated again with `BayesCAP-H-real`.
- **Findings:** mean realised coverage $0.87$ and Winkler score $0.31$ (uniform $0.68$, $0.84$; empirical Bayes $0.84$, $0.32$); at $K=2$ Winkler score $0.29$ (uniform $0.28$, empirical Bayes $0.53$). Winkler score significantly lower than the uniform prior on $36$ pairs and higher on $6$; against empirical Bayes, lower on $11$ (all of `mammographic` and `german` LR, SVM, RF among them) and higher on $11$ (`wine-quality`, `poker_hand` and five pairs below $0.16$). Mean AE $0.039$ (uniform $0.057$, empirical Bayes $0.036$); without `wine-quality` and `poker_hand`, $0.028$ against $0.029$. D4 within $0.05$ of the observed coverage on $0.72$ of the pairs; the prevalence part explains the change of the spread only where $C$ is badly conditioned. NUTS does not mix ($\hat R>1.1$) on $9$ pairs: `hand_digits` (four), `mammographic` LR, kNN, RF (funnel: $V$ does not fix $\nu$) and `poker_hand` SVM, RF; the divergences follow the funnel indicators (Spearman $0.66$), not the posterior-parameter one ($0.26$). `failure_class` misclassifies the funnel pairs, and the Analysis does not use it. *After FIX-8* (`poker_hand` with $K=7$; tables regenerated on 2026-11-13 from `50f17df`, with FIX-2 + FIX-3): empirical Bayes $0.83$, $0.32$; significantly lower than the uniform prior on $38$ pairs; mean AE $0.040$ (empirical Bayes $0.037$); NUTS does not mix on $7$ pairs, all through the funnel (no `poker_hand` pair any more), and on the $68$ pairs that mix H has mean Winkler score $0.332$ against $0.304$ for empirical Bayes; Spearman $0.56$ (funnel) and $0.23$.
- **Card:** WP-10 (§III.3).

## II.18 BayCAP-Hf: a lighter tail on the strength and learned shares of the mistakes, in `main`

[[ext2_baycap]], E2.8. The method `BayesCAP-Hf-real` is `BayCAP(c_prior="hierarchical-f", estimand="realised")` (§II.5), in `experiments/methods.py:BAYCAP_VARIANTS`, run by `main` in the BayCAP pool with the bags, seeds and NUTS settings of the other BayCAP methods; since 2026-11-05 it is in the tables and ranks of `main` and in the coverage vs amplitude plot (§II.5, §V.4), and in no other default plot (`tables/main.py:EXCLUDED_METHODS`). `tables/baycap_hf.py` reads the stored results of `BayesCAP-Hf-real`, `BayesCAP-H-real`, `BayesCAP-EB-real` and `BayesCAP-real` and reports per pair, vanilla accuracy, realised pushforward: AE, coverage, amplitude and Winkler score of the four methods (those of a hierarchical method without its failed bags); four paired bootstrap tests of the mean per-bag difference (AE and Winkler score, BayCAP-Hf minus `BayesCAP-H-real` and minus `BayesCAP-EB-real`), each Holm-adjusted over the pairs; the recall and prevalence terms of the shift of three methods; for both hierarchical methods, the share of divergent bags, the mean number of divergent transitions per bag and the share of divergent draws (`divergence_load`, of `NUTS_DRAWS`$=1\,000$), the largest split-$\hat R$, the smallest effective sample size, and, over the bags with draws, the median of the largest split-$\hat R$ of each bag, the share of bags where it exceeds $1.1$ and the median of the smallest effective sample size of each bag (`mixing_spread`, added on 2026-11-03 after the full run, computed from the stored per-bag diagnostics; the failure rule still uses the largest $\hat R$), the failed bags and the posterior of $\nu$; the posterior of $\kappa$; from $V$, the share of empty off-diagonal cells and the classes perfect and never right; and a failure class for the pairs with more than $1\%$ of the draws divergent (`DIVERGENT_DRAWS`) or $\hat R$ above $1.1$: funnel if the mean posterior $P(\nu>\min_jn_j)$ exceeds $0.05$, else boundary, else other. Pairs with a class are unreliable (`is_unreliable`). `analysis_summary` reduces the table per group of $K$, over all the pairs and over the reliable ones (`scores_reliable`), printed by `--tables`. Outputs: one CSV and two tables (comparison; sampler and posterior), as LaTeX and landscape PDF, under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/baycap_hf/`. `plots/baycap_hf.py` draws three scatters (`tail`, `shares`, `scores`) from the CSV, under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/baycap_hf/`. Tests: `tests/test_baycap.py`, `tests/test_baycap_hf.py`, `tests/test_methods.py`.

- **Run:** on `bcuda` by the user, `main --classic` for `BayesCAP-Hf-real`, JAX on CPU, `cap-ml` `bb20aa5`, from branch `wp-11/baycap-hf`; pilot on 2026-11-02, full run by 2026-11-03 (date and commit of the run not recorded); all $75$ pairs and the three metrics, no failed bag. Tables and plots generated on 2026-11-03 and copied locally with `make pull`. Results under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main/` (method `BayesCAP-Hf-real`), `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/baycap_hf/`, `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/baycap_hf/`.
- **Findings:** mean realised AE $0.035$, coverage $0.89$, Winkler score $0.23$ (`BayesCAP-H-real` $0.039$, $0.87$, $0.31$; empirical Bayes $0.036$, $0.84$, $0.32$); against `BayesCAP-H-real`, significantly lower AE on $23$ pairs and higher on $1$, lower Winkler score on $8$ and higher on none. $23$ pairs are unreliable ($18$ boundary: all of `poker_hand` and `isolet`, four of `nursery`, three of `wine-quality`, `shuttle` MLP; $5$ funnel: four of `hand_digits`, `mammographic` RF). On the $52$ reliable pairs, mean Winkler score $0.231$ against $0.240$ for `BayesCAP-H-real`, never significantly higher, and AE higher on one pair only, so BayCAP-Hf replaces BayCAP-H as the candidate method. The lighter tail removes the funnel on the binary pairs (pairs above $1\%$ of divergent draws $5\to1$, not mixing $3\to0$), not on `hand_digits`, where the data push $\nu$ up. The posterior median of $\kappa$ is below $1$ on $47$ of the $50$ multiclass pairs; the prevalence term of the shift halves ($0.007$ against $0.014$) and the gap to empirical Bayes on `wine-quality` and `poker_hand` closes; with the centred Dirichlet, NUTS diverges on the $18$ boundary pairs (up to $65\%$ of the draws on `poker_hand`). Follow-up: the non-centred shares of E2.9. *After FIX-8* (`poker_hand` with $K=7$; tables regenerated on 2026-11-13 from `50f17df`, with FIX-2 + FIX-3): mean AE $0.036$, coverage $0.88$, Winkler score $0.24$; AE lower than `BayesCAP-H-real` on $25$ pairs; the $23$ unreliable pairs and the $52$ reliable ones are unchanged, and so is the decision; on `poker_hand` $2$–$51\%$ of the draws diverge, and `poker_hand` LR does not mix.
- **Card:** WP-11 (§III.3).

## II.19 BayCAP-Hfn: non-centred shares of the mistakes of BayCAP-Hf, in `main` (pilot only)

[[ext2_baycap]], E2.9. The method `BayesCAP-Hfn-real` is `BayCAP(c_prior="hierarchical-f", estimand="realised", shares="non-centred")` (§II.5), in `experiments/methods.py:BAYCAP_VARIANTS` and `BAYCAP_OPTIONS`, run by `main` in the BayCAP pool with the bags, seeds and NUTS settings of the other BayCAP methods; it is in no table, rank or plot of `main` (`tables/main.py:EXCLUDED_METHODS`). It has the model of `BayesCAP-Hf-real` (§II.18) and samples the shares in other coordinates. `tables/baycap_hfn.py` reads the stored results of `BayesCAP-Hfn-real` and of the four methods of §II.18 on the pairs stored for all five, and reports per pair: AE, coverage, amplitude and Winkler score of the five; two paired bootstrap tests of the mean per-bag difference (AE and Winkler score, BayCAP-Hfn minus `BayesCAP-Hf-real`), Holm-adjusted over the pairs; for both samplers of the model, the NUTS diagnostics of §II.18 (`divergence_load`), the failure class of E2.8 (`tables/baycap_hf.py:failure_class`), the posterior of $\nu$ and $\kappa$ and the mean time per bag (`t_test_ave`); from $V$, the indicators of the check (`mistake_indicators`: columns with no observed mistake, median over the columns of the observed mistakes per off-diagonal cell). `analysis_summary` groups the pairs by their class under BayCAP-Hf. `--check` writes, from the CSV of `tables/baycap_hf.py` and the validation counts, the indicators of the $50$ pairs with $K\ge3$ and their Spearman correlation with the share of divergent draws of BayCAP-Hf (`check_summary`). Outputs: `baycap_hfn.csv`, `baycap_hfn_check.csv` and two tables (comparison; sampler), as LaTeX and landscape PDF, under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/baycap_hfn/`; `plots/baycap_hfn.py` draws three scatters (`sampler`, `model`, `check`) under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/baycap_hfn/`. Tests: `tests/test_baycap.py`, `tests/test_baycap_hfn.py`, `tests/test_methods.py`.

- **Run:** on `bcuda` by the user, `main --classic` for `BayesCAP-Hfn-real`, JAX on CPU, `cap-ml` `bb20aa5`, from branch `wp-12/baycap-hfn` (`f3e429e`), on 2026-11-03; the pilot only, on `poker_hand` LR, `nursery` LR, `isolet` kNN and `letter` kNN, three metrics, no failed bag; the full run on the $75$ pairs was not done (user decision of 2026-11-03). Check, tables and plots generated on 2026-11-03 and copied locally with `make pull`. Results under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main/` (method `BayesCAP-Hfn-real`, four pairs), `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/baycap_hfn/`, `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/baycap_hfn/`.
- **Findings:** the check finds the divergences of BayCAP-Hf following the share of empty off-diagonal cells (Spearman $0.72$), not the observed mistakes per column ($-0.29$; columns with no mistake $0.37$), so the hypothesis of E2.8 is not supported. On the pilot the non-centred form makes the sampler worse on the three boundary pairs (divergent draws $5.2\to45.6\%$, $65.5\to99.8\%$, $34.6\to97.7\%$; largest $\hat R$ up to $2.01$; smallest ESS $3$–$7$) and keeps the control `letter` kNN clean, with the same posterior as BayCAP-Hf ($\ln\kappa$ $-1.394$ for both) and no significant difference of scores; the significant differences on `poker_hand` LR and `nursery` LR are errors of its sampler. BayCAP-Hfn does not replace BayCAP-Hf, which stays the candidate method; analysis in [[ext2_baycap]], E2.9. *After FIX-8* (`poker_hand` with $K=7$; tables regenerated on 2026-11-13 from `50f17df`, with FIX-2 + FIX-3): on the re-run pilot pair `poker_hand` LR ($K=7$) the divergent draws go from $51.1\%$ to $99.5\%$ ($\hat R$ $1.19\to1.83$) and its score differences are no longer significant; only `nursery` LR keeps one; the decision is unchanged.
- **Card:** WP-12 (§III.3).

## II.20 The error split and the oracle 2×2 of the main-table methods

`error_split.py` (pure numpy; tests: `tests/test_error_split.py`) splits the error $\hat A-A(h,U)$ of a method that estimates a contingency table into the prevalence part $e_1=\sum_j(\tilde\pi_j-\pi^Q_j)\tilde d_j$, the recall part $e_2=\sum_j\pi^Q_j(\tilde d_j-d^{\text{pool}}_j)$ and the luck of the bag $e_3=\sum_j\pi^Q_j(d^{\text{pool}}_j-c_j/n_j)$ ([[ext4_uncertainty_budget]] §T.2), with the cross term $x=\sum_j(\tilde\pi_j-\pi^Q_j)(\tilde d_j-d^{\text{pool}}_j)$ and the four oracle estimates $\sum_j\pi_jd_j$, $\pi\in\{\tilde\pi,\pi^Q\}$, $d\in\{\tilde d,d^{\text{pool}}\}$ (§T.5). $\tilde\pi$ and $\tilde d$ are read from the stored mean table `estim_cts`, $\pi^Q$ and $c_j/n_j$ from `true_cts` (both in the `cap-ml` layout, true classes on rows, normalised), and $d^{\text{pool}}$ from the argmax of the pretrain `U_posteriors` against the pool labels. Conventions: a class absent from the bag contributes $0$ to $e_3$; a class with an empty estimated column gets $\tilde d_j:=d^{\text{pool}}_j$ (its contribution goes to $e_1$), and such bags are counted; bags where NUTS failed are dropped and counted. The split uses the true prevalences and the pool labels, so it is an analysis, and the three oracle cells are references.

`tables/error_split.py` applies it to the stored `main` results (vanilla accuracy, $\lvert U\rvert=1000$) of `QuAcc_NxN`, `O-LEAP`, `RQBS` (`SQS-Hbs` since FIX-2, which also adds `SQS-H`), `PQ`, `BayesCAP-real`, `BayesCAP-Hf-real`, `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` and, from WP-18 to 2026-11-23, `SQS-B` (left out since 2026-11-24, user's decision) (`DoC` estimates no table; the methods are `tables/main.py:MAIN_METHODS_BEFORE_BAYESCAP` (the methods of the main table without `BayesCAP`, added there on 2026-11-23) without `DoC`, on the pairs stored for all of them), scored against the realised table of each bag: a per-bag CSV, a per-pair CSV, E4.1 tables A (RMS of the parts and of the total, coloured per row and group, superscript naming the part with the largest RMS, a row with the share of pairs in which each part is the largest) and B (signed means), and the two E4.3 tables (mean AE of the four cells against the realised and against the pool accuracy, with the mean and RMS of $x$); `--tables-set e4.1|e4.3`; checks of the identity and counts of dropped and empty-column bags are printed. `plots/error_split.py` draws the E4.1 bar plots: the mean square of each part, stacked on a linear scale with a dot for the total, one panel per dataset for every method but `QuAcc_NxN` (`--figures bars`), and `QuAcc_NxN` alone, one bar per dataset (`--figures bars-quacc`). Since WP-21, `error_split.py` also splits a part within the cells of E4.2 (§T.4): `cell_parts` gives the fixed part of each bag (the mean of the part over its cell) and its bag part (the deviation from that mean), `fixed_se` the standard error $s/\sqrt R$ of a fixed part from the bag parts of its cell, and `over_seeds` the mean and the standard deviation of the fixed parts of a cell across the $V$ seeds; they are used by `tables/target_size.py` (WP-21, §II.26; besides the per-bag, per-cell, per-seed, per-level and slope tables it writes `recalls`, the gap between the recalls of $h$ on $V$ and on the pool with its two-sample standard deviation and the subsample standard deviation of §T.4, `drift`, the drift of the seed means of $e_2$ from the full-$V$ value, and `diagnostics`, the NUTS diagnostics per method, dataset and level) and change nothing in the tables of E4.1 and E4.3.

- **Run:** 2026-11-10, on `bcuda` (tables from `24b6776`, bar plots from `45a69b5`), `cap-ml` `87b4d9b`, on the stored results of `main` with `PQ` re-run with that commit so that it stores its table (§I.2); outputs under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/error_split/` and `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/main/error_split/`, pulled and PDFs built locally. Checks: the identity holds within $10^{-14}$ on every bag for every method except `O-LEAP` ($8.1\cdot10^{-11}$) and `QuAcc_NxN` (up to $1.5\cdot10^{-6}$ on 11 pairs: its stored estimate is not exactly the trace of its stored mean table); no bag dropped; empty-column bags: `QuAcc_NxN` 838, `RQBS` 910, BayCAP-KDEy 11.
- **Findings** (the Analysis sections of E4.1 and E4.3 in [[ext4_uncertainty_budget]]): $e_3$ (RMS $\approx0.009$–$0.010$) is a common floor that makes up most of the error of the best methods on the easy datasets; on binary datasets $e_2$ dominates and is mostly a constant offset per pair, positive for every method (mostly `mammographic` and `german`, the smallest $V$; smaller for RF and kNN), ascribed as a hypothesis to the finite $V$ (to be tested by E4.2); on multiclass datasets $e_1$ weighs more, and with the true prevalence the best methods come close to the floor; `BayesCAP-real` underestimates the recalls as $K$ grows and `BayesCAP-Hf-real` removes it; `QuAcc_NxN` has very large parts that partly cancel (large negative $x$), and the true prevalence makes it worse. *After FIX-8* (`poker_hand` with $K=7$; tables regenerated on 2026-11-13 from `50f17df`, with FIX-2 + FIX-3): `QuAcc_NxN`, `O-LEAP`, `PQ` and `SQS-Hbs` (formerly `RQBS`) re-run, `SQS-H` added; the readings are unchanged, and on multiclass datasets $e_1$ grows mostly through `poker_hand`, where it carries almost the whole error of every method.
- **Card:** WP-14 (§III.3).

## II.21 The allocation $\rho$ of the validation set in `PrediQuant`

Since `cap-ml` `0f25e9e` (version 0.1.1.12; pinned since `637fdd5`, version 0.1.1.13, by FIX-2 + FIX-3), `PrediQuant` (`cap-ml` `src/cap/models/confidence.py`; tests: `tests/test_prediquant.py`) has a parameter `rho`, the share $\rho=\lvert V_1\rvert/\lvert V\rvert$ of the validation set that trains the quantifier; the rest, $V_2$, is where the candidate bags are drawn from. With `rho=None` (the default, used by `PQ`, §I.2) the split is the stratified 50/50 `train_test_split` seeded by `random_state`, as before. With $\rho\in(0,1)$ (`_split`), the validation indices of each class $\omega_j$ are permuted once with a `RandomState(random_state)` (classes in the order of `val.classes_`) and $V_1$ takes the first $\operatorname{round}(\rho\lvert V_j\rvert)$ of them (half rounded up); since the permutations do not depend on $\rho$, for the same seed $V_1(0.1)\subset V_1(0.3)\subset\dots\subset V_1(0.9)$. A $\rho$ outside $(0,1)$, or one that leaves a class empty in $V_1$ or in $V_2$, raises `ValueError`. After `fit`, `PrediQuant` keeps the indices in $V$ of the two parts (`Vq_idx`, `Vb_idx`), the positions in $V_2$ of the instances of each candidate bag (`sigma_indices`) and the labels of $V_2$ (`Vb_y`); after each `predict_ct_range` call (and so after `predict_ct`, `predict_range`, `predict`), it keeps the estimated prevalence $\hat\pi^Q$ of the target (`last_test_prev`, equal to `q.predict` on the target) and the positions of the selected candidate bags (`last_selected_bags`). `class_support(selected_bags=None)` returns, for each class, the number of distinct instances of that class in $V_2$ that appear in at least one selected bag (by default, the bags of the last prediction), divided by the number of instances of that class in $V_2$. The estimand is unchanged: SQS-S targets the realised table of the target bag. In UNCAWE, the five methods `PQ-rho<ρ>` of `main` (`experiments/methods.py:PQ_RHO_VARIANTS`, E6.2 of [[ext6_sqs_s_design]]) set `rho` and store, per bag, `last_test_prev` and `class_support()` as the columns `pq_test_prev`, `pq_class_support` and `pq_min_class_support` (`experiments/main.py:allocation_columns`); they are excluded from the main and rank tables and from the default plots of `main`. `tables/pq_rho.py` (the quantification error from `pq_test_prev` and `true_cts`, the per-pair means, the failed (pair, level), the summary with the paired Wilcoxon tests against $\rho=0.5$, Holm-adjusted over the measures and levels of each metric) and `plots/pq_rho.py` (each measure against $\rho$, one figure per metric) produce the outputs of E6.2; tests: `tests/test_methods.py`, `tests/test_main_protocol.py`, `tests/test_pq_rho.py`. Run and findings: §II.23. Cards: WP-15, WP-17 (§III.3).

## II.22 E6.1: the distances of SQS-S in `predist`

- **Run:** 2026-11-10, on `bcuda`, `predist` at commit `6cc1aeb` (`make run-predist ARGS="--n-jobs 32"`), `cap-ml` `637fdd5`: the $8$ three-class datasets × the five classifiers ($40$ pairs, none failed), $5$ test bags per pair, the three distances on the same fit; tables at commit `54742e9` (`make run-tables-predist ARGS=--tables`), pulled and PDFs built locally. Results under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/predist/` (schema 4), plots under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/predist/`, tables under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/predist/`. Every score is against the realised table of the test bag.
- **Findings** (the Analysis section of E6.1 in [[ext6_sqs_s_design]]): the decision rule keeps $L_1$: Hellinger and Jensen–Shannon have a lower mean Winkler score ($0.820$ and $0.824$ against $0.861$) but are better than $L_1$ on only $17$ and $16$ of the $40$ pairs (Wilcoxon $p=0.63$, $0.64$); about $80\%$ of that difference comes from the random-point bags of a few pairs where the quantifier misses the test prevalence (`page_block`, `phishing`). For every distance the selected bags are far from the test bag in true prevalence ($0.24$ at the centre, $0.44$ at the vertices), also where the quantifier is accurate: the neighbourhood of a fixed fraction $\gamma=0.1$ of uniform candidates is large at the vertices (hypothesis, recorded in the why of E6.c2).
- **Card:** WP-16 (§III.3).

## II.23 E6.2: the allocation $\rho$ of SQS-S on the `main` protocol

- **Run:** `main` with `PQ-rho0.1`, …, `PQ-rho0.9` (§II.21) on `bcuda`: 2026-11-10, commit `f849f24`, `cap-ml` `637fdd5`, $70$ pairs (`make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods PQ-rho0.1 PQ-rho0.3 PQ-rho0.5 PQ-rho0.7 PQ-rho0.9"`); `poker_hand` on 2026-11-13 after FIX-8, commit `9a64989`, `cap-ml` `93ae246` (the same command with `--datasets poker_hand`); $75$ pairs, $100$ bags, three metrics, no failed (pair, level). Results under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main/` (the methods `PQ-rho*`; the `poker_hand` results before FIX-8 in `main_pre_fix8/`), tables (`make run-tables-pq-rho ARGS=--tables`, commit `0d5c898`) under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/pq_rho/`, plots (`make run-plot-pq-rho`, commit `06f30cd`; the two-panel figures `pq_rho_ae_w_<metric>`, AE and W on a log scale with the mean and the median over the pairs, added on 2026-11-23) under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/pq_rho/`. Every score is against the realised table of the target bag.
- **Findings** (the Analysis section of E6.2 in [[ext6_sqs_s_design]]): the allocation matters only at the edges, and asymmetrically. At $\rho=0.1$ the quantification error doubles ($0.084$ against $0.040$ at $\rho=0.5$), but the absolute error, coverage, amplitude and Winkler score do not differ from $\rho=0.5$ (Holm-adjusted Wilcoxon). At $\rho=0.9$ the Winkler score doubles or triples (vanilla accuracy $0.710$ against $0.316$) and the coverage falls, with an unchanged amplitude: the error of the recalls in $V_b$, shared by all the candidate bags, moves the interval off the target, mostly on the datasets with a small $V$ (`mammographic`, `semeion`, `tictactoe`, `german`). Over $0.3$–$0.7$ the curve is flat; $\rho=0.5$ stays the allocation of `PQ`. The minimum class support of the selected bags is saturated at $1$ and does not measure the reuse of instances within a bag.
- **Card:** WP-17 (§III.3).

## II.24 SQS-B: one validation bag per posterior draw of BayesKDEy, and E3.3

`methods/sqs_b.py:SQSB` (method `SQS-B` of `main`, E3.3 of [[ext3_posterior_propagation]]) is an SQS method with the structure of `cap-ml`'s `RQBS` (`CAPContingencyTable`, `CTCAPWithConfidence`). It builds its own `BayesKDEy` (§II.4) from its hyperparameters (`input_classifier`, `kernel`, `bandwidth`, `bandwidth_grid`, `shrinkage`, the NUTS settings, `temperature`, `prior`, `random_state`), reused through `fit`, `outputs` and `sample_prevalences`, and measures $\hat C$ itself (until WP-25 it wrapped a `QuantifierBayCAP` with a fixed $\hat C$, built by `fixed_c_method`; the results are the same, `tests/test_wp25_restructure.py`); in `main` it is built by `experiments/methods.py:sqs_b` with the configuration and seed of `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` (BayesKDEy, Gaussian kernel, $b=0.1$, MLP input with cross-fitted validation outputs, NUTS with $500$ warm-up iterations and $1000$ draws), so the two methods have the same prevalence draws. For each draw $\pi^{(r)}$ (in double precision, smoothed with $\epsilon=10^{-5}$ as `RQBS`) it draws one bag of `sample_size` $=1000$ instances of $V$ with QuaPy's `sampling_index`, all bags sequentially from the NumPy state seeded by `random_state` ($=0$, `_temp_np_seed` of `cap-ml`; the same `random_state` seeds NUTS and the MLP, as the two seeds of the method were equal in `main` before WP-25), and returns the normalised tables of $h$ on the bags (true classes on rows). Estimand: the realised table of the target sample; it represents $e_1^U$ and $e_3$, not $e_1^V$ nor $e_2$. Per bag, `main` stores the NUTS diagnostics of $\pi$ and the summaries `prev_mean`, `acc_sd`, `sigma_g`, `val_ece`, `sigma_f`, `s_rec`, `s_prec` (§V.3). It runs in the BayCAP pool (`BAYES_POOL_METHODS`, `BAYES_ENV`) and is one of the methods of the main tables, rank tables and coverage vs amplitude plot (§V.4) and of the tables and bar plots of E4.1 and E4.3 (`tables/error_split.py:METHODS`, §II.20). `tables/sqs_b.py` and `plots/sqs_b.py` produce the outputs of E3.3. Tests: `tests/test_sqs_b.py`, `tests/test_sqs_b_tables.py`, `tests/test_methods.py`, `tests/test_main_protocol.py`.

- **Run:** `main` with `SQS-B` on `bcuda` (`JAX=cpu`): 2026-11-13, commit `87ce020`, `cap-ml` `93ae246` (`make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods SQS-B"`), no pilot; $75$ pairs, $100$ bags, three metrics, $31$ minutes, no error. Results under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main/` (method `SQS-B`); tables of E3.3 (`make run-tables-sqs-b ARGS=--tables`, commit `511fba6`) under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/sqs_b/`, plots (`make run-plot-sqs-b`) under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/sqs_b/`; the tables and plots of `main` and of E4.1 and E4.3 regenerated with `SQS-B` the same day. Every score is against the realised table of the target bag.
- **Findings** (the Analysis section of E3.3 in [[ext3_posterior_propagation]]): with the same prevalence draws as `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real`, SQS-B has the centre of the population method `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp` (AE within $0.0003$ on every pair) and about the same width as `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` (median amplitude ratio $1.00$, predicted $1.02$, within $10\%$ on $67$ pairs), but a larger AE on $62$ pairs and a lower coverage ($0.65$ against $0.70$): its forward bag does not use the predictions of $h$ on the target bag. The Holm-adjusted Winkler test finds it worse than `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` on $24$ pairs and better on $2$. Against `SQS-Hbs` it ties on vanilla accuracy (mean Winkler score $0.61$ against $0.66$, Wilcoxon $p=0.46$) and is better on macro-F1 ($0.39$ against $0.51$) and macro-K ($0.42$ against $1.50$). It fails where `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` fails (`mammographic`, `wine-quality`, `poker_hand`, `german`).
- **Card:** WP-18 (§III.3).

## II.25 E5.2: tempering BayCAP-Hf and BayCAP-KDEy in `main`

The eight tempered methods `BayesCAP-Hf-real-t<τ>` and `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-t<τ>`, $\tau\in\{0.5,2,4,8\}$, are methods of `main` outside its tables, ranks and default plots; the code (the `temperature` of `BayCAP`, the factory entries, `experiments/tempering_check.py`) is described in §II.5. Since WP-20, `experiments/main.py:exp_protocol` clears the caches of JAX after each task (Part V, Scheduling). `tables/tempering.py` gives, per metric, method and pair, AE, coverage, amplitude and Winkler score at every $\tau$, the shares of bags with divergent transitions and of divergent draws, the largest split-$\hat R$ and, for BayCAP-KDEy and vanilla accuracy, the observed and predicted widening; the leave-one-dataset-out $\tau_{-d}$ (which uses the labels of the bags of the other $14$ datasets, as a default tuned on a labelled benchmark), the one-sided paired Wilcoxon test over the $15$ datasets of the decision rule, and the global best and per-pair oracle-best $\tau$ as references (they use the target labels); per metric, LaTeX tables of the summary, of $\tau_{-d}$ per dataset and, since WP-23 (2026-11-16, user's request), of the pairs (one row per pair: Winkler score and coverage at every $\tau$, each coloured per row from the worst to the best value as in the tables of E3.1, the best in bold and, for the coverage, the closest to $0.95$; $\tau_{-d}$ and the oracle-best $\tau$). `plots/tempering.py` draws the measures against $\tau$ (also by group of $K$) and the observed against the predicted widening. Tests: `tests/test_tempering.py`, `tests/test_main_protocol.py`, `tests/test_methods.py`. Every score is against the realised table of each bag (`true_cts`).

- **Run:** on `bcuda` (`JAX=cpu`), `cap-ml` `93ae246`: the pilot on `mammographic`, `poker_hand`, `letter` with the five classifiers on 2026-11-14 from `872b966`, and the check of the stored runs at $\tau=1$ on the same $15$ pairs (`main_e52_check`, all $90$ cells equal bit for bit, so the stored `BayesCAP-Hf-real` and `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` are the runs at $\tau=1$); the other $60$ pairs on 2026-11-14/15 from `a27e192` (`make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods <the eight methods>"`, $3$ h $23$ min, no error). Results under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main/`; tables (`make run-tables-tempering ARGS=--tables`, commit `fd881bc`) under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/tempering/`, plots (`make run-plot-tempering`, commit `167529f`) under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/tempering/`.
- **Findings** (the Analysis section of E5.2 in [[ext5_baycap_priors_tempering]]): tempering is retained for neither method, so $\tau=1$ is kept. $\tau_{-d}$ is $2$ for BayCAP-Hf and $8$ for BayCAP-KDEy on every held-out dataset and lowers the mean Winkler score for vanilla accuracy ($0.237\to0.183$ and $0.508\to0.207$), but only on $7$ of the $15$ datasets for both (one-sided Wilcoxon $p=0.36$ and $0.21$; macro-F1 and macro-K agree): three datasets with displaced intervals give all of the gain, and the median per-dataset change is slightly negative. Tempering widens without moving the centre; the widening of BayCAP-KDEy follows $\sqrt{(\tau+r^2)/(1+r^2)}$ (correlation $0.97$ on the log scale) but is larger than predicted on binary datasets; the oracle-best $\tau$ of BayCAP-KDEy is the largest of the grid on $21$ pairs. With BayCAP-Hf a larger $\tau$ lowers the share of divergent draws but raises split-$\hat R$ at $K\ge8$ (median of the largest per pair about $1.8$ at $\tau\ge4$).
- **Card:** WP-20 (§III.3).

## II.26 E4.2b and E4.2a: the error split across $\lvert V\rvert$ and across $\lvert U\rvert$ (study `target_size`)

`experiments/target_size.py` is the study `target_size` (E4.2b and E4.2a of [[ext4_uncertainty_budget]]; `--u-grid` runs E4.2a, the bag sizes `U_SIZES` $=\{100,300,1000,3000,10000\}$ with the full $V$ only, and cannot be combined with `--sizes` or `--levels`; bags larger than the pool repeat instances): for `BayesCAP-Hf-real` and `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` with the settings of `main`, on `mammographic`, `german`, `spambase`, `wine-quality`, `dry-bean`, `letter` with LR and RF, it refits each method on nested, seeded, stratified subsamples of $V$ (levels full, $1/4$, $1/16$, `subsample_index`; a subsampled level runs only where every class keeps at least $10$ instances, `level_available`; $S_V=3$ seeds) and scores it on $R=30$ bags per prevalence (uniform, skewed, near-vertex, `target_prevalences`) of $\lvert U\rvert=1000$ drawn with replacement from the test pool with fixed class counts (`class_counts`, `make_bags`); the bags depend on the dataset, prevalence, size and bag index only, so every $V$ cell of a prevalence sees the same bags. `--counts` writes the class counts of the subsamples without running a method. Results: `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/target_size/u<|U|>/<level>/s<seed>/<domain>/<acc_name>/<dataset>/<classifier>/<method>.parquet`, each row with $\lvert U\rvert$, level, $V$ seed, $\lvert V\rvert$ and its class counts, prevalence, bag index and seed, `config_hash`, `schema_version` and the NUTS diagnostics. `tables/target_size.py` splits every bag (`error_split.split`) and every part within its cell into a fixed and a bag part (§II.20) and writes per-bag, per-cell, per-seed, per-level and slope CSVs, the table of E4.2b, `recalls` (the gap between the recalls of $h$ on $V$ and on the pool, its two-sample standard deviation, the subsample standard deviation of §T.4), `drift` (the drift of the seed means of $e_2$ from the full-$V$ value) and `diagnostics` (NUTS, per size and level), with checks of the identity, the dropped and empty-column bags, $R$, `config_hash` and the shared bags; for E4.2a it also writes `size_slopes` (log–log slopes against $\lvert U\rvert$ of the RMS of the fixed and of the bag parts, full $V$), `cost` (mean fitting time and NUTS time per bag) and the table of E4.2a (`target_size_sizes_v1`, when the full $V$ has two or more sizes). `plots/target_size.py` draws the RMS of the fixed parts against $\lvert V\rvert$ (`--figures v-size`, `--classifier`) and, for E4.2a, the RMS of the fixed (solid) and bag (dashed) parts against $\lvert U\rvert$ with the full $V$ (`--figures u-size`). Since 2026-11-16 (user's request, after WP-22) it also draws the total error $\hat A-A(h,U)$ aggregated over the pairs of both classifiers, against the fraction of $V$ at `--size` (`--figures v-total`, E4.2b) and against $\lvert U\rvert$ with the full $V$ (`--figures u-total`, E4.2a), one column for the binary and one for the multiclass pairs, both methods on the same axes, one colour each: top row, the mean over the pairs of the per-pair AE of the total with the band of the 25th–75th percentile over the pairs; bottom row, the RMS of the total and of its fixed and bag parts (the root of the mean over the pairs of the per-pair mean squares, so that $\text{MSE}_\text{total}=\text{MSE}_\text{fixed}+\text{MSE}_\text{bag}$ holds on the lines); a level present only on some pairs of a group ($1/16$) has an open marker, a dotted segment, an interquartile bar and its number of pairs (`pair_totals`, `group_totals`); output `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/target_size/v_total/` and `u_total/`. Tests: `tests/test_target_size.py`. Every score is against the realised table of each bag (`true_cts`); the split uses the true prevalence of the bag and the labels of the pool (an analysis).

- **Run of E4.2b:** on `bcuda` (`JAX=cpu`), `cap-ml` `93ae246`, on 2026-11-15 from `598d404` (`make run-target-size ARGS="--run"`, $132$ tasks, $49$ minutes, exit $0$, no NUTS failure); the level $1/16$ runs on `spambase`, `dry-bean` and `letter`. Results under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/target_size/` (also pulled to the local machine); tables (`--all`, from `14dcdee`) under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/target_size/`, plots (LR, RF) under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/target_size/v_size/`.
- **Run of E4.2a:** on `bcuda` (`JAX=cpu`), `cap-ml` `93ae246`, on 2026-11-15 from `7e55349` (`make run-target-size ARGS="--run --u-grid"`, $96$ tasks for $\lvert U\rvert\in\{100,300,3000,10000\}$, $40$ minutes, exit $0$; the cells at $\lvert U\rvert=1000$ are those of E4.2b). Results under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/target_size/u<|U|>/v1/s0/` on `bcuda` (only `u1000` is on the local machine); tables (`--tables`, from `c0a81a3`) under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/target_size/`, plots (`--figures u-size v-size`, LR, RF) under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/target_size/u_size/` and `v_size/`, pulled to the local machine; the figures of the total (`make run-plot-target-size ARGS="--figures v-total u-total"`, `target_size_v-total_u1000` and `target_size_u-total`) on `bcuda` on 2026-11-16 from `5d8db54`, exit $0$, pulled with `make pull PULL_DIRS=plots`. Cost per bag from $\lvert U\rvert=100$ to $10000$ (`target_size_cost.csv`): at most $7.9$ times for BayCAP-KDEy, $1.45$ times for BayCAP-Hf.
- **Findings of E4.2b** (its Analysis in [[ext4_uncertainty_budget]]): the offset of $e_2$ on the binary datasets found by E4.1 is the gap between the recalls of $h$ on $V$ and on the test pool, within $1.7$ standard deviations of two independent samples of their sizes, so it comes from the finite $V$ and the finite pool; the fixed part of $e_2$ grows as $V$ shrinks for both methods (median log–log slope $-0.37$ and $-0.44$) and, at $1/16$, shifts systematically rather than spreading over the seeds; the realised-table predictive makes the recalls of BayCAP-KDEy depend on the bag and on its estimated prevalence (§T.3, corrected); where $e_1$ is large (`wine-quality`, `german`) it does not come from the finite $V$. BayCAP-Hf diverges on about $44\%$ of the bags, as in `main`.
- **Findings of E4.2a** (its Analysis in [[ext4_uncertainty_budget]]): with the full $V$, every bag part shrinks as $1/\sqrt{\lvert U\rvert}$ (median log–log slopes about $-0.5$), the bag part of $e_2$ of BayCAP-KDEy included ($-0.49$), as the reweighting $w_i=q_i/p_i$ of the realised-table predictive predicts; the fixed parts barely move (median slopes $-0.02$ to $-0.10$), so the bag part of the total exceeds its fixed part in $4$–$6$ of the $12$ pairs at $\lvert U\rvert=100$ and in none at $10000$, and the offset of $e_2$ on `mammographic` is the same at every size; exceptions: `letter` (the fixed part of $e_2$ falls with $\lvert U\rvert$, a small-bag effect with $K=26$), `wine-quality` with RF (BayCAP-KDEy: $e_1$ and $e_2$ move in opposite directions while the total falls), `wine-quality` with LR (the error of BayCAP-Hf grows with the bag), `german` (the fixed part of $e_2$ of BayCAP-KDEy falls, mostly at the near-vertex prevalence). BayCAP-Hf diverges on $41$–$45\%$ of the bags at every size.
- **E4.4 (card WP-28, implemented, not evaluated; full runs of E4.4a and E4.4b done on 2026-11-21/22, plots made, not analysed):** `--e44a` and `--e44b` run the methods `E44_METHODS` (the two above, `SQS-H`, `SQS-Hbs`, `PQ`, `SQS-B`) on the $15$ datasets of `main` (`main_datasets`, i.e. `experiments/datasets.py:get_filtered_datasets`) with LR only: E4.4a at the sizes `U_SIZES` with the full $V$, E4.4b at $\lvert U\rvert=1000$ with the levels `E44_LEVELS` (full, $1/\sqrt2$, $1/2$, $1/(2\sqrt2)$, $1/4$; folders `v1`, `v1_sqrt2`, `v1_2`, `v1_2sqrt2`, `v1_4`) and no threshold on the classes (`--no-class-threshold`; every class keeps at least one instance); `--datasets`, `--classifiers` and `--methods` restrict a preset. `make_method(name, size)` builds the SQS methods with `sample_size` $=\lvert U\rvert$ (`experiments/methods.py:sqs_h`, `sqs_s`, `sqs_b`, whose default `SAMPLE_SIZE` is that of `main`); the NUTS diagnostics are stored only for the methods that sample with NUTS (`BayCAP`, `QuantifierBayCAP`, `SQSB`); `SQS-H`, `SQS-Hbs` and `PQ` run in a pool with `N_JOBS` of `cap.env` and `THREAD_LIMITS`. `config_hash` keeps the dictionary `LEVELS` of E4.2, so the stored cells keep their hash and are reused (the LR cells of the two Bayesian methods on the six datasets of E4.2, at every $\lvert U\rvert$ with the full $V$ and at $1/4$). `--counts --e44b` writes `v_counts_e44b.csv`, with the flag `below_threshold`. `tables/target_size.py` loads only the grid of E4.2 (six datasets, levels of `LEVELS`). `tables/target_size_scores.py` computes per bag AE, coverage, amplitude and $W$ at $1-\alpha=0.95$ (`tables/main.py:get_acc_*`) and the parts $e_1$, $e_2$, $e_3$; per seed, per pair (mean and standard deviation over the seeds), the summary over the pairs (mean and its standard error, median, 25th and 75th percentiles; also without `poker_hand`), the log–log slopes of mean AE and amplitude, the NUTS diagnostics and the cost; the flag of the cells with a class below $10$ instances is computed from `v_class_counts`; LaTeX tables `target_size_scores_e44a` and `_e44b`. `plots/target_size_scores.py` draws `u-scores` (E4.4a) and `v-scores` (E4.4b): AE (linear) left and $W$ (logarithmic) right against the size (logarithmic), one line per method, mean over the pairs with bars of $\pm1$ standard error, the methods shifted sideways by `DODGE` ($0.05$) times the spacing between consecutive sizes; flagged levels have a grey tick label with $^*$; `--without poker_hand`. Tests: `tests/test_target_size_e44.py`.
- **Cards:** WP-21, WP-22 (§III.3); WP-28 (§III.2).

## II.27 E5.3: the temperature of BayCAP-Hf and BayCAP-KDEy chosen per pair on calibration bags (study `tau_select`)

`experiments/tau_select.py` is the study `tau_select` (E5.3 of [[ext5_baycap_priors_tempering]]): for `BayesCAP-Hf-real` and `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real`, built as in `main` and E5.2, on the $15$ datasets of `main` with their five classifiers, it splits $V$ per class into $V_{\text{cal}}$ ($\lfloor n_j/2\rfloor$ instances of class $j$) and $V_{\text{fit}}$ (the others), seeded by the experiment seed and the dataset (`split_seed`, `split_index`; the same split for every classifier, method and $\tau$), refits both methods on $V_{\text{fit}}$ ($h$ is not retrained) at $\tau\in\{0.5,1,2,4,8\}$ (`TAUS`, the tempered methods of §II.25) and scores them on $S=50$ calibration bags of $1000$ instances drawn from $V_{\text{cal}}$ with QuaPy's UPP, the protocol of the test bags of `main` (`calibration_bags`, seeded by `calibration_seed` of the experiment seed and the dataset, which restores QuaPy's seed afterwards), with `jax.clear_caches()` after each task. Results: `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tau_select/<domain>/<acc_name>/<dataset>/<classifier>/<method>.parquet`, one row per calibration bag with the schema of `main`, the split and bag seeds, the sizes and class counts of $V$, $V_{\text{fit}}$ and $V_{\text{cal}}$, the recalls of $h$ on the full $V$, the prevalence of the bag, `config_hash` and `schema_version`. `tables/tau_select.py` chooses $\tau_{\text{cal}}$ per pair, method and metric (lowest mean calibration Winkler score) and reads the scores of the deployed method, fitted on the full $V$, on the $100$ test bags from the stored results of `main` and E5.2 (the loaders of `tables/tempering.py`, which take the result folder as a parameter); it writes CSVs (`tau_select_{pairs,calibration,summary,tests,datasets,crosstab,nuts}.csv`: AE, coverage, amplitude and Winkler score at $\tau=1$, $\tau_{\text{cal}}$, the leave-one-dataset-out $\tau_{-d}$ of E5.2 and the oracle-best $\tau$ as a reference, also by group of $K$; the one-sided paired Wilcoxon test of the decision rule; the cross-tabulation of $\tau_{\text{cal}}$ against the oracle; $\hat\sigma_{e_2}$ (`sigma_e2`) and the Spearman correlations; the NUTS diagnostics of the calibration runs of BayCAP-Hf) and, per metric, LaTeX tables of the summary, of the cross-tabulation and of the pairs (one row per pair: $K$, $\hat\sigma_{e_2}$, the three selected $\tau$, the test Winkler score and coverage at each, coloured per row from the worst to the best value as in §II.25 with the oracle left out of the scale, $\tau_{\text{cal}}$ and $\tau_{-d}$ highlighted where they equal the oracle, and the calibration Winkler score at $\tau=1$ and $\tau_{\text{cal}}$), and a compact per-pair table for the paper (`compact`, decided on 2026-11-21: one row per classifier and dataset, BayCAP-Hf and BayCAP-KDEy side by side, each with $\tau_{\text{cal}}$, the oracle-best $\tau^\star$ in grey as a reference, and the test AE and Winkler score at $\tau=1$ and $\tau_{\text{cal}}$; the lower of the two, at the printed precision, in bold; $\tau_{\text{cal}}$ highlighted where it equals $\tau^\star$; `_compact_styler`). `plots/tau_select.py` draws $\tau_{\text{cal}}$ against $\hat\sigma_{e_2}$ (`sigma`) and the gain on the calibration bags against the gain on the test bags (`gain`), one point per pair coloured by $K$.
