Tests: `tests/test_tau_select.py`. Every score is against the realised table of each bag (`true_cts`): of the calibration bags from the labels of $V_{\text{cal}}$, of the test bags as in `main`.

- **Run:** on `bcuda` (`JAX=cpu`), `cap-ml` `93ae246`, on 2026-11-16 from `d7bce3b` (`make run-tau-select`, $750$ tasks, from 01:09:45 to 03:46:55, exit $0$, no task error; no separate pilot). Results under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tau_select/classic/` ($2250$ files, $756$ MB, also pulled to the local machine); tables (`--tables`, from `a674a2b`; the per-pair tables from `6069ab2`) under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/tau_select/`, PDFs built locally; plots (the three metrics, from `a674a2b`) under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/tau_select/`.
- **Findings** (the Analysis of E5.3 in [[ext5_baycap_priors_tempering]]): the selection per pair is retained for neither method, so $\tau=1$ is kept. For vanilla accuracy $\tau_{\text{cal}}$ lowers the per-dataset mean Winkler score on $5$ of the $15$ datasets for BayCAP-Hf and on $7$ for BayCAP-KDEy (one-sided Wilcoxon $p=0.66$ and $0.23$; macro-F1 and macro-K agree); the mean goes from $0.237$ to $0.258$ and from $0.508$ to $0.242$ ($\tau_{-d}$: $0.183$, $0.207$; oracle: $0.154$, $0.189$). At $\tau=1$ BayCAP-KDEy undercovers the calibration bags (mean coverage $0.57$), so $\tau_{\text{cal}}=8$ on $50$ pairs, nearly the single $\tau$ of E5.2, and three datasets give almost all of the gain. BayCAP-Hf covers the calibration bags of `german` at $0.98$–$1.00$, so $\tau_{\text{cal}}=0.5$ there, while it is displaced on the test bags, and its score doubles. The gain on the calibration bags does not predict that on the test bags (Spearman $-0.03$ and $0.08$), as §T.9 of the extension predicts, and $\tau_{\text{cal}}$ does not follow $\hat\sigma_{e_2}$ (Spearman $-0.14$ and $0.08$). $\tau_{\text{cal}}$ is above the oracle-best $\tau$ on $0.59$ of the pairs for BayCAP-KDEy, as expected, but on only a third for BayCAP-Hf. The sampler of BayCAP-Hf on $V_{\text{fit}}$ mixes as on the full $V$ in E5.2.
- **Card:** WP-23 (§III.3).

## II.28 The coverage and amplitude table of `main`

`tables/coverage_amplitude.py` (user's decision of 2026-11-18, no card; tests: `tests/test_coverage_amplitude.py`) has the structure of the main table (§I.4): one row per classifier and dataset, one column group per score and, within it, the baselines `DoC`, `QuAcc_NxN`, `O-LEAP` (`tables/main.py:BASELINE_METHODS`) and the other methods of `MAIN_METHODS` except `BayesCAP` (`MAIN_METHODS_BEFORE_BAYESCAP`, user's decision of 2026-11-23). Each cell is the mean over the bags of the pair of the coverage $\mathcal C$ (the share of bags whose interval contains the accuracy of the realised table `true_cts`) and of the amplitude $\mathcal A$ (the width of the interval); both target the realised table of the bag, and the per-bag scores come from `tables/main.py:load_table_frame`, shared with the main table. Colours use the scale of the rank table (`tables/rank.py:_rank_color`, from `BEST_RGB` to white). For coverage, the colour fades linearly with the distance $d=\lvert\bar c-0.95\rvert$, the same for over- and under-coverage, and is white from $d\ge0.20$ (`COVERAGE_D_MAX`); the cell with the smallest $d$ is in bold. For amplitude, lower is better: the colour follows the rank within the row (ties get the average rank), white from rank $6$; the narrowest cell is in bold. There is no significance test, no dagger and no standard deviation. Outputs: one tabular per accuracy measure and the document `main_<domain>_coverage_amplitude.tex` under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/coverage_amplitude/` (`make run-tables-coverage-amplitude ARGS=--tables`, then `--pdf`). Not run yet.

## II.29 E7.1: per-class leave-one-out bandwidths of BayesKDEy, in `main` (pilot only)

[[ext7_kdey_bandwidth]], E7.1. The methods `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-loo` and `SQS-B-loo` (`experiments/methods.py:LOO_VARIANTS`) are `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` and `SQS-B` with BayesKDEy at `bandwidth="loo"` (§II.4: one bandwidth per class selected over `LOO_GRID` by leave-one-out likelihood on the cross-fitted validation outputs of the MLP, densities normalised on the simplex), everything else, seeds included, as the stored methods; they run in the BayCAP pool and are in no table, rank or default plot of `main`. `tables/kdey_loo.py` reads the stored results of the four methods on the pairs stored for all of them and reports, per metric and pair, AE, coverage, amplitude and Winkler score of the four, for each LOO method a paired bootstrap test of the mean per-bag Winkler difference against its stored method (Holm over the pairs), the summary of the selected bandwidths and the share of divergent bags; per class, $n_j$, $\hat b_j$, the edge flag and the leave-one-out log-likelihood per instance at $\hat b_j$ and at $0.1$; per metric, a summary with the pairs significantly worse and better and a two-sided Wilcoxon test over the per-pair means. `plots/kdey_loo.py` draws $\hat b_j$ against $n_j$ per dataset and the Winkler difference against the median $\log_{10}(\hat b_j/0.1)$. Tests: `tests/test_bayes_kdey_loo.py`, `tests/test_kdey_loo_tables.py`, `tests/test_methods.py`.

- **Run:** on `bcuda` at the user's request, `main --classic` for the two methods, JAX on CPU, `cap-ml` `93ae246`, from branch `wp-25/kdey-loo-bandwidth` (`e4ad02c`), on 2026-11-19; the pilot only, on `mammographic` and `wine-quality` with the five classifiers ($10$ pairs), three metrics, no failed cell; the full run was not done (user's decision of 2026-11-19). Tables and plots generated on `bcuda` at `72e430a` and copied locally with `make pull`, PDFs generated locally. Results under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main/` (the two methods, $10$ pairs), `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/kdey_loo/`, `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/kdey_loo/`. In the bandwidth plot the tick labels of `mammographic` overlap and the four columns leave empty panels with two datasets (cosmetic, not fixed).
- **Findings:** the selection picks bandwidths well below $0.1$ (median $0.035$ on `mammographic`, $0.025$ on `wine-quality`, none at an edge of the grid), which fit the class densities better, and both LOO methods are worse than the stored ones on every metric: on vanilla accuracy the mean Winkler score goes from $1.92$ to $3.86$ for `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` (worse on the $10$ pairs) and from $2.31$ to $3.95$ for `SQS-B`; on `wine-quality` the AE roughly doubles, so the posterior moves away from the target prevalence as well as narrowing; on `mammographic`, where the error comes from the rates of $h$ on $V$, little changes. The `"loo"` option is not carried forward; analysis in [[ext7_kdey_bandwidth]], E7.1.
- **Card:** WP-25 (§III.3).

## II.30 E7.2: the bandwidth and then the temperature of BayesKDEy selected per pair on calibration bags, in `main`

[[ext7_kdey_bandwidth]], E7.2a and E7.2b. The methods `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-cal` (`bandwidth="cal"`) and `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-cal-tcal` (`bandwidth="cal"`, `temperature="cal"`) of `experiments/methods.py:CAL_VARIANTS` are `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` with the selections of §II.4 (split of $V$ in halves, $50$ calibration bags from $V_{\text{cal}}$, $b_{\text{cal}}$ by the accuracy criterion with QuaPy's `KDEyML` over `LOO_GRID`, then $\tau_{\text{cal}}\in\{0.5,1,2,4,8\}$ by the mean Winkler score of the vanilla-accuracy intervals on the same bags, the deployed fit on the full $V$), everything else, seeds included, as the stored method; they run in the BayCAP pool and are in no table, rank or default plot of `main`. Tables `tables/kdey_cal.py` (E7.2a against the stored method) and `tables/kdey_tcal.py` (the $2\times2$ ablation with the stored method, E7.2a and the stored `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-t<τ>` at the $\tau_{\text{cal}}$ of E5.3, §II.27), plots `plots/kdey_cal.py` and `plots/kdey_tcal.py`; the gate of the solver `experiments/kdey_cal_solver.py`, `tables/kdey_cal_solver.py` (§II.4).

- **Run:** pilot of E7.2a with EM and the NUTS check on 2026-11-19 (`520580c`, $15$ pairs of `mammographic`, `wine-quality`, `poker_hand`; cells archived in `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main_e72a_pilot_em/`); gate of the solver on 2026-11-20 (`189246a`, failed, SLSQP chosen by the user); joint full run of both methods on `bcuda` at the user's request on 2026-11-20 (`make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-cal BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-cal-tcal"`, commit `e68b5a5`, `cap-ml` `93ae246`, JAX on CPU, 14:05–15:58, exit $0$), all $75$ pairs and three metrics. Tables and plots generated on `bcuda` at `e68b5a5`, copied with `make pull`, PDFs built locally. Results under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main/` (the two methods), `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/kdey_cal/`, `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/kdey_tcal/`, `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/kdey_cal/`, `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/kdey_tcal/`; the check table in `tables/kdey_cal/` is the EM pilot's.
- **Findings:** E7.2a: the calibrated bandwidth (median $0.079$, from $0.013$ to $0.398$, none at an edge) makes `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` worse over the $75$ pairs: on vanilla accuracy mean AE $0.028\to0.031$ and Winkler score $0.508\to0.558$, AE significantly higher on $27$ pairs and lower on $18$ (Holm), mostly on `german` and `connect-4` (AE about doubled); the gains of the pilot datasets hold but are small; the calibration gain is negatively correlated with the test gain (Spearman $-0.33$). E7.2b: the temperature chosen after $b_{\text{cal}}$ ($8$, the edge of the grid, on $41$ pairs) gives the lowest mean Winkler score of the four cells on vanilla accuracy ($0.224$, against $0.508$ stored, $0.242$ for E5.3 at $b=0.1$, $0.558$ for E7.2a), not significantly below E5.3 ($19$ pairs lower, $11$ higher, Wilcoxon $p=0.20$) and above it on macro-F1 and macro-K; as in E5.3 the gain over the stored method comes from `poker_hand`, `wine-quality` and `mammographic`, and on the other $60$ pairs no cell improves on the stored method. No decision rule; neither method enters the main tables of `main`; analysis in [[ext7_kdey_bandwidth]], E7.2.
- **Card:** WP-26 (§III.3).

## II.31 E8.1: Average Confidence with LasCal and the central Poisson-binomial interval, in `main`

[[ext8_average_confidence]], E8.1. The method `AC` (`methods/ac.py:AC`, factory entry in `experiments/methods.py`, `AC_METHOD`) recalibrates the posteriors of $h$ on each bag with LasCal (`lascal_calibrate`: the `lascal` package called with the inputs of `cap-ml`'s `CBPE`, a grid of $100$ temperatures from $0.1$ to $20$; the selected $\theta$ recovered from the calibrated logits; a fallback to the uncalibrated posteriors, flagged, when the package raises or selects no positive temperature), takes the largest calibrated posterior of each instance as its confidence, and stores in `estim_acc_ranges` the probability mass function of the number $K$ of correct predictions (Poisson-binomial, FFT of `cap-ml`'s `PoiBin`). The point estimate is its mean and the interval the central $95\%$ interval of $K/n$ (`CentralPoissonInterval`); `tables/main.py:PMF_INTERVALS` reads the interval of `AC` and of `L-CBPE` (highest-density) from the stored mass function. Vanilla accuracy only (macro-F1 and macro-K NaN); per bag, `lascal_temperature` and `lascal_fallback` (`experiments/main.py`, direct-method path). Estimand: the realised table of each bag (`true_cts`). `AC` is outside the main tables, the rank tables (`tables/rank.py:RANK_ONLY_EXCLUDED`) and the coverage vs amplitude plot; `tables/average_confidence.py` compares it with `L-CBPE` (comparison and check tables). Not yet moved to `cap-ml` (§II.12).

- **Run:** full run on `bcuda` on 2026-11-20, no pilot (`make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods AC"`, commit `f995d1d`, `cap-ml` at the pin of `uv.lock` of that commit, JAX on CPU), $75$ pairs × $100$ bags in `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main/`, about $2$–$4$ s per bag, no error; tables on 2026-11-20 (`make run-tables-average-confidence ARGS=--tables`, commit `74c83c4`) in `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/average_confidence/`.
- **Findings:** on 23 of the 25 binary pairs `AC` reproduces the point estimates of the stored `L-CBPE` to $2.7\times10^{-15}$; on `german` with kNN and RF 4 bags of 200 differ by at most $8.9\times10^{-4}$. No LasCal fallback in the $7{,}500$ bags; the median $\theta$ is at an edge of the grid on $10$ pairs. On the binary pairs the central interval is slightly wider than the highest-density one and has a lower Winkler score on all $25$ pairs (mean $1.712$ against $1.722$). The coverage of `AC` is far below its level (mean $0.176$ over the $75$ pairs, $0$ on $18$), with a mean amplitude of $0.040$ against a mean AE of $0.108$: the interval represents only the luck of the bag given the calibrated confidences. Analysis in [[ext8_average_confidence]], E8.1.
- **Card:** WP-29 (§III.3).

# Part III: Work tracker

This part tracks all implementation and experimentation work of the project: what is scheduled, what is being worked on, and which experiments are implemented but not yet run. The board in §III.1 gives the state at a glance; each open card has an entry in §III.2 with its full description, and completed cards are kept in §III.3.

## III.1 Board

| ID | Title | Kind | Status | Depends on | Updated |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| WP-31 | E3.4: BayCAP-KDEy with the per-instance pushforward and $s=h$ (`BayCAP-KDEy-h-inst`), on the `main` protocol | experiment | Scheduled | — | 2026-11-24 |
| WP-30 | E8.2: rate matrix of $V$ rescaled by a point quantifier (KDEy, EMQ, PACC; oracle reference) | experiment | In progress | — | 2026-11-23 |
| WP-28 | E4.4: AE and Winkler score of six interval methods across $\lvert U\rvert$ and $\lvert V\rvert$ (extended `target_size`) | experiment | Implemented, not evaluated | — | 2026-11-21 |

Kinds: **method** (a CAP method or a component of one), **experiment** (an experiment module together with its tables and plots), **analysis** (tables, plots or post-hoc scores on stored results, with no new runs), **infra** (shared infrastructure), **fix** (a Part IV entry, with ID `FIX-<n>`, once the user schedules it).

Statuses, in board order:

1. **Scheduled:** selected by the user, work not started.
2. **In progress:** code is being written, or code and tests are done and the pilot run has not been done yet. Runs (pilot or full) happen only when the user explicitly asks for them, so a card can stay here after its code and tests are complete.
3. **Implemented, not evaluated:** experiments only. The code, tests and pilot run (requested by the user) are done, but the full experiment has not been run, so no stored results exist yet.
4. **Done:** the card leaves the board, and its entry moves from §III.2 to §III.3.
   - A **method** or **infra** card is done when its code and tests are merged into `devel`; what it implemented is described in Part II. A method that no experiment has evaluated yet is described in Part II as "not evaluated", like `BayCAPFixed` (§II.4) before E3.1 is run.
   - An **experiment** or **analysis** card is done when the full run has produced stored results under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/<experiment>/`; what it implemented and found is described in Part II (or in Part I if the paper reports it), with the result location, the `cap-ml` commit used and the date of the run.
   - A **fix** is done when its code and tests are merged into `devel`: its Part IV entry is removed and the change is noted in Part I or II.
   - Part II (or I) and §III.3 do not repeat each other, and link to each other. Part II describes the code as it is and, for experiments and analyses, the run and the findings. The §III.3 entry keeps the history of the card: its goal as planned, dependencies, deviations, log and `Outcome`, with a reference to the Part II section in place of the run details and the findings.

Rules:

- Only the user adds cards, removes cards, or schedules Part IV fixes. Status changes follow the work and are made in the same change as the code they describe; the `Updated` column holds the date (YYYY-MM-DD) of the last status change.
- Rows are ordered by status, then by ID. Card IDs (`WP-<n>`) and fix IDs (`FIX-<n>`) are never reused.
- A card may cite an extension as its source, but its entry must stand alone: it restates the parts of the extension it needs and stays valid if the extension changes, is cut, or is moved to [[optional_extensions]].
- A card names the Part IV fixes and the other cards it depends on.

## III.2 Entries

Entry template:

```markdown
### WP-<n> <title>

- **Kind / status:** <method | experiment | analysis | infra> / <as in the board>
- **Source:** <extension link, or "new">
- **Goal:** <what the card adds and why>
- **Estimand:** <population table C diag(π) | realised table of the target sample>
- **Scope:** <methods / experiment / tables / plots touched or added>
- **Depends on:** <`FIX-<n>` fixes, `WP-<n>` cards>
- **Comparability:** <effect on existing results; which outputs must be recomputed>
- **Deviations from Part V:** <none | list>
- **Done when:** <observable completion criterion>
- **Commands:** <the `make` commands that run the experiment (pilot and full) and that produce its tables and plots, with their `ARGS`; filled when the code exists>
- **Log:** <dated notes on progress: pilot run, problems, decisions>
- **Outcome:** <filled when the branch is merged: merge commit; decisions, deviations, problems and follow-ups that came up during implementation and are not already in the card>
```

### WP-28 E4.4: AE and Winkler score of six interval methods across $\lvert U\rvert$ and $\lvert V\rvert$ (extended `target_size`)

- **Kind / status:** experiment / Implemented, not evaluated
- **Source:** [[ext4_uncertainty_budget]] (E4.4, §T.7)
- **Goal:** measure whether the intervals of the CAP methods follow the error as the size $\lvert U\rvert$ of the target bag and the size $\lvert V\rvert$ of the validation set change, one size at a time. Background: on large bags the error of BayCAP-Hf and BayCAP-KDEy is mostly an offset shared by the bags, coming from $V$ and the pool, and this offset grows as $V$ shrinks (E4.2, cards WP-21 and WP-22); intervals whose width comes from the bag alone shrink as $\lvert U\rvert^{-1/2}$ and would then miss the offset, where the Winkler score tends to $2/\alpha$ times the offset. Methods, built as in `main` (`experiments/methods.py`): `BayesCAP-Hf-real`, `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real`, `SQS-H`, `SQS-Hbs`, `PQ` (SQS-S), `SQS-B`; `O-LEAP` is left out for its cost (about $4\,490$ s per bag summed over the $15$ LR pairs in `main`). The SQS methods draw bags of `sample_size` $=\lvert U\rvert$ instances from $V$ in every cell, instead of the fixed $1000$ of `main` (`SQS-H`, `SQS-Hbs`, `SQS-B` use it when they predict; `PQ` draws its $10\,000$ candidate bags in `fit`, so it is refitted for each $\lvert U\rvert$). Datasets: the $15$ of `main` and their test pools; classifier LR only ($15$ pairs). Bags as in E4.2: three fixed prevalences per dataset (uniform; a seeded draw from $\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1)$; near-vertex, $0.9$ on the rarest validation class), $R=30$ bags per cell drawn with replacement from the pool, with class counts $\lvert U\rvert\pi_j$ rounded by largest remainder, seeded by the dataset, the prevalence, $\lvert U\rvert$ and the bag index only (shared by every method, $\lvert V\rvert$ level and $V$ seed). Part a (E4.4a): $\lvert U\rvert\in\{100,300,1000,3000,10000\}$ with the full $V$. Part b (E4.4b): $\lvert U\rvert=1000$, the full $V$ and nested seeded stratified subsamples of $V$ at the fractions $1/\sqrt2$, $1/2$, $1/(2\sqrt2)$, $1/4$, $S_V=3$ seeds per level, with the methods refitted on each subsample and $h$ not retrained; for each seed one permutation per class, of which the level of fraction $f$ keeps the first $\max(\operatorname{round}(fn_j),1)$, so the levels are nested with those of E4.2b; no threshold on the instances per class (user's decision of 2026-11-19), so every pair runs at every level, and the cells with a class below $10$ instances are flagged (on the pretrain files: `poker_hand` at $1/2$, $1/(2\sqrt2)$, $1/4$, with $9$, $6$, $5$; `shuttle` at $1/4$, with $9$). Measures (vanilla accuracy): per bag, AE $=\lvert\hat A-A(h,U)\rvert$, the Winkler score at the level of `main` ($1-\alpha=0.95$), coverage and amplitude; the parts $e_1$, $e_2$, $e_3$ of the error split (`error_split.py`) as a diagnostic; per pair, method and size (and level and seed), the means over the $90$ bags; over the pairs, the median and the 25th–75th percentiles; descriptive log–log slopes of the mean AE and of the mean amplitude against each size; NUTS failures and divergences counted, divergent runs kept. No decision rule.
- **Estimand:** the realised table of each bag (`true_cts`), for every method: AE and the Winkler score are scored against $A(h,U)$; the Bayesian methods use the realised-table predictive, the SQS methods bags of the size of the target bag.
- **Scope:**
  - `experiments/target_size.py`: the six methods (an option to choose them; the two of E4.2 by default, so its grids are unchanged); the SQS methods built with `sample_size` equal to the size of the task; the classifier LR and the $15$ datasets of `main` selectable; the levels $1/\sqrt2$, $1/2$, $1/(2\sqrt2)$ with their folders, next to `full`, `1/4`, `1/16`; an option that disables the threshold of $10$ instances per class (`level_available`), with a column flagging the cells below it; `--counts` on the new levels; a `config_hash` that no longer contains the whole `LEVELS` dictionary, so that the stored cells of E4.2 keep their hash and are reused (the LR cells of `BayesCAP-Hf-real` and `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` on its six datasets, at every $\lvert U\rvert$ with the full $V$ and at $1/4$); the methods without NUTS diagnostics handled;
  - a table module: per pair, method and size (and level), mean AE, Winkler score, coverage and amplitude, with the standard deviation over the $V$ seeds in E4.4b; CSVs and LaTeX documents;
  - a plot module: for each part, one figure with two panels, AE on the left and the Winkler score on the right; the size on the horizontal axis (logarithmic; $\lvert U\rvert$ for E4.4a, the fraction of $V$ for E4.4b); one line per method, the median over the $15$ pairs with the band of the 25th–75th percentile; the Winkler axis logarithmic; in E4.4b the levels with a flagged cell marked; a variant without `poker_hand` for the Analysis;
  - `Makefile` targets; tests: the new levels are nested with those of E4.2b and keep at least one instance per class; the threshold option; the SQS methods get `sample_size` equal to the size of the task; the hash of a stored cell of E4.2 is unchanged; the Winkler score equals the amplitude when the interval contains $A$ and adds $2/\alpha$ times the miss otherwise; the table and the plots on synthetic results.
- **Depends on:** none (the study `target_size` of WP-21 and WP-22, done; `SQS-B` of WP-18, done).
- **Comparability:** new cells in the study `target_size`; the stored cells of E4.2 and the results of `main` are unchanged. At $\lvert U\rvert=1000$ the SQS methods are those of `main`; at other sizes they differ only by `sample_size`. The bags have fixed prevalences, so the cells at $\lvert U\rvert=1000$ and the full $V$ are comparable with `main` in distribution, not bag by bag. Cost: $6\,750$ bags per method for E4.4a, $16\,200$ for E4.4b; at $\lvert U\rvert=1000$ a set of $90$ bags on the $15$ pairs takes, from the test times of `main`, about $1.8$ CPU hours for BayCAP-KDEy, $3.1$ for BayCAP-Hf, $5.8$ for `SQS-Hbs`, $0.5$ for `SQS-H`, $0.8$ for `SQS-B`, and $0.7$ of fits of `PQ`; the SQS methods and the fits of `PQ` are expected to grow about linearly with $\lvert U\rvert$, BayCAP-KDEy at most $7.9$ times from $100$ to $10000$ (E4.2a). E4.4b adds the refits on the $12$ subsampled validation sets of each pair, about $28$ CPU hours for BayCAP-KDEy, almost all on `isolet` ($8\,211$ s per fit in `main`, against at most $83$ s elsewhere; to be understood before the run). Memory: the fit of `PQ` keeps $10\,000\times\lvert U\rvert\times K$ floats, about $21$ GB at $\lvert U\rvert=10000$ and $K=26$, twice that while it is built.
- **Deviations from Part V:** the subsampled levels of E4.4b run without the threshold of FIX-8 ($10$ validation instances per class), by the user's decision, with the cells below it flagged.
- **Done when:** the code and tests are merged; the pilot and the full runs of E4.4a and E4.4b are done with stored results.
- **Commands:** class counts of the levels of E4.4b (no method run): `make run-target-size-e44b-counts` (writes `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/target_size/v_counts_e44b.csv`). Pilot (proposed, datasets to be confirmed by the user): `make run-target-size-e44a ARGS="--datasets mammographic letter"` and `make run-target-size-e44b ARGS="--datasets mammographic letter"`. Full runs: `make run-target-size-e44a`, then `make run-target-size-e44b`. Tables: `make run-tables-target-size-scores ARGS=--all` (`--datasets ...` restricts them, e.g. to the pilot). Plots: `make run-plot-target-size-scores` and `make run-plot-target-size-scores ARGS="--without poker_hand --figures v-scores"`. The cost of the pilot is read from `target_size_scores_cost.csv`.
- **Log:** 2026-11-19: card added by the user; E4.4 decided: the six methods (`O-LEAP` left out for its cost), the $15$ datasets of `main` with LR, one size at a time (E4.4a, then E4.4b), the levels of $V$ from $1/\sqrt2$ to $1/4$ without $1/16$ and without a threshold on the classes, `sample_size` $=\lvert U\rvert$ for the SQS methods, AE and Winkler score as the measures, plots with AE and Winkler side by side and the median with the interquartile band. Checks before the card (scratch scripts on `bcuda`, read-only): the class counts of the subsampled levels on the pretrain files, and a fit-only check of the six methods on the four flagged cells (three seeds each), with no error and no non-finite value; on `poker_hand` at $1/(2\sqrt2)$ and $1/4$ the half of $V$ that trains the quantifier of `PQ` keeps $3$ instances of the rarest class, fewer than the folds of PACC, which only warns. Proposed pilot: one small and one large-$K$ dataset at every size and level, to measure the cost of the SQS methods and of `PQ` at $\lvert U\rvert=10000$. 2026-11-19: card started (In progress), branch `wp-28/target-size-intervals`; code and tests written: `experiments/target_size.py` (`E44_METHODS`, `E44_LEVELS`, `ALL_LEVELS` and their folders `v1_sqrt2`, `v1_2`, `v1_2sqrt2`; `--e44a`, `--e44b` presets on the $15$ datasets of `main` with LR, which `--datasets`, `--classifiers`, `--methods` restrict; `--no-class-threshold`; `make_method(name, size)` with `sample_size` $=\lvert U\rvert$ for the SQS methods; NUTS diagnostics stored only for the methods that sample with NUTS; the SQS methods other than SQS-B in a second pool), `experiments/methods.py` (`sqs_h`, and a `sample_size` argument of `sqs_s` and `sqs_b`, defaults unchanged, so `main` is unchanged), `tables/target_size_scores.py`, `plots/target_size_scores.py`, `Makefile` targets, `tests/test_target_size_e44.py` ($22$ tests; $603$ tests OK). Decisions taken in the implementation: (1) `config_hash` keeps the dictionary `LEVELS` of E4.2 and its threshold as they were, rather than dropping them, and the levels of E4.4 are in a separate dictionary, so the hash of the stored cells of E4.2 (`25fc61de00cb`, $R=30$) is unchanged (tested); the hash does not depend on the level nor on the threshold, which do not change the content of a cell; (2) no new column in the result files: the flag of the cells below $10$ instances per class is computed by the table module from `v_class_counts`, already stored, so `SCHEMA_VERSION` stays $1$ (it enters the hash); `--counts` writes the flag too (`below_threshold`); (3) `tables/target_size.py` (E4.2) now loads only the grid of E4.2 (its six datasets and levels), so the new cells of the two Bayesian methods do not enter its tables; (4) as in `main`, `SQS-H`, `SQS-Hbs` and `PQ` run in a pool with `N_JOBS` of `cap.env` and `THREAD_LIMITS`, the NumPyro methods (including `SQS-B`) in the pool with `BAYES_N_JOBS` and `BAYES_ENV`; (5) coverage, amplitude and $W$ come from `tables/main.py:get_acc_*`, the functions of the tables of `main` (FIX-5 not addressed); (6) AE on a linear axis and $W$ on a logarithmic one, as in E4.4; part a of the tables is the set of cells with the full $V$, part b the cells at $\lvert U\rvert=1000$. Pilot not run. 2026-11-21: no pilot (user's decision); full run of E4.4a started on `bcuda` (`make run-target-size-e44a`, `JAX=cpu`, 01:03, commit `79b734e`, which contains the merge `acb5854`; the changes merged since then to `methods/baycap_kdey.py`, `bayes_kdey.py` and `bayesian_quantifier.py` (WP-26) leave the default paths of the six methods unchanged), log `output/runs/target-size-e44a_20261121-010301.out`; the $60$ stored cells of E4.2 (LR, the two Bayesian methods, six datasets, five sizes) were reused, $390$ tasks to run ($165$ NumPyro, $225$ others). E4.4b follows when E4.4a ends, since both include the cell $\lvert U\rvert=1000$ with the full $V$; card Implemented, not evaluated. 2026-11-21: E4.4a ended at 03:52 (exit $0$, $225/225$ and $165/165$ tasks, no error in the log); full run of E4.4b started on `bcuda` at 16:36 (`make run-target-size-e44b`, commit `8a9dfda`, same code as E4.4a), log `output/runs/target-size-e44b_20261121-163608.out`; $1044$ tasks to run ($504$ NumPyro, $540$ others): $15$ datasets $\times$ $6$ methods $\times$ $13$ cells (full $V$ and four levels with three seeds), less the $90$ cells with the full $V$ computed by E4.4a and the $36$ cells at $1/4$ stored by E4.2. 2026-11-22: E4.4b ended at 00:28 (exit $0$, $540/540$ and $504/504$ tasks, no error in the log). 2026-11-23: plots generated on `bcuda` (`make run-plot-target-size-scores`, then with `ARGS="--without poker_hand"`, commit `3066835`), copied locally with `make pull`; tables not yet generated, analysis not written. 2026-11-23: plots changed after trial plots of the full run (user's decision): the median with the 25th–75th percentile band reflected mostly the differences between datasets, so the plots now show the mean over the pairs with bars of $\pm1$ standard error (`summary` gains `<score>_mean` and `<score>_se`; the median and the percentiles stay in the tables), the methods shifted sideways ($5\%$ of the spacing between sizes), no open markers and no legend entry for the flagged levels (their grey tick labels with $^*$ stay); trials with the bootstrap interval of the median, the $t$ interval of the mean, ratios to a reference size, one panel per method and $\pm1$ standard deviation were set aside; plots regenerated on `bcuda`. 2026-11-24: `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real`, one of the six methods, is invalidated by E3.4 (card WP-31; opening notice of Part II); whether E4.4 uses `BayCAP-KDEy-h-inst` instead is for the user to decide.
- **Outcome:** merged into `devel` with `acb5854` (2026-11-20), after rebasing the branch on `devel` (603 tests OK). No deviation from the card beyond the implementation decisions (1)–(6) in the Log. The full run started on 2026-11-21 without a pilot (user's decision; see the Log); still open: the class counts of E4.4b (`make run-target-size-e44b-counts`) and the cost of the BayCAP-KDEy fit on `isolet` (8,211 s for one fit).

### WP-31 E3.4: BayCAP-KDEy with the per-instance pushforward and $s=h$ (`BayCAP-KDEy-h-inst`), on the `main` protocol

- **Kind / status:** experiment / Scheduled
- **Source:** [[ext3_posterior_propagation]] (E3.4, §T.15)
- **Goal:** replace the realised pushforward of BayCAP-KDEy and make $h$ its input classifier. BayCAP-KDEy samples the posterior of $\pi^Q$ with BayesKDEy: per class $\omega_j$, a Gaussian kernel density $\hat f_j$ ($b=0.1$, no shrinkage) of the outputs of the input classifier on $V$, and the likelihood $\prod_n\sum_j\pi_j\hat f_j(z_n)$ of the outputs $z_n$ of the bag, with $\pi\sim\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1)$ and NUTS. The current realised pushforward (`methods/baycap.py:realised_tables`) turns each draw $\pi^{(r)}$ into a table by drawing the true classes of the $n^t_i$ instances that $h$ predicts as $\omega_i$ from $\operatorname{Mult}(n^t_i,\rho^{(r)}_{i\cdot})$, $\rho^{(r)}_{ij}=\hat C_{ij}\pi^{(r)}_j/\sum_\ell\hat C_{i\ell}\pi^{(r)}_\ell$, with $\hat C$ the rates of $h$ on $V$: it uses only the predictions of $h$. The new per-instance pushforward, with input classifier $h$ ($z_n=h(x_n)$), draws the true class of each instance of the bag from $\operatorname{Categorical}(w^{(r)}_{n\cdot})$, $w^{(r)}_{nj}=\pi^{(r)}_j\hat f_j(z_n)/\sum_\ell\pi^{(r)}_\ell\hat f_\ell(z_n)$, untempered whatever $\tau$, and forms the table of (prediction of $h$, drawn class) counts divided by $N^Q$. With $s=h$ these are exact draws of the joint posterior of $\pi$ and of the classes of the bag under the model of BayesKDEy, since the prediction of $h$ is a function of $z_n$; with an MLP input they are not, so the MLP variant is not built. The new method `BayCAP-KDEy-h-inst` becomes the BayCAP-KDEy method of the project (user's decision of 2026-11-24). Pairs, $V$, bags and seeds of `main` ($75$ pairs, $100$ bags of $1000$). Measures: AE, coverage, amplitude, Winkler score at $\alpha=0.05$ for the three metrics of `main`; for vanilla accuracy, per bag and label-free, $\bar\sigma_w=\sqrt{\frac1R\sum_r\frac1{(N^Q)^2}\sum_np_n(1-p_n)}$ with $p_n=w^{(r)}_{n\hat y_n}$, $\bar\sigma_g$ of the cell pushforward, $\bar{\tilde\sigma}_g$ (the cell term with $\rho_{ii}$ replaced by the mean of $p_n$ over the instances predicted as $\omega_i$), the within-class spread of $p_n$, the standard deviations over the draws of the centres $m_w=\frac1{N^Q}\sum_np_n$ and $m=\sum_i\frac{n^t_i}{N^Q}\rho_{ii}$, the difference of their means, and the predicted against the observed ratio of the amplitudes of `BayCAP-KDEy-h-inst` and `BayCAP-KDEy-h-real`; paired bootstrap tests of the mean Winkler difference ($10{,}000$ seeded resamples of the per-bag differences, Holm over the $75$ pairs) against `BayCAP-KDEy-h-real` and against `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real`.
- **Estimand:** realised table of the target sample, scored against `true_cts`, for the new method and the comparison methods. The population counterpart is $\hat C\operatorname{diag}(\pi^{(r)})$ with the same draws, i.e. the stored `BayCAP-KDEy-h`, not re-run.
- **Scope:**
  - `methods/baycap_kdey.py:BayCAPKDEy` (and, if needed, `methods/baycap_quantifier.py`): a new value of `estimand` (e.g. `"realised-instance"`), only with `c_prior="fixed"` and `input_classifier="h"` (`ValueError` otherwise); the log-densities of the bag reused from the quantifier, $\log w$ by log-sum-exp, one categorical draw per instance and draw with a `Generator` seeded by `random_state`, in chunks of draws (memory $R\times N^Q\times K$), tables in the `cap-ml` layout; per-bag summaries `FIXED_SUMMARIES` plus the quantities of the Goal;
  - `experiments/methods.py`: the factory entry `BayCAP-KDEy-h-inst` (configuration and seed of `BayCAP-KDEy-h-real`), in the BayCAP pool;
  - `tables/main.py:MAIN_METHODS`, `plots/shift_profile.py:DEFAULT_METHODS`, `tables/common.py`: `BayCAP-KDEy-h-inst` in place of `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real`, label `\bcapkdey` moved to it;
  - a table module (per-pair comparison table with the scores of `BayCAP-KDEy-h-inst`, `BayCAP-KDEy-h-real`, `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real`, `BayesCAP-Hf-real`, the amplitude ratios, the centre difference and the two tests; CSV, LaTeX, landscape PDF) and a plot module (coverage scatter against `BayCAP-KDEy-h-real`; observed against predicted amplitude ratio), reusing `tables/pushforward.py`;
  - `Makefile` targets; §II.12 updated;
  - tests: with weights equal to $\rho_{i\cdot}$ within each predicted class, the mean and variance of `realised_tables`; predicted-class margins $n^t/N^Q$, tables summing to one; the same prevalence draws as `BayCAP-KDEy-h-real` and `BayCAP-KDEy-h`; $w$ equal to Bayes' rule on a small mixture and independent of `temperature`; the identity $\sigma^2_w=\tilde\sigma^2_g-$ within-class term; the `ValueError`s; determinism; the factory and the main tables.
- **Depends on:** none (FIX-8 and FIX-2 + FIX-3 are done).
- **Comparability:** adds one method to `main` and leaves the stored results of every other method untouched; the existing estimands keep their draws. Comparable with the stored CPU results of E3.1–E3.3 and `BayesCAP-Hf-real` (same bags, same platform). Replacing `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` in the main tables changes the tables, ranks and plots of `main` and the paper artefacts, which are regenerated together (`docs/paper.md`) only on the user's request. The results of the realised BayCAP-KDEy with the cell pushforward and of the experiments built on them are invalidated (opening notice of Part II); this card does not rerun them. Cost: $7500$ NUTS runs, as one method of E3.1, plus $10^6$ categorical draws per bag.
- **Deviations from Part V:** none.
- **Done when:** the code and tests are merged; the full run has stored results of `BayCAP-KDEy-h-inst` under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main/`.
- **Commands:** filled when the code exists (pilot on `mammographic`, `poker_hand`, `letter`; full run; tables and plots of E3.4).
- **Log:** 2026-11-24: card added by the user, Scheduled. Decisions (user, 2026-11-24): the per-instance pushforward with $s=h$ as a new method with a new name, promoted directly to the main section of Extension 3 as E3.4; untempered weights; the MLP and random-$C$ variants discarded for now; the population counterpart is the stored `BayCAP-KDEy-h`; `BayCAP-KDEy-h-inst` becomes the default BayCAP-KDEy method; the results of `BayCAP-KDEy-h-real`, `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` and of everything built on them invalidated; runs and the generation of tables and plots only on the user's request.
- **Outcome:** —

### WP-30 E8.2: rate matrix of $V$ rescaled by a point quantifier (KDEy, EMQ, PACC; oracle reference)

- **Kind / status:** experiment / In progress
- **Source:** [[ext8_average_confidence]] (E8.2, §T.7–T.8)
- **Goal:** measure, on the protocol of `main`, the plainest CAP point estimate built on quantification, as a baseline and starting point of the research. Under prior probability shift the rates $C_{ij}=P(\hat Y=\omega_i\mid Y=\omega_j)$ of $h$ do not change, so the target table is $T=C\operatorname{diag}(\pi^Q)$; the estimate is $\hat T=\hat C\operatorname{diag}(\hat\pi^Q)$, with $\hat C_{ij}=N^V_{ij}/n^V_j$ the rates of $h$ on $V$ and $\hat\pi^Q$ the estimate of a point quantifier; for vanilla accuracy $\hat A=\sum_j\hat\pi^Q_j\hat C_{jj}$. `cap-ml`'s `ContTableTransferCAP` computes it (the table of $V$ normalised to sum to $1$, its true-class rows multiplied by $\hat\pi^Q/\pi^V$; the code stores the transpose). Levels: `CTT-KDEy`, `CTT-EMQ`, `CTT-ACC`, `CTT-PACC`, with QuaPy 0.2.4's `KDEyML`, `EMQ`, `ACC`, `PACC` at their default arguments on `MLP(random_state=qp.environ["_R_SEED"])` trained on $V$ (the classifier and seed of `experiments/methods.py:kdey`, `pacc`); and the reference `CTT-oracle` (uses target labels, never a method): $\hat C\operatorname{diag}(\bar\pi)$ with $\bar\pi_j=n_j/n$ the prevalence of the bag. Pairs, $V$, bags and seeds of `main`: the $75$ pairs, $100$ bags of $1000$ per pair. Measures: per bag, the point estimate and AE of vanilla accuracy, macro-F1 and macro-K; the quantification error $\lVert\hat\pi^Q-\bar\pi\rVert_1$; for vanilla accuracy the two parts $e_\pi=\sum_j(\hat\pi^Q_j-\bar\pi_j)\hat C_{jj}$ and $e_C=\sum_j\bar\pi_j(\hat C_{jj}-\bar C_{jj})$, with $\bar C_{jj}=N_{jj}/n_j$ the recall of $h$ in the bag, such that $e_\pi+e_C=\hat A-A$ ($e_\pi=0$ for the oracle); per pair, means (and RMS of the parts); means over the binary, multiclass and all pairs. No uncertainty, no interval, no decision rule.
- **Estimand:** the three methods target the population table $C\operatorname{diag}(\pi^Q)$; `CTT-oracle` targets the expected realised table given the class counts of the bag, $\mathbb E[N_{ij}/n\mid n_1,\dots,n_K]=C_{ij}\bar\pi_j$. All are scored against the realised table of each bag (`true_cts`); quantification errors against the prevalence of the bag.
- **Scope:**
  - an experiment module of its own (e.g. `experiments/ctt.py`), since `main` stores intervals (`_evaluate_contingency_method` calls `predict_ct_range` and `ci_from_cts`): it reuses `experiments/main.py:_prepare_evaluation`, `test_prot`, `test_prot_true_cts` and the metrics of `experiments/metrics.py:gen_acc_measure`; per pair and level one result file under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/ctt/`, skipped if present, with per bag the estimates and AE, $\hat\pi^Q$, $\bar\pi$, $e_\pi$, $e_C$, and fit and prediction times;
  - a table module: an AE table (rows: pairs grouped by dataset, binary and multiclass blocks with their means, final mean over the $75$ pairs; column groups: the three metrics, with the three methods and the reference set apart; best method in bold) and a split table for vanilla accuracy (per quantifier, the mean quantification error and the RMS of $e_\pi$ and $e_C$; the RMS of the error of the oracle); CSVs and LaTeX documents;
  - a plot module: per quantifier, a panel with the mean AE of vanilla accuracy against the mean quantification error, one point per pair (binary and multiclass markers), joined to the mean AE of `CTT-oracle` on the same pair;
  - `Makefile` targets (pilot, full run, tables, plot); tests: the table equals $\hat C\operatorname{diag}(\hat\pi^Q)$ from the counts of $V$ (transpose handled) and equals the table of $V$ when $\hat\pi^Q=\pi^V$; the oracle equals $\hat C\operatorname{diag}(\bar\pi)$; $e_\pi+e_C=\hat A-A$ and $\lvert e_\pi\rvert\le\frac R2\lVert\hat\pi^Q-\bar\pi\rVert_1$ ($R$ the range of the recalls of $V$) on synthetic tables; determinism of two seeded fits; a class absent from $V$ ($\pi^V_j=0$) detected and reported.
- **Depends on:** none.
- **Comparability:** a new experiment; `main` and its stored results are unchanged. The bags, $V$ and the posteriors of $h$ are those of `main`, so AE is comparable bag by bag with the stored methods of `main`. The KDEy and PACC are those of `main` (same classifier and seed, so the same as in `O-LEAP`, `QuAcc_NxN` and the SQS methods); EMQ on an MLP is new. Cost: per pair three quantifier fits (KDEy and PACC with $5$ folds) and $100$ predictions each; no NUTS.
- **Deviations from Part V:** none. The reference `CTT-oracle` and the error split are plain functions of `experiments/ctt.py`, not classes of `uncawe.methods`: the oracle is a reference, not a method, and the three methods are `cap-ml`'s `ContTableTransferCAP` unchanged, so no code is added to §II.12.
- **Done when:** the code and tests are merged; the full run has stored results under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/ctt/`.
- **Commands:** pilot `make run-ctt ARGS=--pilot` (`mammographic`, `poker_hand`, `letter` with LR, $100$ bags each); full run `make run-ctt`; tables `make run-tables-ctt ARGS=--tables` (documents: `make run-tables-ctt WHERE=local ARGS=--pdf`); plot `make run-plot-ctt`. Results in `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/ctt/` (`ctt` added to the `RESULTS` of the `Makefile` for `pull-results`/`push-results`), tables in `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/ctt/`, plot in `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/ctt/ae_vs_qerr/`.
- **Log:** 2026-11-23: card added by the user. Decisions (user, 2026-11-23): three QuaPy quantifiers in their base form on a seeded MLP trained on $V$; only the oracle reference (no $\hat\pi^Q=\pi^V$ level); a module separate from `main` with its protocol; no comparison with the methods of `main` or of E3.1 for now. 2026-11-23: branch `wp-30/ctt`, card In progress. Code: `experiments/ctt.py` (levels, protocol of `main` via `_prepare_evaluation`, one task per pair and level, `N_JOBS` workers with `THREAD_LIMITS`; one file per pair, level and metric, as every experiment of Part V.1, with the schema of `main`, `estim_acc_ranges` None, and the per-bag columns `test_prev`, `bag_prev`, `q_l1`, `q_failed`, and for vanilla accuracy `e_pi`, `e_c`, `recall_range`; a class absent from $V$ raises a `ValueError` naming it, the task is logged as an error and writes no file; a bag where a quantifier raises or gives a non-finite table gets NaN estimates and `q_failed`; EMQ is a local factory `emq()`, with the classifier and seed of `kdey()`, `pacc()`), `tables/ctt.py` (AE, split and check tables), `plots/ctt.py` (figure `ae_vs_qerr`), colours of the four levels in `plots/common.py`, `Makefile` targets, `tests/test_ctt.py` ($13$ tests). Choices: $\hat\pi^Q$ is read back as the row sums of the estimated table (no second call of the quantifier); `vanilla_acc` divides the trace by the sum of the table, and the estimate of a quantifier sums to $1$ only up to about $10^{-11}$, so $e_\pi$ uses $\hat\pi^Q$ normalised to sum to $1$, and $e_\pi+e_C$ equals the stored $\hat A-A$ to rounding; the split table shows the RMS of $e_C$ once, since $e_C$ does not depend on the quantifier and equals the error of `CTT-oracle` (§T.8), and the check table verifies both. Pilot not run. 2026-11-23: merged (`6cad251`); full run on `bcuda` with no pilot, at the user's request (`make run-ctt`, commit `1e75345`, `cap-ml` at the pin of `uv.lock` of that commit): $75$ pairs × $4$ levels × $3$ metrics, $900$ files in `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/ctt/`, 15:11–15:16, exit $0$, no task error. 2026-11-23: tables on `bcuda` (`make run-tables-ctt ARGS=--tables`); the AE table overflowed the page, so its level labels were shortened (`KDEy`, `EMQ`, `PACC`, `oracle`; branch `wp-30/ctt`, merged `e391988`) and the tables generated again from `e391988`, in `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/ctt/`; documents compiled locally (`make run-tables-ctt WHERE=local ARGS=--pdf`). 2026-11-23 (user's decision, which replaces "no comparison with the methods of `main` for now"): one AE table per metric, with the point estimates of BayCAP-KDEy (`BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real`) and BayCAP-Hf (`BayesCAP-Hf-real`) stored by `main` on the same bags (checked by their true tables), and in each row the five methods coloured from the green of the rank tables (`tables/rank.py:BEST_RGB`, smallest AE) to white (largest), linearly in the AE, the smallest in bold; the oracle neither coloured nor compared; `ctt_ae_<metric>.csv` next to `ctt_ae.csv` (branch `wp-30/ctt`, merged `41994ab`); tables generated again on `bcuda` from `41994ab` (same command), documents compiled locally. 2026-11-23 (user's decision): level `CTT-ACC` added, QuaPy's `ACC` at its default arguments ($5$-fold cross-fitting, solver `minimize`, `norm="clip"`) on the same seeded MLP (`experiments/ctt.py:acc`); colour in `plots/common.py`; the AE tables now colour six methods, the split and check tables and the plot gain its columns and panel; the stored results of the other levels are unchanged. Merged `a409679`; `CTT-ACC` run on `bcuda` (`make run-ctt`, commit `a409679`; the other levels skipped as present): $75$ pairs × $3$ metrics, $225$ files, 16:03–16:04, exit $0$, no task error; tables generated again on `bcuda` from `a409679` and compiled locally; the check table stays clean with `CTT-ACC` (no failed bag, no bound violation, largest residual $4.5\times10^{-16}$). The check table is clean on the $75$ pairs: largest residual of the split $4.5\times10^{-16}$, $e_C$ of the quantifiers equal to that of the oracle, no bag beyond the bound of §T.8, no failed quantifier. Plot and analysis not run.
- **Outcome:** merged into `devel` on 2026-11-23 (`6cad251`, branch `wp-30/ctt`, no rebase), after the user's review. Decisions and deviations taken during implementation (all in the Log): one file per pair, level and metric (Part V.1) instead of one per pair and level; $\hat\pi^Q$ read back as the row sums of the estimated table; $e_\pi$ computed with $\hat\pi^Q$ normalised to sum to $1$ (`vanilla_acc` divides by the sum of the table); the RMS of $e_C$ shown once in the split table, plus a check table; oracle and split as functions of `experiments/ctt.py`, nothing in `uncawe.methods` (§II.12 unchanged). Problem found: some test module leaves `qp.environ["_R_SEED"]` changed (to $5$) for the later ones; `tests/test_ctt.py` fixes the seed itself (follow-up, not scheduled: find the module and restore the seed there).
