## III.3 Completed cards

### WP-29 E8.1: `AC`, Average Confidence with LasCal and the central Poisson-binomial interval, in `main`, against `L-CBPE`

- **Kind / status:** experiment / Done (2026-11-20); run and findings in §II.31
- **Source:** [[ext8_average_confidence]] (E8.1, §T.3, §T.5)
- **Goal:** add Average Confidence (AC; Kivimäki et al., JAIR 2025) to `main` as a baseline on every dataset, and compare it with the stored `L-CBPE` in a separate table. For each target bag of $n$ instances, the posteriors of $h$ are recalibrated with LasCal, using $V$ (posteriors and labels) and the unlabelled bag, through the `lascal` package as `cap-ml`'s wrapper (`cap/calibration/lascal.py`) calls it: logits $\log p$, a grid of $100$ temperatures $\theta$ from $0.1$ to $20$, the $\theta$ with the smallest label-shift calibration error estimated on the bag, calibrated posteriors $\operatorname{softmax}(\log p/\theta)$. The confidence of an instance is its largest calibrated posterior $s_i$, which is the calibrated posterior of the prediction of $h$ (a temperature keeps the argmax, and $\hat y=\arg\max_kp(\omega_k\mid x)$ in `main`). The number of correct predictions is modelled as Poisson-binomial with parameters $s_1,\dots,s_n$; the point estimate is its mean, $\frac1n\sum_is_i$, and the interval is the central $95\%$ interval of $K/n$, $[k_{\text{low}}/n,k_{\text{up}}/n]$ with $k_{\text{low}}=\min\{k:F(k)\ge\alpha/2\}$ and $k_{\text{up}}=\min\{k:F(k)\ge1-\alpha/2\}$, $F$ the cumulative distribution function of $K$ (the interval of the AC paper, user's decision of 2026-11-20; `L-CBPE` uses the highest-density interval instead). Vanilla accuracy only; macro-F1 and macro-K are NaN. Name `AC` in code, tables and plots. On the binary datasets `AC` has the same calibration, point estimate and distribution as `L-CBPE` (`cap-ml`'s `CBPE(calib_method="lascal")`), which takes $\max(\tilde s,1-\tilde s)$, so the two differ only in the interval. Both methods stay out of the main tables, the rank table and the coverage vs amplitude plot (user's decision of 2026-11-20).
- **Estimand:** the realised table of each target bag (`true_cts`): the Poisson-binomial distribution is conditional on the confidences of the bag and describes its realised accuracy; AE, coverage, amplitude and Winkler score are scored against the trace of the realised table.
- **Scope:**
  - a method class in `src/uncawe/methods/` (new entry in §II.12), with the interface of `cap-ml`'s `CBPE`: `fit` keeps the posteriors and labels of $V$; per bag, LasCal through the `lascal` package (as the wrapper does, but keeping the selected $\theta$ and a flag set when the package raises and the uncalibrated posteriors are used, which the wrapper does silently), the Poisson-binomial distribution by the `poibin` FFT as in `CBPE`, the mean as point estimate;
  - a central-interval class with `cap-ml`'s `ConfidenceInterval` interface, built from the probability mass function of $K/n$, so that `tables/main.py` computes coverage, amplitude and Winkler score as for the other methods;
  - `experiments/methods.py`: the factory entry `AC`, run on every dataset; per bag, the extra columns `lascal_temperature` and `lascal_fallback`; `AC` not in `tables/main.py:MAIN_METHODS` (so not in the main tables nor in `plots/amplitude_vs_coverage.py`) and excluded from `tables/rank.py`, as `L-CBPE`;
  - a table module: (1) comparison table, one row per pair (dataset × classifier), one column group per score (AE, coverage, amplitude, Winkler score at $\alpha=0.05$) with the columns `AC` and `L-CBPE` (empty on the multiclass pairs), and two summary rows, the means over the $25$ binary pairs (both methods) and over the $75$ pairs (`AC` only); (2) check table: on the binary pairs, the largest absolute difference of the point estimates of `AC` and `L-CBPE` and the mean differences of their lower and upper bounds, and for `AC` on every pair the number of LasCal fallbacks and the distribution of $\theta$; CSVs and LaTeX documents, following Part V;
  - `Makefile` targets; tests: on binary data `AC` gives the same confidences, point estimate and probability mass function as `CBPE(calib_method="lascal")`; the central interval equals the quantiles of a brute-force Poisson-binomial on a small $n$ and holds at least $1-\alpha$; on synthetic confidences calibrated by construction (seeded) its coverage is close to $0.95$; the temperature keeps the argmax; the fallback flag with a mocked failure of the package; LasCal is deterministic for the same inputs; macro-F1 and macro-K are NaN; the tables on synthetic results.
- **Depends on:** none.
- **Comparability:** adds a method to `main`; the stored results, including `L-CBPE`, are unchanged, and the bags are those of `main`. On the binary pairs the point estimates of `AC` should equal those of the stored `L-CBPE` bag by bag (checked by the check table). Cost: per bag one LasCal fit ($100$ temperatures, each with an estimate of the importance weights and of the calibration error) and one FFT of size $n+1$; no NUTS; $7\,500$ bags on the $75$ pairs.
- **Deviations from Part V:** `AC` takes no `random_state` (§V.2), since it has no random component: LasCal is a deterministic grid search and the Poisson-binomial distribution is exact (tested: two fits on the same inputs give the same output).
- **Done when:** the code and tests are merged; the run of `AC` in `main` on the $75$ pairs (pilot to be decided by the user) is done with stored results, and the tables are produced.
- **Commands:** pilot (proposed, datasets to be confirmed by the user; one binary dataset, where `AC` is checked against the stored `L-CBPE`, and one with many classes): `make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods AC --datasets mammographic letter"`. Full run: `make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods AC"`. Tables (comparison and check, on the pairs where `AC` has stored results, so also on the pilot): `make run-tables-average-confidence ARGS=--tables`, and `make run-tables-average-confidence WHERE=local ARGS=--pdf` to compile the document. The cost of the pilot is read from `t_test_ave` of the stored files.
- **Log:** 2026-11-20: card added by the user; E8.1 decided: AC with LasCal on every dataset, the central interval of the AC paper, vanilla accuracy only, named `AC`, compared with `L-CBPE` in a separate table, both outside the main tables, the rank table and the coverage vs amplitude plot. 2026-11-20: card started (In progress), branch `wp-29/average-confidence`; code and tests written: `methods/ac.py` (`AC`, `CentralPoissonInterval`, `lascal_calibrate`, `recover_temperature`, `poisson_binomial_pmf`; §II.12), `experiments/methods.py` (`AC` in `METHOD_CLASS` and `gen_methods`, `AC_METHOD`), `experiments/main.py` (the direct-method path stores the per-bag columns `lascal_temperature` and `lascal_fallback` of `AC`, vanilla accuracy only), `tables/main.py` (`PMF_INTERVALS`), `tables/rank.py` (`AC` in `RANK_ONLY_EXCLUDED`), `tables/average_confidence.py` (comparison and check tables), `plots/common.py` (colour of `AC`), `Makefile` target `tables-average-confidence`, `tests/test_ac.py` ($19$ tests). Decisions taken in the implementation: (1) the `lascal` package returns the calibrated logits $z/\theta$ and not $\theta$, so `lascal_calibrate` recovers $\theta$ as the ratio of the logits to the calibrated logits on the finite non-zero entries, and treats ratios that disagree as a fallback; (2) the package keeps its initial temperature $-1$ when every loss on the grid is NaN, and then returns the logits divided by $-1$, which reverses the order of the classes (on binary data the largest posterior is unchanged, on $K>2$ it is not); `AC` treats a temperature that is not finite and positive as a fallback (uncalibrated posteriors, flag set); (3) the fallback of `cap/calib.py:LasCal`, which `CBPE` uses, calls the module's own `softmax(preact, temp, biases)` with `axis=1`, so a failure of the package would raise in `L-CBPE` instead of falling back silently (the stored `L-CBPE` results have no error, so no stored bag fell back); only `cap/calibration/lascal.py` falls back silently; both issues are noted in §II.12 for the move to `cap-ml`; (4) `AC` calls the package with the inputs of `CBPE` (posteriors with their dtype, logits $\log p$ as tensors), and on binary data its confidences and probability mass function are equal bit for bit to those of `CBPE(calib_method="lascal")` (tested); the point estimate is the mean $\sum_k(k/n)P(K=k)$ of the stored probability mass function, as in `PoissonConfidenceInterval`, which equals $\frac1n\sum_is_i$ up to rounding (tested to $10^{-10}$); (5) `estim_acc_ranges` of `AC` stores the probability mass function of $K$ ($n+1$ values), as `L-CBPE`; `tables/main.py:get_acc_coverage`, `get_acc_amplitude` and `get_acc_winkler` read it with the interval of the method (`PMF_INTERVALS`: `PoissonConfidenceInterval` for `L-CBPE`, `CentralPoissonInterval` for `AC`) instead of the percentiles of `estim_acc_ranges`, which are wrong for a probability mass function; `L-CBPE` was in no table, so no table changes; a scalar NaN (no estimate) gives NaN scores; (6) the central interval computes $F$ from the probability mass function clipped at $0$ and normalised, since the FFT of `PoiBin` adds the machine epsilon to every value; (7) the tables pair the bags of `AC` and `L-CBPE` by their stored order and raise if their true accuracies differ. Pilot not run. 2026-11-20: full suite $657$ tests OK. A first run of the suite outside `make` (without `JAX_PLATFORMS`) had $3$ failures, the MLP determinism tests of `tests/test_baycap_kdey_hf.py` and `tests/test_wp25_restructure.py`: `tests/test_ac.py` sorts first in a discovery and did not set `JAX_PLATFORMS=cpu` before importing JAX, so on a machine with a GPU JAX started on it, and NUTS on the GPU is not reproducible bit for bit; `tests/test_ac.py` now sets it at its top, as the NUTS test modules do. `make run-test` sets `JAX_PLATFORMS=cpu` and was not affected, nor are the experiments (`JAX ?= cpu` in the `Makefile`, `BAYES_ENV`). Other test modules that import code of the project do not set it themselves (e.g. `tests/test_methods.py`, `tests/test_rank.py`); they sort after the NUTS modules, so a discovery is not affected today. The user asked for the full run of `AC` on `bcuda` without a pilot. 2026-11-20: full run done on `bcuda` (`make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods AC"`, commit `f995d1d`, JAX on CPU), from 22:31 to 22:53, exit $0$, the $75$ pairs stored under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main/` (three metrics each, macro-F1 and macro-K NaN), no error; $t_{\text{test}}$ about $2$–$4$ s per bag. 2026-11-20: tables produced on `bcuda` (`make run-tables-average-confidence ARGS=--tables`, commit `74c83c4`, `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/average_confidence/`); analysis written in [[ext8_average_confidence]], E8.1. The check matches `L-CBPE` to $2.7\times10^{-15}$ on 23 of the 25 binary pairs; on `german` with kNN and RF, 4 bags of 200 differ by at most $8.9\times10^{-4}$, at mid-grid temperatures (near-ties of the LasCal criterion, a hypothesis); no LasCal fallback in the 7,500 bags. Ready for review and merge.
- **Outcome:** merge commit `b17b9b0` (2026-11-20), with the full run and the tables already done on the branch (no pilot, user's decision). Deviations from the card: the scoring helpers of `tables/main.py` became method-aware (`PMF_INTERVALS`), which also corrects the scoring of `L-CBPE` (no table showed it); `AC` treats a temperature that is not finite and positive as a LasCal fallback; `tests/test_ac.py` sets `JAX_PLATFORMS=cpu` at its top. Problems found: the fallback of `cap/calib.py:LasCal` would raise instead of falling back, and the `lascal` package returns $z/(-1)$ when no temperature is selected (both in §II.12 for the move to `cap-ml`); test modules that import JAX-based code without setting `JAX_PLATFORMS` make a discovery outside `make` depend on the order of the modules on a machine with a GPU. Follow-ups (not cards): the 4 bags of `german` where `AC` and the stored `L-CBPE` differ (near-ties of the LasCal criterion, a hypothesis); the hypothesis of §T.4 of [[ext8_average_confidence]], which needs per-bag shifts; the candidates E8.c1–E8.c3.

### WP-27 E5.5: right-side and wrong-side priors under strong shifts, with SQS references (extended `binform`)

- **Kind / status:** experiment / Done (2026-11-20); run in §II.2, findings pending
- **Source:** [[ext5_baycap_priors_tempering]] (E5.5)
- **Goal:** measure whether a prior on $\pi^Q$ that knows only the side of the prior probability shift (mild or severe), and is less extreme than the truth, improves the realised intervals of the methods that accept a prior, while the methods that take no prior stay unchanged. Bags of $\lvert U\rvert=1000$ instances at prevalences drawn from a concentrated distribution $G_c=\operatorname{Dir}(10\cdot\mathbf 1)$ or a sparse one $G_e=\operatorname{Dir}(\tfrac1{10}\mathbf 1)$; for each dataset (shared by its five classifiers) and distribution, $100$ requested prevalences drawn with `np.random.default_rng([_R_SEED, crc32(dataset), stream])` with streams of their own, distinct from those of E5.1 (WP-19) and E5.4 (WP-24). Methods with a prior on $\pi^Q$, each run with the uniform prior $\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1)$, $\operatorname{Dir}(3\cdot\mathbf 1)$ and $\operatorname{Dir}(\tfrac13\mathbf 1)$: `BayesCAP-Hf-real` (BayCAP with the Hf prior on $C$, realised pushforward), `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` (BayesKDEy with an MLP input and a fixed $\hat C$, realised pushforward) and `SQS-B` (one validation bag per posterior draw of BayesKDEy). On each set of bags the prior on the same side as the distribution ($\operatorname{Dir}(3\cdot\mathbf 1)$ under $G_c$, $\operatorname{Dir}(\tfrac13\mathbf 1)$ under $G_e$) is the *right-side* prior and the other the *wrong-side* prior; no prior matches the distribution (user's decision of 2026-11-19). Methods without a prior, run once per distribution as references: `SQS-H`, `SQS-Hbs` and `PQ` (SQS-S), as in `main`. All $75$ pairs of `main`, $\tau=1$, $100$ bags per (pair, distribution), drawn from the test pool with seeded generators as in E5.4 and the same for every method and prior. Measures (vanilla accuracy, macro-F1, macro-K): AE, coverage, amplitude, Winkler score at $\alpha=0.05$; for the methods with a prior, per pair and distribution, $\Delta W_{\text{know}}=W_{\text{uniform}}-W_{\text{right}}$, $\Delta W_{\text{wrong}}=W_{\text{wrong}}-W_{\text{uniform}}$ and the ratio of the mean amplitude of each informative prior to the uniform one; paired Wilcoxon tests over the $75$ per-pair means and over the $15$ per-dataset means; the mean number of classes absent from the realised table of the bags; the share of bags with divergent NUTS transitions per method and prior. No decision rule.
- **Estimand:** the realised table of each bag (`true_cts`), for every method and prior.
- **Scope:**
  - `experiments/binform.py`: a new option next to `--e54` (same `--datasets`, `--classifiers`, `--distributions`, `--methods`; $\lvert U\rvert=1000$ only), reusing the E5.4 code with the parameters of the distributions of the bags ($10$, $\tfrac1{10}$) set separately from those of the priors ($3$, $\tfrac13$); `SQS-B` built as in `main` with `prior=` (`methods/sqs_b.py:SQSB`); `SQS-H`, `SQS-Hbs` and `PQ` built as in `main` and run once per distribution, not once per prior; the distribution and the prior stored with the results; `jax.clear_caches()` after each task; output in a folder of its own per distribution; the default configuration, `--e51` and `--e54` unchanged;
  - new table module: per metric and distribution, one row per method, with the columns uniform / right side / wrong side for the methods with a prior and one column for those without; the means over the pairs of AE, coverage, amplitude and Winkler score, the counts of pairs and datasets with a lower Winkler score than the uniform prior and the $p$-values of the paired tests, also split by $K$ ($K=2$, $3\le K\le7$, $K\ge8$), with the absent classes and the divergent bags; per-pair, per-bag and summary CSVs and LaTeX documents, as for E5.4;
  - new plot module: (a) violins, four figures, one per distribution and role (right side, wrong side), each with two panels side by side, AE and Winkler score of every bag ($75\times100$ per method) on a logarithmic vertical axis, the AE floored at $10^{-5}$; one violin per method and prior: for `BayesCAP-Hf-real`, `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` and `SQS-B` two separate violins (uniform prior and the informative prior of the figure), for `SQS-H`, `SQS-Hbs` and `PQ` one violin, the same in the two figures of a distribution; vanilla accuracy by default, `--metrics` for the others; (b) the scatter of E5.4 (Winkler score with the uniform prior against the right-side and wrong-side priors) for the three methods with a prior;
  - `Makefile` targets (run, tables, plots; the output folder in `RESULTS`); tests: the requested prevalences are reproducible for a seed, lie on the simplex and differ from those of E5.1 and E5.4; the distributions have parameters $10\cdot\mathbf 1$, $\tfrac1{10}\mathbf 1$ and the priors $3\cdot\mathbf 1$, $\tfrac13\mathbf 1$, $\mathbf 1$; `SQS-B` receives the prior; the methods without a prior are built once per distribution; `binform` with its default arguments, `--e51` and `--e54` builds the same tasks and bags as before; the table and the violins rendered on a synthetic result, LaTeX included.
- **Depends on:** none (reuses the code of WP-24, done; `SQSB` from WP-18 and WP-25, done).
- **Comparability:** new bags, so the results are comparable within E5.5 only; not with `main`, E5.1 or E5.4, which are unchanged. The SQS methods and the methods with the uniform prior are built as in `main`, but on other bags. Cost: $2$ distributions $\times$ ($3$ methods $\times$ $3$ priors $+$ $3$ methods without a prior) on the $75$ pairs, $1800$ runs of $100$ bags of $1000$ instances; $450$ BayCAP-Hf runs, half those of E5.4 (which took about $15$ hours on `bcuda`), plus $450$ runs of `SQS-B`, which samples the posterior of BayesKDEy like `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` and draws $R$ bags of $V$ per target bag. Risk: the bags of $G_e$ lie very close to the faces of the simplex, and the sparse prior made BayCAP-KDEy diverge on up to $22\%$ of the sparse bags in E5.4; `SQS-B` samples the same posterior.
- **Deviations from Part V:** as WP-24: one result folder per distribution, `binform_e55/<distribution>/` (§V.1 output location, with `<experiment>` = `binform_e55/<distribution>`); the requested prevalences seeded by the experiment seed, the dataset and the distribution, not by global state.
- **Done when:** the code and tests are merged; the pilot (`connect-4`, `poker_hand`, `letter`, with the five classifiers) and the full run are done with stored results.
- **Commands:**
  - pilot: `make run-binform-e55 ARGS="--datasets connect-4 poker_hand letter"`;
  - full run: `make run-binform-e55` (the cells already stored are skipped, so the pilot cells are reused); `ARGS` narrows it with `--datasets`, `--classifiers`, `--distributions` and `--methods`;
  - tables: `make run-tables-binform-side-prior ARGS=--tables` (CSVs, fragments and documents), PDFs with `make run-tables-binform-side-prior WHERE=local ARGS=--pdf` after `make pull`;
  - plots: `make run-plot-binform-side-prior` (pair violins for all, binary and multiclass pairs, and scatters, vanilla accuracy; `ARGS="--metrics macro-F1 macro-K"` for the other metrics, `--figures bag-violins` for the violins over every bag, `--figures pair-violins` or `--figures winkler` for one kind, `--groups all binary multiclass` for the groups of pairs).
- **Log:** 2026-11-19: card added; E5.5 decided by the user: bags from $\operatorname{Dir}(10\cdot\mathbf 1)$ and $\operatorname{Dir}(\tfrac1{10}\mathbf 1)$ analysed with the priors of E5.4, roles right side / wrong side, no matched prior, the SQS methods added (`SQS-B` with the priors, the others as references), $\lvert U\rvert=1000$ only, the violin figures. 2026-11-19: started on branch `wp-27/side-priors-sqs` (in a git worktree, since the main checkout was in use for WP-26). Code: `experiments/binform.py` (option `--e55`, exclusive with `--e51` and `--e54`, with `--datasets`, `--classifiers`, `--distributions`, `--methods`; `E55_*` constants, the parameters of the distributions of the bags `E55_BAG_ALPHAS` set apart from those of the priors `E54_ALPHAS`; `e55_bag_alpha`, `e55_requested_prevs` with streams $4$ (`centre`) and $5$ (`edge`), `e55_method`, `e55_experiment`, `e55_protocol`, `e55_tasks`, `e55_experiments`; `_prior_method` builds `SQS-B`; `_prior_task` skips the NUTS diagnostics of methods without them, unchanged for E5.1 and E5.4); `experiments/methods.py:sqs_b` takes `prior` (default uniform, so `main` is unchanged); `tables/binform_side_prior.py` and `plots/binform_side_prior.py` (new); `Makefile` (`run-binform-e55`, `run-tables-binform-side-prior`, `run-plot-binform-side-prior`; `binform_e55` in `RESULTS`); tests `tests/test_binform_e55.py`. Deviation from the Scope: the LaTeX summary has one row per (distribution, group of $K$, method, role) with the scores as columns, as in E5.4, rather than one row per method with the roles as columns (which would need about $20$ columns); the CSVs carry the same numbers. The SQS methods without a prior run in the pool of the regular methods of `main`, as in `main`, not in the BayCAP pool. Unit tests: $581$, all passing. 2026-11-19: full run started on `bcuda` at the user's request, no pilot (user's decision, as for E5.4): `make run-binform-e55` (`JAX=cpu`), from `devel` `9c67144` (E5.5 code of `739218c`), `cap-ml` `93ae246`, started 20:44 after the end of the WP-26 run; $1800$ tasks ($75$ pairs, $12$ methods, $2$ distributions); log `output/runs/binform-e55_20261119-204406.out` on `bcuda`. 2026-11-20: the run ended (exit $0$, 10:21; $1800$ tasks, all stored for the three metrics); tables and plots made at the user's request. On the user's request (on `devel`), after noting that the medians of the bag violins rank the SQS methods ahead of BayCAP-Hf while the tables (means) do the opposite, since the Winkler score is skewed by the penalties of the misses: the bag violins also mark the arithmetic mean, and new pair violins (`--figures pair-violins`, files `binform_side_prior_pair_violins_<distribution>_<role>_<metric>`) show the per-pair means over the bags with mean and median, the mean equal to the summary table.
- **Outcome:** merge commit `739218c` (2026-11-19), code and tests only; the card stays In progress until the pilot (and then the full run) is requested and done. Decisions and deviations, also in the Log: (1) the LaTeX summary keeps the layout of E5.4 (one row per distribution, group of $K$, method and role, the scores as columns) instead of one row per method with the roles as columns; the CSVs carry the same numbers; (2) the violins floor the Winkler score at $10^{-5}$ as well as the AE, since a zero score has no logarithm; (3) `SQS-H`, `SQS-Hbs` and `PQ` run in the pool of the regular methods of `main`, as in `main`; (4) developed in a git worktree, removed after the merge. Follow-ups: the pilot, not before the WP-26 pilot on `bcuda` is over (`make run-*` replaces the code in the single remote folder). Closed on 2026-11-20 at the user's request after the full run (2026-11-19/20, exit $0$), the tables and the plots (§II.2); the analysis of E5.5 was not written before closing and is pending. Later change on `devel` (`695b2d5`): the bag violins mark the mean as well as the median, and pair violins show the per-pair means, since the medians of the bag violins rank the SQS methods ahead of BayCAP-Hf while the summary table (means) ranks them behind. Later change on `devel` (2026-11-20, user's request): the violin figures merge the two roles (wrong side, uniform, right side per method with a prior), order the methods SQS-S, SQS-H, SQS-Hbs, SQS-B, BayCAP-Hf, BayCAP-KDEy, hatch the methods without a prior like the uniform prior, split the pairs into all, binary and multiclass, and close the tails of the outline: `Axes.violinplot` evaluates the density only between the smallest and the largest value, so with $75$ pairs the outline ended flat where the density was still high; the density is now extended by two bandwidths beyond them. Then (user's request): the violins over every bag renamed `bag_violins` (`--figures bag-violins`) and no longer drawn by default; the default is the pair violins and the scatters.

### WP-26 E7.2: bandwidth of BayesKDEy, then temperature, selected per pair on calibration bags drawn from half of $V$, for `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` (ablations)

- **Kind / status:** experiment / Done; run and findings in §II.30
- **Source:** [[ext7_kdey_bandwidth]] (E7.2a, E7.2b, §T.7–§T.10)
- **Goal:** measure whether a bandwidth of BayesKDEy selected per pair from $V$ alone, by the accuracy error it causes, improves the realised intervals of `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` (BayesKDEy with an MLP input, cross-fitted validation outputs, Gaussian kernel, fixed $\hat C$ of $h$ on $V$, realised pushforward), which uses $b=0.1$ for every pair. Background: a per-class bandwidth chosen by the leave-one-out likelihood of the class densities (E7.1, card WP-25) fitted the densities better and made the intervals worse on its pilot, the centre moving away from the target. Selection, inside the fit of the method: $V$ split per class into $V_{\text{fit}}$ and $V_{\text{cal}}$, half each (stratified, seeded by the `random_state` of the method); the MLP, its cross-fitted outputs and the recalls $\hat d^{\text{fit}}$ of $h$ on $V_{\text{fit}}$; $S=50$ calibration bags of size $1000$ drawn with replacement from $V_{\text{cal}}$ by QuaPy's UPP (prevalences from $\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1)$, seeded, the same for every $b$), each with its true prevalence $\pi_s$; for each $b$ of the grid $\{10^{-2+0.1k}\}_{k=0}^{20}$ (one bandwidth shared by the classes), the kernel densities on $V_{\text{fit}}$ and the maximum-likelihood estimate $\hat\pi_s(b)$ of the BayesKDEy model on each bag (EM); $b_{\text{cal}}=\arg\min_b\frac1S\sum_s\lvert\hat d^{\text{fit}\top}(\hat\pi_s(b)-\pi_s)\rvert$, ties broken towards $0.1$. This criterion keeps only the part of the accuracy error of the centre that depends on $b$: it leaves out the error of the recalls measured on $V_{\text{fit}}$, an offset common to the bags that a plain accuracy error would let the bandwidth compensate, and the luck of the bag. The deployed method is then fitted on the full $V$ at $b_{\text{cal}}$ exactly as the stored one, so $b_{\text{cal}}=0.1$ reproduces it. New method of `main`: `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-cal`, everything else as `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` (prior $\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1)$, $\tau=1$, NUTS with $500$ warm-up iterations, $1000$ draws, the same seeds). `SQS-B` is left out (user's decision of 2026-11-19). Protocol of `main`: the $75$ pairs, $100$ bags of size $1000$ per pair. Measures (vanilla accuracy, macro-F1, macro-K): AE, coverage, amplitude and Winkler score at $\alpha=0.05$ of both methods; per pair, paired bootstrap tests of the mean per-bag difference of AE and of the Winkler score (cal minus stored, $10{,}000$ seeded resamples, Holm over the pairs), Wilcoxon signed-rank tests over the per-pair means; per pair, label-free on the target: $b_{\text{cal}}$, the calibration criterion over the grid and at $b_{\text{cal}}$ and $0.1$; the Spearman correlation between calibration gain and test AE gain. Pilot check of the approximation: on the pilot pairs, the criterion also computed with the NUTS posterior mean in place of the maximum-likelihood estimate (all $b$ and bags), with both selected bandwidths, the steps between them and the largest difference of the two curves. No decision rule for the method. **Gate of the solver (added on 2026-11-20 by the user's decision, before the full run):** the user prefers to select the bandwidth with the standard KDEy, QuaPy's `KDEyML`, which maximises the same log-likelihood with SciPy's SLSQP, in place of EM. On the $15$ pilot pairs, the selection alone (no target bag, no NUTS) is run with both solvers on the same split, MLP, calibration bags and class densities; per pair: $b_{\text{cal}}$ of each solver, the grid steps between them, the tie gap (the largest over the two criterion curves of the difference between the curve at the two selected bandwidths), the largest difference of the curves, the largest $\lVert\hat\pi^{\text{EM}}-\hat\pi^{\text{SLSQP}}\rVert_1$ (EM converged and not), the fits where SLSQP fails, the fits where each estimate has the larger log-likelihood, the wall time of each solver, and the run time of the method with EM (shared steps, EM fits, deployed fit, and the $100$ target bags timed by the pilot). Rule: the full run selects with SLSQP if (a) on every pilot pair the two solvers select the same bandwidth or the tie gap is below $10^{-3}$, and (b) the extra time of SLSQP summed over the pilot pairs is at most $10\%$ of the run time of the method with EM summed over the same pairs; otherwise it keeps EM. **Temperature step (E7.2b, added on 2026-11-20 by the user's decision; the bandwidth step above is E7.2a):** after $b_{\text{cal}}$, on the same $50$ calibration bags, `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` refitted on $V_{\text{fit}}$ at $b_{\text{cal}}$ ($\hat C$ of $h$ on $V_{\text{fit}}$, realised pushforward) is run with NUTS at each $\tau\in\{0.5,1,2,4,8\}$ (the bag term tempered, as in E5.2); $\tau_{\text{cal}}$ minimises the mean Winkler score at $\alpha=0.05$ against the realised tables of the calibration bags, on vanilla accuracy, ties as in E5.3 (closest to $1$ on the log scale, then smaller); the deployed method is fitted on the full $V$ at $(b_{\text{cal}},\tau_{\text{cal}})$ and its interval used for the three metrics. New method `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-cal-tcal`. The results form a $2\times2$ ablation, $b\in\{0.1,b_{\text{cal}}\}\times\tau\in\{1,\tau_{\text{cal}}\}$: the stored method, `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-cal`, the new method, and, for $(0.1,\tau_{\text{cal}})$, the stored E5.2 results of `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-t<τ>` at the vanilla-accuracy $\tau_{\text{cal}}$ of E5.3 (no new run; its $\tau_{\text{cal}}$ comes from a different split of $V$). Measures: the measures above for the four cells, with the tests of the new method against each other cell; per pair $\tau_{\text{cal}}$ and the calibration Winkler score at each $\tau$; the cross-tabulation of $\tau_{\text{cal}}$ at $b_{\text{cal}}$ against $\tau_{\text{cal}}$ at $b=0.1$; the NUTS diagnostics of the calibration runs. No pilot and no decision rule (user's decisions of 2026-11-20); the full run covers E7.2a and E7.2b together.
- **Estimand:** the realised table of each target bag (`true_cts`), for both methods; the calibration criterion scores the population accuracy $\hat d^{\text{fit}\top}\hat\pi_s$ against $\hat d^{\text{fit}\top}\pi_s$ on bags of $V_{\text{cal}}$, so $b_{\text{cal}}$ uses only the labels of $V$. E7.2b: the realised table of each target bag for the four cells; $\tau_{\text{cal}}$ scores each calibration interval against the realised table of its calibration bag, so it also uses only the labels of $V$.
- **Scope:**
  - `methods/baycap_kdey.py` (and `methods/bayes_kdey.py` where the EM estimate belongs): `bandwidth="cal"` in `BayCAPKDEy` (with `c_prior="fixed"`, the Gaussian kernel and no shrinkage only), with the grid, the number and size of the calibration bags as parameters; the split, the calibration bags, the per-$b$ log-densities of the instances of $V_{\text{cal}}$, the EM estimates, the criterion and the selection inside `fit`, before the deployed fit and without consuming its random state; the selection stored and written per bag ($b_{\text{cal}}$, the criterion curve, its values at $b_{\text{cal}}$ and $0.1$); a diagnostic option for the pilot that also computes the criterion with the NUTS posterior mean;
  - `experiments/methods.py`: the factory entry `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-cal`, excluded from the tables, ranks and default plots of `main`, in the BayCAP pool; labels and colour;
  - a table module and a plot module for E7.2 (summary and per-pair tables per metric, the pilot check table; $b_{\text{cal}}$ per pair by dataset, test gain against calibration gain); `Makefile` targets;
  - tests: the split (stratified, disjoint, covering $V$, reproducible); the calibration bags (reproducible, the same for every $b$); the EM estimate against a generic optimiser on a toy mixture; the criterion invariant to adding a constant to the recalls; with a grid of the single value $0.1$, the draws and tables of `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real`; the selection uses no target bag; the table and plots on synthetic results; §II.4 and §II.12 updated.
  - gate of the solver (implemented and run on 2026-11-20, failed; SLSQP chosen by the user, `CAL_SOLVER` in `experiments/methods.py`): `cal_solver` (`"em"`, default, or `"slsqp"`) in `BayCAPKDEy`; `methods/bayes_kdey.py:slsqp_prevalences` (the optimisation of `KDEyML.aggregate`, with its success flag) and `bag_log_likelihoods`; `BayCAPKDEy.solver_check` (both solvers on the same calibration, timed apart from the shared steps); the study `experiments/kdey_cal_solver.py` and the table `tables/kdey_cal_solver.py` (per-pair table and verdict); tests: SLSQP equal to `KDEyML.aggregate`, EM and SLSQP agreeing on a toy mixture, `solver_check` reproducing the selection of each solver, the gate rule on synthetic rows;
  - E7.2b (implemented on 2026-11-20, not run): `temperature="cal"` in `BayCAPKDEy` (with `bandwidth="cal"` only; the $\tau$ grid as a parameter), the calibration runs inside `fit` after $b_{\text{cal}}$, seeded apart from the deployed fit; per bag, $\tau_{\text{cal}}$, the calibration Winkler score at each $\tau$ and the NUTS diagnostics of the calibration runs; the factory entry `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-cal-tcal` (BayCAP pool, outside the tables, ranks and default plots of `main`); the ablation table (the cell of E5.3 read from the per-pair $\tau_{\text{cal}}$ of `tables/tau_select.py` and the stored E5.2 results), the per-pair table, the cross-tabulation and the ablation plot, in the new modules `tables/kdey_tcal.py` and `plots/kdey_tcal.py` (planned in `tables/kdey_cal.py` and `plots/kdey_cal.py`); tests: $b_{\text{cal}}$ unchanged by the temperature step, a $\tau$ grid $\{1\}$ reproducing `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-cal`, grids $\{0.1\}$ and $\{\tau\}$ reproducing the stored `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-t<τ>`, the tie rule, the calibration Winkler score equal to that of `main`, no target bag used.
- **Depends on:** WP-25 (done: the quantifier class `BayesKDEy` and the grid `LOO_GRID`).
- **Comparability:** adds one method to `main`; the stored results of every other method are untouched; same bags and seeds as `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real`, so the comparison is bag by bag, and on pairs with $b_{\text{cal}}=0.1$ the two coincide. Cost: per pair, one MLP and $21$ kernel-density fits on $V_{\text{fit}}$, the log-densities of $V_{\text{cal}}$ at each $b$, $21\times50$ EM estimates, then the target run as `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` ($75\times100$ NUTS runs); the pilot check adds $21\times50$ NUTS runs per pilot pair ($15{,}750$). E7.2b adds `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-cal-tcal`, with the same bags and NUTS seeds, so the four cells of the ablation are compared bag by bag; per pair $5\times50$ calibration NUTS runs and $100$ target runs ($26{,}250$ in all).
- **Deviations from Part V:** none. Implementation choices not in the plan: the calibration helpers are plain functions in `methods/baycap_kdey.py` (`split_halves`, `calibration_bags`, `calibration_seed`, `class_recalls`, `accuracy_criterion`, `select_from_curve`), the EM estimate is in `methods/bayes_kdey.py` (`em_prevalences`, on the instance counts of each bag, tolerance $10^{-9}$, at most $10^4$ steps), and the class densities at any shared bandwidth in `BayesKDEy.class_log_densities`; the seeds of the split and of the bags are derived from `random_state` and a stream (`calibration_seed`), not from the dataset name as in E5.3, since the method does not know the dataset (so the same split for the five classifiers of a dataset, as planned); ties at the same $\log_{10}$ distance from $0.1$ go to the smaller bandwidth; the summaries also store $\lvert V\rvert$ and $\lvert V_{\text{cal}}\rvert$ (`cal_n_val`, `cal_n_cal`; the first orders the datasets in the tables as in E3.1); the pilot check is turned on by `main --kdey-cal-check`, which sets `cal_nuts_check` on the method, and its NUTS runs count in the training time `t_train` of the pilot cells. E7.2b: the ablation is in its own modules, `tables/kdey_tcal.py` and `plots/kdey_tcal.py`, so that `tables/kdey_cal.py` and `plots/kdey_cal.py` keep E7.2a (two methods) unchanged; the cell of E5.3 recomputes its $\tau_{\text{cal}}$ from the stored calibration runs of `tau_select` with `tables/tau_select.py:cal_taus` rather than reading the CSV of `tables/tau_select.py`, and is NaN on pairs whose runs are not stored; the calibration NUTS runs use one key, `calibration_seed(random_state, CAL_TAU_STREAM)`, for every bag and $\tau$ (as `main` uses one key for its target bags), and the realised tables of a calibration bag a generator seeded by the same seed (`BayesianQuantifier.run_nuts` gained an optional `seed`); the summaries also store the mean coverage of the calibration intervals at each $\tau$ (`cal_tau_coverage`), and the NUTS diagnostics per $\tau$ as the runs with a divergent transition, the largest split-$\hat R$ and the smallest effective sample size over the bags; the calibration runs count in `t_train`.
- **Done when:** the code and tests of E7.2a, of the gate of the solver and of E7.2b are merged; the gate is run (done on 2026-11-20: failed; the user chose SLSQP anyway); the pilot of E7.2a (`mammographic`, `wine-quality`, `poker_hand`, with the five classifiers, $15$ pairs, with the NUTS check, done) and the joint full run of `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-cal` and `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-cal-tcal` are done with stored results.
- **Commands:**
  - pilot (with the NUTS check): `make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-cal --datasets mammographic wine-quality poker_hand --kdey-cal-check"` (the five classifiers, $15$ pairs);
  - gate of the solver: `make run-kdey-cal-solver` (the $15$ pilot pairs by default; `ARGS="--datasets ... --classifiers ..."` narrows them), then `make pull-results RESULTS=kdey_cal_solver` and `make run-tables-kdey-cal-solver WHERE=local ARGS=--all` (CSVs `kdey_cal_solver_pairs.csv` and `kdey_cal_solver_gate.csv`, the per-pair table with the verdict in its caption);
  - full run (E7.2a and E7.2b together): `make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-cal BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-cal-tcal"` (all $75$ pairs: the EM pilot cells are archived in `main_e72a_pilot_em`, outside `main`);
  - tables of the archived EM pilot: `make run-tables-kdey-cal WHERE=local ARGS="--tables --cal-experiment main_e72a_pilot_em --table-dir <dir>"`;
  - tables: `make run-tables-kdey-cal ARGS=--tables` (CSVs of pairs, summary, curves and check, fragments and documents; `--metrics` narrows the metrics), PDFs with `make run-tables-kdey-cal WHERE=local ARGS=--pdf` after `make pull`;
  - plots: `make run-plot-kdey-cal` (`bandwidths` and `gain`; `ARGS="--figures gain --metrics macro-F1"` selects figures and metrics);
  - ablation of E7.2b (needs the stored `main`, E5.2 and `tau_select` results): `make run-tables-kdey-tcal ARGS=--tables` (CSVs `kdey_tcal_pairs.csv`, `_summary`, `_crosstab`, `_nuts`; per metric the summary and per-pair tables, and the cross-tabulation; `--metrics` narrows the metrics), PDFs with `ARGS=--pdf`; plot `make run-plot-kdey-tcal` (`ablation`; `ARGS="--metrics macro-F1"`).
- **Log:** 2026-11-19: card added; E7.2 decided with one bandwidth shared by the classes, the criterion on the accuracy error that the bandwidth controls (recalls of $V_{\text{fit}}$, true prevalences of the bags), the maximum-likelihood estimate in the criterion checked against NUTS on the pilot, `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` only, and the pilot on `mammographic`, `wine-quality` and `poker_hand` (user's decisions). 2026-11-19: card In progress on branch `wp-26/kdey-cal-bandwidth`; `bandwidth="cal"` in `BayCAPKDEy` (selection in `select_bandwidth`, EM in `bayes_kdey.py:em_prevalences`), the method `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-cal`, `main --kdey-cal-check`, `tables/kdey_cal.py`, `plots/kdey_cal.py`, Makefile targets, tests (`tests/test_kdey_cal.py`, `tests/test_kdey_cal_tables.py`, `tests/test_methods.py`; the $551$ tests of the suite pass); §II.4 and §II.12 updated; no run yet. 2026-11-19: merged (`bd5e866`); pilot launched on `bcuda` at the user's request. 2026-11-19: pilot done (`make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-cal --datasets mammographic wine-quality poker_hand --kdey-cal-check"`, commit `520580c`, `cap-ml` `93ae246`, JAX on CPU, 16:01–20:41, exit $0$): the $15$ pairs and the three metrics stored under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main/`, no failed cell; plots not generated; the pilot Analysis of E7.2 written in [[ext7_kdey_bandwidth]]: the maximum-likelihood selection equals the NUTS one on $14$ of $15$ pairs (on `poker_hand` with LR the posterior-mean curve falls at large $b$ and reaches its minimum at the edge $b=1$), $b_{\text{cal}}\neq0.1$ on $13$ pairs, AE lower on $11$ pairs and higher on none (vanilla accuracy, Holm), EM at the cap of $10^4$ steps in $177$ of $1050$ fits per pair on `poker_hand`. ### WP-24 E5.4: concentrated and sparse prevalence distributions for BayCAP-Hf and BayCAP-KDEy (extended `binform`)

- **Kind / status:** experiment / Done; run and findings in §II.2
- **Source:** [[ext5_baycap_priors_tempering]] (E5.4)
- **Goal:** measure how much knowing *how extreme* the prior probability shift is (rather than its direction, studied by E5.1, card WP-19) improves the realised intervals of `BayesCAP-Hf-real` (BayCAP with the Hf prior on $C$, realised pushforward) and `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` (BayesKDEy with an MLP input and a fixed $\hat C$, realised pushforward), and what the wrong belief costs. Two symmetric distributions of the target prevalences, both with mean $\mathbf 1/K$: concentrated, $G_c=\operatorname{Dir}(3\cdot\mathbf 1)$ (mild shifts), and sparse, $G_e=\operatorname{Dir}(\tfrac13\mathbf 1)$ (severe shifts: a few classes dominate, the others are nearly absent). For each dataset (shared by its five classifiers and both bag sizes) and each distribution, $100$ requested prevalences, drawn with `np.random.default_rng([_R_SEED, crc32(dataset), stream])` with streams of their own, distinct from those of E5.1. Each set of bags is analysed with three priors on $\pi^Q$: uniform $\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1)$, $\operatorname{Dir}(3\cdot\mathbf 1)$ and $\operatorname{Dir}(\tfrac13\mathbf 1)$; for each distribution, the prior equal to it is well informed and the other is ill informed. All $75$ pairs of `main`, bag sizes $\lvert U\rvert\in\{100,1000\}$, $\tau=1$, $100$ bags per (pair, bag size, distribution), drawn from the test pool with seeded generators as in E5.1 and the same for every prior and method. Measures (vanilla accuracy, macro-F1, macro-K): AE, coverage, amplitude, Winkler score at $\alpha=0.05$; per pair and distribution, $\Delta W_{\text{know}}=W_{\text{uniform}}-W_{\text{well}}$, $\Delta W_{\text{wrong}}=W_{\text{ill}}-W_{\text{uniform}}$ and the ratio of the mean amplitude of each informative prior to the uniform one; paired Wilcoxon tests over the $75$ per-pair means and over the $15$ per-dataset means; the mean number of classes absent from the realised table of the bags; the share of bags with divergent NUTS transitions per prior. No decision rule.
- **Estimand:** the realised table of each bag (`true_cts`), for every prior and method.
- **Scope:**
  - `experiments/binform.py`: a new option next to `--e51` (same `--datasets`, `--classifiers`, `--sizes`, `--methods`), with the two distributions, their seeded requested prevalences, the three priors for the two methods (built as in E5.1 through `experiments/methods.py:fixed_c_method(..., prior=...)` and the Hf factory), the distribution and the prior stored with the results, `jax.clear_caches()` after each task; output in a folder of its own per bag size and distribution; the default configuration and `--e51` unchanged;
  - check how macro-F1 and macro-K treat a class absent from the realised table of a bag (frequent under $G_e$ at large $K$), and record it in the card before the pilot;
  - new table module: per method, metric and bag size, the $2\times3$ summary (rows the distribution, columns uniform / well informed / ill informed) of the means over the pairs of AE, coverage, amplitude and Winkler score, the counts of pairs and datasets with a lower Winkler score than the uniform prior and the $p$-values of the paired tests, also split by $K$ ($K=2$, $3\le K\le7$, $K\ge8$), with the absent classes and the divergent bags; per-pair and summary CSVs and LaTeX documents, as for E5.1;
  - new plot module: per method and distribution, the per-pair Winkler score with the uniform prior against that with the well-informed and the ill-informed prior, coloured by $K$, one panel per bag size, with the diagonal;
  - `Makefile` targets (run, tables, plots; the output folder in `RESULTS`); tests: the requested prevalences are reproducible for a seed, lie on the simplex, differ from those of E5.1 and are shared by the classifiers and bag sizes; the prior parameters are $3\cdot\mathbf 1$, $\tfrac13\mathbf 1$, $\mathbf 1$; `binform` with its default arguments and with `--e51` builds the same tasks and bags as before; the table and the plot rendered on a synthetic result, LaTeX included.
- **Depends on:** none (reuses the code of WP-19, done).
- **Comparability:** new bags, so the results are comparable within E5.4 only, with the uniform prior as the reference within each distribution; not with `main`, the stored `binform` results or E5.1, which are unchanged. $G_c$ is the $G$ of E5.1 with its mean at the centre, so E5.1 and E5.4 differ in the mean of the distribution and of the priors, but their bags differ too. Cost: $2$ distributions $\times$ $3$ priors $\times$ $2$ bag sizes $\times$ $2$ methods on the $75$ pairs ($1800$ runs of $100$ bags, twice E5.1); BayCAP-KDEy samples $\pi$ alone and is cheap, BayCAP-Hf costs about one run of `BayesCAP-Hf-real` in `main` per (distribution, prior, bag size). Risk: with $\tfrac13<1$ the prior density is unbounded at the faces of the simplex, where the bags of $G_e$ lie, so NUTS may diverge more, mostly for BayCAP-Hf.
- **Deviations from Part V:** as WP-19: the bag size $\lvert U\rvert=100$ differs from `SAMPLE_SIZE` (it is a varied factor); one result folder per (distribution, bag size), `binform_e54/<distribution>/u<size>/` (§V.1 output location, with `<experiment>` = `binform_e54/<distribution>/u<size>`); the requested prevalences seeded by the experiment seed, the dataset and the distribution, not by global state.
- **Done when:** the code and tests are merged; the pilot (`connect-4`, `poker_hand`, `letter`, with the five classifiers) and the full run are done with stored results.
- **Commands:**
  - pilot: `make run-binform-e54 ARGS="--datasets connect-4 poker_hand letter"`;
  - full run: `make run-binform-e54` (the cells already stored are skipped, so the pilot cells are reused); `ARGS` narrows it with `--datasets`, `--classifiers`, `--distributions`, `--sizes` and `--methods`;
  - tables: `make run-tables-binform-centre-edge ARGS=--tables` (CSVs, fragments and documents), PDFs with `make run-tables-binform-centre-edge WHERE=local ARGS=--pdf` after `make pull`;
  - plots: `make run-plot-binform-centre-edge` (vanilla accuracy; `ARGS="--metrics macro-F1 macro-K"` for the other metrics).
- **Log:** 2026-11-18: card added; E5.4 promoted directly to the main section of the extension, with $\lambda=3$ and $\beta=1/3$ (user's decision). 2026-11-18: started on branch `wp-24/centre-edge-prior`. Code: `experiments/binform.py` (option `--e54` with `--distributions`, exclusive with `--e51`; `E54_*` constants, `e54_alpha`, `e54_prior`, `e54_requested_prevs` with streams $2$ (`centre`) and $3$ (`edge`) of the generator of E5.1, `e54_method`, `e54_experiment`, `e54_protocol`, `e54_tasks`, `e54_experiments`; method names with the suffix `_uniform`, `_centre` or `_edge`, the prior named by its distribution, the roles well/ill derived in the table; columns `distribution` and `prior_alpha` stored with `bag_size` and `requested_prev`); the body of the E5.1 task moved into `_prior_task`, shared by E5.1 and E5.4, and the construction of the method into `_prior_method`, with no change to what E5.1 computes or stores (a test checks the arguments that `_e51_task` passes); `tables/binform_centre_edge.py` (new); `plots/binform_centre_edge.py` (new), which draws its panels with `plots/binform_prior.py:plot_winkler`, now taking the priors, labels and title as arguments (defaults those of E5.1, so its figures do not change); `Makefile` (`run-binform-e54`, `run-tables-binform-centre-edge`, `run-plot-binform-centre-edge`; `binform_e54` in `RESULTS`); tests `tests/test_binform_e54.py`. Unit tests: $496$, all passing. **Check of the metrics on absent classes** (Scope; `AbsentClassMetricsTest`): all three metrics stay finite on bags with absent classes. Vanilla accuracy is unaffected. Macro-K scores an absent class $2\,\mathrm{spec}-1$, so $1$ when no instance is predicted as that class. Macro-F1 is scored, as everywhere, with the smoothed metric (`acc_from_ct(..., type="selection")`, Part IV FIX-6): an absent class never predicted gets $F_1=2\varepsilon/(2\varepsilon+2(K-1)\varepsilon)=1/K$ instead of the $1$ of the unsmoothed metric, and an absent class that receives some predictions gets about $0$ with both. The truth and the intervals use the same metric, so the scores are consistent, but under $G_e$ macro-F1 rewards an interval that predicts the absent classes as poorly classified; this is recorded, not changed (the protocol of `main` is kept). Not run (runs on request). 2026-11-18/19: full run without a pilot (user's request), `make run-binform-e54` on `bcuda` from `fb49895`, 21:47–13:08, $1800$ tasks, no error; the memory stayed bounded (about $94$ GB for the user's processes) with `jax.clear_caches()`. 2026-11-19: branch rebased on `devel` (`e2201e1`; conflict only on the board row of WP-25). Tables (`--tables` on `bcuda`, `--pdf` locally) and plots generated from `e2201e1`, from a worktree of the branch; the Analysis of E5.4 written in the extension and the run and findings in §II.2. The violin plot discussed on 2026-11-18 is not implemented (no decision yet).
- **Outcome:** merge commit `e121fe5` (2026-11-19). Decisions and deviations: (1) no pilot: the full run was started directly (user's decision of 2026-11-18), so the check of the sampler under $\beta<1$ planned for the pilot was made on the full run (the sparse prior makes BayCAP-KDEy diverge on up to $22\%$ of the sparse bags, BayCAP-Hf not more than with the uniform prior; Analysis of E5.4); (2) the full run took about $15$ hours ($21{:}47$–$13{:}08$), not the half day first estimated, BayCAP-Hf being about half as fast as BayCAP-KDEy per task; (3) the run used `fb49895`; the branch was rebased twice on `devel` before the merge (`e2201e1`, then `9d6a0fb`), with conflicts only on the board rows, and the code that produced the results is unchanged by the rebases (the second one brings in the WP-25 refactor of BayesKDEy, which leaves the results of `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` unchanged); tables and plots were generated from `e2201e1` from a git worktree of the branch; (4) macro-F1 scores absent classes with the smoothed metric (FIX-6), recorded in the Log and in the Analysis, not changed; (5) the violin plot discussed during the run was not implemented: it moved, with a different design, to the follow-up E5.5 (bags from $\operatorname{Dir}(10\cdot\mathbf 1)$ and $\operatorname{Dir}(\tfrac1{10}\mathbf 1)$, priors $\operatorname{Dir}(3\cdot\mathbf 1)$ and $\operatorname{Dir}(\tfrac13\mathbf 1)$, $\lvert U\rvert=1000$ only, with SQS-B informed and SQS-H, SQS-Hbs, SQS-S as uninformable references; user's decision of 2026-11-19, not yet written in the extension). Follow-ups: E5.5; the hypotheses left open by the Analysis (why the sparse prior widens BayCAP-Hf; the divergences of BayCAP-KDEy under the sparse prior; `poker_hand` with BayCAP-KDEy).

### WP-25 E7.1: per-class leave-one-out bandwidth for BayesKDEy in `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` and `SQS-B`

- **Kind / status:** experiment / Done (pilot only; full run not done, user decision); run and findings in §II.29
- **Source:** [[ext7_kdey_bandwidth]] (E7.1)
- **Goal:** measure whether a bandwidth selected from $V$ alone improves the methods built on the posterior of BayesKDEy. BayesKDEy estimates, for each class $\omega_j$, the density of the outputs of its input classifier (an MLP trained on $V$, validation outputs cross-fitted on $5$ folds) by a Gaussian kernel density; `main` uses one bandwidth $b=0.1$ for every class and dataset. The card makes the bandwidth of `methods/baycap_kdey.py:BayCAPKDEy` a hyperparameter that takes either a positive number (or `"scott"`/`"silverman"`, as now) or `"loo"`: with `"loo"`, each class gets its own $\hat b_j=\arg\max_{b\in\mathcal G}\sum_{i=1}^{n_j}\ln\hat p_{j,-i}(z_{j,i})$, the leave-one-out log-likelihood of the class density on its $n_j$ validation outputs, over the grid $\mathcal G=\{10^{-2+0.1k}:k=0,\dots,20\}$ (log-spaced from $0.01$ to $1$, containing $0.1$; a parameter `bandwidth_grid`), computed in closed form from the pairwise distances of the class; a class with $n_j<2$ keeps $0.1$. Because the outputs lie on the simplex ($K-1$ dimensions) while QuaPy's Gaussian kernel is normalised in $K$, the per-class densities and the selection use the kernel normalised in $K-1$ dimensions (log-density of `KernelDensity` plus $\tfrac12\ln(2\pi b_j^2)$); with a shared bandwidth the extra factor cancels in the posterior, with per-class bandwidths it would reweight the classes by $1/b_j$ and bias the selection towards small $b$. `SQSB` (`methods/sqs_b.py`) takes its quantifier as a configured `BayCAPKDEy`, so it gets the selection through it, with the same draws as the BayCAP method on the same quantifier. Two new methods of `main`: `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-loo` (BayesKDEy with the MLP input, fixed $\hat C$, realised pushforward, `bandwidth="loo"`) and `SQS-B-loo` (SQS-B on that quantifier); everything else as `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` and `SQS-B` (Gaussian kernel, no shrinkage, prior $\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1)$, $\tau=1$, NUTS with $500$ warm-up iterations, $1000$ draws and the same seeds, bags of `SQS-B` as in E3.3). Protocol of `main`: the $75$ pairs and $100$ bags of size $1000$ per pair. Measures (vanilla accuracy, macro-F1, macro-K): AE, coverage, amplitude, Winkler score at $\alpha=0.05$; per pair, the difference of the mean Winkler score between each LOO method and its stored counterpart, with paired bootstrap tests ($10{,}000$ seeded resamples of the $100$ per-bag differences, Holm-adjusted over the $75$ pairs) and a Wilcoxon signed-rank test over the $75$ per-pair means; per pair and class, label-free: $\hat b_j$, $n_j$, whether $\hat b_j$ is at an edge of $\mathcal G$, the leave-one-out log-likelihood per instance at $\hat b_j$ and at $0.1$. No oracle bandwidth (user's decision of 2026-11-19). No decision rule.
- **Estimand:** the realised table of each bag (`true_cts`), for the four methods; the selection of $\hat b_j$ uses no target labels and no target bag.
- **Scope:**
  - **Step 1, restructure (no change of behaviour; user's decision of 2026-11-19).** `QuantifierBayCAP` (`methods/baycap_quantifier.py`) mixes a Bayesian quantifier (input classifier $h$ or a cross-fitted MLP, class densities or calibration, NUTS on $\pi$) with a CAP method (the rates $\hat C$ of $h$ on $V$ and the pushforward of each draw to a table), and `SQSB` borrows a whole BayCAP method only for the first part and for $\hat C$. The quantifiers become classes of their own: a base `BayesianQuantifier` (input classifier, cross-fitted validation outputs, NUTS, diagnostics, `val_ece_`; `fit(val, posteriors)`, `outputs(X, posteriors)`, `sample_prevalences(outputs)`, `last_nuts_diagnostics()`) and two subclasses, `BayesKDEy` (kernel densities; parameters `kernel`, `bandwidth`, `bandwidth_grid`, `shrinkage`) and `BayesMAPLS` (QuaPy's `BayesianMAPLS` model), named so as not to clash with QuaPy's classes. `BayCAPKDEy` and `BayCAPMAPLS` build their quantifier from their own hyperparameters, passed through, and keep $\hat C$, the pushforward and the summaries; their constructors do not change, so neither do the factory entries of `main`. `c_prior="hierarchical-f"` (E3.2), which samples $(\pi,C)$ jointly, stays in `BayCAPKDEy` and reuses the outputs and kernel densities of its `BayesKDEy`. `SQSB` takes the hyperparameters of `BayesKDEy` (`bandwidth` included), builds its own `BayesKDEy` and measures $\hat C$ itself for its summaries; it no longer accepts a BayCAP method as quantifier. A golden test, written before the restructure on a small seeded dataset, records the posterior draws, tables and summaries of `BayCAPKDEy` (fixed and `hierarchical-f`, inputs $h$ and MLP, both pushforwards), `BayCAPMAPLS` and `SQSB`, and the restructured classes must reproduce them exactly (same MLP seed, same `PRNGKey(random_state)` of NUTS, same densities, and for `SQSB` the same seeds of the quantifier and of the bags), so that the stored results stay valid.
  - **Step 2, the selection.** `bandwidth="loo"` and `bandwidth_grid` in `BayesKDEy` (with the Gaussian kernel only, and in `BayCAPKDEy` with `c_prior="fixed"` only; an error otherwise), the selection and the per-class densities, the selected bandwidths stored (`bandwidths_`); numeric bandwidths and `"scott"`/`"silverman"` unchanged; `bandwidth` passed through by `BayCAPKDEy` and `SQSB`, whose summaries report the selected bandwidths;
  - `experiments/methods.py`: the factory entries `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-loo` and `SQS-B-loo`, excluded from the tables, ranks and default plots of `main`; per bag, the selected $\hat b_j$, the class sizes and the edge flags;
  - a table module and a plot module for E7.1 (summary table, per-pair table, $\hat b_j$ against $n_j$ per dataset, Winkler difference against the median of $\log_{10}(\hat b_j/0.1)$); `Makefile` targets;
  - tests: the closed-form leave-one-out log-likelihood equals the brute-force one on a small set; the corrected density integrates to $1$ on the simplex surface for $K=2,3$ (data far from the edges); the selection finds a known optimum on a toy set and returns $0.01$ when all outputs of a class are duplicated; with a numeric bandwidth the log-densities and the posterior draws are identical to the current ones; `SQS-B-loo` and `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-loo` share their prevalence draws; §II.12 updated (the methods in `src/uncawe/methods/` that should move to `cap-ml`).
- **Depends on:** none (WP-8 and WP-18, which added `BayCAPKDEy` and `SQSB`, are done).
- **Comparability:** the stored results of `main` are unchanged (the shared-bandwidth path is not modified); the two new methods add rows to `main` with the seeds of their stored counterparts, so they differ from them only through the bandwidths. Cost: one run of `main` on `bcuda` with `JAX=cpu` for the two methods, $2\times75\times100$ NUTS runs, as `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` and `SQS-B`; the selection costs $O(n_j^2)$ per class (distances computed in chunks), negligible against NUTS.
- **Deviations from Part V:** none expected.
- **Done when:** the code and tests are merged; the pilot (`mammographic` and `wine-quality` with the five classifiers, $10$ pairs; user's decision of 2026-11-19) and the full run are done with stored results.
- **Commands:**
  - pilot: `make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-loo SQS-B-loo --datasets mammographic wine-quality"` (the five classifiers, $10$ pairs);
  - full run: `make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-loo SQS-B-loo"` (the cells already stored are skipped, so the pilot cells are reused);
  - tables: `make run-tables-kdey-loo ARGS=--tables` (CSVs of pairs, summary and classes, fragments and documents; `--metrics` narrows the metrics), PDFs with `make run-tables-kdey-loo WHERE=local ARGS=--pdf` after `make pull`;
  - plots: `make run-plot-kdey-loo` (bandwidths per class and effect on vanilla accuracy; `ARGS="--metrics macro-F1 macro-K"` for the other metrics).
- **Log:** 2026-11-19: card added; E7.1 decided with per-class bandwidths, no oracle reference, the grid $\{10^{-2+0.1k}\}_{k=0}^{20}$, and the bandwidth as a hyperparameter of `BayCAPKDEy` (a number or `"loo"`) with `SQSB` on BayesKDEy itself (user's decisions). 2026-11-19: the restructure of Step 1 added as the first step of the card (`BayesianQuantifier`, `BayesKDEy`, `BayesMAPLS`, used by `BayCAPKDEy`, `BayCAPMAPLS` and `SQSB`, with `bandwidth` a hyperparameter of `SQSB` too), and the pilot set to the $10$ pairs of `mammographic` and `wine-quality` (user's decisions). 2026-11-19: started on branch `wp-25/kdey-loo-bandwidth`. Step 1: frozen copies of `baycap_quantifier`, `baycap_kdey`, `baycap_mapls` and `sqs_b` at `devel` `40166c8` in `tests/_reference_wp25/` (the golden test takes the form of a live comparison with these copies, as `tests/_reference_baycap_fixed.py` for WP-13, rather than stored arrays, so that it does not depend on the versions of JAX and scikit-learn) and `tests/test_wp25_restructure.py`, written before the restructure; `methods/bayesian_quantifier.py` (new), `methods/bayes_kdey.py` (new; `kdey_model` and `_KernelDensities` moved from `baycap_kdey.py`), `methods/bayes_mapls.py` (new; the model of BayesianMAPLS moved from `baycap_mapls.py`), `QuantifierBayCAP` built on a quantifier (`_make_quantifier`, `quantifier_`, properties `mlp_`, `val_outputs_`, `val_ece_`, `quantifier_classes_`, `kde_`, `train_prevalence_` for the code and tests that read them), `SQSB` with the hyperparameters of `BayesKDEy` and a single `random_state` for NUTS, the MLP and the bags (deviation from the card, which kept two seeds: in `main` the two seeds were both the experiment seed, so the results are unchanged, and the golden test uses equal seeds); `experiments/methods.py:sqs_b`; tests updated to the new API (`test_sqs_b.py`, `test_methods.py`, `test_baycap_kdey_hf.py`). The golden test passes. Step 2: `bandwidth="loo"` in `BayesKDEy` (`loo_log_likelihood`, `simplex_log_density`, `_SelectedKernelDensities`, `LOO_GRID`, `LOO_SUMMARIES`), passed through by `BayCAPKDEy` and `SQSB`; `LOO_VARIANTS`, `loo_method` and `fixed_c_method(..., bandwidth=)` in `experiments/methods.py`; labels and colours of the two methods; `tables/kdey_loo.py`, `plots/kdey_loo.py`, `Makefile` targets `run-tables-kdey-loo` and `run-plot-kdey-loo`; tests `tests/test_bayes_kdey_loo.py` (closed form against brute force, normalisation on the simplex for $K=2,3$, selection, duplicates at the lower edge, class of one output, constant difference from QuaPy's densities with one bandwidth, draws shared by the two methods) and `tests/test_kdey_loo_tables.py`. The `n_val` of the tables is read from the stored class sizes, not from the pretrain files. Unit tests: $497$, all passing. 2026-11-19: pilot run on `bcuda` at the user's request (`make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-loo SQS-B-loo --datasets mammographic wine-quality"`, commit `e4ad02c`, JAX on CPU, 13:20–13:27): the $10$ pairs and the three metrics of both methods stored under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main/`, exit code $0$, no failed cell; time per bag about $1.6$–$2.5$ s, fit $0.8$–$9.4$ s (the leave-one-out selection included). Tables and plots of the pilot generated on `bcuda` at the user's request (commit `72e430a`; `make run-tables-kdey-loo ARGS=--tables`, `make run-plot-kdey-loo`), pulled with `make pull`, PDFs generated locally: on the $10$ pairs the selection gives median bandwidths $0.025$ (`wine-quality`) and $0.035$ (`mammographic`), none at an edge of the grid, the same for the five classifiers (the selection depends only on $V$ and the MLP), and both LOO methods have a higher mean Winkler score than the stored ones on every metric (pilot only, see the conversation of 2026-11-19). In the bandwidth plot the tick labels of `mammographic` overlap and the grid of four columns leaves empty panels with two datasets (cosmetic, to fix).
