### WP-23 E5.3: selecting $\tau$ per pair on validation bags for BayCAP-Hf and BayCAP-KDEy (new study `tau_select`)

- **Kind / status:** experiment / Done
- **Source:** [[ext5_baycap_priors_tempering]] (E5.3, the former candidate E5.c2)
- **Goal:** choose the temperature $\tau$ of `BayesCAP-Hf-real` (BayCAP with the Hf prior on $C$, realised pushforward, tempered jointly on the validation counts and the bag counts) and of `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` (BayesKDEy with an MLP input and a fixed $\hat C$, realised pushforward, tempered on the bag term) for each (dataset, classifier) pair, using no label outside $V$, and test whether it beats $\tau=1$. E5.2 (WP-20) found that a single $\tau$ chosen on the other datasets helps only the few datasets whose interval is displaced, and that the oracle-best $\tau$ varies across pairs. For each of the $15$ datasets of `main`, $V$ is split per class into $V_{\text{fit}}$ and $V_{\text{cal}}$, half each (stratified, seeded per dataset, the same split for its five classifiers); both methods are refitted on $V_{\text{fit}}$ ($h$ is not retrained) and run at $\tau\in\{0.5,1,2,4,8\}$ on $S=50$ calibration bags per pair, of size $1000$, at prevalences uniform on the simplex, drawn with replacement from $V_{\text{cal}}$; $\tau_{\text{cal}}$ is, per pair, method and metric, the value with the lowest mean Winkler score on the calibration bags (scored against their realised tables, from the labels of $V_{\text{cal}}$). The deployed method is fitted on the full $V$ (user's decision of 2026-11-15): its scores on the $100$ test bags of `main` at $\tau_{\text{cal}}$ are read from the stored results of `main` and E5.2, so no test run is needed. Decision rule (user's decision of 2026-11-15): per method, for vanilla accuracy, the selection is retained if $\tau_{\text{cal}}$ lowers the per-dataset mean Winkler score (mean over the five pairs) with respect to $\tau=1$, by a one-sided paired Wilcoxon signed-rank test over the $15$ datasets with $p<0.05$; otherwise $\tau=1$ is kept. Reported for comparison: the leave-one-dataset-out $\tau$ of E5.2; as a reference, the oracle-best $\tau$ of each pair. Reading (derived in §T.9 of the extension): with the deployed method fitted on the full $V$, the recall error on the calibration bags is uncorrelated with that of the deployed method on the test bags, so $\tau_{\text{cal}}$ reflects the expected size of the error from $V$ and systematic biases, not the actual displacement of a pair, and it has twice the standard deviation, so $\tau_{\text{cal}}$ is expected to be too large. Diagnostics: $\tau_{\text{cal}}$ against the oracle-best $\tau$ (cross-tabulation); the Spearman correlation across pairs between $\tau_{\text{cal}}$ and $\hat\sigma_{e_2}=\sqrt{\frac{2}{K(K+1)}\sum_j\hat d_j(1-\hat d_j)/n^s_j}$ (recalls $\hat d_j$ and class sizes $n^s_j$ of $h$ on the full $V$), and its ratio to the half-amplitude at $\tau=1$; the Spearman correlation between the gain on the calibration bags and the gain on the test bags; the NUTS diagnostics of the calibration runs of BayCAP-Hf.
- **Estimand:** the realised table of each test bag (`true_cts`) and of each calibration bag.
- **Scope:**
  - new `experiments/tau_select.py`: the seeded stratified split of $V$ (the subsampling helper of WP-21 may be shared, if it exists by then); the calibration bags; the two methods built as in `main` and E5.2, refitted on $V_{\text{fit}}$ at the five values of $\tau$, with `jax.clear_caches()` after each task; per calibration bag the measures of `main`, the NUTS diagnostics of BayCAP-Hf, and the split seed, bag seed, `config_hash` and `schema_version`; output under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tau_select/`;
  - new table module: $\tau_{\text{cal}}$ per pair, method and metric; on the test bags (stored results of `main` and E5.2), the means over the pairs of AE, coverage, amplitude and Winkler score at $\tau=1$, $\tau_{\text{cal}}$, the leave-one-dataset-out $\tau$ and the oracle-best $\tau$ (reference), also split by $K$, the test of the decision rule; the cross-tabulation; $\hat\sigma_{e_2}$ and the correlations;
  - new plot module: $\tau_{\text{cal}}$ against $\hat\sigma_{e_2}$, the gain on the calibration bags against the gain on the test bags, one point per pair, coloured by $K$;
  - `Makefile` targets; tests: the split is stratified, disjoint, covers $V$ and is reproducible; the calibration bags contain only instances of $V_{\text{cal}}$; $\tau_{\text{cal}}$ does not change when the test scores change; $\hat\sigma_{e_2}$ on a hand-computed example; the table and the plots on a synthetic result, rendering the figures and building the LaTeX;
  - this document: Part II when done (and §II.12 if a method changes; none is expected).
- **Depends on:** none (the stored results of `main` and E5.2, WP-20, done).
- **Comparability:** no stored result changes; the calibration runs are not comparable with `main` (methods fitted on half of $V$, bags from the other half), but the scores on the test bags are those of `main` and E5.2 and directly comparable with them. Cost: $75$ pairs $\times$ $2$ methods $\times$ $5$ values of $\tau$ $\times$ $50$ calibration bags ($37{,}500$ NUTS runs, about $60\%$ of E5.2), mostly BayCAP-Hf.
- **Deviations from Part V:** none from the rules. Choices made in the code, which the card left open: the calibration bags are drawn with QuaPy's UPP on $V_{\text{cal}}$, the protocol of the test bags of `main` (`pretrain/data.py:with_test_data`; user's decision of 2026-11-16, replacing a first version with its own $\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1)$ sampler): the prevalences are uniform on the simplex, the class counts are $\operatorname{round}(1000\pi_j)$ with the remainder assigned to classes drawn with probabilities $\pi$, and the instances of each class are drawn with replacement, as in `main` (QuaPy's `sampling_index` always draws with replacement); the protocol is seeded by a seed of the experiment seed and the dataset (`calibration_seed`), so the bags are the same for every classifier, method and $\tau$. QuaPy's `temp_seed` writes the seed of a protocol into `qp.environ["_R_SEED"]` and does not restore it, which would change the experiment seed and with it the default `random_state` of the methods built after the bags; `calibration_bags` restores it (tested). The split puts $\lfloor n_j/2\rfloor$ instances of class $j$ in $V_{\text{cal}}$ and the other $\lceil n_j/2\rceil$ in $V_{\text{fit}}$, with a generator seeded by the experiment seed and the dataset (a class with fewer than $2$ instances in $V$ is an error; the smallest class of the $15$ datasets has about $18$). The recalls of $h$ on the full $V$ and its class counts are stored with every calibration row, so $\hat\sigma_{e_2}$ is computed by the table module without loading the pretrain files. The loaders of `tables/tempering.py` (`load_method`, `load_all`, `pair_table`) take the result folder as a parameter (default `main`), so that the calibration runs are read and scored by the same code as the test runs; the outputs of `tables/tempering.py` do not change.
- **Done when:** the code and tests are merged; the pilot (`mammographic`, `poker_hand`, `letter`, with the five classifiers) and the full run are done with stored calibration results.
- **Commands:** pilot `make run-tau-select ARGS="--pilot"` ($15$ pairs, $150$ tasks); full run `make run-tau-select` (the $75$ pairs, $750$ tasks, the stored ones skipped); tables `make run-tables-tau-select ARGS=--tables` (on `bcuda`) and `make run-tables-tau-select WHERE=local ARGS=--pdf`; plots `make run-plot-tau-select` (vanilla accuracy; `ARGS="--metrics vanilla_accuracy macro-F1 macro-K"` for all three).
- **Log:** 2026-11-15: card added (user's decision). 2026-11-15: started on `wp-23/tau-select`; `experiments/tau_select.py` (split of $V$, calibration bags, tasks per pair, method and $\tau$), `tables/tau_select.py` ($\tau_{\text{cal}}$, comparison with $\tau=1$, $\tau_{-d}$ and the oracle on the stored test runs, decision rule, cross-tabulation, $\hat\sigma_{e_2}$, correlations, NUTS diagnostics), `plots/tau_select.py` (`sigma`, `gain`), `Makefile` targets, `tests/test_tau_select.py`; unit tests pass; pilot not run. 2026-11-16: the calibration bags drawn with QuaPy's UPP instead of the study's own sampler (user's decision), with the experiment seed restored after the protocol; the columns `bag_seed` and `requested_prev` replaced by `calibration_seed` and `bag_prev`; unit tests pass; pilot not run. 2026-11-16: full run on `bcuda` at commit `d7bce3b` (user's request; no separate pilot, since the full run includes the three pilot datasets): $750$ tasks ($15$ datasets $\times$ $5$ classifiers $\times$ $2$ methods $\times$ $5$ values of $\tau$), from 01:09:45 to 03:46:55, exit $0$, no errors, peak memory about $114$ GB with `BAYES_N_JOBS=16`; $2250$ result files (one per task and metric), $756$ MB, in `output/uncawe/tau_select/classic/`; tables and plots not yet generated. 2026-11-16: tables (`--tables`) and plots (all three metrics) generated on `bcuda` at commit `a674a2b`, pulled, and the PDFs built locally; not yet analysed. 2026-11-16: per-pair LaTeX tables added for E5.3 (`tables/tau_select.py`, `pairs`: per pair, $K$, $\hat\sigma_{e_2}$, $\tau_{\text{cal}}$, $\tau_{-d}$ and the oracle-best $\tau$, the test Winkler score and coverage at each, the calibration Winkler score at $\tau=1$ and $\tau_{\text{cal}}$) and for E5.2 (`tables/tempering.py`, `pairs`), user's request; the E5.2 table is on this branch because `tables/tempering.py` is already changed here (§II.25 updated); unit tests pass; tables not regenerated. 2026-11-16: tables generated on `bcuda` from `378b654`, PDFs built locally. 2026-11-16: in both per-pair tables the test Winkler scores and coverages are coloured per row and group from the worst (red) to the best value (green, bold), with the scale of the tables of E3.1 (`tempering.score_styles`, which reuses `baycap_fixed._score_cell_styles`; coverage: closest to $0.95$); in E5.3, $\tau_{\text{cal}}$ and $\tau_{-d}$ are highlighted where they equal the oracle-best $\tau$ (user's request); the documents load `xcolor` with the `table` option; unit tests pass. 2026-11-16: tables regenerated on `bcuda` from `8b3853f`, PDFs built locally. 2026-11-16: in the per-pair table of E5.3 the oracle columns are left out of the colour scale (user's request; E5.2 has no oracle score column); unit tests pass. 2026-11-16: E5.3 tables regenerated on `bcuda` from `6069ab2`, PDFs built locally. 2026-11-16: E5.3 analysed in the extension document (`65c7a2c`): the selection per pair is not retained for either method ($p=0.66$ for BayCAP-Hf, $0.23$ for BayCAP-KDEy), $\tau=1$ kept. 2026-11-16: merged into `devel` (`cc8ab5f`, user's request) and closed: Part II §II.27, this entry moved to §III.3.
- **Outcome:** merged into `devel` on 2026-11-16 (`cc8ab5f`), after the user's review, without a rebase (`devel` had not moved since the branch started). Part II: §II.27 (the code, the run and the findings). Deviations from **Done when:** no separate pilot (user's request): the full run includes the three pilot datasets. Decisions taken during the card and not in its first Scope: the calibration bags drawn with QuaPy's UPP (user's decision, under **Deviations from Part V**); per-pair LaTeX tables for E5.3 and, on this branch, for E5.2 (`tables/tempering.py`, §II.25), with the test Winkler scores and coverages coloured per row from the worst to the best value as in the tables of E3.1, the oracle-best $\tau$ left out of the colour scale and, in E5.3, $\tau_{\text{cal}}$ and $\tau_{-d}$ highlighted where they equal it (user's requests); the subsampling helper of WP-21 was not shared, since the split of E5.3 is a half per class, not a nested subsample. Problems: QuaPy's `temp_seed` leaves the seed of a protocol in `qp.environ["_R_SEED"]`; `calibration_bags` restores it (tested). Unrelated changes carried by the branch: the commits `6069ab2` and `2c3f477` also contain the plots of the total error of the study `target_size` (`plots/target_size.py`, `--figures v-total u-total`, `tests/test_target_size.py`, §II.26, and the Representation of E4.2a and E4.2b in [[ext4_uncertainty_budget]]; user's request of 2026-11-16, after WP-22), which were in the working tree when those commits were made; they belong to no card and are on `devel` through this merge. Follow-ups (not scheduled): why the calibration error, which has twice the variance of the deployed error (§T.9 of the extension), does not show for BayCAP-Hf (a possible reason, not checked: the method fitted on half of $V$ has a wider posterior on the recalls); the decision leaves $\tau=1$ for both methods, so no change to `main`.

### WP-22 E4.2a: the error split across $\lvert U\rvert$ with the full $V$ (study `target_size`)

- **Kind / status:** experiment / Done
- **Source:** [[ext4_uncertainty_budget]] (E4.2a, §T.3, §T.4)
- **Goal:** measure how the parts $e_1$, $e_2$, $e_3$ of the error (defined in WP-21) change with the size $\lvert U\rvert$ of the target bag when $V$ is held fixed (the full one), for `BayesCAP-Hf-real` and `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real`, to see which part dominates on small and on large bags. The bag parts should shrink as the bag grows, including the bag part of $e_2$ of BayCAP-KDEy: its $\hat C$ is fixed at the rates of $h$ on $V$, but the realised-table predictive reweights the recalls read from its table by $w_i=q_i/p_i$, the observed over the expected share of the predictions of $h$ in class $\omega_i$, which fluctuates with the bag. The fixed part of $e_2$ of BayCAP-KDEy should move with $\lvert U\rvert$ only through the error of its estimated prevalence, which also changes $w$ (so $e_1$ and $e_2$ move together, as on `wine-quality` with RF in WP-21); that of BayCAP-Hf, whose $C$ is also updated by the bag, may move for both reasons. (Reworded on 2026-11-15 at the user's request, after the correction of §T.3 of [[ext4_uncertainty_budget]] by WP-21; the card said before that the fixed part of $e_2$ of BayCAP-KDEy, whose recalls come from $V$ alone, should not move.) Grid: $\lvert U\rvert\in\{100,300,1000,3000,10000\}$ (bags larger than the pool repeat instances); the datasets, classifiers, prevalences and $R$ of WP-21; a cell is (prevalence, $\lvert U\rvert$). Measures: the RMS over the cells of the fixed parts and over the bags of the bag parts, and their fitted log–log slope against $\lvert U\rvert$ (descriptive).
- **Estimand:** the realised table of each bag (`true_cts`), as in WP-21.
- **Scope:**
  - `experiments/target_size.py` (WP-21): the grid over $\lvert U\rvert$ with the full $V$; the cell at $\lvert U\rvert=1000$ is reused from WP-21, not run again;
  - table: per method and dataset, the RMS of the fixed and bag parts of $e_1$, $e_2$, $e_3$ at each $\lvert U\rvert$;
  - plot: RMS of each part against $\lvert U\rvert$ (log–log), one panel per method, one line per dataset (LR), solid for the fixed part and dashed for the bag part;
  - `Makefile` targets; tests: the grid builds the requested sizes; bags larger than the pool repeat instances; the table and the plot on a synthetic result.
- **Depends on:** WP-21 (the study, the sampler, the split within a cell, $R$).
- **Comparability:** as WP-21; same output directory, rows distinguished by $\lvert U\rvert$. Cost: $6\times2\times4\times3\times R=4\,320$ new NUTS runs per method with $R=30$. The likelihood of BayCAP-Hf uses the counts of the bag, so its cost should barely depend on $\lvert U\rvert$; that of BayCAP-KDEy has one term per instance of the bag, so its cost should grow about linearly with $\lvert U\rvert$, about $10$ times at $\lvert U\rvert=10000$; the pilot measures both.
- **Deviations from Part V:** those of WP-21, which the study keeps (results in one folder per ($\lvert U\rvert$, level, seed), `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/target_size/u<|U|>/v1/s0/...` for this card; all the tasks in one BayCAP pool; posteriors of $h$ indexed in the stored ones); the bag size `SAMPLE_SIZE` of the shared constants (Part V.1) is the varied factor, so the grid `U_SIZES` replaces it, as that rule allows. No other.
- **Done when:** the code and tests are merged; the pilot (one dataset, every $\lvert U\rvert$, to measure the cost) and the full run are done with stored results.
- **Commands:** pilot (proposal: `letter`, the largest $K$ and so the costliest per bag, LR): `make run-target-size ARGS="--run --u-grid --datasets letter --classifiers LR"`; full: `make run-target-size ARGS="--run --u-grid"` (the cells of the pilot and those at $\lvert U\rvert=1000$ of WP-21 exist and are skipped); tables: `make run-tables-target-size ARGS=--tables` (the table of E4.2a, `target_size_sizes_v1`, is written when the full $V$ has two or more sizes; `WHERE=local ARGS=--pdf` to compile); plot: `make run-plot-target-size ARGS="--figures u-size"` (`--classifier`, default LR); cost per bag: `target_size_cost.csv` of the tables; results merged with `make pull-results RESULTS=target_size`.
- **Log:** 2026-11-11: card added (user's decision). 2026-11-15: started on `wp-22/target-size-u`. Code and tests done: `experiments/target_size.py` (`U_SIZES`, `--u-grid`, which runs the sizes $100$, $300$, $1000$, $3000$, $10000$ with the full $V$ and excludes `--sizes` and `--levels`; positive sizes checked; seeds, bags and schema unchanged, so `schema_version` stays $1$ and the stored cells of WP-21 at $\lvert U\rvert=1000$ are reused), `tables/target_size.py` (`size_slope_table`, `target_size_size_slopes.csv`: per method and pair, the log–log slopes against $\lvert U\rvert$ of the RMS of the fixed and of the bag parts; `cost_table`, `target_size_cost.csv`: the mean fitting time and NUTS time per bag of each method, pair, size and level; `sizes_frame`, the table of E4.2a; the diagnostics are now per size too; `slope_table` keeps only the groups with two or more levels; each LaTeX table is written only when its data exist), `plots/target_size.py` (`--figures u-size`; a figure without data is skipped with a message), `tests/test_target_size.py` (the grid builds the requested sizes and skips the stored cells; bags larger than the pool repeat instances; the slopes, the table, the cost, the diagnostics per size and the plot on a synthetic result; the CSVs, the LaTeX documents and the figures written); unit tests: $421$ OK. Effect on the outputs of WP-21 (none on stored results): `target_size_diagnostics.csv` gains the column `bag_size` (same counts at $\lvert U\rvert=1000$); `target_size_slopes.csv` is unchanged on the results of WP-21 (every group there has two or more levels). 2026-11-15: the pilot is skipped (user's decision, a deviation from the rule "Pilot first" of Part V.1 and from **Done when:**), so the cost per bag at each $\lvert U\rvert$ is measured on the full run (`target_size_cost.csv`); the full run is started (`make run-target-size ARGS="--run --u-grid"`). 2026-11-15: full run on `bcuda` from `7e55349` (`make run-target-size ARGS="--run --u-grid"`), 19:42–20:22, exit code 0: 96 of 96 tasks done (2 methods, 6 datasets, 2 classifiers, $\lvert U\rvert\in\{100,300,3000,10000\}$, full $V$; the cells at $\lvert U\rvert=1000$ of WP-21 skipped), no error in the experiment log; results in `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/target_size/u<|U|>/v1/s0/`. NUTS time per bag (LR, from the experiment log) at $\lvert U\rvert=100$ and $10000$: BayCAP-KDEy from $1.3$–$1.7$ s to $1.6$ s (`mammographic`) – $13.6$ s (`letter`), far less than the tenfold growth expected (the likelihood per instance is a small part of the cost below $\lvert U\rvert=1000$); BayCAP-Hf from $2.7$–$9.7$ s to $3.0$–$11.4$ s, almost flat, as expected. 2026-11-15: tables (`make run-tables-target-size ARGS=--tables`) and plots (`make run-plot-target-size ARGS="--figures u-size v-size --classifier LR|RF"`) generated on `bcuda` from `c0a81a3`, pulled to the local machine, PDFs compiled locally (`WHERE=local ARGS=--pdf`). Checks: $684$ cells ($342$ per method), each with $R=30$ and the same `config_hash`; identity within $1.1\cdot10^{-15}$; no bag dropped; every shared bag has the same realised table in all its cells; $2$ bags of BayCAP-KDEy have an empty estimated column (`letter`, RF, $\lvert U\rvert=100$, near-vertex prevalence, where each of the $25$ other classes expects $0.4$ instances). 2026-11-15: Analysis of E4.2a written in [[ext4_uncertainty_budget]] (numbers from the CSVs of `c0a81a3`); [[extensions]] summary updated. Findings: the bag parts shrink as $1/\sqrt{\lvert U\rvert}$ (median slopes about $-0.5$), the bag part of $e_2$ of BayCAP-KDEy included, as the Goal predicts; the fixed parts barely move (median slopes $-0.02$ to $-0.10$), so the bag part of the total exceeds the fixed part in $4$–$6$ of $12$ pairs at $\lvert U\rvert=100$ and in none at $10000$; exceptions: `letter` (small-bag effect on the fixed part of $e_2$), `wine-quality` RF (BayCAP-KDEy: $e_1$ and $e_2$ grow in opposite directions with the bag, the total falls), `wine-quality` LR (the error of BayCAP-Hf grows with the bag).
- **Outcome:** merged into `devel` on 2026-11-15 (`7cc8d02`), after the user's review, without a rebase (`devel` had not moved since the branch started). Part II: §II.26 (the code, the run and the findings of E4.2a, beside those of E4.2b). Deviations from **Done when:** no pilot (user's decision), so the cost per bag was measured on the full run: BayCAP-KDEy grows at most $7.9$ times from $\lvert U\rvert=100$ to $10000$, less than the tenfold growth the card expected, and BayCAP-Hf $1.45$ times. Decisions taken during the card and not in its first Scope: the Goal was reworded before the run after the correction of §T.3 (BayCAP-KDEy has a bag part of $e_2$); `--u-grid` cannot be combined with `--sizes` or `--levels`; the results of the new sizes stay on `bcuda` (only the tables and plots were pulled). Additions to the Scope: `target_size_cost.csv` and the NUTS diagnostics per size, so that the cost and the divergences per $\lvert U\rvert$ come from the repository; the figure `u-size` also for RF. Problems: `make pull` with `PULL_DIRS` copied the tables and plots into the results folder `target_size/` instead of `tables/` and `plots/`; the misplaced files were removed by hand and the right folders synced (no result was touched); BayCAP-Hf diverges on $41$–$45\%$ of the bags at every size, as in `main`, and its values carry this caveat, the strongest on `wine-quality` with LR, where every bag diverges. Follow-ups, not acted on and not added as candidates: the hypotheses of the Analysis (the bag alone cannot identify $C$ and $\pi$ together, so the offset of $e_2$ does not shrink with $\lvert U\rvert$ for BayCAP-Hf; the biased limit of the prevalence of BayCAP-Hf on `wine-quality` with LR) are untested; the split of the population table of BayCAP-KDEy, already a follow-up of WP-21, would also separate the coupling of $e_1$ and $e_2$ on `wine-quality` with RF; the interaction of the two sizes is candidate E4.c13.

### WP-21 E4.2b: the error split across $\lvert V\rvert$, with seeded subsamples of $V$, at $\lvert U\rvert=1000$ (new study `target_size`)

- **Kind / status:** experiment / Done
- **Source:** [[ext4_uncertainty_budget]] (E4.2b, §T.4)
- **Goal:** measure how the error that comes from the validation set $V$ grows as $V$ shrinks, for `BayesCAP-Hf-real` (BayCAP with the Hf prior on $C$, realised pushforward) and `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` (BayesKDEy with an MLP input and a fixed $\hat C$, realised pushforward), with the settings of `main`. It tests the hypothesis that the offset of the recall part on the binary datasets of `main` (positive and shared by every method, largest on `mammographic`, $\lvert V\rvert=291$, about $+0.05$) comes from the finite $V$. The error of each bag splits exactly as $e_1+e_2+e_3$, with $e_1=\sum_j(\tilde\pi_j-\pi^Q_j)\tilde d_j$ (prevalences), $e_2=\sum_j\pi^Q_j(\tilde d_j-d^{\text{pool}}_j)$ (recalls against the recalls of $h$ on the test pool) and $e_3=\sum_j\pi^Q_j(d^{\text{pool}}_j-c_j/n_j)$ (luck of the bag), as in `error_split.py`. A cell is (prevalence, $\lvert V\rvert$ level, $V$ seed) with $R$ bags; within a cell each part splits into a fixed part (its mean over the bags) and a bag part (the deviation of each bag). Grid:
  - datasets: `mammographic` ($K=2$, $\lvert V\rvert=291$, the offset case), `german` ($K=2$, $350$, ill-conditioned, offset of $e_2$ for BayCAP-Hf only), `spambase` ($K=2$, $1610$, binary control without offset, whose subsamples reach the size of the small binary validation sets), `wine-quality` ($K=5$, $2262$, ill-conditioned, $e_1$ dominates), `dry-bean` ($K=7$, $4764$, well-conditioned, near the floor), `letter` ($K=26$, $7000$, large $K$; chosen over `isolet`, where BayCAP-Hf diverges, and `poker_hand`, which depends on FIX-8); classifiers LR and RF (user's decision of 2026-11-11);
  - $\lvert V\rvert$ levels: the full $V$ and stratified subsamples of $1/4$ and $1/16$, each subsampled level only on the datasets where every class keeps at least $10$ validation instances (user's decision of 2026-11-15: the threshold applies to $1/4$ too, not only to $1/16$); nested ($V_{1/16}\subset V_{1/4}\subset V$) and seeded; the methods are refitted on each subsample, $h$ is not retrained, and the split $L/V$ is unchanged;
  - $V$ seeds: $S_V$ per subsampled level (the full $V$ has one);
  - prevalence: three fixed vectors per dataset: uniform $\mathbf 1/K$, one skewed vector (a seeded draw from $\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1)$), one near-vertex vector ($0.9$ on the rarest validation class, the rest uniform);
  - bags: $R$ per cell, drawn with replacement from the test pool, $\lvert U\rvert=1000$, with the class counts $\lvert U\rvert\pi_j$ rounded by largest remainder, the same for every bag of a prevalence; the bags of a prevalence and size are the same for every $\lvert V\rvert$ level and $V$ seed (user's decision of 2026-11-15), so that $e_3$ is the same across the $V$ cells and the spread over the seeds of the fixed parts carries no bag-to-bag noise;
  - $R$ and $S_V$ fixed by the pilot (proposal: $R=30$, $S_V=3$), and used also by WP-22; the pilot also checks the per-class counts of the subsamples (`wine-quality` has rare classes; `letter` has about $17$ instances per class at $1/16$).
- **Estimand:** the realised table of each bag (`true_cts`), for both methods; the split uses the true prevalence of the bag and the labels of the pool (an analysis, not a method).
- **Scope:**
  - new `experiments/target_size.py`: the grid (dataset, classifier, method, prevalence, $\lvert U\rvert$, $\lvert V\rvert$ level, $V$ seed); a sampler of bags with fixed prevalence at any $\lvert U\rvert$, drawn with replacement from the pool, seeded per bag from the dataset, prevalence, $\lvert U\rvert$ and bag index (not from the $\lvert V\rvert$ level and $V$ seed, user's decision of 2026-11-15); nested seeded stratified subsampling of $V$; the two methods built as in `main` and refitted on each subsample; output in `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/target_size/`, every row with $\lvert U\rvert$, $\lvert V\rvert$ level, $V$ seed, prevalence, bag seed, `config_hash` and `schema_version`;
  - `error_split.py`: the fixed and bag parts of a cell; the mean and standard deviation over the $V$ seeds;
  - new table module: per method, dataset and $\lvert V\rvert$ level, the RMS of the fixed and bag parts of $e_1$, $e_2$, $e_3$, and the mean and standard deviation over the seeds of the fixed part of $e_2$, next to its value at the full $V$;
  - new plot module: RMS of the fixed part of each part against $\lvert V\rvert$ (log–log), one panel per method, one line per dataset (LR), one point per seed and the line through the means over the seeds;
  - `Makefile` targets; tests: the bags have the requested class counts and size; the subsamples are nested, stratified and reproducible for a seed; the bag parts of a cell sum to $0$; over many subsamples the mean of a count-based recall tends to its value on the full $V$; the table and the plot on a synthetic result;
  - this document: Part II (and §II.12 if a method changes; none is expected).
- **Depends on:** none (FIX-8 is not needed: `poker_hand` is not in the grid).
- **Comparability:** new study; `main` and its outputs are unchanged. Its bags have fixed prevalences instead of APP ones, so the cell with the full $V$ is comparable with `main` in distribution, not bag by bag. Cost with $R=30$, $S_V=3$: $6\times2\times3\times(1+S_V)\times R=4\,320$ NUTS runs per method for the full $V$ and the $1/4$ level, plus $2\times3\times S_V\times R=540$ per method for each dataset with the $1/16$ level (corrected on 2026-11-15 from $1\,080$); with the $1/16$ level on three datasets (counts check of 2026-11-15), $5\,940$ runs per method in $132$ tasks of $90$ bags, at the cost per run of `main` ($\lvert U\rvert=1000$); the cells with the full $V$ are reused by WP-22.
- **Deviations from Part V:** no pilot (user's decision of 2026-11-15): $R$ and $S_V$ are the proposals $30$ and $3$. Results go to `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/target_size/u<|U|>/<level>/s<seed>/<domain>/<acc_name>/<dataset>/<classifier>/<method>.parquet` (level `v1`, `v1_4` or `v1_16`), one folder per ($\lvert U\rvert$, level, seed) below the layout of Part V.1, as `binform_e51/u<|U|>/`; all the tasks run in one BayCAP pool, so each `ExperimentOutcome` carries its own folder (the new optional field `experiment`, §II.9). The posteriors of $h$ on $V$ and on the bags are indexed in the stored `V_posteriors` and `U_posteriors`, not looked up through `PreTrainedClassifier.predict_proba` as in `main`: the values are the same, the lookup is skipped. The tables are descriptive: no colours, no bold, no $^\dagger$ and no test (Part V.4), as the tables of WP-14.
- **Done when:** the code and tests are merged; the pilot (which fixes $R$ and $S_V$ and checks the per-class counts) and the full run are done with stored results.
- **Commands:** class counts of the subsamples, no method run: `make run-target-size ARGS="--counts"`; pilot: `make run-target-size ARGS="--run --datasets mammographic spambase letter --classifiers LR"` (options `--methods`, `--levels`, `--n-v-seeds`, default $3$, `--n-bags`, default $30$, `--sizes`, default $1000$); full: `make run-target-size ARGS="--run"`; tables: `make run-tables-target-size ARGS=--tables` (`--size`, default $1000$; `WHERE=local ARGS=--pdf` to compile); plot: `make run-plot-target-size` (`--classifier`, default LR, `--size`); results merged with `make pull-results RESULTS=target_size`.
- **Log:** 2026-11-11: card added (user's decision), with the datasets above. 2026-11-15: started on `wp-21/target-size`; two decisions of the user before the code: the bags are shared by every $\lvert V\rvert$ level and $V$ seed of a prevalence and size (the card seeded them by level and seed too), and the threshold of $10$ instances per class applies to both subsampled levels. Code and tests done: `experiments/target_size.py` (with `--counts`, the class counts of the subsamples without any method run), `error_split.py` (`cell_parts`, `fixed_se`, `over_seeds`), `tables/target_size.py` (per-bag, per-cell, per-seed, per-level and slope CSVs, the table of E4.2b, checks of the identity, of the dropped and empty-column bags, of $R$, of `config_hash` and of the shared bags), `plots/target_size.py` (`--figures v-size`), the field `experiment` of `ExperimentOutcome`, the `Makefile` targets, `tests/test_target_size.py`. 2026-11-15: counts check on `bcuda` from `db9a6ab` (`make run-target-size ARGS="--counts"`, no method run; `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/target_size/v_counts.csv`): $\lvert V\rvert$ (smallest class) at $1/4$ / $1/16$: `mammographic` 73 (35) / 18 (9), `german` 87 (26) / 22 (7), `spambase` 403 (159) / 101 (40), `wine-quality` 566 (17) / 142 (4), `dry-bean` 1193 (46) / 298 (11), `letter` 1755 (64) / 437 (16); so $1/4$ runs on all six datasets and $1/16$ only on `spambase`, `dry-bean` and `letter`; the counts are the same for every seed (only the kept instances change). 2026-11-15: the pilot is skipped (user's decision, a deviation from the rule "Pilot first" of Part V.1), so $R=30$ and $S_V=3$ are the proposals of the card, not fixed by a pilot; the full run is started. 2026-11-15: full run on `bcuda` from `598d404` (`make run-target-size ARGS="--run"`), 14:25–15:14, exit code 0: 132 of 132 tasks done (2 methods, 6 datasets, 2 classifiers, the levels and seeds above; the level $1/16$ skipped on `mammographic`, `german` and `wine-quality` as in the counts check), no error in the experiment log; time per bag of NUTS about $9.5$ s for BayCAP-Hf and $2.6$ s for BayCAP-KDEy on `letter`, about $2.7$ s for BayCAP-Hf on `spambase`; results in `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/target_size/`. 2026-11-15: results pulled to the local machine (`make pull-results RESULTS=target_size`); `tables/target_size.py` extended with the recall gap between $V$ and the pool and the subsample standard deviation of §T.4 (`recalls`), the drift of the seed means of $e_2$ (`drift`) and the NUTS diagnostics (`diagnostics`), with tests (`14dcdee`); tables, PDF and plots (LR, RF) generated locally from `14dcdee`; Analysis of E4.2b written in `docs/ext4_uncertainty_budget.md`. Findings: the hypothesis of E4.1 holds as "finite $V$ and finite pool"; the fixed part of $e_2$ grows as $V$ shrinks and drifts at $1/16$; the realised-table predictive makes the recalls of BayCAP-KDEy depend on the bag, against the reading of §T.3. BayCAP-Hf diverges on $44\%$ of the bags, as in `main`; no NUTS failure. 2026-11-15, at the user's request: §T.3 of [[ext4_uncertainty_budget]] corrected for the realised-table predictive (the recalls read from the table of a fixed $\hat C$ are reweighted by $w_i=q_i/p_i$, the observed over the expected share of predictions of $\omega_i$; derivation), with §T.4, the reasoning of E4.2 and E4.2a and the Analysis (`2a48447`); unit tests: $411$ OK; card closed.
- **Outcome:** merged into `devel` on 2026-11-15 (`7fc4dc6`), at the user's request, without a rebase (`devel` had not moved since the branch started). Part II: §II.26 (the study, the run and the findings), §II.20 (the helpers of `error_split.py`), §II.9 (the field `experiment` of `ExperimentOutcome`). Deviations from **Done when:** no pilot (user's decision), so $R=30$ and $S_V=3$ were not fixed by a pilot; the check of the standard errors after the run finds $R=30$ sufficient (standard error of the fixed part of $e_2$ at most $0.0052$), while $S_V=3$ is too few to extrapolate the spread over the seeds to the full $V$. Decisions taken during the card and not in its first Scope: the bags are shared by every $\lvert V\rvert$ level and $V$ seed; the threshold of $10$ instances per class applies to both subsampled levels, so $1/16$ runs on three datasets only; the tables and plots were generated locally after `make pull-results` (the PDF needs `pdflatex`). Additions to the Scope, made for the Analysis so that its numbers come from the repository: the outputs `recalls` (the gap between the recalls of $h$ on $V$ and on the pool, its two-sample standard deviation, and the subsample standard deviation of §T.4), `drift` and `diagnostics` of `tables/target_size.py` (`14dcdee`). Findings that changed a document beyond the Analysis: §T.3 of [[ext4_uncertainty_budget]], which put BayCAP-KDEy among the methods whose recalls come from $V$ alone, was wrong for its realised-table predictive and was corrected (`2a48447`). Problems: none in the run (exit $0$, no NUTS failure); BayCAP-Hf diverges on about $44\%$ of the bags at every level, as in `main`, and its values carry this caveat. Follow-ups, not acted on and not added as candidates: the split of the population table of BayCAP-KDEy, to separate the coupling of $e_1$ and $e_2$ through $w$ from the error of $\hat C$; more $V$ seeds at $1/4$ on `mammographic` and `german`; the goal of WP-22 still says that the fixed part of $e_2$ of BayCAP-KDEy "should not move" with $\lvert U\rvert$, which §T.3 now qualifies (it moves through the error of the prevalence, and its bag part shrinks).

### WP-20 E5.2: tempering BayCAP-Hf and BayCAP-KDEy, $\tau$ chosen by leaving one dataset out

- **Kind / status:** experiment / Done
- **Source:** [[ext5_baycap_priors_tempering]] (E5.2)
- **Goal:** measure whether tempering lowers the Winkler score of `BayesCAP-Hf-real` and `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` when the temperature $\tau$ is chosen on the labelled bags of the other datasets, without the labels of the target dataset (a default tuned on a labelled benchmark: it assumes that the best $\tau$ carries over to a new dataset and that the shift in use resembles the benchmark's). Tempering divides the log-likelihood by $\tau$, which counts the data $\tau$ times less and widens the posterior by about $\sqrt\tau$ without moving its centre. Sweep $\tau\in\{0.5,1,2,4,8\}$ on the protocol of `main` ($75$ pairs, $100$ bags of size $1000$, uniform prior on $\pi^Q$); BayCAP-Hf is tempered jointly, its log-likelihoods of the validation counts and of the predicted counts of the bag both divided by $\tau$ and its priors unchanged; BayCAP-KDEy on the log-likelihood of the bag, its only one. For each of the $15$ datasets $d$ and per method and metric, $\tau_{-d}$ is the value with the lowest mean, over the $70$ pairs of the other $14$ datasets, of the per-pair mean Winkler score, and the pairs of $d$ are scored at $\tau_{-d}$. Decision rule (user's decision of 2026-11-10): for each method and for vanilla accuracy, tempering is retained if $\tau_{-d}$ lowers the per-dataset mean Winkler score (mean over the five pairs of the dataset) with respect to $\tau=1$, by a paired Wilcoxon signed-rank test over the $15$ datasets with $p<0.05$; otherwise $\tau=1$ is kept. References, chosen with the target labels: the oracle-best $\tau$ of each pair and the global best $\tau$ over all pairs. Also measured: for BayCAP-KDEy, the observed widening of the amplitude with respect to $\tau=1$ against the prediction $\sqrt{(\tau+r^2)/(1+r^2)}$ with $r=\bar\sigma_g/\sigma_p$ stored per bag; for BayCAP-Hf, the NUTS diagnostics at every $\tau$.
- **Estimand:** the realised table of each bag (`true_cts`), for every $\tau$.
- **Scope:**
  - `methods/baycap.py`: argument `temperature` (positive, default $1$) of `BayCAP`, scaling by $1/\tau$ the log-likelihoods of the validation counts and of the bag counts (e.g. `numpyro.handlers.scale` on the two observed sites), for every `c_prior`, the priors unscaled; its entry in §II.12 updated;
  - `experiments/methods.py`: entries `BayesCAP-Hf-real-t<τ>` and `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-t<τ>` for $\tau\in\{0.5,2,4,8\}$ (`BayCAPKDEy` already has `temperature`), excluded from the tables, ranks and default plots of `main`; the NUTS diagnostics of the Hf entries stored as for `BayesCAP-Hf-real`;
  - new table module: per method and metric, the means over the pairs at every $\tau$, the leave-one-dataset-out selection and the test of the decision rule, $\tau_{-d}$ per dataset, the global best $\tau$ and the oracle-best $\tau$ per pair (references), and for BayCAP-KDEy the observed and predicted widening;
  - new plot module: the measures against $\tau$, the mean over the pairs in bold and each pair as a faint line, also split by $K$; for BayCAP-KDEy, observed against predicted widening;
  - `Makefile` targets; tests: `temperature=1` reproduces the current draws for every `c_prior` and the same `random_state`; with `c_prior="uniform"`, a large $V$ and a large $\tau$, the posterior mean of each column of $C$ matches $(f_{\cdot j}/\tau+1)/(n^s_j/\tau+K)$ within Monte Carlo error; $\tau_{-d}$ never depends on the scores of $d$; the table and the plot on a synthetic result.
- **Depends on:** FIX-8 (done, `272e809`; the classes of `poker_hand` too small for $V$; the stored runs at $\tau=1$ of `poker_hand` are re-run by it).
- **Comparability:** adds methods to `main`; the stored results of every other method are unchanged. The runs at $\tau=1$ are the stored `BayesCAP-Hf-real` and `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` if the reproduction test passes with the code that produced them, otherwise they are run again. Cost: $4$ values of $\tau$ $\times$ $2$ methods on the $75$ pairs ($600$ runs of $100$ bags); for BayCAP-Hf each value costs about one run of `BayesCAP-Hf-real`; at $\tau=0.5$ the sampler may diverge more.
- **Deviations from Part V:** none. The tempered methods are added to `main` (§V.1, methods of the experiments; user's decision of 2026-11-10 recorded in this card), with the seeds, bags and output paths of `main`; the reproduction check writes to its own folder `main_e52_check` (§V.1 output location).
- **Done when:** the code and tests are merged; the pilot ($3$ datasets with the five classifiers) and the full run are done with stored results.
- **Commands:**
  - pilot: `make run-main-classic ARGS="--datasets <d1> <d2> <d3> --methods BayesCAP-Hf-real-t0.5 BayesCAP-Hf-real-t2 BayesCAP-Hf-real-t4 BayesCAP-Hf-real-t8 BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-t0.5 BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-t2 BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-t4 BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real-t8"`, and the check of the untempered runs on the same pairs: `make run-tempering-check ARGS="--run --datasets <d1> <d2> <d3>"`, then `make run-tempering-check ARGS=--compare` (pilot datasets: `mammographic`, `poker_hand`, `letter`, one per group of $K$, chosen by Claude at the user's request; the check is started after the tempered run, so that two BayCAP pools do not run together);
  - full run: `make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods <the eight methods above>"` (the cells already stored are skipped); the stored untempered runs are reused, since the check of the pilot reproduced them;
  - tables: `make run-tables-tempering ARGS=--tables` (CSVs, fragments and documents), PDFs with `make run-tables-tempering WHERE=local ARGS=--pdf` after `make pull`;
  - plots: `make run-plot-tempering` (vanilla accuracy; `ARGS="--metrics macro-F1 macro-K"` for the other metrics, `--figures` to select `lines`, `lines_k`, `widening`).
- **Log:** 2026-11-10: card added (user's decision). 2026-11-14: started on branch `wp-20/tempering` (worktree `../uncawe-wp20`, so that the main directory stays on `wp-19/binform-prior`). Code: `methods/baycap.py` (`temperature` of `BayCAP`, `_tempered`); `experiments/methods.py` (`TEMPERATURE_LEVELS`, `TEMPERED_BASES`, `TEMPERED_VARIANTS`, `baycap_method`, `tempered_method`, `fixed_c_method(..., temperature)`; the eight methods yielded last by `gen_methods`, so the order of the other methods and their tasks is unchanged); `experiments/tempering_check.py`; `tables/tempering.py`; `plots/tempering.py`; `Makefile` (`run-tempering-check`, `run-tables-tempering`, `run-plot-tempering`; `main_e52_check` in `RESULTS`); tests `tests/test_tempering.py`, `tests/test_methods.py` (the BayCAP pool).
