### WP-19 E5.1: the prior on $\pi^Q$ for BayCAP-Hf and BayCAP-KDEy (extended `binform`)

- **Kind / status:** experiment / Done
- **Source:** [[ext5_baycap_priors_tempering]] (E5.1)
- **Goal:** measure how much the knowledge of the distribution $G$ of the target prevalences improves the realised intervals of `BayesCAP-Hf-real` (BayCAP with the Hf prior on $C$, realised pushforward) and `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` (BayesKDEy with an MLP input and a fixed $\hat C$, realised pushforward), and what a prior with a wrong mean costs. Under the protocol of `main` the bags are uniform on the simplex, so the default uniform prior is already the well-specified one; E5.1 draws its bags from a non-uniform $G=\operatorname{Dir}(3K\mu^\star)$, whose concentration grows with the number of classes $K$ so that $G$ is about three times less spread than the uniform distribution on every dataset. For each dataset (shared by its five classifiers) two means $\mu^\star$ and $\mu^\dagger$ are drawn from $\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1)$ with a seed fixed per dataset, and drawn again until each is at $L_1$ distance at least $0.5$ from the centre $\mathbf 1/K$ and they are at $L_1$ distance at least $0.5$ from each other. Three priors on $\pi^Q$: uniform $\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1)$; well specified, $\operatorname{Dir}(3K\mu^\star)=G$; misspecified, $\operatorname{Dir}(3K\mu^\dagger)$. All $75$ pairs of `main`, bag sizes $\lvert U\rvert\in\{100,1000\}$, $\tau=1$, $100$ bags per (pair, bag size), drawn from the test pool with seeded generators and the same for every prior and method. Measures (vanilla accuracy, macro-F1, macro-K): AE, coverage, amplitude, Winkler score at $\alpha=0.05$; per pair the value of the knowledge $\Delta W_{\text{know}}=W_{\text{uniform}}-W_{\text{well-specified}}$, the cost of wrong knowledge $\Delta W_{\text{wrong}}=W_{\text{misspecified}}-W_{\text{uniform}}$, and the ratio of the amplitudes of the well-specified and uniform priors; paired Wilcoxon tests over the $75$ per-pair means. No decision rule.
- **Estimand:** the realised table of each bag (`true_cts`), for every prior and method.
- **Scope:**
  - `experiments/binform.py`: options for the datasets (all those of `main`), the bag size (written in the output path) and the methods; the $G$ of E5.1, with the seeded rule for the two means, which are stored with the results; factory entries for the two methods with each of the three priors. The default configuration (`connect-4`, the old `BayesCAP` with its two priors, $c=10$, $\lvert U\rvert=1000$) is unchanged.
  - new table module: per method, metric and bag size, the means over the pairs of AE, coverage, amplitude and Winkler score for each prior, $\Delta W_{\text{know}}$, $\Delta W_{\text{wrong}}$, the number of pairs with a lower Winkler score than the uniform prior and the $p$-values of the paired tests, also split by $K$ ($K=2$, $3\le K\le7$, $K\ge8$);
  - new plot module: per method, the per-pair Winkler score with the uniform prior against that with each informative prior, coloured by $K$, one panel per bag size, with the diagonal;
  - `Makefile` targets; tests: the means are reproducible for a seed, lie on the simplex and satisfy the distance conditions; the concentrations are $3K\mu$; `binform` with its default arguments builds the same tasks and bags as before; the table and the plot on a synthetic result.
- **Depends on:** FIX-8 (done, `272e809`; the classes of `poker_hand` too small for $V$; its pre-trained classifiers and splits change).
- **Comparability:** new bags, so the results are comparable within E5.1 only, not with `main` nor with the stored `binform` results, which are unchanged. Cost: $2$ methods $\times$ $3$ priors $\times$ $2$ bag sizes on the $75$ pairs ($900$ runs of $100$ bags); BayCAP-KDEy samples $\pi$ alone and is cheap, BayCAP-Hf costs about one run of `BayesCAP-Hf-real` in `main` per (prior, bag size).
- **Deviations from Part V:** the bag size $\lvert U\rvert=100$ differs from `SAMPLE_SIZE` (it is the varied factor, as §V.1 allows); the results of each bag size go to their own folder `binform_e51/u<size>/` (§V.1 output location, with `<experiment>` = `binform_e51/u<size>`); the means $\mu^\star,\mu^\dagger$ and the requested prevalences are drawn with `np.random.default_rng([_R_SEED, crc32(dataset), stream])`, a seed derived from the experiment seed and the dataset, not from global state (the default `binform` keeps `np.random.RandomState(_R_SEED)`).
- **Done when:** the code and tests are merged; the pilot ($3$ datasets with the five classifiers) and the full run are done with stored results.
- **Commands:**
  - pilot: skipped (user's decision of 2026-11-14);
  - full run: `make run-binform-e51` (the cells already stored are skipped); `ARGS` narrows it with `--datasets`, `--classifiers`, `--sizes` and `--methods`;
  - tables: `make run-tables-binform-prior ARGS=--tables` (CSVs, fragments and documents), PDFs with `make run-tables-binform-prior WHERE=local ARGS=--pdf` after `make pull`;
  - plots: `make run-plot-binform-prior` (vanilla accuracy; `ARGS="--metrics macro-F1 macro-K"` for the other metrics).
- **Log:** 2026-11-10: card added (user's decision). 2026-11-14: started on branch `wp-19/binform-prior` (FIX-8 done). Code: `experiments/binform.py` (option `--e51`; `e51_means`, `e51_concentration`, `e51_prior`, `e51_requested_prevs`, `e51_method`, `e51_protocol`, `e51_tasks`, `e51_experiments`, `parse_args`; the default run unchanged); `experiments/methods.py` (`fixed_c_method` takes the prior on $\pi$, uniform by default, so `main` is unchanged); `tables/binform_prior.py`; `plots/binform_prior.py`; `Makefile` (`run-binform-e51`, `run-tables-binform-prior`, `run-plot-binform-prior`; `binform_e51` in `RESULTS`); tests `tests/test_binform_e51.py`. The prior Dirichlet parameter is stored as a vector also for the uniform prior ($\mathbf 1$). Expected risk, not acted on: where $3K\mu_k<1$ the prior density is unbounded at the faces of the simplex, so NUTS may report more divergent transitions with the informative priors; the table reports the share of divergent bags per prior. 2026-11-14: no pilot (user's decision); full run started on `bcuda` (`make run-binform-e51`). 2026-11-14: the run crashed twice, at 05:26 and 11:14, after about $170$ tasks of BayCAP-Hf in one pool: a worker failed to allocate memory for a JIT compilation (`LLVM ERROR: Unable to allocate section memory`, then a segmentation fault), with about $34$ GB per worker and $500$ GB in total; no task had failed with an error, and each restart (`make run-binform-e51`, same commit) skipped the stored tasks. Cause: every bag builds and compiles its own NUTS sampler (`MCMC` per call of `sample_posterior` and `_run_nuts`, with `jit_model_args=False`), and each worker keeps the compiled code in the caches of JAX, about $3$ GB per task of BayCAP-Hf. Fix (user's request): `e51_protocol` calls `jax.clear_caches()` after each task (the task body moved to `_e51_task`); it does not change the results, since nothing compiled is reused across bags; tests in `tests/test_binform_e51.py`. Measured locally (single process, $12$ tasks of `BayesCAP-Hf-real_uniform` with $10$ bags each, nothing stored): the RSS stays at $2.52$–$2.61$ GB with the call and grows from $2.55$ to $5.96$ GB without it; each task is about $10$–$25$ s slower, since some helpers compiled once per task are no longer kept. The running E5.1 run uses the code without the call. Possible follow-up, not acted on: one sampler per task with `jit_model_args=True` would compile once per task instead of once per bag (methods code, a Part IV fix). 2026-11-14: full run completed at 14:40 (after the second restart, commit `c7fa408`): $900$ tasks, $2700$ result files under `binform_e51/u1000/` and `binform_e51/u100/`, no task error. 2026-11-14: tables generated on `bcuda` (`make run-tables-binform-prior ARGS=--tables`, from `3ba7c59`) and their PDFs locally (`WHERE=local ARGS=--pdf`); plots for the three metrics. 2026-11-15: the plot titles use the metric label (`ACC_LABELS`); plots generated again locally (`make run-plot-binform-prior WHERE=local`, from `093732d`), because a concurrent push of the WP-20 worktree to the same remote folder (`rsync --delete`) removed the files of this branch from `bcuda` while the plot job started. Analysis of E5.1 written in [[ext5_baycap_priors_tempering]] (the NUTS diagnostics not analysed, user's decision); Status of the extension and its summary in [[extensions]] updated. 2026-11-15: branch merged into `devel` and card closed at the user's request; unit tests: $359$, all passing.
- **Outcome:** merged into `devel` on 2026-11-15 (`d1c406b`), at the user's request, without a rebase (`devel` had not moved since the branch was created). Part II: §II.2 (the code, the run and the findings). Deviations from **Done when**: no pilot (user's decision of 2026-11-14). Decisions taken during the card and not in its first Scope: one result folder per bag size (`binform_e51/u<size>/`); the means and the requested prevalences seeded with `np.random.default_rng([_R_SEED, crc32(dataset), stream])`; `experiments/methods.py:fixed_c_method` takes the prior on $\pi$; the prior stored as a vector also when uniform; plots for the three metrics, with the metric label in the title; the NUTS diagnostics stored and tabulated but not analysed (user's decision of 2026-11-15). Problems: the full run stopped twice when a worker could not allocate memory for a JIT compilation, because every bag compiles its own NUTS sampler and each worker kept the compiled code (about $3$ GB per task of BayCAP-Hf); fixed by `jax.clear_caches()` after each task of `e51_protocol` (`814ec61`, measured locally: flat RSS instead of growing), after the run, which was resumed with the stored tasks skipped; a concurrent push of the WP-20 worktree to the same folder on `bcuda` (`rsync --delete`) removed the files of this branch while a plot job started, so the plots were generated again locally. Follow-ups, not acted on: building one sampler per task with `jit_model_args=True` would compile once per task instead of once per bag in every BayCAP method (code of `methods/`, a possible Part IV fix); one remote folder per branch, or no concurrent runs from two worktrees. Findings that bear on later work: the gain of the well-specified prior is concentrated on a few datasets and falls on pairs that undercover, against the predictions of §T.2 and §T.4 of the extension; the misspecified prior costs several times more than the right one gains; on `wine-quality` both informative priors help, possibly by shrinkage (not tested).

### WP-18 E3.3: SQS-B on BayesKDEy with an MLP input, on the `main` protocol

- **Kind / status:** experiment / Done
- **Source:** [[ext3_posterior_propagation]] E3.3 (former candidate E3.c1), decided by the user on 2026-11-10.
- **Goal:** add SQS-B, an SQS method that draws one validation bag of $N^Q=1000$ instances from $V$ for each draw $\pi^{(r)}$ of the posterior of BayesKDEy (Gaussian kernel, $b=0.1$, $\lambda=0$, $\pi\sim\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1)$, $\tau=1$) on an MLP trained on $V$ (validation outputs cross-fitted on $5$ folds), and returns the tables of $h$ on these bags; run it on the $75$ pairs of `main` and compare it with `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` (same prevalence draws, only the bridge from prevalence to table differs), `SQS-Hbs`, `SQS-H` and `BayesCAP-Hf-real`.
- **Estimand:** realised table of the target sample; scored against `true_cts`.
- **Scope:**
  - `methods/sqs_b.py:SQSB` (`CAPContingencyTable`, `CTCAPWithConfidence`, as `cap-ml`'s `RQBS`): quantifier fitted on $V$; prevalence draws from `BayCAPKDEy(c_prior="fixed", input_classifier="mlp")` (reused, not copied); draws smoothed as `RQBS` ($\epsilon=10^{-5}$); one bag per draw with QuaPy's `sampling_index` on all of $V$ (class counts rounded, remainder at random, sampling with replacement), all bags drawn sequentially from the NumPy state seeded by `random_state` at each call; tables in the `cap-ml` layout; per-bag summaries `prev_mean`, `acc_sd`, `sigma_g`, `sigma_f`, `val_ece`, $S_{\text{rec}}$, $S_{\text{prec}}$, and NUTS diagnostics.
  - `experiments/methods.py`: factory entry `SQS-B` (NUTS: $500$ warm-up, $1000$ draws, the seed and MLP `random_state` of `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real`), in `BAYES_POOL_METHODS` (`BAYES_ENV`, JAX on CPU).
  - `tables/main.py`: `SQS-B` in `MAIN_METHODS` (main tables, rank tables, coverage vs amplitude plot), LaTeX macro `\sqsb`.
  - New table and plot scripts for E3.3: per-pair comparison table (coverage, amplitude, Winkler score of the five methods; AE of `SQS-B` and `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp`; observed and predicted amplitude ratio $\sqrt{(\sigma_p^2+\bar\sigma_f^2)/(\sigma_p^2+\bar\sigma_g^2)}$; paired bootstrap Winkler tests, $10{,}000$ resamples, Holm-adjusted over the $75$ pairs, against `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` and `SQS-Hbs`), the two coverage scatters and the width scatter; `Makefile` targets.
  - Tests: draws equal to those of `BayCAPKDEy` for the same seeds; bag sizes and class counts; tables of $h$ on the bags, summing to one; determinism; `sigma_f` and the identity $a(1-a)=N^Q\sigma_f^2+S_{\text{rec}}$; factory configuration and inclusion in the tables of `main`.
  - This document: §II.12 entry (code to move to `cap-ml`).
- **Depends on:** FIX-8 (done, `272e809`; the classes of `poker_hand` too small for $V$), and the re-run of `main` in FIX-2 + FIX-3 (done, re-run complete on 2026-11-13; `SQS-H`, `SQS-Hbs` after the seeding fix).
- **Comparability:** adds one method to `main` and leaves the stored results of every other method untouched; adding it to `MAIN_METHODS` changes the mean ranks of the other methods in the rank tables of `main` (not their scores), which are regenerated. Comparable with the CPU results of E3.1 and `BayesCAP-Hf-real`, and with `SQS-H(bs)` after FIX-2 + FIX-3, all after FIX-8.
- **Deviations from Part V:** none. `SQSB` imports `cap-ml`'s private `_temp_np_seed` (`cap.models.confidence`), to seed its bags exactly as `RQBS`.
- **Done when:** the code and tests are merged, the full run ($7500$ NUTS runs) is stored under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main/` (the pilot was skipped by the user's decision of 2026-11-13), and the tables and plots of E3.3, of `main` and of E4.1 and E4.3 are regenerated.
- **Commands:**
  - pilot: skipped (user's decision of 2026-11-13);
  - full run: `make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods SQS-B"` (the cells already stored are skipped);
  - tables and plots of E3.3: `make run-tables-sqs-b ARGS=--tables`, `make run-plot-sqs-b`; PDFs with `make run-tables-sqs-b WHERE=local ARGS=--pdf` after `make pull`;
  - tables and plots of `main` regenerated with `SQS-B`: `make run-tables-main ARGS=--tables`, `make run-tables-rank ARGS=--tables`, `make run-plot-avc`, `make run-plot-rank`, `make run-tables-error-split ARGS=--tables`, `make run-plot-error-split`.
- **Log:** 2026-11-10: card added and scheduled by the user, after E3.3 was written into [[ext3_posterior_propagation]] (`ecb51fa`). 2026-11-13: started on branch `wp-18/sqs-b`; the user kept the MLP input of BayesKDEy after FIX-8, although it fails on `poker_hand` (E3.1, P4), which E3.3 reports. Code: `methods/sqs_b.py` (`SQSB`, `forward_bag_variances`, `luck_spreads`, `bag_tables`); `experiments/methods.py` (`SQS-B` after `SQS-Hbs` in `METHOD_CLASS`, `SQS_B`, `SQS_B_QUANTIFIER`, `fixed_c_method`, which now builds the eight methods of E3.1 and the quantifier of `SQS-B` with the same code, so their configurations cannot drift; `BAYES_POOL_METHODS`); `experiments/main.py` (diagnostics and summaries of `SQSB` stored per bag); `tables/main.py` (`MAIN_METHODS`, `\sqsb`), `tables/rank.py` (`\sqsb`), `tables/common.py`, `plots/common.py` (labels, colour); `tables/sqs_b.py`, `plots/sqs_b.py`, `Makefile` targets `tables-sqs-b`, `plot-sqs-b`. Decisions taken in the implementation: (1) the draws of NUTS are single precision, and `sampling_index` (through `np.random.choice`) rejects prevalences whose sum differs from $1$ in double precision, so the draws are converted to double precision before the smoothing of `RQBS`; the summaries use the draws as returned, as those of `BayCAPKDEy`; (2) the tables and bar plots of E4.1 and E4.3 (`tables/error_split.py:METHODS`, derived from `MAIN_METHODS`) include `SQS-B` (user's decision of 2026-11-13; not in the first Scope): they restrict to the pairs stored for all their methods, so they are regenerated after the full run; (3) the comparison table also shows the AE of `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` next to those of `SQS-B` and `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp` (P15 compares the three); (4) the per-pair CSV records the largest difference of the stored $\bar\pi$ of `SQS-B` and `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real`, which checks that the run shares the prevalence draws. Unit tests: `tests/test_sqs_b.py`, `tests/test_sqs_b_tables.py`, and new cases in `tests/test_methods.py` and `tests/test_main_protocol.py`; all $338$ pass. 2026-11-13, at the user's request: the pilot is skipped, so the card is "Implemented, not evaluated" without a pilot; the full run `make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods SQS-B"` started on `bcuda` at 20:49 from `87ce020` (`cap-ml` `93ae246`, `JAX=cpu`, log `output/runs/main-classic_20261113-204906.out`). The run ended at 21:20 (exit 0, $31$ minutes; $2$–$3$ s per bag; the longest fit, `isolet` LR, $395$ s), $225$ files ($75$ pairs × $3$ metrics), no error or warning in the experiment log. 2026-11-13, at the user's request, on `bcuda` from `511fba6`, each exit 0: `make run-tables-sqs-b ARGS=--tables`, `make run-plot-sqs-b`, `make run-tables-main ARGS=--tables`, `make run-tables-rank ARGS=--tables`, `make run-plot-avc`, `make run-plot-rank`, `make run-tables-error-split ARGS=--tables`, `make run-plot-error-split`; `make pull`; PDFs locally with `WHERE=local ARGS=--pdf` of `tables-sqs-b`, `tables-main`, `tables-rank`, `tables-error-split`, each exit 0. Checks: $75$ pairs in every table; `SQS-B` and `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` have the same $\bar\pi$ on every bag (largest difference $0$), so the run shares the prevalence draws; in the error split the identity holds for `SQS-B` within $1.5\cdot10^{-15}$, with no dropped and no empty-column bag (the warning on the identity gap is that of `QuAcc_NxN`, as before). 2026-11-13, at the user's request: Analysis of E3.3 written in [[ext3_posterior_propagation]] (Status, P15–P18) and summary in [[extensions]], from the per-pair CSV of E3.3 and the tables of `main` (macro-F1, macro-K), with the extra tests and counts computed outside the scripts. Left: the user's review, the merge and the closing of the card.
- **Outcome:** merged into `devel` on 2026-11-14 (`2368301`), at the user's request, without a rebase (`devel` had not moved since the branch was created). Part II: §II.24 (the code, the run and the findings). Deviations from **Done when**: no pilot (user's decision of 2026-11-13). Decisions taken during the card and not in its first Scope: `SQS-B` is in the tables and bar plots of E4.1 and E4.3; the comparison table shows the AE of `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` too; the per-pair CSV checks that the run shares the prevalence draws (largest difference of $ar\pi$ $0$ on every bag); `experiments/methods.py:fixed_c_method` builds the eight methods of E3.1 and the quantifier of `SQS-B` with the same code. Problems: the draws of NUTS are single precision and `sampling_index` rejects them, so `SQSB` converts them to double precision before the smoothing. Findings that bear on later work: the forward bag of SQS-B has the centre of the population method and about the width of the realised pushforward, but is worse than `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` (Holm-adjusted Winkler test: lower on $2$ pairs, higher on $24$), because it does not use the predictions of $h$ on the target bag; it ties with `SQS-Hbs` on vanilla accuracy and beats it on macro-F1 and macro-K, which E3.c9 could attribute to the quantifier or to the posterior; the predicted amplitude ratio of §T.14 overstates the widening where $V$ is small, possibly through the variance of the centre $m(\pi)$ of the realised pushforward (§T.8, not stored per bag). Follow-ups (proposals, not cards): E3.c9; storing $\operatorname{Var}(m(\pi))$ per bag for the realised methods.

### WP-17 E6.2: the allocation $\rho$ of SQS-S on the `main` protocol

- **Kind / status:** experiment / Done
- **Source:** [[ext6_sqs_s_design]] (E6.2)
- **Goal:** describe how SQS-S changes when the share $\rho=\lvert V_q\rvert/\lvert V\rvert$ of the validation set that trains its quantifier moves away from $0.5$. A larger $\rho$ gives a better quantifier, so the selected bags are closer to the target in true prevalence; a smaller $\rho$ gives a larger $V_b$, so the recalls of $h$ inside the bags, shared by all the bags, are closer to its true recalls. The card runs SQS-S at $\rho\in\{0.1,0.3,0.5,0.7,0.9\}$ with nested splits (WP-15) on the full `main` protocol ($75$ pairs, $100$ APP target bags of size $1000$ per pair, the seeds of `main`), with no outer seeds (the pairs are the replicates). It is an analysis only, with no decision rule (user's decision of 2026-11-10): `PQ` keeps $\rho=0.5$ with the current split.
- **Estimand:** the realised table of each target bag (`true_cts`), for every level.
- **Scope:**
  - `experiments/methods.py`: five factory entries `PQ-rho0.1`, …, `PQ-rho0.9`: `PrediQuant` with PACC(MLP), $n=10{,}000$ candidates, $\gamma=0.1$, $L_1$, $m=1000$, as `PQ`, and `rho` set; the level $0.5$ is run too, because the nested split differs from the split of `PQ`; excluded from the main and rank tables and from the default plots of `main`;
  - per target bag, for these methods only: $\hat\pi^Q$ and the minimum over the classes of the class support of the selected bags (WP-15); the quantification error $\frac1K\sum_j\lvert\hat\pi^Q_j-\pi^Q_j\rvert$ is computed by the table from $\hat\pi^Q$ and `true_cts`;
  - new table module: for each metric (vanilla accuracy, macro-F1, macro-K), one row per measure (quantification error, absolute error, coverage, amplitude, Winkler score, class support) and one column per level, with the mean over the $75$ pairs; the cells that differ from $\rho=0.5$ with a Holm-adjusted paired Wilcoxon $p<0.05$ are marked;
  - new plot module: each measure against $\rho$, the mean over the pairs in bold and each pair as a faint line, one panel per measure, one figure per metric;
  - `Makefile` targets; tests: the five methods are built with the right `rho`; the extra columns are written for these methods only; the table and the plot on a synthetic result.
- **Depends on:** WP-15 (the `rho` parameter and the stored $\hat\pi^Q$ and selected indices, with the pin bump); FIX-8 (the classes of `poker_hand`, done), added on 2026-11-10.
- **Comparability:** adds the rows of five methods to `main`; the stored results of the other methods, including `PQ`, are unchanged. At $\rho=0.1$ small datasets may have classes with fewer than $5$ instances in $V_q$, which PACC's internal $5$-fold cross-validation cannot handle: such (pair, level) failures are recorded and counted, not silently dropped.
- **Deviations from Part V:** the pilot uses the five classifiers and the $100$ bags of `main` (the card's design), not LR and few bags (§V.1, *Pilot first*): `main` has a fixed number of bags, and the failures at $\rho=0.1$ depend on the pair.
- **Done when:** the code and tests are merged; the pilot ($3$ datasets, e.g. `mammographic`, `wine-quality` and a large dataset, with the five classifiers) and the full run (about $4$ hours on `bcuda`, from the $47$ minutes of the `PQ` re-run of 2026-11-09) are done at the user's request and the tables and plots are stored; the Analysis section of E6.2 is written.
- **Commands:** on `bcuda` (the default), from the branch of the card, after `make setup`. Pilot: `make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods PQ-rho0.1 PQ-rho0.3 PQ-rho0.5 PQ-rho0.7 PQ-rho0.9 --datasets mammographic wine-quality poker_hand"`. Full run: `make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods PQ-rho0.1 PQ-rho0.3 PQ-rho0.5 PQ-rho0.7 PQ-rho0.9"` (the pilot pairs are skipped). Both use the tmux session `uncawe-main-classic`, shared with the re-run of FIX-2 + FIX-3: not at the same time. `make logs EXP=main-classic` shows the output, `make explog EXP=main` the experiment log, where a failed (pair, level) is a warning `gave error`. Tables: `make run-tables-pq-rho ARGS=--tables`; plots: `make run-plot-pq-rho`; `make pull`; PDF locally: `make run-tables-pq-rho WHERE=local ARGS=--pdf`.
- **Log:** 2026-11-10: card added (user's decision). 2026-11-10: started on branch `wp-17/pq-rho`. `experiments/methods.py`: `PQ_RHO_LEVELS`, `PQ_RHO_VARIANTS` (`PQ-rho0.1`, …, `PQ-rho0.9`) in `METHOD_CLASS` and `gen_methods`, built by `sqs_s(acc, rho)`, which also builds `PQ` (`rho=None`, unchanged); in the regular pool; left out of the main and rank tables and of the coverage vs amplitude plot by `tables/main.py:EXCLUDED_METHODS` (they are not in `MAIN_METHODS`) and not in `plots/common.py:DEFAULT_METHODS`. `experiments/main.py:allocation_columns`: for a `PrediQuant` with `rho` set, per bag, `pq_test_prev` ($\hat\pi^Q$, length $K$), `pq_class_support` (per class, length $K$) and `pq_min_class_support`; the per-class vector is stored as well as its minimum (not in the card's scope; it costs nothing and leaves the class open to later analyses); their time is left out of `t_test_ave`, as for the diagnostics of BayCAP. A failed fit (e.g. at $\rho=0.1$) is caught by `_evaluate_contingency_method` as for every method: no file is written and the log has a warning. New `tables/pq_rho.py`: the measures of the card per bag and per pair; a (pair, level) with no stored result, among the pairs with at least one level, is a failure, listed in `pq_rho_failures.csv` and counted in the caption; the summary uses the pairs that have every level, so that every column is a mean over the same pairs; Holm over the $4\times6$ tests of each metric (implementation choices, not in the card). New `plots/pq_rho.py`: one figure per metric, six panels, faint line per pair, bold mean over the same pairs as the summary, nominal line on coverage. `Makefile`: `tables-pq-rho`, `plot-pq-rho`. Tests: `tests/test_methods.py` (`PQRhoFactoryTest`), `tests/test_main_protocol.py` (the extra columns for `PQ-rho*` only, on a `PrediQuant` with known candidate bags), `tests/test_pq_rho.py` (table and plot on synthetic results); unit tests: 300, all passing. Not run. 2026-11-10: at the user's request, the full run without a pilot (user's decision) on `bcuda`, from `f849f24` (`cap-ml` `637fdd5`), `make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods PQ-rho0.1 PQ-rho0.3 PQ-rho0.5 PQ-rho0.7 PQ-rho0.9"`, from 18:09 to 18:52 (log `output/runs/main-classic_20261110-180912.out`, exit status $0$): $375$ tasks, $365$ stored ($225$ files, $75$ pairs × three metrics, at $\rho\in\{0.3,0.5,0.7\}$, $210$ at $\rho\in\{0.1,0.9\}$); $10$ failed (pair, level), `poker_hand` with each of the five classifiers at $\rho=0.1$ and $\rho=0.9$: its class $7$ has $3$ validation instances, so $\operatorname{round}(0.1\cdot3)=0$ leaves it empty in $V_q$ and $\operatorname{round}(0.9\cdot3)=3$ empty in $V_b$ (`ValueError` of `PrediQuant._split`). The card expected failures at $\rho=0.1$ only, from PACC's cross-validation; none of those occurred. The summary of `tables/pq_rho.py` will therefore use the $70$ pairs without `poker_hand`. Tables and plots not generated yet. 2026-11-10: the card is paused until FIX-8 is done (user's decision): FIX-8 drops the classes with an expected count in $V$ below $10$ (on `poker_hand`, class 7: $K=7$, smallest class about $18$ instances in $V$), pretrains `poker_hand` again and re-runs its results, including the five `PQ-rho*` methods; the tables of E6.2 are then generated on the $75$ pairs. 2026-11-13: FIX-8 and FIX-2 + FIX-3 closed; `devel` merged into the branch (`9a64989`; conflict only in the board), card resumed; unit tests: $315$, all passing. 2026-11-13, at the user's request, on `bcuda` from `9a64989` (cap-ml `93ae246`, after `make setup`): `make run-main-classic ARGS="--datasets poker_hand --methods PQ-rho0.1 PQ-rho0.3 PQ-rho0.5 PQ-rho0.7 PQ-rho0.9"`, 17:03–17:06, exit 0, $25$ of $25$ tasks stored ($75$ files), no failure: with $K=7$ the smallest class of `poker_hand` (class 6, $18$ instances in $V$) leaves $2$ in $V_q$ at $\rho=0.1$ and $2$ in $V_b$ at $\rho=0.9$, and PACC's cross-validation accepts them. Each level now has $225$ files ($75$ pairs × $3$ metrics). 2026-11-13, at the user's request, on `bcuda` from `9a67e5a`: `make run-tables-pq-rho ARGS=--tables` (exit 0; $75$ common pairs, no failed (pair, level)); `make run-plot-pq-rho` (exit 1 when started together with the tables, before `pq_rho_pairs.csv` existed; started again after them, exit 0); `make pull`; `make run-tables-pq-rho WHERE=local ARGS=--pdf`, exit 0. The minimum class support is $1$ on every pair and level except `connect-4` and `shuttle` at $\rho=0.1$ ($1-1.4\cdot10^{-5}$ and $1-1.0\cdot10^{-5}$), so the panel of the plot shows only the offset of the axis. 2026-11-13, at the user's request: `plots/pq_rho.py` draws coverage and class support on a fixed range $[-0.05,1.05]$ without axis offset (test added; unit tests of E6.2 pass), `make run-plot-pq-rho` on `bcuda` from `06f30cd`, exit 0, `make pull`. Analysis of E6.2 written in [[ext6_sqs_s_design]], with the summary in [[extensions]]; the bias of the point estimate, $\lvert V\rvert$ per dataset and the means of `PQ` computed with read-only scratch scripts on the stored results of `main` on `bcuda`. The minimum class support, as defined (union of the selected bags), is saturated and does not measure the reuse of instances within a bag (Analysis).
- **Outcome:** merged into `devel` on 2026-11-13 (`1eaf665`), at the user's request, without a rebase (`devel` had been merged into the branch at `9a64989`, after FIX-8 and FIX-2 + FIX-3). Part II: §II.21 (the code) and §II.23 (the run and the findings). Deviations from **Done when**: no pilot; the full run was started directly (user's decision of 2026-11-10). Problems: the run of 2026-11-10 failed on `poker_hand` at $\rho=0.1$ and $\rho=0.9$ with every classifier, because its class 7 had $3$ instances in $V$ and the split left it empty in $V_q$ or $V_b$ (not, as the card expected, from PACC's cross-validation at $\rho=0.1$); the card was paused, FIX-8 dropped that class ($K=7$), and `poker_hand` was run again after it, with no failure ($2$ instances of class 6 in $V_q$ at $\rho=0.1$ are accepted by PACC). Changes that came up during the card: `plots/pq_rho.py` draws coverage and class support on a fixed range without axis offset (the saturated class support was drawn as an offset axis). Findings that bear on later work: the minimum class support, defined on the union of the selected bags, is $1$ almost everywhere and does not measure the reuse of instances within a bag, which §T.3 of [[ext6_sqs_s_design]] describes; a per-bag measure would be needed (not proposed as a card). Follow-ups: none scheduled; $\rho=0.5$ stays the allocation of `PQ`.

### FIX-8 The smallest class of poker_hand is too small for the validation set

- **Kind / status:** fix / Done (2026-11-13)
- **Source:** Part IV, FIX-8, scheduled by the user on 2026-11-10, after the run of E6.2 (card WP-17), where `poker_hand` failed at $\rho=0.1$ and $\rho=0.9$ with every classifier.
- **Goal:** keep only the classes of a multiclass dataset that have, in expectation, at least $k=10$ instances in $V$, and re-run what uses `poker_hand`. quapy drops the classes with fewer than `min_class_support=100` instances of the full collection, but the training part is then capped at $25{,}000$ instances and halved into $T$ and $V$, so a class with $s_j$ instances of the $n$ of the collection has about $\lvert V_j\rvert\approx s_j\,n_{\text{train}}/(2n)$ instances in $V$, with $n_{\text{train}}=\min(0.7\,n,25{,}000)$. The rule keeps class $\omega_j$ when $s_j\,n_{\text{train}}/(2n)\ge k$, that is, it uses the threshold $s_j\ge\max\bigl(100,\lceil 2k\,n/n_{\text{train}}\rceil\bigr)$, with $n$ the size of the collection after quapy's filter at $100$ (user's decisions of 2026-11-10: $k=10$, applied to every multiclass dataset, the cap of $25{,}000$ unchanged). On the datasets of `main` it changes only `poker_hand` ($n=1{,}024{,}985$, threshold $820$): class 7 ($236$ instances, $3$ in $V$) is dropped, $K$ goes from $8$ to $7$, and the smallest class (class 6, $1{,}460$ instances) has about $18$ instances in $V$. The smallest class in $V$ of every other multiclass dataset of `main` has at least $37$ instances (`shuttle`; counts of 2026-11-10 from the current pipeline, see the Log), so nothing else changes; binary datasets are not filtered.
- **Estimand:** unchanged for every method; the fix changes the data of `poker_hand`, not what the methods estimate.
- **Scope:**
  - `cap-ml` (owned by the user; committed by the user): `fetch_UCIMulticlassDataset` (`cap/data/datasets.py`) takes a `min_class_support` and passes it to quapy's `fetch_UCIMulticlassDataset`; pin bump in UNCAWE (`pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock`).
  - UNCAWE: `src/uncawe/pretrain/uci.py` computes the threshold of the rule for a dataset (`min_class_support`) and wraps the fetch (`fetch_uci_multiclass`), used by every call of `fetch_UCIMulticlassDataset`: `pretrain/classic.py`, `pretrain/data.py:load_from_collection` (the loading of $V$ and $U$ by `main` and the other experiments), `experiments/datasets.py`, `pretrain/dataset.py:sort_datasets_by_size`, `plots/binform_simplex.py`; tests in `tests/test_uci_filter.py` (the threshold of `poker_hand` is $820$, its $7$ classes in $T$, $V$, $U$ and at least $10$ instances of each in $V$, every other multiclass dataset stored locally at the threshold $100$). The floor of $100$ keeps quapy's filter where the rule is weaker: without the cap, $n_{\text{train}}=0.7\,n$ and $\lceil 2k\,n/n_{\text{train}}\rceil=\lceil20/0.7\rceil=29$, which would bring back classes with $29$ to $99$ instances.
  - Pretraining: the five `poker_hand` classifiers (LR, kNN, SVM, MLP, RF) are moved to a backup and pretrained again (`pretrain/classic.py` skips the classifiers already stored and has no dataset filter; the file names carry $K$, so the new files are `*_poker_hand_7_*`, but the old `*_poker_hand_8_*` must be moved, since `pretrain/data.py` loads every `*_info.pkl` of the folder and would rebuild $V$ and $U$ with $K=7$ for the old classifiers); $T$, $V$ and $U$ of `poker_hand` all change, since they are stratified splits of a different collection.
  - Stored results: every `poker_hand` file of `main` (all methods, the three metrics) is moved to `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main_pre_fix8/`, same tree. The files of `B-CBPE`, `BE-CBPE` and `QuAcc_1xN2`, which the code no longer builds (§II.7), are moved and not re-run. The other backup trees (`main_pre_fix3`, `main_22_10_26`, `main_03_06_26`, `main_pq_pre_fix1`, `main_pq_pre_wp14`) are not touched.
  - Runs in `main` on `poker_hand` ($5$ pairs, $100$ bags, three metrics), with the methods grouped by source: §I.2 (`DoC`, `L-CBPE`, `BayesCAP`); FIX-2 + FIX-3 (`QuAcc_NxN`, `O-LEAP`, `SQS-H`, `SQS-Hbs`, `PQ`), covered by the re-run of that card if it runs after this fix; Extension 2 (`BayesCAP-real`, E2.3; `BayesCAP-EB`, `BayesCAP-EB-real`, E2.4; `BayesCAP-H-real`, E2.7; `BayesCAP-Hf-real`, E2.8; `BayesCAP-Hfn-real`, E2.9, on `poker_hand` × LR only, the pair of its pilot); Extension 3 (the eight methods of E3.1, `BayCAP-{KDEy,EMQ}-{h,mlp}` and their `-real` variants; the two of E3.2, `BayCAP-KDEy-Hf-{h,mlp}-real`); Extension 6 (`PQ-rho0.1`, …, `PQ-rho0.9`, E6.2, card WP-17). $29$ methods in all.
  - Tables and plots regenerated (no new runs): `main` (main tables, rank tables, coverage vs amplitude, AE vs Winkler, rank heatmap); E2.1 (`tables/estimand_gap.py`); E2.2 (`tables/displacement.py`, `plots/displacement.py`); E2.3 (`tables/pushforward.py`, `plots/pushforward.py`); E2.4 (`tables/eb_prior.py`, `plots/eb_prior.py`); E2.6 (`tables/hierarchical.py`, `plots/hierarchical.py`, which read the counts of $V$); E2.7, E2.8, E2.9 (`tables/baycap_h.py`, `tables/baycap_hf.py`, `tables/baycap_hfn.py` with `--check`, and their plots); E3.1, E3.2 (`tables/baycap_fixed.py`, `tables/baycap_kdey_hf.py`, their plots); E4.1 and E4.3 (`tables/error_split.py`, `plots/error_split.py`); E6.2 (`tables/pq_rho.py`, `plots/pq_rho.py`, then on $75$ pairs). Not affected: E2.5 (`eb_binary`, binary datasets), Extension 5 (`binform`, three-class datasets), E6.1 (`predist`, three-class datasets), the toy simulations; E4.2 has not been run.
  - Documents: the numbers of Part I, Part II and of the Analysis sections of the extensions that include `poker_hand` are updated from the regenerated tables, marked as after FIX-8; a conclusion that changes is reported to the user, not rewritten silently. This document: the Part IV entry FIX-8 removed when done, the change noted in Part I (§I.1) and in the Part II sections of the experiments re-run.
- **Depends on:** none. Order with FIX-2 + FIX-3 (then In progress, re-run of `main` pending): FIX-8 first, so that `poker_hand` is re-run once and the tables of `main` are regenerated once for both fixes.
- **Comparability:** the results on `poker_hand` before and after the fix are not comparable ($K=8$ against $K=7$, different $T$, $V$, $U$ and classifiers); the old ones stay in `main_pre_fix8/`, and the paper's `poker_hand` rows change. The other $70$ pairs of `main` and every other experiment are unchanged. The ranking of the datasets by size, which selects the datasets of `main`, is unchanged ($n$ drops by $236$).
- **Deviations from Part V:** none.
- **Done when:** the `cap-ml` code is committed and pinned, the UNCAWE code and tests pass, `poker_hand` is pretrained again, the runs above are stored, the tables and plots are regenerated, the documents are updated, and the branch is merged.
- **Commands:** all on `bcuda` (the default), from the branch of the fix, after `make push` and `make setup`. (1) Move the old classifiers: `<CAP_DATA>/pretrain/classic/*_poker_hand_8_*` to `<CAP_DATA>/pretrain_pre_fix8/classic/`. (2) Pretrain: `make run-pretrain-classic` (only `poker_hand` is trained; `make explog EXP=pretrain.classic` shows the log). (3) Move every `output/uncawe/main/**/poker_hand/**` file to the same path under `output/uncawe/main_pre_fix8/`. (4) Runs in `main`: `make run-main-classic ARGS="--datasets poker_hand --methods <methods>"`, the methods and their order in the Scope, the Bayesian ones one at a time; `BayesCAP-Hfn-real` with `--classifiers LR`; the FIX-2 + FIX-3 set through the re-run of that card. (5) Tables and plots: the commands of the cards of the experiments listed in the Scope. Filled in detail when the runs are scheduled.
- **Log:** 2026-11-10: card added and scheduled by the user, after the run of E6.2 (WP-17), which is paused until this fix is done. ### FIX-2 + FIX-3 SQS-H as in the paper, `RQBS` renamed `SQS-Hbs`, seeded randomness of the surrogate quantifiers, pin bump, re-run in `main`

- **Kind / status:** fix / Done (2026-11-13)
- **Source:** Part IV, FIX-3 (unseeded randomness), scheduled by the user on 2026-11-10; FIX-2 (SQS-H is implemented as a bootstrap variant), added to the card by the user on 2026-11-10, during the first re-run, because it changes the same method and needs the same re-run.
- **Goal (FIX-2):** run SQS-H as defined in the paper (§4.1) next to the variant run so far. The paper's SQS-H trains the quantifier $q$ on $V$, estimates the target prevalence once, $\hat\pi^Q=q(U)$, and draws $m=1000$ bags of size $1000$ from $V$ at $\hat\pi^Q$; the method run by `main` as `RQBS` estimates the prevalence on each of $m$ bootstrap resamples of $U$ and draws one bag at each estimate $\hat\pi^{*(r)}$. In `cap-ml` (version 0.1.1.13), `RQBS` gets a parameter `bootstrap` (default `True`): `True` is the current variant, with the draws of version 0.1.1.11; `False` is the paper's SQS-H, its bags drawn from the same seeded NumPy state. In UNCAWE (user's decision of 2026-11-10): the method `RQBS` is renamed `SQS-Hbs` (H with a bootstrap) in the code and in the stored results (file name and `method` column, `scripts/migrate/rename_method.py`), and the paper's variant is added to `main` as `SQS-H`. Both are shown in the main tables, the rank tables, the coverage vs amplitude plot and the error split (`tables/main.py:MAIN_METHODS`), with the LaTeX macros `\sqsh` and `\sqshbs` (SQS-H$_\text{bs}$) in place of `\rqbs`; the tables of E3.1 and E3.2 (`tables/baycap_fixed.py`, `tables/baycap_kdey_hf.py`) and `binform` keep only `SQS-Hbs`, the method they were run with.
- **Goal (FIX-3):** make the four non-Bayesian methods of `main` that use a surrogate quantifier reproducible from the experiment seed, and re-run them in `main`. Three sources of randomness ignore the seed: (1) the `MLPClassifier()` of the quantifiers built by `experiments/methods.py`, `kdey()` (KDEy(MLP), in `QuAcc_NxN` and `O-LEAP`) and `pacc()` (PACC(MLP), in `RQBS` and `PQ`), whose initial weights and mini-batch order come from the global NumPy state, so that every fit trains a different MLP; (2) the bootstrap of the target prevalence in `RQBS`, computed by QuaPy's `AggregativeBootstrap.predict_conf`, which received the constructor argument `random_state` (`None` by default) instead of the resolved seed and, in any case, draws its resamples in joblib workers (`Parallel(n_jobs=-1)`) from their own global NumPy state; (3) the bags that `RQBS` draws from $V$ with `sampling_index`, called without a `random_state`. In UNCAWE (1): `kdey()` and `pacc()` build `MLPClassifier(random_state=qp.environ["_R_SEED"])`. In `cap-ml` (2) and (3) (version 0.1.1.11): `RQBS` no longer wraps its quantifier in `AggregativeBootstrap`; `fit` trains the quantifier on $V$ (as `AggregativeBootstrap` did with one training sample), and `predict_ct_range` draws the $m=1000$ bootstrap resamples of the target's classifier outputs, aggregates and smooths each of them and draws one bag of size $1000$ from $V$ per estimate, sequentially, from the global NumPy state seeded by `random_state` at each call and restored afterwards, without writing `qp.environ["_R_SEED"]` (`_temp_np_seed`). The method is unchanged in distribution. The other sources were checked and are seeded or deterministic: the bootstrap wrappers `BootstrapCTCAP` and `BootstrapDirectCAP` (`np.random.RandomState(qp.environ["_R_SEED"])` at each call), the split of $V$ and the candidate bags of `PrediQuant`, `DoC`, the internal $5$-fold cross-validation of PACC and KDEy (stratified, not shuffled), the KDEy aggregation (started at the uniform prevalence); `L-CBPE` draws no random numbers. The BayCAP methods (`BayCAP`, `BayCAPKDEy`, `BayCAPMAPLS`) are not affected: NUTS, their MLP and their multinomial draws are seeded.
- **Estimand:** the realised table of the target sample, for the four re-run methods and for `SQS-H` (both SQS-H variants describe the realised table of a bag of $V$ at the estimated prevalence and are scored against `true_cts`).
- **Scope:** `cap-ml` (owned by the user; committed by the user): `src/cap/models/confidence.py` (`RQBS`, `_temp_np_seed`; `47643b1`, version 0.1.1.11, for FIX-3; the parameter `bootstrap`, `637fdd5`, version 0.1.1.13, for FIX-2), `tests/test_rqbs.py`, README changelog. UNCAWE: `experiments/methods.py` (`kdey()`, `pacc()`; `SQS-H` and `SQS-Hbs` in `METHOD_CLASS` and `gen_methods`), `experiments/binform.py` (`SQS-Hbs`), the tables and plots that name the method (`tables/main.py`, `tables/rank.py`, `tables/common.py`, `tables/error_split.py`, `tables/baycap_fixed.py`, `tables/baycap_kdey_hf.py`, `tables/binform.py`, `plots/common.py`), `scripts/migrate/rename_method.py`, tests (`tests/test_methods.py`: `SeededQuantifierTest`; `tests/test_rename_method.py`; the method name in the other tests), the pin bump (`pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock`); this document (§I.2, §II.2, §II.3, §II.4, §II.20, §V.4, the FIX-2 and FIX-3 entries of Part IV removed when done). The stored `RQBS.parquet` files are renamed `SQS-Hbs.parquet` in `main`, `main_pre_fix3` and `binform`; the older backups (`main_03_06_26`, `main_22_10_26`) keep the old name. The stored results of `QuAcc_NxN`, `O-LEAP`, `SQS-Hbs` and `PQ` in `main` are re-run, and `SQS-H` is run for the first time (the old ones moved to `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main_pre_fix3/`, same tree as `main/`); the tables of `main`, the rank tables, the coverage vs amplitude plot and the tables and plots of the error split and oracle 2×2 (§II.20) are regenerated. `binform` (which runs `O-LEAP` and `RQBS`) is not re-run by this card (user's decision of 2026-11-10: it will be re-run for Extension 5); `predist` (`PrediQuant` with `kdey()`, now seeded) is not re-run either.
- **Depends on:** none.
- **Comparability:** `SQS-Hbs` is `RQBS` under a new name: with `bootstrap=True` the draws of `cap-ml` 0.1.1.13 are those of 0.1.1.11 (`tests/test_rqbs.py` rebuilds them step by step), so the results of the first re-run (below) stay valid and are kept. `SQS-H` is new; its intervals are expected to be narrower than those of `SQS-Hbs`, which adds the sampling variability of $\hat\pi^Q$. The main tables, the rank tables, the coverage vs amplitude plot and the error split gain a column for `SQS-H`, and the ranks of the other methods can change by it. The pin bump also brings `cap-ml` `0f25e9e` (WP-15, version 0.1.1.12, the parameter `rho` of `PrediQuant`), which leaves `PQ` unchanged with `rho=None` (the split is the same `train_test_split`). The new results of the four methods are reproducible, but their stored results cannot be reproduced draw by draw: they differ from the new ones within the run-to-run variation of each method (measured for `PQ` in WP-14: the same MAE, bag-by-bag differences up to $3\cdot10^{-4}$ for vanilla accuracy). The paper's tables and figures for QuAcc, LEAP, SQS-H and SQS-S come from unseeded runs and change by that variation. The other methods of `main` are unchanged: the pin bump brings no change to `cap-ml` other than `RQBS` (`git diff 87b4d9b 47643b1`: `confidence.py`, where only `RQBS` and the new helper `_temp_np_seed` change, `tests/test_rqbs.py`, README, version).
- **Deviations from Part V:** none; the fix brings the four methods into line with the rule on seeds of §V.1.
- **Done when:** the `cap-ml` code and tests are committed, the pin is bumped, the UNCAWE tests pass, the re-run results of the four methods are stored in `main` for the $75$ pairs and the tables and plots listed in Scope are regenerated (runs at the user's request), and the branch is merged.

### WP-16 E6.1: the distances of SQS-S in `predist`, PACC(MLP), five classifiers, summary tables

- **Kind / status:** experiment / Done
- **Source:** [[ext6_sqs_s_design]] (E6.1)
- **Goal:** compare the $L_1$ distance used by SQS-S to select the candidate bags whose estimated prevalence is close to that of the target with Hellinger and Jensen–Shannon, which weigh differences in rare classes more, on the corrected `PrediQuant`. The `predist` diagnostic (§II.3) fits `PrediQuant` once per (dataset, classifier) pair and scores the three distances on the same $5$ test bags of size $1000$ (centre, three vertices at $1-0.01$, the random point farthest from them). The card extends it from one classifier (LR) to the five classifiers of `main` on the $8$ three-class datasets ($40$ pairs, $200$ test bags), with PACC(MLP), the quantifier of `PQ`, in place of KDEy(MLP) (user's decision of 2026-11-10), and adds the summary tables. Decision rule (user's decision of 2026-11-09 and 2026-11-10): $L_1$ is kept unless another distance has a lower mean Winkler score over the $200$ test bags, a lower per-pair mean Winkler score on a majority of the $40$ pairs, and a paired Wilcoxon signed-rank test over the $40$ per-pair means with $p<0.05$.
- **Estimand:** the realised table of each test bag (vanilla accuracy), for every distance.
- **Scope:**
  - `experiments/predist.py`, `predist_sampling.py`, `predist_config.py`: `--classifiers` accepting several classifiers (default: the five of `main`), replacing the LR-only filter `is_lr_classifier`; all (dataset, classifier) pairs in one parallel pool through `run_tasks` with `worker_environment`, as `main`; PACC(MLP) with the configuration of `PQ` in place of `kdey()`; the other settings of `PrediQuant` as `PQ` in `main`;
  - per test bag and distance, the mean $L_1$ distance between the true prevalences of the selected bags and the true prevalence of the test bag (stored, or computed from the stored `selected_val_true_prevs`); `RESULT_SCHEMA_VERSION` bumped;
  - new `tables/predist.py`: a summary table (one row per distance: means over the $200$ test bags of the absolute error, coverage, amplitude, Winkler score and true-space distance; the number of pairs where the distance has the best Winkler score; the $p$-value of the paired Wilcoxon test against $L_1$) and a per-region table (the same means split by centre, vertices, random point);
  - the ternary plots of `plots/predist.py`, unchanged, for every pair;
  - `Makefile` target for the tables; tests: one task per (dataset, classifier) pair; the quantifier is PACC(MLP); the true-space distance on a synthetic case; the table on a synthetic result.
- **Depends on:** FIX-1 (done, `fcdabf2`).
- **Comparability:** the stored `predist` results (LR, KDEy(MLP), re-run on 2026-11-09 with the fixed `PrediQuant`) are not comparable with the new ones; they are moved to `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/predist_kdey/` (results and plots) before the full run; those computed before FIX-1 stay in `predist_pre_fix1/`. `main` is not affected.
- **Deviations from Part V:** `predist` keeps its own parallel pool (`experiments/predist.py:run`, `cap.utils.commons.parallel` inside `worker_environment(THREAD_LIMITS)`, with the workers shut down before and after), which does what `run_tasks` does, instead of `run_tasks` itself: `run_tasks` saves `ExperimentOutcome`s in the `main` schema, while `predist` writes its own schema incrementally per (distance, pair). The tables are descriptive apart from the Wilcoxon test of the decision rule: no colour gradient and no $^\dagger$ mark (Part V.4), the best value of each measure in bold.
- **Done when:** the code and tests are merged; the full run over the $40$ pairs is done on `bcuda` at the user's request and its tables and plots are stored; the Analysis section of E6.1 is written, with the outcome of the decision rule.
- **Commands:** before the full run, move the stored results: `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/predist/` to `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/predist_kdey/` and `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/predist/` to `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/predist_kdey/` (on `bcuda`; schema 3, so `predist` would ignore them anyway). Pilot: `make run-predist ARGS="--datasets cmc --n-jobs 5"` (one dataset, the five classifiers). Full run: `make run-predist ARGS="--n-jobs 32"` (the $8$ datasets, the five classifiers, $40$ pairs in one pool; $32$ workers, within `cap.env["N_JOBS"]` on `bcuda`, Part V.1). Tables: `make run-tables-predist ARGS=--tables` on `bcuda`, then `make run-tables-predist WHERE=local ARGS=--pdf` after `make pull`. The ternary plots are written by the run itself (`--no-plot` disables them).
- **Log:** 2026-11-10: card added (user's decision). 2026-11-10: started; code and tests on `wp-16/predist-distances`: `--classifiers` (default the five of `main`) in place of the LR-only filter, PACC(MLP) with the settings of `PQ` (`build_prediquant`), the prediction through `PrediQuant.predict_range` and `last_selected_bags`, the columns `quantifier`, `region`, `true_dist_l1_mean`, `RESULT_SCHEMA_VERSION` 4, `tables/predist.py`, `make run-tables-predist`, `tests/test_predist.py`. 2026-11-10: at the user's request, without a pilot (deviation from the pilot-first rule, user's decision), the stored results of schema 3 were moved on `bcuda` to `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/predist_kdey/` and `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/plots/predist_kdey/`, and the full run `make run-predist ARGS="--n-jobs 32"` was done on `bcuda` (commit `6cc1aeb`, `cap-ml` `637fdd5`, 15:17–15:19, exit 0): $40$ pairs completed, none failed, $600$ rows ($40$ pairs × $5$ test bags × $3$ distances), $200$ ternary plots. 2026-11-10: tables generated on `bcuda` (`make run-tables-predist ARGS=--tables`, commit `ed55171`), pulled, PDFs built locally; outputs under `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/tables/predist/`; decision rule: $L_1$ kept (Hellinger and Jensen–Shannon have a lower mean Winkler score but are better than $L_1$ on only $17$ and $16$ of the $40$ pairs, Wilcoxon $p=0.63$ and $0.64$). 2026-11-10: after looking at the ternary plots, two diagnostics added to `tables/predist.py` at the user's request (`test_prev_l1_error`, the $L_1$ error of the estimated prevalence of the test bag; `pred_dist_l1_mean`, the mean $L_1$ distance of the selected bags from it in estimated-prevalence space) and a per-pair, per-region CSV (`predist_pair_region.csv`); tables re-generated on `bcuda` (commit `54742e9`), pulled, PDFs built locally; Analysis section of E6.1 written in [[ext6_sqs_s_design]]; the why of E6.c2 extended with the size of the neighbourhood at the vertices (user's decision). 2026-11-10: merged and closed.
- **Outcome:** merged into `devel` on 2026-11-10 (`35a46d2`), at the user's request, without a rebase (`devel` had not moved). Part II: §II.3 (the code) and §II.22 (the run and the findings). Decisions and changes that came up during the card: `predist` keeps its own parallel pool instead of `run_tasks` (Deviations); the prediction goes through the public `PrediQuant.predict_range` and `last_selected_bags` instead of the private `_predict_test_prior` and `_predict_closest_idx`, with the same selected bags; the full run was done without a pilot (user's decision); after the plots, two diagnostics and a per-pair, per-region CSV were added to `tables/predist.py` (user's decision), not in the card's scope; the decision rule keeps $L_1$. Follow-ups: the size of the neighbourhood at the vertices, set by $\gamma$ and the uniform candidates, is recorded in the why of E6.c2 (not scheduled); WP-17 (E6.2) is next for Ext. 6.

### WP-15 Allocation $\rho$ of the validation set in `PrediQuant` (`cap-ml`), nested splits, pin bump

- **Kind / status:** method / Done
- **Source:** [[ext6_sqs_s_design]] (E6.2, Implementation)
- **Goal:** SQS-S (`cap-ml` `PrediQuant`, `PQ` in `main`) splits the validation set $V$ into $V_q$, which trains the quantifier, and $V_b$, from which the candidate bags are drawn, at a fixed $50\%/50\%$ (a stratified `train_test_split`). Add a parameter `rho` to `PrediQuant` so that the share $\rho=\lvert V_q\rvert/\lvert V\rvert$ can be varied, with nested splits across levels, and keep per prediction the quantities needed to analyse it. Behaviour (user's decision of 2026-11-09, option (a)): `rho=None` (default) keeps the current `train_test_split(test_size=0.5, random_state=random_state, stratify=y)`, so `PQ` is unchanged; with `rho` in $(0,1)$, for each class $\omega_j$ one permutation of its validation indices seeded by `random_state`, and $V_q$ the first $\operatorname{round}(\rho\lvert V_j\rvert)$ indices of each class, so that $V_q(0.1)\subset V_q(0.3)\subset\dots\subset V_q(0.9)$ for the same seed. After each prediction `PrediQuant` keeps the estimated prevalence $\hat\pi^Q$ of the target bag and the indices (in $V_b$) of the instances of the selected bags, from which the class support is computed: for each class, the number of distinct instances of that class in $V_b$ that appear in at least one selected bag, divided by the number of instances of that class in $V_b$.
- **Estimand:** unchanged: SQS-S targets the realised table of the target bag.
- **Scope:** `cap-ml` (owned by the user; the commit is pushed by the user): `cap/models/confidence.py` (`PrediQuant`: `rho`, the nested split, the stored $\hat\pi^Q$ and selected indices), `tests/test_prediquant.py`, README changelog. UNCAWE: the pin bump (`pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock`), §I.2 (the `rho` parameter, `PQ` with `rho=None`), §II.21 and §II.12 if UNCAWE code depends on the new attributes. Tests: with `rho=None` the split has the same indices as `train_test_split`; with `rho` set, $V_q$ and $V_b$ are disjoint, cover $V$, have $\operatorname{round}(\rho\lvert V_j\rvert)$ instances of class $\omega_j$ in $V_q$, and are nested across levels; the quantifier never sees $V_b$ and a target equal to a candidate bag gets the bag's estimate, at every $\rho$; the stored $\hat\pi^Q$ equals `q.predict` on the target; the class support on bags whose instances are known by construction.
- **Depends on:** FIX-1 (done, `fcdabf2`).
- **Comparability:** with `rho=None` the stored `PQ` results are unchanged; the pin bump must bring no other change to `PrediQuant` or to the other `cap-ml` methods used by `main` (to check on the diff between `e9e77cd` and the new commit), otherwise the affected methods are listed and re-run.
- **Deviations from Part V:** none expected.
- **Done when:** the `cap-ml` code and tests are committed and pushed, the pin is bumped, and the UNCAWE tests pass.
- **Commands:** none (no run).
- **Log:** 2026-11-10: card added (user's decision). 2026-11-10: started on branch `wp-15/prediquant-rho` (worktree `../uncawe-wp15`), from `devel` `5a998ed`, while the FIX-3 re-run of `main` runs on `bcuda` from `fix-3/seed-randomness`; the `cap-ml` change is written on top of `47643b1` (FIX-3), so the pin bump of this card also brings the seeded `RQBS` and the card is merged after FIX-3. 2026-11-10: `PrediQuant` code, tests and changelog written in `cap-ml` (`rho`, `_split`, `Vq_idx`/`Vb_idx`, `sigma_indices`, `Vb_y`, `last_test_prev`, `last_selected_bags`, `class_support`; a $\rho$ outside $(0,1)$ or one that empties a class in $V_1$ or $V_2$ raises `ValueError`, an addition to the card); `cap-ml` tests: `tests/test_prediquant.py` 25, all passing, the collectable suite 155, all passing (six legacy modules fail at import also before the change); committed and pushed by the user as `0f25e9e` (version 0.1.1.12). `git diff 47643b1 0f25e9e` touches only `PrediQuant` (and its tests, README, version); since `devel` pins `87b4d9b`, the bump also brings `47643b1` (seeded `RQBS`, FIX-3). Pin bumped to `0f25e9e` in `pyproject.toml` and `uv.lock` (`uv lock` updated only `cap-ml`); UNCAWE unit tests: 271, all passing (`9ad4fd5`). 2026-11-10: at the user's request, branch rebased on `devel`, which meanwhile pins `cap-ml` `637fdd5` (version 0.1.1.13, FIX-2 + FIX-3), a descendant of `0f25e9e`: the pin bump of this card was dropped and `devel`'s pin kept; `git diff 87b4d9b 637fdd5` touches only `PrediQuant` (this card) and `RQBS` (FIX-2 + FIX-3, which re-runs its methods). §I.2 reduced to a pointer and the description moved to §II.21; card closed. UNCAWE unit tests after the rebase: 277, all passing.
- **Outcome:** merged into `devel` on 2026-11-10 (`a313f8f`), at the user's request, after a rebase on `devel`. Part II: §II.21. Decisions and changes that came up during the card: the work ran in a separate worktree while the FIX-3 run used the main checkout; `rho` outside $(0,1)$ or emptying a class in $V_1$ or $V_2$ raises `ValueError` (not in the card's spec); the card's own pin bump (`0f25e9e`) was dropped at the rebase, because FIX-2 + FIX-3 had meanwhile pinned `637fdd5`, which contains it; the six `cap-ml` test modules that fail at import (`quacc`, `OUT_DIR`) are pre-existing and not touched. Follow-ups: WP-17 (E6.2) uses `rho`, `last_test_prev` and `class_support`; when it does, add the `PQ-rho*` methods to `experiments/methods.py` outside `MAIN_METHODS`.

### WP-14 E4.1 + E4.3: the error split and the oracle 2×2 of the main-table methods on stored `main` results

- **Kind / status:** analysis / Done (2026-11-10)
- **Source:** [[ext4_uncertainty_budget]] (E4.1, E4.3)
- **Result:** §II.20 (code, run, findings)
- **Goal (as planned):** for each main-table method that estimates a contingency table, split its error $\hat A-A(h,U)$ into the prevalence part $e_1=\sum_j(\tilde\pi_j-\pi^Q_j)\tilde d_j$, the recall part $e_2=\sum_j\pi^Q_j(\tilde d_j-d^{\text{pool}}_j)$ and the luck of the bag $e_3=\sum_j\pi^Q_j(d^{\text{pool}}_j-c_j/n_j)$, where $\tilde\pi_j$ and $\tilde d_j$ are the column sums and the recalls of the method's mean table, $\pi^Q$ and $c_j/n_j$ come from the realised table of the bag, and $d^{\text{pool}}$ are the recalls of $h$ on the test pool, which stand for the population recalls; the aim is to see which part drives the error of each method in the paper's setting (E4.1). After estimation, replace the prevalences with the true ones and the recalls with the pool ones, in a $2\times2$ design whose interaction is the cross term $x=\sum_j(\tilde\pi_j-\pi^Q_j)(\tilde d_j-d^{\text{pool}}_j)$, to measure how much the error falls when each component is known (E4.3). Methods: `QuAcc_NxN`, `O-LEAP`, `RQBS`, `PQ`, `BayesCAP-real`, `BayesCAP-Hf-real`, `BayCAP-KDEy-mlp-real` (`DoC` estimates no table and is left out). Setting: vanilla accuracy, $\lvert U\rvert=1000$, 100 bags per pair, 75 pairs. E4.1 and E4.3 share one card (user's decision of 2026-11-09) but keep separate representations.
- **Estimand:** the realised table of each bag (`true_cts`), for every method. The split and the oracle cells use the true prevalence of the bag and the labels of the pool, so they are an analysis, not methods; the three oracle cells are references. E4.3 also scores against the pool accuracy $\sum_j\pi^Q_jd^{\text{pool}}_j$, a reference that removes $e_3$.
- **Scope:**
  - new `src/uncawe/error_split.py` (pure numpy, to be reused by E4.2): $\tilde\pi$ and $\tilde d$ from a stored `estim_cts` (transposed: true classes on rows; normalised); $\pi^Q$ and $c_j/n_j$ from `true_cts`; $d^{\text{pool}}$ from the argmax of the pretrain `U_posteriors` against the pool labels; the parts $e_1$, $e_2$, $e_3$, the cross term $x$ and the four oracle estimates. Conventions (user's decision of 2026-11-09): a class absent from the bag ($n_j=0$) contributes $0$ to $e_3$; a class with an empty estimated column ($\tilde\pi_j=0$) gets $\tilde d_j:=d^{\text{pool}}_j$, so its whole contribution goes to $e_1$; such bags are counted;
  - new `tables/error_split.py`:
    - a per-bag CSV;
    - E4.1 table A (RMS): one row per pair (dataset × classifier, grouped by dataset), a binary and a multiclass block, each closed by a mean row and by a row with the share of pairs in which each part is the largest; column groups $e_1$ (7 methods), $e_2$ (7), $e_3$ (one column, the same for every method) and total error (7). In each row of the $e_1$, $e_2$ and total groups, colours run from the worst (red) to the best (green) method, as `tables/baycap_fixed.py:_score_cell_styles`, with the best in bold; $e_3$ is not coloured. The total-error cell of each method carries a superscript $^{1}$, $^{2}$ or $^{3}$ naming its part with the largest RMS (user's decision of 2026-11-09). A note says that the mean squares of the parts do not add up to that of the total, because of the cross products;
    - E4.1 table B (signed means): the same layout and blocks, with the mean of $e_1$, $e_2$ and $e_3$ over the bags, without colouring (user's decision of 2026-11-09);
    - E4.3 table: the same rows and blocks; column groups for the four cells $(\tilde\pi,\tilde d)$, $(\pi^Q,\tilde d)$, $(\tilde\pi,d^{\text{pool}})$, $(\pi^Q,d^{\text{pool}})$, each with the mean AE of the 7 methods against the realised accuracy, coloured in each row and group as table A; a group with the mean and the RMS of $x$, not coloured. The scores against the pool accuracy go in a second table with the same layout;
  - new `plots/error_split.py`: the E4.1 bar plot (mean square of each part, stacked, one bar per method, one panel per dataset);
  - `Makefile` targets `tables-error-split` and `plot-error-split`;
  - tests: $e_1+e_2+e_3=\hat A-A(h,U)$ on random tables; reading a transposed table gives the column sums and recalls of the paper's orientation; with $\tilde\pi=\pi^Q$ and $\tilde d=d^{\text{pool}}$, $e_1=e_2=0$; the four oracle errors equal $e_1+e_2+e_3$, $e_2+e_3$, $e_1-x+e_3$ and $e_3$; the conventions for $n_j=0$ and $\tilde\pi_j=0$; the cell styles and superscripts on a synthetic table.
- **Depends on:** FIX-1 (done, `fcdabf2`; the gate of Extension 4 marked as met in `80dfb24`); the stored `main` results with `PQ` re-run; the pretrain files of the test pool.
- **Comparability:** reads stored results, except `PQ`: `cap-ml` `e9e77cd` stores no table for `PQ` (`PrediQuant` was a direct method), so the card re-runs `PQ` in `main` with `cap-ml` `87b4d9b` (version 0.1.1.10), where `PrediQuant` is a contingency-table method whose table range is the tables of the selected candidate bags and whose mean table has, for the vanilla accuracy, the trace of the old estimate (user's decision of 2026-11-10). The selection of the bags is unchanged, so the re-run was expected to give the stored estimates; it does not, because `PQ` varies from run to run (its quantifier's MLP is unseeded, FIX-3), and the new `PQ` results, with the same error level, replace the FIX-1 ones (kept in `main_pq_pre_wp14/`; see the log). The `PQ` columns of the main and rank tables and of the coverage vs amplitude plot change accordingly. No other method changes (`git diff e9e77cd 87b4d9b` touches only `PrediQuant`).
- **Deviations from Part V:** the tables are descriptive, with no $^\dagger$ marks and no Wilcoxon test (§V.4, content practice); the E4.1 and E4.3 tables are in one module, `tables/error_split.py`, selected with a `--tables-set` option (the analogue of `--figures`, §V.5). Choices made in the implementation (2026-11-09, on the branch, open to the user's review): $e_3$ and the cell $(\pi^Q,d^{\text{pool}})$ use no estimate, so each has a single column computed on all the bags of the pair, while the part with the largest RMS of a method compares its $e_1$ and $e_2$ with the $e_3$ of the bags it kept; the row "largest part" of table A shows, for each method, the share of pairs in which $e_1$ is largest under the $e_1$ group, $e_2$ under the $e_2$ group and $e_3$ under the total group ($e_3$ has one column); table B and the E4.3 tables close each block with the mean row only; the group of $x$ has a mean and an RMS column per method (14 columns); the standalone documents are on A3 landscape; the bar plot averages the mean squares over the classifiers of each dataset and marks the mean square of the total error with a dot. 2026-11-10 (user's decision): the bar plot leaves out `QuAcc_NxN`, whose errors flatten the other bars, and shows it in a separate figure, `--figures bars-quacc` (one bar per dataset); the scale stays linear, since a log scale distorts stacked segments.
- **Done when:** the code and tests are merged; the full analysis is run on `bcuda` at the user's request and its tables and plot are stored; the Analysis sections of E4.1 and E4.3 are written. Checks: $\lvert e_1+e_2+e_3-(\texttt{estim\_accs}-\texttt{true\_accs})\rvert<10^{-9}$ on every bag kept; the dropped bags (NUTS failures) and the bags with an empty estimated column are counted per pair and method.
- **Commands:** re-run of `PQ` on `bcuda` (the default), from the branch of the card, after moving every `output/uncawe/main/**/PQ.parquet` to the same path under `output/uncawe/main_pq_pre_wp14/`: `make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods PQ"` (no pilot). Then the analysis, no pilot. Tables (on `bcuda`): `make run-tables-error-split ARGS=--tables` (both sets; one set with `ARGS="--tables --tables-set e4.1"` or `e4.3`); PDFs where `pdflatex` is installed: `make run-tables-error-split WHERE=local ARGS=--pdf`. Bar plots: `make run-plot-error-split` (both figures; one with `ARGS="--figures bars"` or `bars-quacc`).

First run of the tables and plot on `bcuda` (exit status $0$): every `PQ` bag dropped, since `main` stores no table for `PQ`, a direct method in `cap-ml` `e9e77cd`; `QuAcc_NxN` has an identity gap up to $1.5\cdot10^{-6}$ on 11 pairs (stored `estim_accs` against the trace of the stored mean table). 2026-11-10: at the user's decision, `PrediQuant` made a contingency-table method in `cap-ml` (written here, committed by the user as `87b4d9b`, version 0.1.1.10); `pyproject.toml` and `uv.lock` pin it (`uv lock` updated only `cap-ml`); unit tests: 270, all passing (`a9ea4ed`). `make setup` on `bcuda` (`cap-ml` 0.1.1.10 installed); the $225$ `PQ.parquet` files of `main` moved to `output/uncawe/main_pq_pre_wp14/`; full run `make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods PQ"` from `a9ea4ed`, 00:20–00:35, log `output/runs/main-classic_20261110-002029.out`: exit status $0$, $225$ of $225$ files, no errors. ### FIX-1 + FIX-4 SQS-S fix in `cap-ml`, `cap-ml` pinned to a commit, `PQ` re-run in `main`

- **Kind / status:** fix / Done (2026-11-09)
- **Source:** Part IV, FIX-1 (SQS-S fits its quantifier on all of $V$, and estimates the candidate bags and the target with different estimators) and FIX-4 (`cap-ml` taken from a branch, `uv.lock` not tracked); scheduled together by the user on 2026-11-09, because the SQS-S fix lives in `cap-ml` and reaches UNCAWE only through a new pin.
- **Goal:** make `PQ` the paper's SQS-S and make the `cap-ml` version of every run explicit. In `cap-ml` (commit `e9e77cd`, version 0.1.1.9): `PrediQuant` trains its quantifier on $V_1$ only, so that it never sees the bag-sampling subset $V_2$, and estimates the prevalences of the candidate bags and of the target with the same pipeline, the quantifier's own classifier followed by its aggregation; the classifier $h$ is used only for the contingency tables of the bags; `reuse_h` is removed; tests in `tests/test_prediquant.py`. In UNCAWE: `pyproject.toml` takes `cap-ml` at that commit and `uv.lock` is tracked. Then `PQ` is re-run in `main` (classic domain, $75$ pairs, the three metrics, $100$ bags per pair, the seeds of `main`), so that the stored `PQ` results are those of the fixed SQS-S (added to the card by the user on 2026-11-09). `predist` is not re-run by this card.
- **Estimand:** realised table of the target sample, as `PQ` in `main` (unchanged).
- **Scope:** `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock`, `.gitignore`; this document (Part I, the convention on the `cap-ml` pin in Part V, the entries IV.1 and IV.4 removed); the status of FIX-1 in [[ext4_uncertainty_budget]]; the stored `PQ` results of `main` (the old ones moved to `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main_pq_pre_fix1/`, same tree as `main/`).
- **Depends on:** none.
- **Comparability:** from the previously locked `cap-ml` commit (`bb20aa5`) to `e9e77cd` only `PrediQuant` changes (`git diff bb20aa5 e9e77cd`), so no other method of `main` is affected. The stored `PQ` results of `main` and all `predist` results were computed with the old `PrediQuant` and are not comparable with new ones; the `PQ` results of `main` are re-run by this card, the `predist` results are not. Which `cap-ml` commit produced the stored results of the other methods is not recorded (`uv.lock` was not tracked).
- **Deviations from Part V:** none.
- **Done when:** the pin and the tracked `uv.lock` are merged into `devel`, the unit tests pass, and the re-run `PQ` results are stored in `main` for the $75$ pairs; §I.2 then records the run (`cap-ml` commit, date) in place of "not re-run".
- **Commands:** on `bcuda` (the default), from the branch of the card (the remote working tree is pushed and the environment synced from the tracked `uv.lock`). Old results moved aside first (`main` skips the cells already stored): every `output/uncawe/main/**/PQ.parquet` to the same path under `output/uncawe/main_pq_pre_fix1/`. Full run: `make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods PQ"`; `make logs EXP=main-classic` shows the output, `make explog EXP=main` the experiment log. No pilot (user's decision of 2026-11-09).
- **Log:** 2026-11-09: scheduled by the user; started on branch `fix-1-4/sqs-s-cap-pin`. The `PrediQuant` fix was written in `cap-ml` and pushed by the user as `e9e77cd`. `pyproject.toml` pins `cap-ml` at `e9e77cdb4e25dde3b43cdd070a80beda0a066c22`; `uv lock` updated only `cap-ml` (`bb20aa5` → `e9e77cd`); `uv.lock` removed from `.gitignore` and tracked. Unit tests: 254, all passing with the new pin (`c329fc2`).
