### WP-12 E2.9: BayCAP-Hfn, BayCAP-Hf with non-centred shares of the mistakes, on the pairs of `main`

- **Kind / status:** experiment / Done (check and pilot only; full run not done, user decision)
- **Source:** [[ext2_baycap]] (E2.9, §T.17)
- **Goal:** run BayCAP-Hf (`BayesCAP-Hf-real`, WP-11) with the shares of the mistakes sampled in a non-centred form, the same model, to remove the divergences of its centred Dirichlet. Background: BayCAP-Hf has $m\sim U(0,1)$, $1/(1+\nu)\sim U(0,1)$, recalls $C_{jj}\mid m,\nu\sim\operatorname{Beta}(\nu m,\nu(1-m))$, and the off-diagonal cells of column $j$ equal to $(1-C_{jj})s_j$ with $s_j\mid\kappa\sim\operatorname{Dir}(\kappa\mathbf 1_{K-1})$ and $1/(1+\kappa)\sim U(0,1)$. Its run (E2.8) found it the best BayCAP prior on the $52$ pairs where NUTS is reliable, but more than $1\%$ of the draws divergent on $18$ pairs where BayCAP-H was clean (all of `poker_hand` and `isolet`, `nursery` LR, SVM, MLP, RF, `wine-quality` LR, SVM, RF, `shuttle` MLP), where the posterior median of $\kappa$ is about $e^{-3}$ to $e^{-1.7}$ and the shares of columns with few observed mistakes follow $\kappa$ through a funnel.
  - *Method:* `BayesCAP-Hfn-real`: the model of `BayesCAP-Hf-real`; for $K\ge3$, for each off-diagonal cell, $G'_{ij}\sim\operatorname{Gamma}(\kappa+1,1)$ and $e_{ij}\sim\operatorname{Exp}(1)$, $\ln g_{ij}=\ln G'_{ij}-e_{ij}/\kappa$, $s_{ij}=g_{ij}/\sum_{k\ne j}g_{kj}$ (computed in logs), which gives exactly $s_j\mid\kappa\sim\operatorname{Dir}(\kappa\mathbf 1_{K-1})$. Recalls and $\nu$ centred as in BayCAP-Hf; likelihood, priors, NUTS settings and default seed as `BayesCAP`; realised pushforward only; no bag discarded for divergences. With $K=2$ the model and the coordinates are those of BayCAP-Hf.
  - *Before any run:* a prior-only unit test that the new shares have the law $\operatorname{Dir}(\kappa\mathbf 1_{K-1})$ for $\kappa\in\{0.05,0.2,1,5\}$ and $K\in\{3,8,26\}$ (means $1/(K-1)$, variance $(K-2)/((K-1)^2((K-1)\kappa+1))$, a Kolmogorov–Smirnov test of one share against $\operatorname{Beta}(\kappa,(K-2)\kappa)$); and, at no cost, on the stored E2.8 results, per pair with $K\ge3$ from $V$, the number of columns with no observed mistake and the median over the columns of $(n_j-f_{jj})/(K-1)$, with their Spearman correlation across the $50$ pairs with the share of divergent draws of BayCAP-Hf, next to that of the share of empty off-diagonal cells.
  - *Runs:* in `main`, all $75$ pairs, the $100$ UPP bags of size $1000$ of `main`, the three metrics of `main`; a pilot first on `poker_hand` LR, `nursery` LR, `isolet` kNN and `letter` kNN (the control, a table with many mistakes per column, reliable in E2.8).
  - *Measures* (vanilla accuracy unless stated): AE, coverage, amplitude and Winkler score next to the stored `BayesCAP-Hf-real`, `BayesCAP-H-real`, `BayesCAP-EB-real` and `BayesCAP-real`; paired bootstrap tests of the mean difference of AE and of the Winkler score, BayCAP-Hfn minus `BayesCAP-Hf-real`, Holm over the $75$ pairs; per bag, the NUTS diagnostics on the same quantities as E2.8 ($\pi^Q$, $C$, $m$, $\rho$, $1/(1+\kappa)$; divergences, largest split-$\hat R$, smallest effective sample size) and failed bags; per pair, the share of divergent bags, the mean number of divergent transitions per bag, the share of divergent draws and the failure class of E2.8 ($\hat R$ above $1.1$ or more than $1\%$ of the draws divergent; funnel if the mean posterior $P(\nu>\min_jn_j)$ exceeds $0.05$, else boundary, else other) for both methods; per bag, the posterior of $m$, $\ln\nu$, $P(\nu>\min_jn_j)$, $\ln\kappa$, $P(\kappa<1)$; the mean time per bag of both methods.
  - *Tables and plots:* a comparison table and a sampler table (one row per pair, by dataset and $K$; CSV, LaTeX, landscape PDF), with the indicators of the check from $V$; three scatters: share of divergent draws of BayCAP-Hfn against BayCAP-Hf, marked by the failure class of E2.8; Winkler score and posterior median of $\ln\kappa$ of the two, reliable and unreliable pairs of E2.8 marked apart; share of divergent draws of BayCAP-Hf against the observed mistakes per off-diagonal cell and against the share of empty cells.
  - *Reading, fixed before the run:* judged on the sampler only; on the $52$ pairs reliable in E2.8 the scores and the posteriors of $\ln\kappa$ and $\ln\nu$ must agree with BayCAP-Hf (otherwise the run is not used until understood); it works if the $18$ boundary pairs pass the failure rule and no reliable pair gets a failure class; it replaces BayCAP-Hf as the candidate method if it has fewer unreliable pairs and no new ones; if the control gets worse, a mixed form (non-centred only in the columns with few observed mistakes) is proposed as a candidate.
- **Estimand:** realised table of the target bag for `BayesCAP-Hfn-real`; scored against the realised table (`true_cts`). The check uses only $V$ and stored diagnostics.
- **Scope:** `methods/baycap.py` (an option for the coordinates of the shares of `c_prior="hierarchical-f"`, default the centred form of WP-11, which keeps its sites and draws; docstring; tests); `experiments/methods.py` (`BayesCAP-Hfn-real` in `BAYCAP_VARIANTS`, excluded from the tables, ranks and plots of `main`); a new table and plot module reusing `tables/baycap_hf.py`; labels in `tables/common.py` and `plots/common.py`; `Makefile` targets; §II.5, §II.12 of the work plan.
- **Depends on:** WP-11 (`c_prior="hierarchical-f"`, the stored results of `BayesCAP-Hf-real`, `tables/baycap_hf.py`); WP-10 and WP-6 (stored `BayesCAP-H-real`, `BayesCAP-EB-real`, `BayesCAP-real` with diagnostics).
- **Comparability:** adds one method to `main`; the stored results of every other method are untouched; same bags, seeds and NUTS settings as `BayesCAP-Hf-real`, so the comparison is bag by bag, but the draws differ for $K\ge3$ (NUTS moves in other coordinates), so the two are compared as samplers of the same posterior; until its results are stored, every pair counts as incomplete for `check_missing.py` (§V.1).
- **Deviations from Part V:** the pilot uses LR and kNN on four datasets, chosen for the boundary pairs of E2.8 and one control.
- **Done when:** `BayesCAP-Hfn-real` has stored results with diagnostics for all $75$ pairs and the three metrics, the check and the E2.9 tables and plots are generated; the results are described in Part II with their location, the `cap-ml` commit and the date of the run.
- **Commands:** all on `bcuda` (the default), each in its own tmux session; `make logs EXP=<target>` shows the output, `make explog EXP=main` the experiment log.
  - *Check, before the pilot (no NUTS run):* `make run-tables-baycap-hfn ARGS=--check` (reads the CSV of `tables/baycap_hf.py` and the validation counts; writes `baycap_hfn_check.csv`), then `make pull`.
  - *Pilot:* the four pairs of the card, in two commands, since `main` crosses `--datasets` with `--classifiers`: `make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods BayesCAP-Hfn-real --datasets poker_hand nursery --classifiers LR"`, then `make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods BayesCAP-Hfn-real --datasets isolet letter --classifiers kNN"` (the BayCAP pool runs on CPU, `BAYES_ENV`).
  - *Full:* `make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods BayesCAP-Hfn-real"` ($75\times100=7\,500$ NUTS runs, slower than WP-11 at large $K$, cost to be read from the pilot); cells already stored, e.g. by the pilot, are skipped.
  - *Tables:* `make run-tables-baycap-hfn ARGS=--tables` (needs the stored results of `BayesCAP-Hf-real`, `BayesCAP-H-real`, `BayesCAP-EB-real` and `BayesCAP-real`; it works on the pairs stored for all five methods, so also after the pilot), then `make pull` and `make run-tables-baycap-hfn WHERE=local ARGS=--pdf` where `pdflatex` is installed.
  - *Plots:* `make run-plot-baycap-hfn` (reads the CSVs of the tables and of the check; `ARGS="--figures sampler"` for one figure), then `make pull`.
- **Log:** 2026-11-03: card added at the user's request. 2026-11-03: implementation started on branch `wp-12/baycap-hfn`. `BayCAP(shares=...)` in `methods/baycap.py`: `"centred"` (default) keeps the Dirichlet site of WP-11 and its draws; `"non-centred"` samples `h_shares_gamma` ($\operatorname{Gamma}(\kappa+1,1)$) and `h_shares_exp` ($\operatorname{Exp}(1)$), as $(K, K-1)$ event sites, and makes `h_shares` deterministic (`noncentred_shares`, a softmax of $\ln G'-e/\kappa$ over each column); only allowed with `c_prior="hierarchical-f"`. The NUTS diagnostics and the posterior summaries are computed on the same sites as for BayCAP-Hf, not on the new ones. `BayesCAP-Hfn-real` is in `BAYCAP_VARIANTS` with the prior of BayCAP-Hf and in a new `BAYCAP_OPTIONS` (`{"shares": "non-centred"}`), which `gen_methods` passes to `BayCAP`, so the `(estimand, c_prior)` pairs of `BAYCAP_VARIANTS` and their users are unchanged; it is left out of the tables, ranks and plots of `main` (`tables/main.py:EXCLUDED_METHODS`). New modules `tables/baycap_hfn.py` (comparison and sampler tables, `analysis_summary` by the class of BayCAP-Hf in E2.8; `--check` for the check of the hypothesis, from the CSV of `tables/baycap_hf.py` and the validation counts, with `check_summary`) and `plots/baycap_hfn.py` (`sampler`, `model`, `check`); `make` targets `tables-baycap-hfn`, `plot-baycap-hfn`. Implementation choices (not in the card): the failure class of both samplers is recomputed with `tables/baycap_hf.py:failure_class` (for BayCAP-Hf it equals that of the E2.8 CSV); the bootstrap generator of each test depends on the seed, the pair and the score; the time per bag is the mean of the stored `t_test_ave`; the prior-only test of the law of the shares runs in float64 (`jax.enable_x64`), and its Kolmogorov–Smirnov test is on $\min(s,1-s)$, with $1-s$ the sum of the other shares, since in float64 a share of $\operatorname{Beta}(0.05,0.05)$ rounds to $1$ about $8\%$ of the time. Tests in `tests/test_baycap.py`, `tests/test_baycap_hfn.py`, `tests/test_methods.py`; 196 tests pass. Not run. 2026-11-03: check run by the user (`--check`): Spearman correlation with the share of divergent draws of BayCAP-Hf $0.72$ for the share of empty off-diagonal cells, $0.37$ for the columns with no observed mistake, $-0.29$ for the median mistakes per cell, so the hypothesis of E2.8 is not supported (kept for the analysis, user decision). 2026-11-03: pilot run by the user (both commands), tables and plots generated: BayCAP-Hfn diverges more than BayCAP-Hf on the three boundary pairs (`nursery` LR $5.2\to45.6\%$ of the draws, `poker_hand` LR $65.5\to99.8\%$, `isolet` kNN $34.6\to97.7\%$) and keeps the control `letter` kNN clean, with the same posterior; the reading rule fails on every boundary pair of the pilot. User decision: no full run, BayCAP-Hf stays the candidate method. 2026-11-03, outside the card, on `devel` and merged into the branch: the median over the bags of the largest split-$\hat R$, the share of bags above $1.1$ and the median smallest ESS in `tables/baycap_hf.py` (`mixing_spread`, `04ffe61`); candidate E2.c7, a reference run of BayCAP-Hf on its unreliable pairs (`9fd31d8`). E2.9 analysis written; card closed at the user's request; results in §II.19. 197 tests pass.
- **Outcome:** merged into `devel` on 2026-11-03 (`d59c5e1`), at the user's request; `devel` had been merged into the branch twice (`d7febe7`, `dbde9d1`) for the two changes outside the card, so no rebase. Deviation from `Done when`: closed after the check and the pilot, without the full run (user decision), because the pilot failed the reading rule fixed before the run on all three boundary pairs; results are stored for four pairs only. The fallback of the card, a form non-centred only in the columns with few observed mistakes, was not proposed, because `isolet` kNN, the pair with the fewest mistakes per cell, also got worse. Problems: the non-centred form keeps the model (same posterior on the control) but moves the funnel to the cells that $V$ informs (a hypothesis, not tested). The non-centred code stays as a tested option of `BayCAP` (`shares`), not used by any method of the paper. Unit tests: 197, all passing at the merge. Follow-ups: candidate E2.c7 (a reference run of BayCAP-Hf on its unreliable pairs); a cell-wise mix of the two forms, recorded as a hypothesis in E2.9; whether `BayesCAP-Hf-real` enters the tables, ranks and plots of `main` is not decided.


Cards that are done, most recent first. Each entry keeps the history of the card (goal as planned, dependencies, deviations, log, outcome); what the card implemented and, for experiments and analyses, the run and the findings are described in the Part II (or Part I) section it points to, and are not repeated here.

### WP-11 E2.8: BayCAP-Hf, a lighter tail on the strength and learned shares of the mistakes, on the pairs of `main`

- **Kind / status:** experiment / Done (2026-11-03)
- **Source:** [[ext2_baycap]] (E2.8, §T.16)
- **Result:** §II.18 (code, run, findings)
- **Goal (as planned):** run a variant of BayCAP-H (`BayesCAP-H-real`, WP-10) with two fixes, directly on all pairs (user decision of 2026-11-02: no zero-cost checks first). Background: BayCAP-H puts a hierarchical prior on the recalls ($m\sim U(0,1)$, $1/\sqrt{1+\nu}\sim U(0,1)$, $C_{jj}\mid m,\nu\sim\operatorname{Beta}(\nu m,\nu(1-m))$) and shares the mistakes of each true class by $\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1_{K-1})$. Its run (E2.7) found that (1) NUTS does not mix on $7$ pairs (`mammographic` LR, kNN, RF; `hand_digits`, all four) because $V$ does not fix $\nu$ and the posterior keeps the heavy tail of the prior, $P(\nu>x)=(1+x)^{-1/2}$, reaching a funnel; (2) on `wine-quality` and `poker_hand` the uniform shares fill the cells of $C$ that are empty on $V$, displacing the estimated prevalences, and the empirical-Bayes prior does clearly better there.
  - *Method:* `BayesCAP-Hf-real`, `BayCAP` with a variant of the hierarchical prior: $m\sim U(0,1)$; $\rho=1/(1+\nu)\sim U(0,1)$, i.e. $p(\nu)=(1+\nu)^{-2}$; the recalls as in BayCAP-H; the off-diagonal cells of column $j$ equal to $(1-C_{jj})s_j$ with $s_j\mid\kappa\sim\operatorname{Dir}(\kappa\mathbf 1_{K-1})$ and $1/(1+\kappa)\sim U(0,1)$, i.e. $p(\kappa)=(1+\kappa)^{-2}$ (no $\kappa$ and no shares for $K=2$). Likelihood, prior on $\pi^Q$, NUTS settings and default seed as `BayesCAP`; realised pushforward only; centred parametrisation, no other sampler, no bound on $\nu$ or $\kappa$, no bag discarded for divergences.
  - *Runs:* in `main`, all $75$ pairs, the $100$ UPP bags of size $1000$ of `main`, the three metrics of `main`; a pilot first on `mammographic` LR, `hand_digits` kNN, `wine-quality` LR, `poker_hand` LR.
  - *Measures* (vanilla accuracy unless stated): AE, coverage, amplitude and Winkler score next to the stored `BayesCAP-H-real`, `BayesCAP-EB-real` and `BayesCAP-real`; paired bootstrap tests of the mean difference of the Winkler score and of AE, BayCAP-Hf minus `BayesCAP-H-real` and minus `BayesCAP-EB-real`, Holm over the $75$ pairs; the mean shift split into recall and prevalence terms (as `tables/displacement.py`) for BayCAP-Hf, `BayesCAP-H-real` and `BayesCAP-EB-real`; per bag, the NUTS diagnostics (divergences, largest split-$\hat R$, smallest effective sample size) and failed bags; per bag, the posterior mean of $m$, the median and $90\%$ interval of $\ln\nu$, $P(\nu>\min_jn_j)$, the median and $90\%$ interval of $\ln\kappa$, $P(\kappa<1)$; per pair, from $V$, the share of empty off-diagonal cells and the classes perfect and never right; a failure class for each pair with largest split-$\hat R$ above $1.1$ or more than $1\%$ of its draws divergent (changed after the pilot from more than $10\%$ divergent bags, see the Log): funnel if the mean posterior $P(\nu>\min_jn_j)$ exceeds $0.05$, else boundary (a class perfect or never right on $V$, or posterior median of $\kappa$ below $1$ with an empty off-diagonal cell), else other.
  - *Tables and plots:* a comparison table and a sampler-and-posterior table (one row per pair, by dataset and $K$; CSV, LaTeX, landscape PDF); three scatters: largest split-$\hat R$ of BayCAP-Hf against BayCAP-H; posterior median of $\kappa$ against the share of empty off-diagonal cells, coloured by the change of AE; Winkler score of BayCAP-Hf against BayCAP-H and against the empirical-Bayes method.
- **Estimand:** realised table of the target bag for `BayesCAP-Hf-real`; scored against the realised table (`true_cts`). The split of the shift uses target labels, so it is an analysis.
- **Scope:** `methods/baycap.py` (the variant of the hierarchical prior, the summaries of $\kappa$; docstring with estimand and represented sources; tests); `experiments/methods.py` (`BayesCAP-Hf-real` in `BAYCAP_VARIANTS`) and `experiments/main.py` (optional per-bag columns, §V.3); a new table and plot module, or an extension of `tables/baycap_h.py` and `plots/baycap_h.py`; labels in `tables/common.py` and `plots/common.py`; `Makefile` targets; §II.5, §II.12 and §V.3 of the work plan.
- **Depends on:** WP-10 (`c_prior="hierarchical"`, the stored results of `BayesCAP-H-real`, `tables/baycap_h.py`); WP-6 (stored `BayesCAP-real` and `BayesCAP-EB-real` with diagnostics).
- **Comparability:** adds one method to `main`; the stored results of every other method are untouched; same bags, seeds and NUTS settings as `BayesCAP-H-real`, so the comparison is bag by bag. Whether `BayesCAP-Hf-real` enters the tables, ranks and default plots of `main` is decided by the user; until its results are stored, every pair counts as incomplete for `check_missing.py` (§V.1).
- **Deviations from Part V:** the pilot uses four datasets with LR (except `hand_digits` kNN, a funnel pair of E2.7; `hand_digits` LR mixed), chosen for the two fixes.
- **Done when:** `BayesCAP-Hf-real` has stored results with diagnostics for all $75$ pairs and the three metrics, and the E2.8 tables and plots are generated; the results are described in Part II with their location, the `cap-ml` commit and the date of the run.
- **Commands:** all on `bcuda` (the default), each in its own tmux session; `make logs EXP=<target>` shows the output, `make explog EXP=main` the experiment log.
  - *Pilot:* the four pairs of the card, in two commands, since `main` crosses `--datasets` with `--classifiers`: `make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods BayesCAP-Hf-real --datasets mammographic wine-quality poker_hand --classifiers LR"`, then `make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods BayesCAP-Hf-real --datasets hand_digits --classifiers kNN"` (the BayCAP pool runs on CPU, `BAYES_ENV`).
  - *Full:* `make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods BayesCAP-Hf-real"` ($75\times100=7\,500$ NUTS runs); cells already stored, e.g. by the pilot, are skipped.
  - *Tables:* `make run-tables-baycap-hf ARGS=--tables` (needs the stored results of `BayesCAP-H-real`, `BayesCAP-EB-real` and `BayesCAP-real`; it works on the pairs stored for all four methods, so also after the pilot), then `make pull` and `make run-tables-baycap-hf WHERE=local ARGS=--pdf` where `pdflatex` is installed.
  - *Plots:* `make run-plot-baycap-hf` (reads the CSV of the tables; `ARGS="--figures tail"` for one figure), then `make pull`.
- **Log:** 2026-11-02: card added at the user's request. 2026-11-02: implementation started on branch `wp-11/baycap-hf`. `BayCAP(c_prior="hierarchical-f")` in `methods/baycap.py`: sites `h_m`, `h_rho` ($\rho=1/(1+\nu)$, uniform; `strength_from_rho` gives $\nu=1/\rho-1$), deterministic `h_nu`, `h_recalls` as in BayCAP-H; for $K\ge3$, `h_rho_kappa` ($1/(1+\kappa)$, uniform), deterministic `h_kappa`, and `h_shares` $\sim\operatorname{Dir}(\kappa\mathbf 1_{K-1})$ in every column. The NUTS diagnostics cover $\pi^Q$, $C$, $m$, $\rho$ and $1/(1+\kappa)$. `last_posterior_summaries` returns the summaries of BayCAP-H plus `KAPPA_SUMMARIES` (median, $5\%$ and $95\%$ quantiles of $\ln\kappa$, $P(\kappa<1)$; NaN with $K=2$); a bag where NUTS cannot start is kept as NaN with `nuts_failed=1`, as for BayCAP-H. `BayesCAP-Hf-real` is in `BAYCAP_VARIANTS`, so `main` runs it in the BayCAP pool; it is left out of the tables and ranks of `main` (`tables/main.py:EXCLUDED_METHODS`) and of the coverage vs amplitude plot until the user decides. New modules `tables/baycap_hf.py` (comparison table, sampler-and-posterior table, `analysis_summary`) and `plots/baycap_hf.py` (`tail`, `shares`, `scores`); `make` targets `tables-baycap-hf`, `plot-baycap-hf`. Implementation choices (not in the card): the scores and tests of a hierarchical method leave out its failed bags, and the paired tests use the bags where neither BayCAP-Hf nor BayCAP-H failed; each test (AE or Winkler score, against BayCAP-H or the empirical-Bayes method) is Holm-adjusted over the pairs on its own; the bootstrap generator of each test depends on the seed, the pair, the reference and the score; the posterior median of $\kappa$ of a pair, in the failure class, is the exponential of the mean over the bags of the median of $\ln\kappa$; the split of the shift uses the mean table of each bag (`estim_cts`) against `true_cts`, as `tables/displacement.py:error_terms`. The pilot runs in two commands, LR on three datasets and kNN on `hand_digits`, since `main` crosses the datasets with the classifiers. Tests in `tests/test_baycap.py`, `tests/test_baycap_hf.py`, `tests/test_methods.py`; 180 tests pass. Not run. 2026-11-02: pilot run by the user (both commands) and tables generated. Every pilot pair still fails the rule of the failure class (`mammographic` LR and `hand_digits` kNN `funnel`; `wine-quality` LR and `poker_hand` LR `boundary`, divergent in every bag), but the share of divergent bags alone does not tell rare from systematic divergences. So the sampler table gets, for BayCAP-Hf and BayCAP-H, the mean number of divergent transitions per bag and the share of divergent draws (`divergence_load`, over the bags with draws; `NUTS_DRAWS` is `BOOTSTRAP_ITERS` of `experiments/methods.py`, $1\,000$). These are computed from the stored `nuts_divergences`, with no new NUTS run. 2026-11-02: tables rerun by the user on the pilot. Divergent transitions per bag, BayCAP-H → BayCAP-Hf: `mammographic` LR $49.3\to8.8$, `hand_digits` kNN $15.1\to1.6$, `wine-quality` LR $0.05\to33.8$, `poker_hand` LR $0.75\to654.6$ (of $1\,000$ draws). A fix of the sampling of the shares (a non-centred parametrisation of their Gammas) was discussed and not adopted: the full run goes first, as it is. Deviation from the card, user decision: a pair fails on the share of its draws that are divergent (above $1\%$, `DIVERGENT_DRAWS`), not on the share of bags with divergent transitions (above $10\%$), since the latter counts a bag with a single divergent transition as divergent and set `hand_digits` kNN ($0.16\%$ of the draws divergent) next to `poker_hand` LR ($65\%$); `failure_class` takes that share and `analysis_summary` counts the pairs above it (`<prefix>_above_draws`). Pairs with a failure class are unreliable: reported, flagged, and left out of the decision on BayCAP-Hf. 181 tests pass. 2026-11-03: full run by the user on `bcuda` (all $75$ pairs, three metrics, no failed bag; date and commit of the run not recorded here); tables and plots generated on 2026-11-03 and copied locally. Analysis written in [[ext2_baycap]] (E2.8). `analysis_summary` now also gives the scores over the pairs without a failure class (`scores_reliable`, `is_unreliable`), which the decision rule of E2.8 needs; computed on the stored CSV, no rerun. 2026-11-03: card closed at the user's request; results in §II.18.
- **Outcome:** merged into `devel` on 2026-11-03 (`c90fcff`), at the user's request; no rebase needed. Decisions beyond the card are in the log: after the pilot, the mean number of divergent transitions per bag and the share of divergent draws in the sampler table, and the failure rule on the share of divergent draws ($1\%$) instead of the share of divergent bags ($10\%$), with unreliable pairs left out of the decision (user decision); the scores over the reliable pairs in `analysis_summary`. A non-centred parametrisation of the shares was discussed after the pilot and deferred until the full run; after it, the user moved it directly to the main section as E2.9 (shares only; the funnel of $\nu$ on `hand_digits` left as it is). Problems: with the centred Dirichlet the learned shares make NUTS diverge on $18$ pairs, and the lighter tail does not remove the funnel on `hand_digits`, so $23$ pairs are unreliable. The date and commit of the full run were not recorded. Unit tests: 181, all passing at the merge. Follow-ups: E2.9, the non-centred shares; whether `BayesCAP-Hf-real` enters the tables, ranks and plots of `main` is not decided; the tables, ranks and plots of `main` with `BayesCAP-H-real` (WP-10) are still not generated.

### WP-10 E2.7: BayCAP-H, the hierarchical prior on the recalls of BayCAP, on the pairs of `main`

- **Kind / status:** experiment / Done (2026-11-02)
- **Source:** [[ext2_baycap]] (E2.7, §T.14, §T.15)
- **Result:** §II.17 (code, run, findings)
- **Goal (as planned):** run BayCAP with a hierarchical prior on the recalls, as it is, to measure its coverage and its sampling problems before any countermeasure is chosen. Background: the empirical-Bayes prior of `BayesCAP-EB(-real)` plugs in a fitted typical recall $\hat m$ and strength $\hat\nu$; it removes the large-$K$ displacement of the uniform prior but collapses at $K=2$ and makes NUTS diverge when a Dirichlet parameter falls below $1$. The zero-cost screens of WP-9 (E2.6) found that a hierarchical prior barely depends on the prior on $\nu$ and that the classes can be pooled, but could not predict its effect on the width of the interval, and expect NUTS to struggle on pairs with a class perfect or never right on $V$ (boundary) and on pairs where $V$ supports nearly complete pooling (funnel).
  - *Method:* `BayesCAP-H-real`, BayCAP (`methods/baycap.py:BayCAP`) with a third prior on $C$: $m\sim U(0,1)$; $u=1/\sqrt{1+\nu}\sim U(0,1)$, i.e. $p(\nu)=\frac12(1+\nu)^{-3/2}$; the recalls $C_{jj}\mid m,\nu\sim\operatorname{Beta}(\nu m,\nu(1-m))$ independently; the off-diagonal cells of column $j$ equal to $(1-C_{jj})s_j$ with $s_j\sim\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1_{K-1})$; $(m,u)$ sampled by NUTS with $C$ and $\pi^Q$. Likelihood, prior on $\pi^Q$, NUTS settings and default seed as `BayesCAP`; realised pushforward only; centred parametrisation, no other sampler, no bound on $\nu$, no bag discarded for divergences.
  - *Runs:* in `main`, all $75$ pairs ($15$ datasets × $5$ classifiers), the $100$ UPP bags of size $1000$ of `main`, the three metrics of `main`; a pilot first on `mammographic`, `shuttle`, `poker_hand` and `isolet` with LR (funnel and boundary cases).
  - *Measures* (vanilla accuracy unless stated): AE, coverage, amplitude and Winkler score next to the stored `BayesCAP-real` and `BayesCAP-EB-real`; paired bootstrap tests of the mean Winkler difference against each of the two, Holm over the $75$ pairs, as in WP-6; per bag, the NUTS diagnostics of WP-6 (divergences, largest split-$\hat R$, smallest effective sample size) and failed bags; the posterior mean of $m$, the median and $90\%$ interval of $\ln\nu$, $P(\nu>\min_jn_j)$; the posterior mean and covariance of $\pi^Q$; the D4 prediction of coverage and amplitude for the same prior on the grid of WP-9 (`tables/hierarchical.py`, with the prior on $u$ added to `NU_PRIORS`); the residual variance $s^2-V_C(\bar\pi)$ of the accuracy draws of `BayesCAP-real` and of BayCAP-H ($V_C$ from the grid) and the prevalence part $\bar r^\top\Sigma_\pi\bar r$ of BayCAP-H; the share of divergent bags against the D3 indicators of WP-9 (posterior-parameter risk, classes perfect or never right on $V$, funnel risk).
  - *Tables and plots:* a comparison table and a sampler-and-checks table (one row per pair, by dataset and $K$; CSV, LaTeX, landscape PDF); four scatters: coverage against the two stored methods, observed against predicted coverage and amplitude (D4), residual variance against the uniform one and against its prevalence part, divergences against the D3 indicators.
- **Estimand:** realised table of the target bag for `BayesCAP-H-real`; scored against the realised table (`true_cts`). The D4 checks use target labels only for scoring, so they are analyses.
- **Scope:** `methods/baycap.py` (a third `c_prior`, the posterior moments of $\pi^Q$ kept for the records; docstring with estimand and represented sources; tests); `experiments/methods.py` (`BayesCAP-H-real` in `BAYCAP_VARIANTS`) and `experiments/main.py` (optional per-bag columns, §V.3); `tables/hierarchical.py` (`NU_PRIORS`); a new module in `tables/` and one in `plots/`; labels in `tables/common.py` and `plots/common.py`; `Makefile` targets; §II.5, §II.12 and §V.3 of the work plan.
- **Depends on:** WP-6 (the BayCAP methods, the NUTS diagnostics and the stored results of `BayesCAP-real` and `BayesCAP-EB-real` with diagnostics), WP-9 (the grid posterior, the D4 prediction and the D3 indicators of `tables/hierarchical.py`).
- **Comparability:** adds one method to `main`; the stored results of every other method are untouched; same bags, seeds and NUTS settings as the stored BayCAP methods, so the comparison is bag by bag. Whether `BayesCAP-H-real` enters the tables, ranks and default plots of `main` is decided by the user; until its results are stored, every pair counts as incomplete for `check_missing.py` (§V.1).
- **Deviations from Part V:** the pilot uses four datasets with LR, chosen for the risks of D3.
- **Done when:** `BayesCAP-H-real` has stored results with diagnostics for all $75$ pairs and the three metrics, and the E2.7 tables and plots are generated; the results are described in Part II with their location, the `cap-ml` commit and the date of the run.
- **Commands:** all on `bcuda` (the default), each in its own tmux session; `make logs EXP=<target>` shows the output, `make explog EXP=main` the experiment log.
  - *Pilot:* `make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods BayesCAP-H-real --datasets mammographic shuttle poker_hand isolet --classifiers LR"` (four pairs; the BayCAP pool runs on CPU, `BAYES_ENV`).
  - *Full:* `make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods BayesCAP-H-real"` ($75\times100=7\,500$ NUTS runs, cost to be estimated from the pilot); cells already stored, e.g. by the pilot, are skipped.
  - *Tables:* `make run-tables-baycap-h ARGS=--tables` (needs the stored results of `BayesCAP-real` and `BayesCAP-EB-real`; it works on the pairs stored for all three methods, so also after the pilot), then `make pull` and `make run-tables-baycap-h WHERE=local ARGS=--pdf` where `pdflatex` is installed.
  - *Plots:* `make run-plot-baycap-h` (reads the CSV of the tables; `ARGS="--figures d3"` for one figure), then `make pull`.
  - *Main tables, ranks and the coverage vs amplitude plot* now include `BayesCAP-H-real` (user's request of 2026-10-29): `make run-tables-main`, `make run-tables-rank`, `make run-plot-avc`, after the full run.
- **Log:** 2026-10-29: card added at the user's request. 2026-10-29: implementation started on branch `wp-10/baycap-h`; `BayCAP(c_prior="hierarchical")` in `methods/baycap.py` (sites `h_m`, `h_u`, deterministic `h_nu`, `h_recalls` in a plate over the classes, `h_shares` only for $K\ge3$, since $\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1_1)$ is the constant $1$; `p_c_cond_y` is a deterministic site built by `hierarchical_rates`); the NUTS diagnostics of this prior also cover $m$ and $u$; per bag, `last_posterior_summaries` returns the posterior mean of $m$, the median and $5\%$/$95\%$ quantiles of $\ln\nu$, $P(\nu>\min_jn_j)$, the posterior mean of the recalls, and the posterior mean and covariance of $\pi^Q$ (stored flat), which `experiments/main.py` stores as optional columns next to the diagnostics (§V.3). The posterior mean of the recalls is stored too, since the prevalence part $\bar r^\top\Sigma_\pi\bar r$ of §T.15 needs it. Decision (not in the card): if NUTS finds no valid initial parameters on a bag, that bag gets tables of NaN, NaN diagnostics and `nuts_failed=1`, so the other bags of the pair are kept (otherwise one failure drops the 100 bags of the pair); the other priors keep raising as before; the E2.7 scores and tests leave those bags out and report their share. `BayesCAP-H-real` is added to `BAYCAP_VARIANTS`, so `main` runs it in the BayCAP pool; `experiments/eb_binary.py` now lists its four methods explicitly, so E2.5 is unchanged. At the user's request, `BayesCAP-H-real` is in the tables and ranks of `main` and in the coverage vs amplitude plot (`plots/amplitude_vs_coverage.py:PLOT_METHODS`), not in the other default plots. `tables/hierarchical.py:NU_PRIORS` now holds log-density functions of $\ln\nu$, with the prior `unifu` of BayCAP-H ($u$ uniform, restricted to the grid); the screens of E2.6 keep their three priors (`SCREEN_PRIORS`), so the E2.6 outputs are unchanged. Decision (not in the card): `tables/baycap_h.py` computes D3 and D4 for `unifu` with the functions of `tables/hierarchical.py` instead of reading the CSV of E2.6, which would need a rerun of E2.6 with the new prior; the numbers are the same. Implementation choices: $\bar\pi$ of $V_C(\bar\pi)$ is the prevalence of the mean table of each bag (`estim_cts`) for both methods, as in D4; a pair with more than $10\%$ divergent bags is "funnel" when the mean posterior $P(\nu>\min_jn_j)$ of the run exceeds $0.5$ (`FUNNEL_HIGH`). New modules `tables/baycap_h.py`, `plots/baycap_h.py` (four figures: `coverage`, `d4`, `spread`, `d3`), tests in `tests/test_baycap.py`, `tests/test_baycap_h.py`, `tests/test_main_protocol.py`, `tests/test_methods.py`, `tests/test_hierarchical.py`, `tests/test_eb_binary.py`; 161 tests pass. `make` targets `tables-baycap-h`, `plot-baycap-h`. Not run. 2026-11-02: full run by the user on `bcuda` (all $75$ pairs, three metrics, no failed bag; date and commit of the run not recorded here); tables and plots generated on 2026-11-02 and copied locally. Analysis written in [[ext2_baycap]] (E2.7). The failure classes of `tables/baycap_h.py:failure_class` misclassify the funnel pairs (boundary checked first; funnel threshold $0.5$ too high for `mammographic`), so the Analysis reads the failures from $\hat R$ and the posterior of $\nu$; the rule is unchanged. 2026-11-02: card closed at the user's request; results in §II.17.
- **Outcome:** merged into `devel` on 2026-11-02 (`a818ba8`), at the user's request; no rebase needed (`devel` had no new commits). Decisions beyond the card are in the log: failed bags kept as NaN with `nuts_failed=1` (none occurred in the run); D3 and D4 computed in `tables/baycap_h.py` with the functions of `tables/hierarchical.py`; `BayesCAP-H-real` in the tables and ranks of `main` (user's request). Problems: `failure_class` checks the boundary first and calls a pair a funnel only above $P(\nu>\min_jn_j)=0.5$, so it misclassifies `hand_digits` SVM and RF and the `mammographic` pairs; the Analysis reads the failures from $\hat R$ and the posterior of $\nu$, and the rule is left unchanged. The date and commit of the run were not recorded. Unit tests: 161, all passing at the merge. Follow-ups: the tables, ranks and plots of `main` with `BayesCAP-H-real` are not generated yet; BayCAP-Hf (E2.8, card WP-11), with a lighter tail on $\nu$ against the funnel and learned shares of the mistakes against the gap with the empirical-Bayes prior on `wine-quality` and `poker_hand`; the narrowing of the spread on pairs with high recalls is not explained.

### WP-9 E2.6: zero-cost screens for a hierarchical prior on the recalls of BayCAP

- **Kind / status:** analysis / Done (2026-10-29)
- **Source:** [[ext2_baycap]] (E2.6, §T.14)
- **Result:** §II.16 (code, run, findings)
- **Goal (as planned):** before implementing a hierarchical prior on the recalls of BayCAP (a prior on the typical recall $m$ and strength $\nu$ of the Beta prior of the recalls, instead of the plug-in $(\hat m,\hat\nu)$ of the empirical-Bayes prior), screen four possible problems at no run cost, on the 75 pairs of `main`, vanilla accuracy (the binary datasets of E2.5 stay in E2.5, user decision of 2026-10-29).
  - *Grid posterior:* $p(m,\nu\mid V)$ from the validation counts alone (product of beta-binomial likelihoods of the diagonal counts), on the grid of `scripts/toys/ext2_hierarchical.py` (200 values of $m$ in $[0.0025,0.9975]$, 200 of $\ln\nu$ in $[\ln0.1,\ln10^4]$), uniform prior on $m$, three priors on $\nu$: $\nu^{-3/2}$, log-uniform, flat; mixture mean and variance of each recall and the variance of accuracy due to $C$, $V_C(\pi)$, by the laws of total expectation and variance.
  - *D4, prediction validated on EB:* per bag, the stored draws of `BayesCAP` / `BayesCAP-real` moved by $\Delta=\sum_j\bar\pi_j(\mathbb E_{\text{new}}[r_j]-\mathbb E_{\text{u}}[r_j])$ ($\bar\pi$ from `estim_cts`) and rescaled so that their variance replaces $V_{C,\text{u}}(\bar\pi)$ with $V_{C,\text{new}}(\bar\pi)$; coverage, amplitude, Winkler score of the predicted draws; validated against the stored `BayesCAP-EB` / `BayesCAP-EB-real`, then applied to the three hierarchical priors.
  - *Diagnosis of D4:* per bag, the change of the centre of accuracy between the stored uniform and empirical-Bayes posterior-mean tables, split exactly into a recall part $R=\sum_j\hat\pi^{u}_j(\hat r^{EB}_j-\hat r^{u}_j)$ and a prevalence part $P=\sum_j(\hat\pi^{EB}_j-\hat\pi^{u}_j)\hat r^{EB}_j$; the EB prediction repeated with the stored $P$ added to the shift, and with the shift replaced by the stored $R+P$; their coverage, amplitude and Winkler score.
  - *D1:* ratio of the 90% widths of the posterior and prior of $\ln\nu$; predicted recall-term change and $V_C$ at the uniform prevalence and at the vertices; range of the D4 predictions over the three priors.
  - *D2:* leave-one-class-out predictive p-value of each $f_{jj}$ and vertex shift $z_j=(\mathbb E[r_j\mid V]-f_{jj}/n_j)/\operatorname{sd}(r_j\mid V)$; per pair the smallest p-value, the classes below $0.01$, the largest $\lvert z_j\rvert$.
  - *D3:* $P(\min(\nu m,\nu(1-m))<1\mid V)$ and the 90% width of $\ln\nu$; amended with the parameters of the posterior of the recalls, $P(\min_j\min(a_j,b_j)<1\mid V)$ with $a_j=\nu m+f_{jj}$, $b_j=\nu(1-m)+n_j-f_{jj}$, the number of classes perfect ($f_{jj}=n_j$) and never right ($f_{jj}=0$) on $V$, the funnel indicator $P(\nu>\min_jn_j\mid V)$, and the share of cells of $C$ with an empirical-Bayes posterior Dirichlet parameter $\hat\alpha_{ij}+f_{ij}$ below $1$; all against the stored share of divergent bags of `BayesCAP-EB`.
  - *Tables and plots:* a validation table and a screens table (one row per pair, by dataset and $K$; CSV, LaTeX, landscape PDF); six scatters (validation, diagnosis of D4, prediction, sensitivity, exchangeability, sampler risk).
- **Estimand:** the D4 predictions keep the estimand of the method they start from (population for `BayesCAP`, realised for `BayesCAP-real`), all scored against the realised table (`true_cts`); D1–D3 use only $V$. D4 uses target labels for scoring, so it is an analysis, not a method.
- **Scope:** a new module in `src/uncawe/tables/` and one in `src/uncawe/plots/`; `Makefile` targets; tests; §II of the work plan. No change to `methods/`, `experiments/` or stored results.
- **Depends on:** WP-6 and WP-7 (stored results of the four BayCAP methods with NUTS diagnostics, `tables/eb_prior.py`, `tables/displacement.py:load_validation_counts`).
- **Comparability:** reads stored results only; nothing is rerun or deleted.
- **Deviations from Part V:** none expected.
- **Done when:** the E2.6 tables and plots, with the diagnosis of D4, are generated on all pairs of `main`, and described in Part II with their location and the date.
- **Commands:** on `bcuda` (the default; it needs the pretrain files of the domain `classic` and the stored results of the four BayCAP methods in `main`).
  - *Tables:* `make run-tables-hierarchical ARGS=--tables`, which also prints the summaries of the Analysis; then `make pull` and `make run-tables-hierarchical WHERE=local ARGS=--pdf` where `pdflatex` is installed.
  - *Plots:* `make run-plot-hierarchical` after the tables, since the plots read their CSV (`ARGS="--figures validation"` for one figure); then `make pull`.
- **Log:** 2026-10-29: card added by the user. 2026-10-29: started on branch `wp-9/hierarchical-screens`; code and unit tests done, not run (§II.16). Decisions taken during the implementation: the grid posterior keeps, for the mixture moments and the predictive p-values, only the grid points that carry $1-10^{-6}$ of its mass; the plots read the CSV of the tables instead of recomputing the grid posteriors; the pairs of `main` are labelled `E2.4` and those of `eb_binary` `E2.5` (marked $^\ddagger$ in the tables, ringed in the plots); a predicted draw whose variance would not be positive is left out and counted (`<pushforward>_<prior>_pred_undefined`). 2026-10-29: first run by the user on the pairs of `main` (E2.4 source only). The D4 validation missed on a few pairs (wine-quality LR/SVM, poker_hand, nursery MLP); hypothesis: D4 keeps the estimated prevalences fixed, while the stored empirical-Bayes prior also changes the off-diagonal cells of $C$ and, through them, the prevalences. Added the diagnosis of D4 (`centre_split`, the `ebprev` and `ebcentre` predictions, the `centre` figure). By decision of the user, the binary datasets of E2.5 were dropped from E2.6 (no `--sources`, no marks or rings). The run is to be repeated. 2026-10-29: D3 amended by decision of the user, since NUTS samples the posterior, whose Beta parameters include the counts of $V$: added `posterior_alpha_risk`, `funnel_risk`, `eb_posterior_low_share` and the counts of perfect and never-right classes; the `sampler` figure has four panels, one per indicator. 2026-10-29: second run by the user, with the diagnosis and the amended D3 (§II.16); analysis written in [[ext2_baycap]] (E2.6); card closed by decision of the user, who judged D1 and D2 resolved and D3 and D4 open, to be settled by a run of the model (E2.7).
- **Outcome:** merged into `devel` on 2026-10-29 (`2b6c22d`), after the user's go-ahead; no rebase needed (`devel` had no new commits). Decisions and changes during the card, beyond the log: the diagnosis of D4 and the amendment of D3 were added after the first run, on the user's requests; the binary datasets of E2.5 were dropped from E2.6 (user decision); the width ratio of $\ln\nu$ (D1) turned out not to be meaningful for the flat prior, whose own $90\%$ width in $\ln\nu$ is small, and is not used in the analysis. Unit tests: 137, all passing at the merge. Follow-ups: the run of the hierarchical model (E2.7, card WP-10), which needs the prior $u=1/\sqrt{1+\nu}\sim U(0,1)$ on $\nu$ in `NU_PRIORS` (not among the three priors of E2.6) for its D4 check; the hypothesis that D4 misses the spread through the variance of the estimated prevalences, tested by E2.7; the D1 width measure, if D1 is used again, should report the width of the posterior of $\ln\nu$ directly.

### WP-7 E2.5: the empirical-Bayes prior of BayCAP on all the binary datasets of `quapy`

- **Kind / status:** experiment / Done (2026-10-29)
- **Source:** [[ext2_baycap]] (E2.5, §T.10)
- **Result:** §II.15 (code, run, findings)
- **Goal (as planned):** test whether the failures of the empirical-Bayes prior on binary datasets found by E2.4 (WP-6) appear on every binary problem, and whether diagnostics computed from the validation set alone predict which pairs fail. Background: with $K=2$ the strength $\hat\nu$ of the Beta prior on the recalls is fitted from a single difference between two recalls, so it often reaches its upper bound $10^4$ and the plug-in prior then drops the uncertainty of its centre $\hat m$ (interval too narrow); with a moderate $\hat\nu$ it pulls the true-positive and true-negative rates together.
  - *Datasets:* the binary datasets of `quapy` (`UCI_BINARY_DATASETS`) not used by `main`: `transfusion`, `breast-cancer`, `wdbc`, `ionosphere`, `haberman`, `spectf`, `sonar`, `balance.1`, `balance.3`, `cmc.1`, `cmc.2`, `cmc.3`, `ctg.1`, `ctg.2`, `ctg.3`, `iris.1`, `iris.2`, `iris.3`, `wine.1`, `wine.2`, `wine.3`, `wine-q-red`, `wine-q-white`, `yeast`, `pageblocks.5` ($25$ datasets).
  - *Pretraining:* the $5$ classifiers of `pretrain/classic.py:gen_classifiers` on these datasets, with the split of `cap-ml`'s `fetch_UCIBinaryDataset`, stored so that `main` does not see them (`pretrain/classic.py:gen_datasets` and `experiments/datasets.py:get_filtered_datasets` unchanged).
  - *Experiment:* a new experiment, separate from `main`, with the bag protocol of `main` ($100$ UPP bags of size $1000$ per pair, same seeds) and the four methods of `experiments/methods.py:BAYCAP_VARIANTS` (`BayesCAP`, `BayesCAP-real`, `BayesCAP-EB`, `BayesCAP-EB-real`), storing results and NUTS diagnostics in the format of `main` under its own folder of `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/`; `--datasets`, `--classifiers` and `--methods` filters; a `make run-<name>` target.
  - *Measures, per pair:* $n^s_1$, $n^s_2$, $\hat r_1$, $\hat r_2$; the gap statistic $z=(\hat r_1-\hat r_2)/\sqrt{\hat r_1(1-\hat r_1)/n^s_1+\hat r_2(1-\hat r_2)/n^s_2}$; $\hat m$, $\hat\nu$, bound flags, $\operatorname{sd}(\hat m)$, the left-out standard deviation; the check of E2.4 (predicted and observed change of the recall term) and the predicted pull $\sum_j\pi_j(1-w_j)(\hat m-\hat r_j)$, averaged over the bags; for the four methods, coverage, amplitude, Winkler score, $\mathcal C_s$, shift, $R$, $R_0$, $P$, the Winkler tests of E2.4 (empirical Bayes minus uniform within each pushforward, Holm over the pairs of E2.5) and the NUTS diagnostics.
  - *Tables and plots:* reading the new results together with the $25$ binary pairs of `main` (E2.4, marked): a comparison table and a fit table, one row per pair; three scatters: $\lvert z\rvert$ against $\hat\nu$ (log scale), the left-out standard deviation (log scale) against the change of realised coverage, and the predicted against the observed change of the recall term. Reuse `tables/eb_prior.py` and `tables/displacement.py:pair_table`.
- **Estimand:** population table for `BayesCAP` and `BayesCAP-EB`, realised table for `BayesCAP-real` and `BayesCAP-EB-real`; all four scored against the realised table of each bag (`true_cts`). The fit diagnostics use only the labels of $V$; the check and the error terms use target labels, so they are an analysis.
- **Scope:** `pretrain/` (a pretraining entry point or option for the new datasets); a new experiment module in `src/uncawe/experiments/` and its entry point; new modules in `src/uncawe/tables/` and `src/uncawe/plots/`; `Makefile` targets; tests. `main`, its pretrained classifiers, results, tables and ranks are unchanged.
- **Depends on:** WP-6 (the four BayCAP methods, the NUTS diagnostics, `tables/eb_prior.py`); stored `main` results for the binary pairs of E2.4.
- **Comparability:** new pretrained classifiers and a new results folder; nothing stored is rerun or deleted. The pairs are comparable with the binary pairs of E2.4 (same protocol, methods and seeds) but are not part of the grid of `main`. Cause 4 (the finite test pool) is not studied (user's decision of 2026-10-28); datasets with very small test pools (`iris.x`, `wine.x`) are kept and flagged. The sizes of the datasets, and whether the $5$ classifiers train on the smallest ones, are to be checked first.
- **Deviations from Part V:** none.
- **Done when:** the full runs of the four methods have stored results with diagnostics for all pairs, and the E2.5 tables and plots are generated; the results are described in Part II with their location, the `cap-ml` commit and the date of the runs.
- **Commands:** all on `bcuda` (the default), each in its own tmux session; `make logs EXP=<target>` shows the output, `make explog EXP=eb_binary` the experiment log.
  - *Sizes check (no training):* `make run-pretrain-classic-binary ARGS=--sizes`, then `make logs EXP=pretrain-classic-binary`: $|L|$, $|V|$, $|U|$ and the class counts of the $25$ datasets.
  - *Pretraining:* `make run-pretrain-classic-binary` (all $25$ datasets, $5$ classifiers; `ARGS="--datasets ..."` restricts it); log `make explog EXP=pretrain.classic_binary`.
  - *Pilot:* `make run-eb-binary ARGS="--datasets wdbc iris.1 --classifiers LR"` (the four methods on a medium and a very small test pool).
  - *Full, one method per run:* `make run-eb-binary ARGS="--methods BayesCAP"`, then the same with `BayesCAP-real`, `BayesCAP-EB` and `BayesCAP-EB-real` (each $125\times100=12\,500$ NUTS runs, cost to be estimated from the pilot); cells already stored, e.g. by the pilot, are skipped.
  - *Tables:* `make run-tables-eb-binary ARGS=--tables` (also needs the stored `main` results of the four methods), then `make pull` and `make run-tables-eb-binary WHERE=local ARGS=--pdf` where `pdflatex` is installed.
  - *Plots:* `make run-plot-eb-binary` (`ARGS="--figures fit"` for one figure), then `make pull`.
- **Log:** 2026-10-28: card added by the user. 2026-10-28: started on branch `wp-7/eb-binary`; code and unit tests done, no run yet (§II.15). The new pairs use the pretrain domain `classic_binary` (`pretrain/classic_binary.py`), so `main`, which reads the domain `classic`, does not see them; `pretrain/classic.py:pretrain` takes the datasets and the domain. The experiment reuses the protocol functions of `experiments/main.py`, which take the results folder as an argument (default `main`). Decisions taken during the implementation: the predicted pull $\sum_j\pi_j(1-w_j)(\hat m-\hat r_j)$ is identically the prior part $R_0$ of the empirical-Bayes methods, because $\frac{f_{jj}+\hat\nu\hat m}{n^s_j+\hat\nu}-\hat r_j=(1-w_j)(\hat m-\hat r_j)$, so the fit table shows $R_0$ under the uniform prior and the pull, not a third column; the Winkler tests of the rows of `main` keep the Holm adjustment of E2.4 (over its $75$ pairs), those of E2.5 are adjusted over the pairs of E2.5; the pairs of `main` are marked with $^\ddagger$ in the tables and with a ring in the plots; the dataset sizes have not been checked yet (sizes check in `Commands`). 2026-10-29: pretraining of the $5$ classifiers on the $25$ datasets (2026-10-28, without the sizes check) and the full runs of the four methods, in one invocation of `experiments/eb_binary.py` with `--methods BayesCAP BayesCAP-real BayesCAP-EB BayesCAP-EB-real` (2026-10-28 14:33 to 2026-10-29 01:45, `bcuda`, `JAX=cpu`, commit `ffa378f`, `cap-ml` commit `bb20aa5`), all $500$ cells stored; tables and plots generated on 2026-10-29. Analysis not written yet. 2026-10-29: analysis written in [[ext2_baycap]] (E2.5, and §T.10 on the pull with a fitted $\hat\nu$); `tables/eb_binary.py` gets the pool size, the smaller Beta parameter, the amplitude ratio and `analysis_summary`, which produce its numbers (§II.15); card closed.
- **Outcome:** merged in `c9c6b91` (2026-10-29); the conflicts with `devel` (status table of [[ext2_baycap]], board) were resolved by keeping both sides. Sizes check not run before the pretraining: $\lvert V\rvert$ ranges from $53$ (`iris.x`) to $1916$ (`pageblocks.5`) and $\lvert U\rvert$ from $45$ to $1642$, and $9$ datasets have $\lvert U\rvert<100$ (`iris.x`, `wine.x`, `haberman`, `sonar`, `spectf`). On `iris.1` every classifier is perfect on $V$, so the gap statistic is undefined and $\hat\nu$ reaches its lower bound $10^{-3}$, with $\hat m$ at its upper bound; NUTS diverges on every bag of the empirical-Bayes methods there. The analysis added `n_test`, `eb_alpha_min`, `realised_amplitude_ratio` and `analysis_summary` to `tables/eb_binary.py`, on the same branch, merged again into `devel`. Follow-ups (not scheduled): the binary under-coverage of both priors points to cause 4; the divergences of the uniform prior on the small datasets are not explained.

### WP-6 E2.4: BayCAP with an empirical-Bayes prior on the recalls

- **Kind / status:** experiment / Done (2026-10-28)
- **Source:** [[ext2_baycap]] (E2.4)
- **Result:** §II.14 (code, run, findings); the BayCAP methods of `main` and `c_prior` in §II.5; the `BayCAP` row of §II.12
- **Goal (as planned):** evaluate the empirical-Bayes prior on the columns of $C$ (WP-5) against the $\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1)$ prior, with both pushforwards, each method in its own run. The $\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1)$ prior pulls every recall towards $1/K$, which displaces BayCAP's interval on datasets with large $K$ and accurate classifiers; the empirical-Bayes prior pulls each recall towards the typical recall $\hat m$ of the classifier, with strength $\hat\nu$, both estimated from $V$ by maximum likelihood of the beta-binomial.
  - *Methods:* two new methods of `main`, excluded by default from the plots of `main`, like `BayesCAP-real`, and from its tables and ranks except `BayesCAP-EB-real` (user's request of 2026-10-27): `BayesCAP-EB` (`BayCAP`, `estimand="population"`, `c_prior="empirical-bayes"`) and `BayesCAP-EB-real` (`estimand="realised"`), with the prior on $\pi^Q$, the NUTS settings and the seed of `BayesCAP`. `BayesCAP` is built as `BayCAP(acc, estimand="population")` instead of `cap-ml`'s `ACC_BayesCAP` (user's decision of 2026-10-26), so that it can store the diagnostics.
  - *NUTS diagnostics:* `BayCAP` exposes, for each `predict_ct_range` call, the number of divergent transitions, the largest split-$\hat R$ (one chain split in two halves) and the smallest effective sample size over the parameters of $\pi^Q$ and $C$; the evaluation of contingency methods in `experiments/main.py` stores them as new per-bag columns of the results, for `BayesCAP`, `BayesCAP-real`, `BayesCAP-EB` and `BayesCAP-EB-real`. `BayesCAP` and `BayesCAP-real` are run again to store them.
  - *Check (no runs):* per pair, the mean over the bags of $\sum_j\pi^{(b)}_j(\tilde r_j-r^{(b)}_j)$, with $\pi^{(b)}$ and $r^{(b)}_j$ the true class proportions and realised recalls of bag $b$, and of its absolute value, for three estimates of the recalls from $V$ alone: plain $f_j/n_j$, $\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1)$ $\frac{f_j+1}{n_j+K}$, empirical Bayes $\frac{f_j+\hat\nu\hat m}{n_j+\hat\nu}$; also $\hat m$, $\hat\nu$, bounds reached, and the standard deviation left out by empirical Bayes, $\sum_j\pi_j(1-w_j)\operatorname{sd}(\hat m)$ with $w_j=n_j/(n_j+\hat\nu)$ and $\pi$ uniform.
  - *Tables and plots:* a comparison table, one row per pair grouped by dataset and ordered by $K$, with coverage, amplitude and Winkler score of the four methods and, within each pushforward, the paired bootstrap test of the mean Winkler difference of E2.3 (empirical Bayes minus $\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1)$, Holm-adjusted over the $75$ pairs); a prior table with $K$, minimum and median $n_j$, the check, $R$, $R_0$ and $P$ of `BayesCAP` and `BayesCAP-EB`, and the diagnostics of the four methods; a scatter of the predicted against the observed change of the recall term, with a panel validating the $\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1)$ check against $R$ of `BayesCAP`; a scatter of coverage with the empirical-Bayes prior against coverage with $\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1)$, one panel per pushforward; the E2.2 table and scatters of the two new methods (`tables/displacement.py` and `plots/displacement.py` with `--method`).
- **Estimand:** population table for `BayesCAP` and `BayesCAP-EB`, realised table for `BayesCAP-real` and `BayesCAP-EB-real`; all four scored against the realised table (`true_cts`). The check and the error measures use target labels, so they are an analysis.
- **Scope:** `experiments/methods.py` (`BayesCAP` from `BayCAP`, the two new methods); `methods/baycap.py` (the diagnostics); `experiments/main.py` (the diagnostic columns); the exclusion lists of `tables/main.py` and `plots/common.py`; a new table module in `src/uncawe/tables/` (reusing `tables/pushforward.py` for the bootstrap test and `tables/displacement.py` for the validation counts and the decomposition) and a new plot module in `src/uncawe/plots/`; `Makefile` targets; tests; §I.2 (`BayesCAP` built from `BayCAP`), §II.5, §II.12.
- **Depends on:** WP-5; stored `main` results and pretrain data; `BayesCAP` on CPU (§I.2).
- **Comparability:** `BayesCAP` and `BayesCAP-real` are rerun, and their stored parquet files must be deleted first, because `main` skips existing results. Before deleting, `scripts/bench/xla_vs_stored.py` must show that `BayCAP(estimand="population")` reproduces the stored `BayesCAP` results on CPU; the rerun then changes no stored measure and only adds the diagnostic columns, and the paper's tables are unaffected. The other methods of `main` are untouched.
- **Deviations from Part V:** none expected.
- **Done when:** the full runs of the four methods have stored results with diagnostics, and the E2.4 tables and plots are generated; the results are described in Part II with their location, the `cap-ml` commit and the date of the runs.
- **Commands:** runs on `bcuda` with `JAX=cpu` (the `Makefile` default), one method per run, in this order. Each full run is $75\times100=7500$ NUTS runs, expected to take about as long as the CPU recomputation of `BayesCAP` (about $46$ minutes, §I.2).
  - *Pilot:* `make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods BayesCAP-EB --datasets german dry-bean letter --classifiers LR"`, then the same with `--methods BayesCAP-EB-real`; then `make run-tables-eb-prior ARGS=--tables` (the table keeps the pairs stored for all four methods; the diagnostics of `BayesCAP` and `BayesCAP-real` are NaN until their rerun). The pilot results stay in `<OUT_DIR>/uncawe/main/` and the full run skips them.
  - *Full, new methods:* `make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods BayesCAP-EB"`, then `make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods BayesCAP-EB-real"`.
  - *Check before the rerun:* `make run-bench-xla-stored ARGS="--methods BayesCAP BayesCAP-real"` (default: 5 datasets, LR; `--datasets` and `--classifiers` widen it); every row must have `identical=True`.
  - *Rerun with diagnostics:* delete the stored files of the two methods (all three metrics), listing them first: `ssh bcuda 'find /home/volpi/uncawe-devel/output/uncawe/main -name BayesCAP.parquet -print'` and then the same with `-delete`, and likewise with `-name BayesCAP-real.parquet`; then `make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods BayesCAP"` and `make run-main-classic ARGS="--methods BayesCAP-real"`.
  - *Tables and plots:* `make run-tables-eb-prior ARGS=--tables` and `make run-plot-eb-prior` on `bcuda`; the E2.2 outputs of the new methods with `make run-tables-displacement ARGS="--tables --method BayesCAP-EB"` and `make run-plot-displacement ARGS="--method BayesCAP-EB"` (and the same with `BayesCAP-EB-real`); after `make pull`, `make run-tables-eb-prior WHERE=local ARGS="--pdf --table-dir output/uncawe/tables/eb_prior"` (and likewise for the displacement tables) where `pdflatex` is installed.
- **Log:** 2026-10-26: card added by the user. 2026-10-26: started on branch `wp-6/eb-prior-eval`; code and unit tests done, no run yet. The four BayCAP methods are built from `experiments/methods.py:BAYCAP_VARIANTS`. The NUTS diagnostics are computed after each `predict_ct_range` call from the draws of $\pi^Q$ and $C$ kept by `BayCAP` (`last_nuts_diagnostics`), outside the timed calls, so `t_test_ave` measures what it measured before. Decision taken during the implementation: in `tables/displacement.py` the prior part $R_0$ of an empirical-Bayes method uses its own prior, $\frac{f_{jj}+\hat\nu\hat m}{n^s_j+\hat\nu}$ (so $R_0$ is always "the shift the method's prior adds to the recalls of $V$"); for `BayesCAP` and `BayesCAP-real` it is unchanged. The E2.4 table module (`tables/eb_prior.py`) writes the two tables of the card from one per-pair CSV, on the pairs where all four methods are stored, with NaN diagnostics for results stored without them, so that the pilot can run before the rerun of `BayesCAP` and `BayesCAP-real`. 2026-10-27: `BayesCAP-EB-real` added to the tables and ranks of `main` at the user's request, and `BayesCAP-real`, `BayesCAP-EB` and `BayesCAP-EB-real` to the coverage vs amplitude plot (not to the other default plots); a fix of the E2.4 plot labels (`\mathbf{1}` needs braces in mathtext). 2026-10-26: full runs of the four methods by the user on `bcuda` (`cap-ml` `bb20aa5`), `BayesCAP` and `BayesCAP-real` after deleting their stored files; the rerun reproduces the scores used by E2.3 (identical coverage on every pair, Winkler scores within $2\cdot10^{-15}$). 2026-10-27: E2.4 tables and plots and the E2.2 outputs of the two new methods generated by the user and copied locally. 2026-10-28: the E2.4 analysis written in [[ext2_baycap]].
- **Outcome:** merged into `devel` on 2026-10-28 (`77301e1`), after the user's go-ahead; the branch was rebased on `devel` first, without conflicts. Decisions during the card: `BayesCAP` built from `BayCAP` (user's decision of 2026-10-26); $R_0$ of an empirical-Bayes method uses its own prior (log); `BayesCAP-EB-real` in the tables and ranks of `main`, and the three E2 methods in the coverage vs amplitude plot (user's requests of 2026-10-27). The check before the rerun (`scripts/bench/xla_vs_stored.py`) is not recorded in the log; the rerun itself reproduces the stored scores of E2.3, which is what the check was meant to guarantee. Unit tests: 101, all passing at the merge. Follow-ups: (1) on binary datasets the beta-binomial fit has two counts, $\hat\nu$ can reach its upper bound, and the empirical-Bayes prior lowers coverage (`mammographic`, `german`); (2) concentrations below $1$ make NUTS diverge on $18$ pairs and prevent mixing on `isolet`, so the results of the empirical-Bayes methods on those pairs are not reliable; (3) the strong pooling on `nursery` MLP, with a small left-out standard deviation, is not explained (guess: one class with a much lower recall); (4) the tables and rank tables of `main` and the coverage vs amplitude plot were generated again by the user on 2026-10-27, with `BayesCAP-EB-real` and on the CPU results of `BayesCAP` (this closes the follow-up of WP-4).

### WP-5 Empirical-Bayes prior on the columns of $C$ for `BayCAP`

- **Kind / status:** method / Done (2026-10-26)
- **Source:** [[ext2_baycap]] (E2.4, §T.9)
- **Result:** §II.5 (`c_prior`, not evaluated); the `BayCAP` row of §II.12
- **Goal (as planned):** add to `BayCAP` (`src/uncawe/methods/baycap.py`) a hyperparameter `c_prior` that selects the prior on the columns of $C$. `"uniform"` (default) is $\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1_K)$, the current model, unchanged. `"empirical-bayes"` gives column $j$ the prior $\operatorname{Dir}(\alpha_{\cdot j})$ with $\alpha_{jj}=\hat\nu\hat m$ and $\alpha_{ij}=\hat\nu(1-\hat m)/(K-1)$ for $i\ne j$, so that the recall $C_{jj}$ has the prior $\operatorname{Beta}(\hat\nu\hat m,\hat\nu(1-\hat m))$. $(\hat m,\hat\nu)$ are fitted in `fit` by maximising the beta-binomial log-likelihood $\sum_j[\ln B(f_j+\nu m,\,n_j-f_j+\nu(1-m))-\ln B(\nu m,\,\nu(1-m))]$ of the diagonal counts $f_j$ of the validation table, out of the $n_j$ validation instances of each class, numerically over $(\operatorname{logit}m,\ln\nu)$, within $\nu\le10^4$ and $m\in[10^{-3},1-10^{-3}]$; the fit stores $\hat m$, $\hat\nu$, whether a bound was reached, and $\operatorname{sd}(\hat m)$ from the curvature of the log-likelihood at its maximum. Everything else of the BayesianCC model is unchanged: the likelihoods of $V$ and of the target predicted counts, the prior on $\pi^Q$, NUTS, the seed, and both estimands. In the `cap` layout a column of $C$ is a row of `p_c_cond_y`, so the concentration matrix is built transposed. Why: the $\operatorname{Dir}(\mathbf 1)$ prior pulls every recall towards $1/K$, which displaces BayCAP's interval on datasets with large $K$ and accurate classifiers; the empirical-Bayes prior pulls each recall towards the typical recall of the classifier.
- **Estimand:** as `BayCAP`: population table with `estimand="population"`, realised table with `estimand="realised"`.
- **Scope:** `methods/baycap.py` (`c_prior`, the fit of $(\hat m,\hat\nu)$, the concentration matrix in `model`); `tests/test_baycap.py`; §II.5 and the `BayCAP` row of §II.12. Not in this card: the methods of `main`, the diagnostics and the E2.4 analysis (WP-6).
- **Depends on:** none.
- **Comparability:** with `c_prior="uniform"` the model and the NUTS draws are unchanged, so no stored result changes.
- **Deviations from Part V:** none expected.
- **Done when:** code and tests are merged into `devel`, and §II.5 describes `c_prior` as not evaluated. The tests check that the log-likelihood matches `scipy.stats.betabinom`; that the fit recovers $(m,\nu)$ on synthetic counts with many classes; that, when all classes share one recall, the fit reaches the upper bound of $\nu$ and gives $\hat m=\sum_jf_j/\sum_jn_j$; that the concentration matrix has diagonal $\hat\nu\hat m$ and off-diagonal sums $\hat\nu(1-\hat m)$, in the `cap` layout; and that `c_prior="uniform"` reproduces the current draws for a fixed `random_state`.
- **Commands:** none (method only).
- **Log:** 2026-10-26: card added by the user. 2026-10-26: started on branch `wp-5/eb-prior`; code and unit tests done (§II.5). The fit uses L-BFGS-B with the analytic gradient from three starting values of $\ln\nu$ ($0$, $3$, $6$) and the pooled recall; besides the bounds of the card it bounds $\nu\ge10^{-3}$, as a numerical safeguard (reported as a bound reached, like the others). $\operatorname{sd}(\hat m)$ is NaN when $\hat m$ is on a bound; when $\hat\nu$ is on a bound, $\nu$ is treated as fixed and only the curvature in $\operatorname{logit}m$ is used. When all classes share one recall, $\hat m$ at $\nu=10^4$ differs from the pooled recall by $O(1/\nu)$ (e.g. $3\cdot10^{-5}$), so the test allows $10^{-4}$.
- **Outcome:** merged into `devel` on 2026-10-26 (`cb8d953`), after the user's review. Deviations from the card are in the log (the safeguard $\nu\ge10^{-3}$, the handling of $\operatorname{sd}(\hat m)$ on a bound, the tolerance on the pooled recall). The concentration matrix is symmetric, so it is the same in the paper's layout and in the `cap` layout. The branch also added `make test`, which runs the unit tests in the foreground (`Makefile`, commit `cfb964c`), not part of the card. Unit tests: 80, all passing at the merge. Follow-up: the evaluation is WP-6 (E2.4).
